## Part XVII. Polyfold theory for categories (HWZ Chapter 17, §§17.1–17.8)

Chapter 17 (Springer 2021, pp. 743–842; 2017 numbers in parentheses) carries polyfold theory from spaces to categories. Instead of a topological space with a class of ep-groupoid structures, the starting point is a groupoidal category $C$ (all morphisms invertible, finitely many between any two objects) whose orbit space $|C|$ carries a metrizable topology, a GCT. A polyfold structure on $C$ is a choice, for every object $\alpha$, of a set $F(\alpha)$ of local uniformizers $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ at $\alpha$, together with M-polyfold structures on the transition sets $M(\Psi, \Psi')$. Every covering family $\Psi$ of uniformizers gives an ep-groupoid $X_\Psi$, the model, with an equivalence $\Gamma_\Psi : X_\Psi \to C$; any two models are related by a canonical generalized isomorphism $f_{\Psi, \Psi'}$, and every notion on $C$ is defined through uniformizers and shown to agree with the corresponding notion of Chapters 7–16 on the models. §17.1 (2017: §17.1, pp. 743–767) defines GCTs and the translation groupoid (Definitions 17.1.1, 17.1.2 (2017: 17.1, 17.2)), local uniformizers, transition sets and uniformizer constructions (Definitions 17.1.3, 17.1.5, 17.1.6, Remarks 17.1.4, 17.1.7 (2017: Definitions 17.3–17.5, Remark 17.1)) and polyfold structures (Definition 17.1.8 (2017: 17.6)); it studies the transition sets (Lemmas 17.1.9, 17.1.10, 17.1.13, Proposition 17.1.11, Definition 17.1.12 (2017: Lemma 17.1 for 17.1.9)), builds the model $X_\Psi$ (Theorem 17.1.14, Lemma 17.1.15 (2017: Theorem 17.1, Lemma 17.2)), compares models (Theorem 17.1.18 (2017: 17.2), Definitions 17.1.20, 17.1.21, Theorem 17.1.27 (2017: Remark 17.2)), transfers (LR), (LUC), (ZHOU) and the polyfold class to $|C|$ (Definition 17.1.22 – Theorem 17.1.26) and treats regularity, the level category $C_1$ and the degeneracy index (Theorem 17.1.28, Corollary 17.1.29, Definition 17.1.30 (2017: Theorem 17.3, Definition 17.7)). §17.2 (2017: §17.2, pp. 767–784) constructs tangent vectors and $T_\alpha C$ (Lemma 17.2.1 – Proposition 17.2.5 (2017: Lemma 17.3 – Proposition 17.2)), the tangent category $TC$ and the tangent uniformizers (Definition 17.2.6 – Lemma 17.2.10), the natural topology and M-polyfold structure of the tangent transition sets (Lemma 17.2.11 – Proposition 17.2.16 (2017: Lemma 17.5 – Proposition 17.5)) and the tangent polyfold (Theorem 17.2.17, Remark 17.2.18 (2017: Theorem 17.4, Remark 17.3)). §17.3 (2017: §17.3, pp. 784–787) defines subpolyfolds in two ways and proves them equivalent (Definitions 17.3.1, 17.3.2, Remark 17.3.3, Proposition 17.3.4 (2017: Definitions 17.11, 17.12, Remark 17.4)), gives the induced structure (Lemma 17.3.5, Proposition 17.3.6 (2017: Lemma 17.6, Proposition 17.6)) and treats faces (Definition 17.3.7, Proposition 17.3.8 (2017: Definition 17.13, Proposition 17.7)). §17.4 (new in the 2021 edition, pp. 787–812) builds the tame boundary category $bC$ of a tame polyfold: its objects and morphisms (Definitions 17.4.1–17.4.4), the face uniformizers $\Psi_\theta$ (Definition 17.4.5, Lemmas 17.4.6–17.4.9), the face transition M-polyfolds (Lemma 17.4.10 – Theorem 17.4.16), the topology of $|bC|$ (Definition 17.4.17 – Proposition 17.4.25) and the polyfold structure of $bC$ (Theorem 17.4.26), with the diagrams of pp. 809–812. §17.5 (2017: §17.4, pp. 812–817) defines branched ep⁺-subcategories, their tangents and orientations (Definitions 17.5.1, 17.5.3, 17.5.4, 17.5.6, 17.5.8, Propositions 17.5.2, 17.5.5, 17.5.7, Remark 17.5.9 (2017: Definitions 17.14–17.18, Propositions 17.8–17.10, Remark 17.5)). §17.6 (2017: §17.5, pp. 817–824) builds the Whitney sums and the de Rham complex of $C$ and proves that the integrals, the boundary integrals and Stokes' theorem do not depend on the model (Proposition 17.6.1, Definitions 17.6.2, 17.6.3, 17.6.5, Theorems 17.6.4, 17.6.6, 17.6.7 (2017: Proposition 17.11, Definitions 17.19–17.21, Theorems 17.5–17.7)), with, new in the 2021 edition, their effective forms under (LUC) (Theorems 17.6.8–17.6.10). §17.7 (2017: §17.6, pp. 824–828) defines vector bundle GCTs, strong bundle uniformizers and strong polyfold bundle constructions (Definitions 17.7.1–17.7.4 (2017: Definitions 17.22–17.25)). §17.8 (2017: §17.7, pp. 828–842) defines proper covering functors of GCTs, proper covering models and uniformizers and proper polyfold covering functors (Definitions 17.8.1, 17.8.3, 17.8.4, 17.8.10, 17.8.12, 17.8.13 (2017: Definitions 17.26–17.31)), studies the models (Remark 17.8.5, Proposition 17.8.6 (new in the 2021 edition), Lemmas 17.8.7–17.8.9 (2017: Remark 17.7, Lemmas 17.7–17.9), Remark 17.8.11), the transition sets (Lemma 17.8.14, Proposition 17.8.15 (2017: Lemma 17.10, Proposition 17.12)) and the global model (17.104), and shows that pull-backs along proper polyfold covering functors preserve branched ep⁺-subcategories (Proposition 17.8.16 (2017: Proposition 17.13)).

This Part follows the book's order. §§XVII.1–XVII.19 treat §17.1: GCTs and the translation groupoid (§XVII.1), uniformizers and transition sets (§XVII.2), uniformizer constructions with the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (§XVII.3), polyfold structures (§XVII.4), Lemma 17.1.9 (§XVII.5), the properness property (§XVII.6), the natural charts and the structure maps of the model (§XVII.7), Theorem 17.1.14 and Lemma 17.1.15 (§XVII.8), weak polyfold structures (§XVII.9), the comparison construction and Theorem 17.1.18 (§§XVII.10–XVII.11), the covering category and the composition law (§XVII.12), the properties of $C$ through its models (§XVII.13), Proposition 17.1.25 (§XVII.14), Theorem 17.1.26 (§XVII.15), Theorem 17.1.27 (§XVII.16), regularity and the degeneracy index (§XVII.17), Theorem 17.1.28 (§XVII.18) and the levels with Corollary 17.1.29 (§XVII.19). §§XVII.20–XVII.29 treat §17.2: the maps $\sigma$, $T\sigma$ (§XVII.20), tangent vectors and $T_\alpha C$ (§§XVII.21–XVII.22), the tangent category (§XVII.23), the tangent uniformizers with Lemma 17.2.10 (§XVII.24), the tangent transition sets (§§XVII.25–XVII.26), Proposition 17.2.16 (§XVII.27), Theorem 17.2.17 (§XVII.28) and Remark 17.2.18 (§XVII.29). §§XVII.30–XVII.34 treat §17.3: the two definitions of subpolyfolds (§§XVII.30–XVII.31), the restricted uniformizers and the induced structure (§§XVII.32–XVII.33) and faces (§XVII.34). §§XVII.35–XVII.49 treat §17.4: the faces $F_{U(\bar o)}$ (§XVII.35), the objects, morphisms and forgetful functor of $bC$ (§§XVII.36–XVII.37), the face uniformizers (§§XVII.38–XVII.39), the face transition sets and their M-polyfold structures (§§XVII.40–XVII.42), the topology of $|bC|$ (§§XVII.43–XVII.46), Theorem 17.4.26 (§XVII.47) and the diagrams of pp. 809–812 (§§XVII.48–XVII.49). §XVII.50 collects the witnesses and what is not formalized in §§17.1–17.4. The sections on §§17.5–17.8 follow.

**What is proved, and what is not.** All eight sections of Chapter 17 are formalized. In §§17.1–17.4:

*Formalized as printed.* Definitions 17.1.1, 17.1.2, 17.1.5, 17.1.8, 17.1.12, 17.1.17, 17.1.20, 17.1.21, 17.1.22, Remarks 17.1.4, 17.1.16, 17.1.19, Lemmas 17.1.13, 17.1.15, Theorems 17.1.14, 17.1.18, 17.1.26, Propositions 17.1.23, 17.1.24, Corollary 17.1.29, the paragraph on p. 752, the construction of pp. 755–756, the regularity filtration and the levels $C^i$ (§§XVII.1–XVII.19). In §17.2: the maps $\sigma$, $T\sigma$, Lemmas 17.2.1, 17.2.7, 17.2.9, 17.2.11, Definitions 17.2.2, 17.2.3, 17.2.13, Remark 17.2.8 and Propositions 17.2.4, 17.2.12, 17.2.14, 17.2.15 (§§XVII.20–XVII.26). In §17.3: Definitions 17.3.1, 17.3.2, 17.3.7, Remark 17.3.3, the restricted uniformizers, Lemma 17.3.5 and Proposition 17.3.8 (§§XVII.30–XVII.34). In §17.4: the good open neighbourhoods (17.53)–(17.55), Definitions 17.4.1, 17.4.12, 17.4.14, Lemmas 17.4.10, 17.4.11, 17.4.15, 17.4.18 and Theorem 17.4.16 (§§XVII.35–XVII.43). Where the text leaves a verification to the reader ("one easily verifies", "obvious", "we leave the details to the reader"), it is written out; these are the points G.194, G.198–G.212 and G.214, all settled.

*Restated.* Definition 17.1.3, with "at $\alpha$" a property of the uniformizer and the centring at the point over $\alpha$ a separate predicate (§XVII.2); Remark 17.1.7 (b), (c), (1)–(3), with the data over $F(\phi' \circ \phi)$ and $F(\phi') \circ F(\phi)$ identified (§XVII.3); the composition of comparison diagrams on pp. 761–765, proved as a composition law in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ rather than on diagrams (§XVII.12); Theorem 17.1.28, with $M_{F^1}(L, L')$ built from one chosen $\Psi$ with $L = \Psi^1$; for every pair of uniformizers with the same lift it does not hold as we read it (§XVII.18; Findings, Chapter 17, item 16); Definition 17.1.30, with $d$ a function on objects constant on isomorphism classes (§XVII.17); Proposition 17.2.5, with $\mu_C$ given by its maps and the functor laws (§XVII.22); Definition 17.2.6 and Definition 17.4.2, whose classes over all uniformizers are represented through one reference uniformizer per object (§§XVII.23, XVII.36); Remark 17.2.18 through the charts $S_\Psi$ (§XVII.29); Proposition 17.3.6 with a chosen ambient transition set; for every pair of uniformizers with the same restriction it does not hold as we read it (§XVII.33; Findings, Chapter 17, item 16); Lemma 17.4.13 with faces in the sense of Definition 2.5.18 (§XVII.41); Definition 17.4.17 with "$z \in Q$" (§XVII.43); the diagram of pp. 811–812 with $bX$ the tame boundary ep-groupoid of Theorem 7.6.9 (§XVII.49).

*Stronger.* The bound on p. 749, an equality for non-empty fibres; Lemma 17.1.10 and Proposition 17.1.11 for every polyfold structure, without continuity of $\hat\Psi$, by a counting argument (§XVII.6); Theorem 17.1.27 with $\tau$ a natural isomorphism (§XVII.16); Proposition 17.2.16 with $T\Gamma_\Psi$ an equivalence (§XVII.27); Definitions 17.4.3 and 17.4.4 with the target determined by the source and $\phi$, and $B_C$ faithful (§XVII.37).

*Explicit.* Definition 17.1.6 with the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, which the text uses from p. 752 on and which Definition 17.1.8 does not imply (§XVII.3; point G.196); Proposition 17.3.4, which needs it (§XVII.31); Definition 17.4.5, with the group of a uniformizer fixing the point $\bar o$ over $\alpha$ (§XVII.38; point G.195).

*Narrower.* The statements of §17.2 and §17.4 that rest on Lemma 17.1.9 are proved with $\hat\Psi$ continuous, which Definition 17.1.8 presumably intends: the construction on p. 783 and Theorem 17.2.17 (§XVII.28), Lemma 17.4.6, Lemmas 17.4.8, 17.4.9 (§§XVII.38–XVII.39), Theorem 17.4.19 with $|bC|$ for $|C|$, Lemmas 17.4.20, 17.4.22, Corollary 17.4.21, Propositions 17.4.23–17.4.25, Theorem 17.4.26 and the diagrams (17.82)–(17.84) (§§XVII.44–XVII.48), the last ones also with $G$ fixing $\bar o$.

*Does not hold as we read it.* Two statements, with the form that is proved. (1) Lemma 17.1.9, and with it Lemma 17.2.10: Definition 17.1.8 does not tie the topology of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$ to $G \times O$, and a twisted structure on $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action has $\hat\Psi$ not continuous; the bijectivity and the sheet decomposition hold for every structure, and the lemma holds when $\hat\Psi$ is continuous (§§XVII.5, XVII.24; Findings, Chapter 17, item 1). (2) Proposition 17.1.25, under the (LUC) and under the (LR) reading, on the GCT $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action; it rests on Theorem 10.3.19 and holds for effective $C$ or an effective target model (§XVII.14; Findings, Chapter 17, item 2, and Chapter 10, item 58). Two literal forms are also shown not to hold, as the reason for a reading: Lemma 17.4.13 with Definition 2.5.19 and Definition 17.4.17 with "$z \in |bC|$" (§§XVII.41, XVII.43).

*In §§17.5–17.8.* Branched ep⁺-subcategories, their tangents and orientations, the forms and the integrals, the strong polyfold bundle constructions and the proper covering functors are formalized; Proposition 17.6.1 with $\hat\Psi$ continuous, as Theorem 17.2.17 (point G.222); Theorems 17.6.7 and 17.6.10 as printed, since Stokes on the ep-groupoid level holds for every ep-groupoid with no sc-smooth partitions of unity (Remark 9.5.19, Part IX; point G.153 settled); Remark 17.5.9 in the direction "⇒" with $\hat\Psi$ continuous; Remark 17.8.11 (2) for a connected model; Proposition 17.8.16 (4) with a proper covering uniformizer at the points of the support. The sentence after Definition 17.8.1 that Definition 17.8.4 will make $|P| : U(y_i) \to U(z)$ a homeomorphism does not hold as we read it (Findings, Chapter 17, item 12). The Morita equivalence of p. 838 (point G.232) and the strong bundle uniformizer constructions over a proper covering (point G.234, read through models) are not formalized separately.

*Points.* G.194–G.215 belong to §§17.1–17.4. All are settled, except G.197 for Remark 17.1.7 (a) and G.213 for Remark 17.4.7, which have nothing to formalize (§XVII.50).

**The decisions that shape the chapter.**

(1) *A polyfold structure is data on a GCT.* A uniformizer is a structure holding an M-polyfold $O$, a finite group $G$ acting sc-smoothly, and a functor $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ that is injective on objects and fully faithful, whose orbit map is an open embedding; its objects and groups live in the universe of $C$. A uniformizer construction assigns to every object a set $F(\alpha)$ of uniformizers at $\alpha$; a polyfold structure adds an M-polyfold structure on every transition set $M(\Psi, \Psi') = \{(q, \phi, q')\}$ with the properties (a)–(c) of Definition 17.1.8. Nothing else is assumed; every further condition the text uses is a named hypothesis.

(2) *Models carry the theory.* For a covering family $\Psi$ the formalization builds the ep-groupoid $X_\Psi$ on $\bigsqcup O_\lambda$ and $\bigsqcup M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\lambda'})$ with Part VII's notion of ep-groupoid, and the equivalence $\Gamma_\Psi$; the comparison $f_{\Psi, \Psi'}$ is a morphism of Chapter 10's localization $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$, through the model of the disjoint union $\Psi \sqcup \Psi'$. Every property of $C$ (Definition 17.1.22), the polyfold class on $|C|$, the levels, the tangent, subpolyfolds, faces, the tame boundary, and in §§17.5–17.8 branched subcategories, forms, integrals and proper coverings are defined through uniformizers and proved to agree with the notion of the earlier chapters on one, and then every, model.

(3) *Three standing conditions, named and witnessed.* $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (`HasUniformizers`, or footprints covering $|C|$, `HasCoveringUniformizers`), which Definition 17.1.6 does not ask and the text uses from p. 752 on; $\hat\Psi$ continuous (`HatContinuous`), which Definition 17.1.8 presumably intends and without which Lemma 17.1.9 does not hold as we read it; $G$ fixing the point over $\alpha$ (`IsCentredAt`, `IsCentred`), which the text presumes when it calls $G$ the isotropy group of $\alpha$. Each enters exactly where the text uses it (§XVII.50).

(4) *Classes over all uniformizers live one universe up.* Tangent vectors $[\alpha, \Psi, h]$ and the objects of $bC$ are classes over all uniformizers at $\alpha$; they are represented in the fibre of one reference uniformizer per object and identified with the text's classes by an explicit equivalence, so that $TC$ and $bC$ are GCTs in the universe the later chapters need.

(5) *Choices the text leaves open.* Where several uniformizers have the same lift $\Psi^1$, restriction $\Psi_A$ or face uniformizer $\Psi_\theta$, the text does not say which ambient transition set gives the new one its structure; one is chosen, and every statement holds for it. That the result does not depend on the choice is not claimed by the text and is not formalized.

(6) *Effective forms for uniqueness.* As in Part XVI, statements that rest on Theorem 10.3.19 for non-effective targets (Proposition 17.1.25) are proved in their effective forms, and the rest of the chapter does not depend on the uniqueness of $f_{\Psi, \Psi'}$: it uses the comparison classes and their orbit maps, which are canonical.

Throughout, $C$ is a GCT, $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ a local uniformizer with $G$ finite and discrete, $(F, M)$ a polyfold structure on $C$, $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ the transition set of triples $(q, \phi, q')$ with $\phi : \Psi(q) \to \Psi'(q')$, with source $s$, target $t$, unit, inversion and multiplication, and $\hat\Psi(g, p) = (p, \Psi(g, p), g \ast p)$. A covering family is a family of uniformizers whose footprints $|\Psi(O)|$ cover $|C|$; $X_\Psi$ is its model and $\Gamma_\Psi : X_\Psi \to C$ the canonical equivalence. Ep-groupoids, (LR), (LUC), (ZHOU), effectiveness and tameness are those of Chapters 7 and 8 (Parts VII–VIII); generalized isomorphisms and $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ are Chapter 10's (Part X); M-polyfolds, sub-M-polyfolds, faces and the degeneracy index are Part I's. The witnesses are built on $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with $x \mapsto -x$ (the reflection), $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action, slice structures on $\mathbb R^n$, and the quadrant $[0, \infty)^2$ with and without the swap of its coordinates.

### XVII.1 Definitions 17.1.1 and 17.1.2 (2017: Definitions 17.1, 17.2): groupoidal categories with topology and the translation groupoid, p. 744

**Statement** (same). *Definition 17.1.1* (p. 744) ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GroupoidalCategory⟧ ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GCT⟧: a groupoidal category consists of a type of objects and a type of morphisms in one universe, a groupoid structure on them (structure maps $s, t, m, u, \iota$, every morphism invertible), and finiteness of every morphism set $\mathrm{mor}(\alpha, \beta)$; a GCT is a groupoidal category $C$ together with a topology on its orbit space $|C|$, the set of isomorphism classes of objects ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GroupoidalCategory.OrbitSpace⟧, which is metrizable. *Definition 17.1.2* (p. 744) ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · definition_17_1_2⟧: for a finite group $G$ acting on an M-polyfold $O$ so that every $g \ast \cdot$ is sc-smooth, the ep-groupoid of the action (Chapter 7) has the translation groupoid $G \ltimes O$ as its underlying groupoid, and its orbit space $G\backslash O = |G \ltimes O|$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · metrizableSpace_translation_orbitSpace⟧.

*Readings.* "The class of objects" is a type $\mathrm{Obj}$ of a fixed universe, the one in which the M-polyfolds of the later definitions live, so the condition (1) of Definition 17.1.1, that $|C|$ be a set, holds by construction. Condition (3), that all morphisms are isomorphisms, is the groupoid structure; condition (2) is the field of finiteness. The homomorphism $\gamma : G \to \mathrm{Diff}_{sc}(O)$ of Definition 17.1.2 is an action of $G$ on $O$ with every $g \ast \cdot$ sc-smooth; each $g \ast \cdot$ is then an sc-diffeomorphism with inverse $g^{-1} \ast \cdot$. The finite group carries the discrete topology, so that $G \times O$ is an M-polyfold.

**The groupoidal category $G \ltimes O$.** The translation groupoid has objects $O$, morphisms $G \times O$, $s(g, x) = x$ and $t(g, x) = g \ast x$. As a groupoidal category ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GroupoidalCategory.translation⟧ its morphism sets are finite because $\mathrm{mor}(x, y)$ lies in $G \times \{x\}$ and $G$ is finite. With the quotient topology on $|G \ltimes O|$ and the metrizability below it is a GCT ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GCT.translation⟧.

**Proof of Definition 17.1.2.** The text says that one easily verifies that $G \ltimes O$ is an ep-groupoid and that $G\backslash O$ is metrizable, and indicates the averaged metric. *Step 1, the ep-groupoid.* The ep-groupoid of the action is Chapter 7's construction ⟦Chapter7/NonVacuity · EpGroupoid.Examples.actionEpGroupoid⟧, whose underlying groupoid is the translation groupoid by definition; this is the first half of the statement and settles G.194. *Step 2, the averaged metric.* The M-polyfold $O$ is metrizable. For a finite group acting by homeomorphisms, Chapter 8 averages a metric $d_0$ of $O$ over $G$ and takes $\bar d([q], [q']) = \min_{g, g'} d(g \ast q, g' \ast q')$, which is a metric for the quotient topology of $O/G$ ⟦Chapter8/BundleOrbitParacompact · metrizableSpace_orbitRel_quotient_finite⟧. *Step 3, transport.* The orbit space of the translation groupoid is homeomorphic to the quotient $O/G$ by the orbit relation ⟦Chapter7/NonVacuity · EpGroupoid.Examples.actionOrbitHomeomorph⟧, and metrizability passes along an embedding, so $|G \ltimes O|$ is metrizable.

**The witness.** The GCT $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, with $\mathbb Z/2 = \{\pm 1\}$ acting by $x \mapsto -x$ ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GCT.reflection⟧, has $|C| = \mathbb R/\pm 1$ and positive dimension, and the object $0$ has the automorphism $(-1, 0)$, which is not the unit ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GCT.reflection_exists_ne_unit⟧. This GCT, the reflection, is the base of most witnesses of the chapter.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Nowhere in substance. The universe convention replaces "the class of objects is a set" by a typing condition. The GCT of Definition 17.1.1 without its topology is the groupoidal category, which is also Definition 19.1.1, so Chapter 19 starts from the same structure.

### XVII.2 Definition 17.1.3 (2017: Definition 17.3), Remark 17.1.4 and Definition 17.1.5 (2017: Definition 17.4): local uniformizers, footprints and transition sets, pp. 745–746

**Statement.** *Definition 17.1.3* (p. 745; restated) ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer⟧: a local uniformizer of the GCT $C$ consists of an M-polyfold $O$, a finite discrete group $G$ acting on $O$ with every $g \ast \cdot$ sc-smooth, and a functor $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ which is injective on objects, full and faithful, such that the induced map $|\Psi| : G\backslash O \to |C|$ is a homeomorphism onto an open subset. It is at $\alpha$ if $\Psi(q_0) = \alpha$ for some $q_0 \in O$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsAt⟧. Its footprint is $|\Psi(O)|$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.footprint⟧, open in $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.isOpen_footprint⟧, and $\Psi$ is tame if $O$ is tame ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.IsTame⟧. *Remark 17.1.4* (p. 745, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.remark_17_1_4⟧: $\Psi$ corestricts to a functor from $G \ltimes O$ to the full subcategory of $C$ on the objects $\Psi(O)$, bijective on objects and on morphisms. *Definition 17.1.5* (p. 745; same) ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.Trans⟧: the transition set $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ of two uniformizers is the set of tuples $(q, \phi, q')$ with $q \in O$, $q' \in O'$ and $\phi : \Psi(q) \to \Psi'(q')$, with the structure maps $s, t, u, \iota, m$ of p. 746.

*Readings.* The uniformizer is defined in two layers. The first, `Uniformizer`, has no topology on $C$ and is the uniformizer of Definition 19.1.2 as well; the second adds condition (3), that $|\Psi|$ is an open embedding. "At $\alpha$" is a predicate on the uniformizer, not a field, so that one uniformizer can serve several objects; with "at $\alpha$", "a homeomorphism onto an open neighbourhood of $|\alpha|$" is the open embedding. The 2017 condition that $O$ be paracompact is part of the M-polyfold here, as in the footnote of the 2021 text.

*The point over $\alpha$* (G.195). Definition 17.1.3 does not ask that $G$ fixes the point $q_0$ over $\alpha$. The proof of Lemma 17.1.9 writes $G = G_\alpha$, p. 792 uses it, and Definition 19.1.2 speaks of the isotropy group. The formalization keeps Definition 17.1.3 as printed and names the stronger condition: $\Psi$ is centred at $\alpha$ if $\Psi(q_0) = \alpha$ for some $q_0$ fixed by all of $G$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.FixesPoint⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsCentredAt⟧. It is assumed only where the text uses it (§17.4 from Definition 17.4.5 on).

**Full faithfulness in terms of the structure maps.** Every later argument uses the following two facts. *Fullness* ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.exists_smul_eq_mor⟧: every $\phi : \Psi(q) \to \Psi(q')$ is $\Psi(g, q)$ for some $g$ with $g \ast q = q'$; this is fullness of the functor, read through Chapter 10's characterization ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · GroupoidFunctor.full_iff⟧. *Injectivity on morphisms* ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.mor_injective⟧: if $\Psi(g, q) = \Psi(h, q')$, the sources give $\Psi(q) = \Psi(q')$, so $q = q'$ by injectivity on objects; the targets give $g \ast q = h \ast q'$ likewise; faithfulness then gives $(g, q) = (h, q')$.

**The isotropy group of a centred uniformizer** ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.isotropyEquiv⟧. If $q$ is fixed by $G$, then $g \mapsto \Psi(g, q)$ is a group isomorphism $G \cong \mathrm{Aut}(\Psi(q))$. It lands in the automorphisms because $g \ast q = q$; it is a homomorphism because $\Psi$ preserves composition and $(g, q) \circ (h, q) = (gh, q)$ when $h \ast q = q$; it is injective by the injectivity on morphisms and surjective by fullness. So "centred" is exactly the reading $G = G_\alpha$.

**Proof of Remark 17.1.4.** *Step 1, the functor.* Send $q$ to $\Psi(q)$ viewed as an object of the full subcategory on $\Psi(O)$, and $(g, q)$ to $\Psi(g, q)$, a morphism between two objects of $\Psi(O)$; it preserves source, target, units and composition because $\Psi$ does. *Step 2, bijective on objects*: injective by injectivity of $\Psi$ on objects, surjective because the subcategory has exactly the objects $\Psi(q)$. *Step 3, bijective on morphisms*: injective by the injectivity on morphisms; a morphism $\phi : \Psi(q) \to \Psi(q')$ of the subcategory is $\Psi(g, q)$ by fullness. The ep-groupoid structure on the subcategory is the one transported from $G \ltimes O$, the ep-groupoid of the uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.ep⟧, along this isomorphism of groupoids.

**The topology of a uniformizer.** The quotient map $O \to G\backslash O$ is open, because the saturation of an open set $W$ is $\bigcup_g g^{-1} W$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidStr.isOpenMap_π_translation⟧. Hence $q \mapsto |\Psi(q)|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.continuous_π_obj⟧ and open ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.isOpenMap_π_obj⟧ from $O$ to $|C|$, and a class $z$ lies in the footprint iff $z = |\Psi(q)|$ for some $q$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.mem_footprint_iff⟧. These are the topological facts used in Proposition 17.1.11 and Lemma 17.1.15.

**Definition 17.1.5 and the structure maps.** A tuple of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is a structure with three entries $q$, $\phi$, $q'$ and the two equations $s(\phi) = \Psi(q)$, $t(\phi) = \Psi'(q')$; it is identified with the text's subset of $O \times \mathrm{mor}(C) \times O'$ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.equivWeakFibredProduct⟧. The set is defined for arbitrary maps $O \to \mathrm{Obj}$ and $O' \to \mathrm{Obj}$ into a groupoid, with no topology, so that it serves §19.1 as well. The structure maps of p. 746 are: $s(q, \phi, q') = q$ and $t(q, \phi, q') = q'$ (the first and third entries); the unit $q \mapsto (q, 1_{\Psi(q)}, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.unit⟧; the inversion $(q, \phi, q') \mapsto (q', \phi^{-1}, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.inv⟧; the composite $(q, \phi' \circ \phi, q'')$ of two tuples with composable morphisms ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.comp⟧; and the multiplication $m$ on the fibred product $M(\Psi', \Psi'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.mul⟧, which is the composite, since $s(q', \phi', q'') = t(q, \phi, q')$ makes the morphisms composable ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.mul_eq_comp⟧. The groupoid laws hold entrywise from those of $C$: associativity ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.comp_assoc⟧, the unit laws ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.comp_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.unit_comp⟧, the inverse laws ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.comp_inv⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.inv_comp⟧, $\iota \circ \iota = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.inv_inv⟧ and $(b \circ a)^{-1} = a^{-1} \circ b^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.inv_comp_rev⟧. Two tuples are equal iff their three entries are, so each law is the corresponding law of $C$ in the middle entry.

**The witnesses.** For a translation GCT $G \ltimes O$ the identity functor is a local uniformizer at every object, with footprint all of $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.tautological⟧: it is injective, full and faithful trivially, and its orbit map is the identity. On the reflection GCT it is the reflection uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.reflection⟧; it is centred at $0$, with $G = \mathbb Z/2 \cong \mathrm{Aut}(0)$, and $G$ acts non-trivially, since $-1$ moves $1$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.reflection_isCentredAt⟧. It is tame, $\mathbb R$ being tame ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.reflection_isTame⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The base point is a predicate, not data. The condition "$G$ is the isotropy group" is not in Definition 17.1.3; it is the separate predicate `IsCentredAt` with the isomorphism $G \cong G_\alpha$, used in §17.4. Definition 17.1.5 is formalized for any groupoid and any two maps into its objects; the structure maps of p. 746 are those of this general transition set.

### XVII.3 Definition 17.1.6 (2017: Definition 17.5) and Remark 17.1.7 (2017: Remark 17.1): uniformizer constructions, the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, functoriality and transport data, pp. 746–747

**Statement.** *Definition 17.1.6* (p. 746; explicit) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction⟧: a uniformizer construction assigns to every object $\alpha$ a set $F(\alpha)$ of local uniformizers, each of them at $\alpha$. *Remark 17.1.7* (pp. 746–747; restated): (b) a functorial uniformizer construction ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.Functorial⟧ makes every $F(\phi) : F(\alpha) \to F(\alpha')$ a bijection ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.remark_17_1_7⟧; (c) transport data $(\gamma, \sigma, \tau)$ from $\Psi$ to $\Psi' = F(\phi)(\Psi)$ ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.LocalUniformizer.TransportData⟧ make $\Psi(g, q)$ correspond to $\Psi'(\gamma(g), \sigma(q))$ ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.LocalUniformizer.TransportData.mor_eq⟧; and the compatibilities (1)–(3) hold iff the data of $\phi' \circ \phi$ are the composite of the data of $\phi$ and of $\phi'$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.Functorial.TransportSystem.remark_17_1_7_compatible_iff⟧.

*Readings.* The category $C^-$ of Definition 17.1.6 has the objects of $C$ and only the identities, so a functor $C^- \to \mathrm{SET}$ is a family of sets $F(\alpha)$. The uniformizers of $F$ are those that lie in some $F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.Unif⟧; they form a type one universe up.

**The standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$** (G.196). Definition 17.1.6 allows $F(\alpha) = \emptyset$. From p. 752 on the text picks uniformizers without comment: a covering family on p. 752, a uniformizer at a given object in Propositions 17.2.4 and 17.3.4, and throughout Chapter 19. The formalization names the two forms of the condition. `HasUniformizers` is $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.HasUniformizers⟧. `HasCoveringUniformizers`, the weaker one, says that the footprints of all uniformizers of $F$ cover $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.HasCoveringUniformizers⟧. A covering family (p. 752) is a family $(\Psi_\lambda)_{\lambda \in \Lambda}$ of uniformizers of $F$, indexed by a type of the universe of $C$, whose footprints cover $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.CoveringFamily⟧. Where the text picks uniformizers the condition is carried as a named hypothesis (`hU`, `hS`, `hc`); every witness structure of the chapter has it.

*Proof that $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ gives covering footprints* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.hasCoveringUniformizers_of_hasUniformizers⟧. A class $z = |\alpha|$; pick $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$; $\Psi$ is at $\alpha$, so $|\alpha|$ lies in its footprint.

*Proof that a covering family exists iff the footprints cover $|C|$* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.exists_coveringFamily_iff⟧. One direction is immediate. Conversely, choose for every class $z$ a uniformizer whose footprint contains $z$; the family indexed by $|C|$ itself, a type of the universe of $C$, covers.

*Proof that Definition 17.1.8 does not imply it.* The construction with $F \equiv \emptyset$ is a polyfold structure in the sense of Definition 17.1.8 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.emptyConstruction⟧: there are no uniformizers, so there are no transition sets and the conditions (a)–(c) hold vacuously. On the reflection GCT, $F(0) = \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_hasUniformizers_empty⟧, and the footprints do not cover the non-empty $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_hasCoveringUniformizers_empty⟧. The example is degenerate on purpose: it shows only that the condition has to be stated, and the reading is that the text means it.

**Remark 17.1.7 (b).** A functorial construction gives, for every morphism $\phi : \alpha \to \alpha'$, a map $F(\phi)$ sending $F(\alpha)$ into $F(\alpha')$, with $F(1_\alpha) = \mathrm{id}$ and $F(\phi' \circ \phi) = F(\phi') \circ F(\phi)$ on $F(\alpha)$. *Proof that $F(\phi)$ is a bijection.* $F(\phi^{-1})$ maps $F(\alpha')$ into $F(\alpha)$, and $F(\phi^{-1}) \circ F(\phi) = F(\phi^{-1} \circ \phi) = F(1_\alpha) = \mathrm{id}$ on $F(\alpha)$, and symmetrically; so the two maps are inverse bijections. Witness: on the reflection GCT, $F \equiv \{\Psi_0\}$ with $\Psi_0$ the reflection uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.reflectionUniformizerConstruction⟧ and $F(\phi) = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.reflectionFunctorial⟧.

**Remark 17.1.7 (c).** Transport data from $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ to $\Psi' : G' \ltimes O' \to C$ are a group isomorphism $\gamma : G \to G'$, an sc-diffeomorphism $\sigma : O \to O'$ with $\sigma(g \ast q) = \gamma(g) \ast \sigma(q)$, so that $\bar\sigma = \gamma \ltimes \sigma$ is a functor, and a natural transformation $\tau : \Psi \to \Psi' \circ \bar\sigma$, that is morphisms $\tau(q) : \Psi(q) \to \Psi'(\sigma(q))$ with $\tau(g \ast q) \circ \Psi(g, q) = \Psi'(\gamma(g), \sigma(q)) \circ \tau(q)$. Two consequences: $\Psi(q)$ and $\Psi'(\sigma(q))$ are isomorphic through $\tau(q)$ ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.LocalUniformizer.TransportData.isomorphic⟧, and $\Psi'(\gamma(g), \sigma(q)) = \tau(g \ast q) \circ \Psi(g, q) \circ \tau(q)^{-1}$, by composing the square with $\tau(q)^{-1}$ on the right. Witness: for $k \in \mathbb Z/2$, $\gamma = \mathrm{id}$, $\sigma = k \ast \cdot$ and $\tau(q) = (k, q)$ are transport data on the reflection uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.LocalUniformizer.reflectionTransport⟧; the square is $(kg, q) = (gk, q)$, true since $\mathbb Z/2$ is abelian; for $k = -1$, $\sigma$ is the reflection, not the identity ⟦Chapter17/FunctorialUniformizers · GCT.LocalUniformizer.reflectionTransport_σ_ne⟧.

**Remark 17.1.7 (1)–(3).** A transport system assigns transport data from $\Psi$ to $F(\phi)(\Psi)$ to every morphism $\phi$ and every $\Psi \in F(s(\phi))$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.Functorial.TransportSystem⟧. The text says that "in many cases" (1) $\sigma(\phi', F(\phi)(\Psi)) \circ \sigma(\phi, \Psi) = \sigma(\phi' \circ \phi, \Psi)$, (2) the same for $\gamma$, and (3) $\tau(\phi', F(\phi)(\Psi))(\sigma(\phi, \Psi)(q)) \circ \tau(\phi, \Psi)(q) = \tau(\phi' \circ \phi, \Psi)(q)$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.Functorial.TransportSystem.CompatibleSigma⟧ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.Functorial.TransportSystem.CompatibleGamma⟧ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.Functorial.TransportSystem.CompatibleTau⟧ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.Functorial.TransportSystem.Compatible⟧. Two readings enter. In (3) the printed $\sigma(\psi, \Psi)$ is read as $\sigma(\phi, \Psi)$, the only map that makes the composite defined. And (1), (2) compare maps into the objects and groups of $F(\phi')(F(\phi)(\Psi))$ and of $F(\phi' \circ \phi)(\Psi)$; these uniformizers are equal by the functoriality of $F$, and the data are identified along that equality ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.LocalUniformizer.TransportData.castTarget⟧. Condition (3) needs no identification because $\tau$ takes values in the morphisms of $C$.

*Proof of the reformulation.* *Step 1, composite data* ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.LocalUniformizer.TransportData.comp⟧. Transport data $D : \Psi \to \Psi'$ and $D' : \Psi' \to \Psi''$ compose to $\gamma' \circ \gamma$, $\sigma' \circ \sigma$ and $\tau(q) = \tau'(\sigma(q)) \circ \tau(q)$. Equivariance is $\sigma'(\sigma(g \ast q)) = \sigma'(\gamma(g) \ast \sigma(q)) = \gamma'(\gamma(g)) \ast \sigma'(\sigma(q))$. The square is pasted from the two squares: $\tau'(\sigma(g \ast q)) \circ \tau(g \ast q) \circ \Psi(g, q) = \tau'(\gamma(g) \ast \sigma(q)) \circ \Psi'(\gamma(g), \sigma(q)) \circ \tau(q) = \Psi''(\gamma'\gamma(g), \sigma'\sigma(q)) \circ \tau'(\sigma(q)) \circ \tau(q)$, using associativity in $C$ at each step. *Step 2.* Transport data are determined by $\gamma$, $\sigma$ and $\tau$, since $\sigma^{-1}$ is the inverse of $\sigma$. So (1)–(3) together say that the data of $\phi' \circ \phi$, moved along $F(\phi' \circ \phi)(\Psi) = F(\phi')(F(\phi)(\Psi))$, equal the composite of Step 1; conversely the equality of data gives (1)–(3) componentwise.

*Witnesses.* With $\sigma(\phi, \Psi_0) = k \ast \cdot$ for $\phi = (k, x)$, the system on the reflection GCT ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.reflectionTransportSystem⟧ satisfies (1)–(3) ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.reflectionTransportSystem_compatible⟧: (1) is $(k'k) \ast q = k' \ast (k \ast q)$, and (2), (3) hold by definition. The system with $\sigma \equiv -\cdot$ for every $\phi$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.reflectionConstTransportSystem⟧ consists of transport data for every $\phi$, but (1) does not hold at $\phi = \phi' = 1_0$: it would need $(-\cdot) \circ (-\cdot) = -\cdot$, and $-(-1) = 1 \ne -1$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.not_compatibleSigma_reflectionConst⟧. So (1)–(3) are a genuine condition beyond the existence of transport data.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Remark 17.1.7 (a), that a uniformizer construction is usually a recipe with set-many choices, has no mathematical content (G.197). The remark's "often" and "in many cases" become definitions with witnesses; none of (b), (c), (1)–(3) is used later in §17.1. The condition $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ is stated once, in two strengths, and carried as a named hypothesis where the text picks uniformizers.

### XVII.4 Definition 17.1.8 (2017: Definition 17.6): polyfold structures on a GCT, p. 747

**Statement** (same). ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction⟧ A polyfold structure $(F, M)$ on the GCT $C$ consists of (1) a uniformizer construction $F$ and (2) for any two uniformizers $\Psi, \Psi'$ of $F$ a Hausdorff paracompact topology and an M-polyfold structure on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, such that (a) $s : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to O$ and $t : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to O'$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms; (b) the unit map $u : O \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$ and the inversion $\iota : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to M(\Psi', \Psi)$ are sc-smooth; (c) the multiplication $m : M(\Psi', \Psi'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi'')$ is sc-smooth. The structure is tame if all its uniformizers are tame ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsTame⟧, and a polyfold is a GCT with a polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · CategoryPolyfold⟧.

*Readings.* The M-polyfold structure is attached to the pair of uniformizers $\Psi, \Psi'$, as the notation $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ says, not to the objects at which they sit. Condition (c) asks sc-smoothness on the fibred product with its sub-M-polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · MPolyfold.IsScSmoothOnFibredProduct⟧: since $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism and $t$ sc-smooth, $M(\Psi', \Psi'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is a sub-M-polyfold of the product (Proposition 2.5.20, Part II) ⟦Chapter2/FibredProduct · MPolyfold.isSubMPolyfold_fibredProduct⟧. The transition set with its topology and M-polyfold structure is the transition M-polyfold ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.Tr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transPolyfold⟧; on it (a)–(c) are restated as lemmas ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_trSrc⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_trTgt⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trUnit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trInv⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trMul⟧.

*What the definition does not say.* Nothing in (a)–(c) ties the topology of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$ to that of $G \times O$ along the bijection $\hat\Psi$ of Lemma 17.1.9. The witnesses below show both behaviours: in the reflection and slice structures $\hat\Psi$ is continuous, in the twisted structure on $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ it is not. Lemma 17.1.9 below is where this matters.

**The witnesses: twisted structures on a translation GCT.** Let $C = K \ltimes U$ be a translation GCT and $F(x) = \{\Psi_0\}$ for every $x$, with $\Psi_0$ the identity uniformizer. The transition set $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$ is in bijection with $K \times U$, and every automorphism of the groupoid $K \ltimes U$ over the identity of $U$ gives an M-polyfold structure.

*Step 1, twists* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TranslationTwist⟧. A twist is a bijection $\beta$ of $K \times U$ that commutes with $s$ and $t$ and preserves units, inverses and composition. *Step 2, the bijection $e_\beta$* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistMap⟧: $e_\beta(g, p) = (p, \beta(g, p), g \ast p)$, a bijection $K \times U \to M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$ with inverse $a \mapsto \beta^{-1}(\phi)$ for $a = (q, \phi, q')$. *Step 3, transport.* Give $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$ the topology for which $e_\beta$ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistHomeomorph⟧ and the M-polyfold structure of $K \times U$ moved along it ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistM⟧. An M-polyfold structure moves along a homeomorphism through the descent of Chapter 9 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · MPolyfold.transport⟧, and the homeomorphism is then an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · MPolyfold.isScDiffeoOn_transport⟧. *Step 4, (a)–(c).* Through $e_\beta$, the structure maps $s, t, u, \iota, m$ of $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$ are those of the ep-groupoid $K \ltimes U$. For $s$: $s \circ e_\beta = s_{K \ltimes U}$ because $\beta$ preserves the second entry, so $s = s_{K \ltimes U} \circ e_\beta^{-1}$ is a composite of local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧; likewise $t$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧. For $u$: $e_\beta(1, q) = u(q)$ since $\beta(1, q) = (1, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_isScSmoothMap_unit⟧. For $\iota$: $\iota(e_\beta(g, p)) = e_\beta(g^{-1}, g \ast p)$, since $\beta$ preserves inverses ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_isScSmoothMap_inv⟧. For $m$: the map $e_\beta^{-1} \times e_\beta^{-1}$ carries the fibred product of $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$ sc-smoothly into that of $K \times U$, and $m = e_\beta \circ m_{K \ltimes U} \circ (e_\beta^{-1} \times e_\beta^{-1})$, since $\beta$ preserves composition ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_isScSmoothMap_mul⟧. *Step 5, the structure* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistConstruction⟧: every $F(x)$ is non-empty ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧, and the structure is tame if $U$ is ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistConstruction_isTame⟧.

*The reflection structure.* With the identity twist on the reflection GCT one obtains the polyfold structure $F(x) = \{\Psi_0\}$, $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0) = \mathbb Z/2 \times \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction⟧, with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧, tame ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction_isTame⟧, with a non-trivial action. It is the non-degenerate witness of Definition 17.1.8.

**The witnesses: structures from slices, and two overlapping uniformizers at one object.** The twisted structures have one uniformizer. To witness that $F(\alpha)$ may hold several uniformizers with different groups whose transition sets are non-trivial, the formalization builds structures from slices. *Step 1, slices* ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TranslationSlice⟧. A slice of $K \ltimes U$ is a non-empty open $V \subset U$ with a subgroup $H \le K$ preserving $V$ such that $k \ast v \in V$ for some $v \in V$ forces $k \in H$; the inclusion $H \ltimes V \to K \ltimes U$ is then full, faithful and injective on objects, and a local uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TranslationSlice.unif⟧. *Step 2, the transition sets.* $M(\Psi_D, \Psi_{D'})$ embeds into $K \times U$ by $a \mapsto \phi$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEmbedding_mor⟧ (injective because the uniformizers are injective on objects), with image the open set $\{(k, u) : u \in V_D,\ k \ast u \in V_{D'}\}$; it receives the topology and the M-polyfold structure of this open subset ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.MorTr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.morTrM⟧. Part I's open-subset structure needs a non-empty subset, which is why the slices are asked to overlap up to $K$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.Overlapping⟧; this is a condition of the construction, not of the text. *Step 3, (a)–(c)* follow from those of $K \ltimes U$ through the embedding ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.slice_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.morTr_isScSmoothMap_inv⟧ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.slice_isScSmoothMap_mul⟧, and give the polyfold structure $F(x) = \{\Psi_D : x \in V_D\}$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction⟧, with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ when the slices cover $U$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧, tame if $U$ is ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction_isTame⟧. *Step 4, the witness* ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlapConstruction⟧. On the reflection GCT take the slices $\mathbb R$ with $\mathbb Z/2$, $(-2, 2)$ with $\mathbb Z/2$ (centred at $0$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlapUnif₁_isCentredAt⟧) and $(1, 3)$ with the trivial group. The structure is tame ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlapConstruction_isTame⟧, its footprints cover ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlapConstruction_hasCoveringUniformizers⟧, and $F(3/2)$ contains the uniformizers of $(-2, 2)$ and $(1, 3)$, with a non-trivial and a trivial group, so they differ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlap_two_uniformizers⟧. Their transition set contains $(3/2, 1, 3/2)$ and $(-3/2, (-1, -3/2), 3/2)$, and its source map is not onto ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlap_transition_nonempty⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Every field is the printed condition; the transition M-polyfold is a type synonym of the transition set, so that each $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ carries its own instances. The witnesses go beyond the text, which gives none at this point; Chapter 19 builds the polyfold structures of the text's applications.

### XVII.5 Lemma 17.1.9 (2017: Lemma 17.1): the map $\hat\Psi : G \ltimes O \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$, p. 748

**Statement** (does not hold as we read it; holds when $\hat\Psi$ is continuous; the set-theoretic part holds in general). For $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ the text states that $\hat\Psi(g, p) = (p, \Psi(g, p), g \ast p)$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.hat⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hat⟧ is an sc-diffeomorphism from the morphism M-polyfold $G \times O$ of $G \ltimes O$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.morPolyfold⟧ onto $M(\Psi, \Psi)$; that the sets $U(q, g, q)$ are pairwise disjoint open sets covering $M(\Psi, \Psi)$, with $s : U(q, g, q) \to O$ an sc-diffeomorphism; and that $t(p, \phi, p') = g \ast p$ on $U(q, g, q)$.

The claim in Lean ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_lemma_17_1_9⟧ is the negation of: for every GCT $C$, polyfold structure $S$ and uniformizer $\Psi$ of $S$, $\hat\Psi$ is an sc-diffeomorphism with inverse $\hat\Psi^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hatInv⟧. What holds: $\hat\Psi$ is a bijection ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_9_bijective⟧; the sheets $U(q, g, q) = \hat\Psi(\{g\} \times O)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hatSheet⟧ are pairwise disjoint and cover $M(\Psi, \Psi)$, with $t = g \ast s$ on each and $\hat\Psi(g, q) \in U(q, g, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_9_decomposition⟧, with no hypothesis; and if $\hat\Psi$ is continuous, it is an sc-diffeomorphism, every sheet is open and $s$ maps each sheet sc-diffeomorphically onto $O$, with inverse $p \mapsto \hat\Psi(g, p)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_9_of_continuous⟧.

*Readings.* The text's $U(q, g, q)$, characterized by $s$ being an sc-diffeomorphism on it, does not single out a set; it is read as $\hat\Psi(\{g\} \times O)$, as the proof uses it. The hypothesis $\Psi(q) = \alpha$ is not used by the first claim and does not appear. The proof step in question is "from $s \circ \hat\Psi = \mathrm{pr}$, $\hat\Psi$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, since $s$ is one". A set-theoretic section of a local homeomorphism is a local homeomorphism only if it is continuous, and Definition 17.1.8 does not tie the topology of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$ to $G \times O$. Definition 17.1.8 presumably intends $\hat\Psi$ continuous (equivalently, the sheets open), which every polyfold structure built in Chapter 19 satisfies (Findings, Chapter 17, item 1).

**Proof of the bijectivity** ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.hat_bijective⟧. *Injective:* $\hat\Psi(g, p) = \hat\Psi(g', p')$ gives $\Psi(g, p) = \Psi(g', p')$ in the middle entry, and $\Psi$ is injective on morphisms (Definition 17.1.3 above). *Surjective:* for $(q, \phi, q') \in M(\Psi, \Psi)$, fullness gives $g$ with $g \ast q = q'$ and $\Psi(g, q) = \phi$, so $(q, \phi, q') = \hat\Psi(g, q)$. The bijection is recorded as an equivalence of types ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.hatEquiv⟧, which defines $\hat\Psi^{-1}$.

**Proof of the decomposition.** *Disjoint:* an element of $U(q, g, q) \cap U(q, g', q)$ is $\hat\Psi(p) = \hat\Psi(p')$ with $p_1 = g$, $p'_1 = g'$; injectivity gives $p = p'$, so $g = g'$. *Covering:* $a = \hat\Psi(\hat\Psi^{-1}(a))$ lies in the sheet of the first entry of $\hat\Psi^{-1}(a)$. *The target:* on the sheet of $g$, $t(\hat\Psi(g, p)) = g \ast p = g \ast s(\hat\Psi(g, p))$.

**Proof under continuity of $\hat\Psi$.** *Step 1, $\hat\Psi$ is sc-smooth* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_hat_of_continuous⟧. Sc-smoothness is local. Take $x = (g, p)$. By Definition 17.1.8 (a) there are an open $W \ni \hat\Psi(x)$ and an sc-diffeomorphism $s : W \to V$ with inverse $\sigma$. The set $N = (\{g\} \times O) \cap \hat\Psi^{-1}(W)$ is open: the sheet $\{g\} \times O$ is open since $G$ is discrete ⟦Chapter6/Discrete · isOpen_sheet⟧, and $\hat\Psi^{-1}(W)$ is open because $\hat\Psi$ is continuous; this is the step where continuity enters. On $N$, $s(\hat\Psi(y)) = y_2$, so $\hat\Psi = \sigma \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$ there, a composite of sc-smooth maps. *Step 2, $\hat\Psi^{-1}$ is sc-smooth* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_hatInv_of_continuous⟧. Take $a$, let $g$ be the first entry of $\hat\Psi^{-1}(a)$, and take a sheet $s : W \to V$ of $s$ through $a$. The map $p \mapsto \hat\Psi(g, p)$ is continuous, so $V'' = V \cap \{p : \hat\Psi(g, p) \in W\}$ is open, and so is $O_a = W \cap s^{-1}(V'')$, which contains $a$. For $b \in O_a$, both $b$ and $\hat\Psi(g, s(b))$ lie in $W$ and have the same source, so they are equal by injectivity of $s$ on $W$; hence $\hat\Psi^{-1}(b) = (g, s(b))$ on $O_a$, which is sc-smooth as $p \mapsto (g, p)$ ⟦Chapter6/Discrete · isScSmoothMap_sheet⟧ composed with $s$. *Step 3, the sheets are open*: $U(q, g, q) = (\hat\Psi^{-1})^{-1}(\{g\} \times O)$, and $\hat\Psi^{-1}$ is continuous by Step 2. *Step 4, $s$ on a sheet*: $s$ is sc-smooth on the open sheet by (a); its inverse $p \mapsto \hat\Psi(g, p)$ is sc-smooth by Step 1; and the two are inverse because $s(\hat\Psi(g, p)) = p$ and every element of the sheet is $\hat\Psi(g, p_2)$.

**Proof that the statement does not hold as we read it.** The example is a twisted structure (Definition 17.1.8 above) on $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action.

*Step 1, the twist.* Let $\beta(g, p) = (g, p)$ for $p \le 0$ and $\beta(g, p) = (g^{-1}, p)$ for $p > 0$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3TwistFun⟧. It is an involution; it commutes with $s$ and $t$ (both are the projection to $p$, the action being trivial); it fixes units; it preserves inverses; and it preserves composition, since composable morphisms have the same $p$ and $g \mapsto g^{-1}$ is a homomorphism of the abelian group $\mathbb Z/3$. So it is a twist ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Twist⟧.

*Step 2, $\beta^{-1}$ is not continuous* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_continuous_z3Twist⟧. $\beta^{-1} = \beta$. Along $t \mapsto (g_1, t)$, with $g_1$ a generator, the first entry of $\beta$ is $g_1$ for $t \le 0$ and $g_1^{-1} \ne g_1$ for $t > 0$; a continuous map from the connected $\mathbb R$ to the discrete $\mathbb Z/3$ is constant, so $\beta$ is not continuous.

*Step 3, the polyfold structure* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Construction⟧. By the twisted construction, $F(x) = \{\Psi_0\}$ and $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$ carries the structure of $\mathbb Z/3 \times \mathbb R$ moved along $e_\beta$; (a)–(c) of Definition 17.1.8 hold, as proved for every twist. The uniformizer is centred at $0$, with $G = G_0 = \mathbb Z/3$; every $F(x)$ is non-empty; and the structure is tame ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3_isCentredAt⟧ (with $\mathbb R$ tame ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · rnPolyfold_isTame⟧). So every hypothesis of the text holds, including the readings $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ and $G = G_\alpha$.

*Step 4, $\hat\Psi = e_\beta \circ \beta^{-1}$* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_hat_eq⟧. $\hat\Psi(\beta(y)) = (y_2, \beta(y), y_1 \ast y_2) = e_\beta(y)$, because $\beta$ preserves the second entry and the target. Since $e_\beta$ is a homeomorphism, $\hat\Psi$ is continuous iff $\beta^{-1}$ is ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_continuous_hat⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_not_continuous_hat⟧. By Step 2, $\hat\Psi$ is not continuous ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3_not_continuous_hat⟧.

*Step 5, conclusion.* An sc-diffeomorphism is continuous, so $\hat\Psi$ is not one. Concretely, the sheet $U(0, g_1, 0) = \hat\Psi(\{g_1\} \times \mathbb R)$ consists of $e_\beta(g_1, p)$ for $p \le 0$ and $e_\beta(g_1^{-1}, p)$ for $p > 0$: it is not open, and $s$ is not a homeomorphism on it.

**The witnesses of the continuous form.** For the reflection structure, $\hat\Psi = e_{\mathrm{id}}$ is continuous ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_continuous_hat⟧, so the lemma holds ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_lemma_17_1_9⟧, with two distinct sheets, that of $-1$ not being the unit sheet ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_hatSheet_ne⟧. For every slice structure $\hat\Psi$ is continuous, since the topology of the transition sets is induced from $K \times U$ and $\hat\Psi$ followed by the embedding is $(g, p) \mapsto (g, p)$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction_continuous_hat⟧; this covers the structure with two overlapping uniformizers at one object. Neither witness is weak: both have a non-trivial action.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The lemma is split into its set-theoretic part, which holds for every polyfold structure, and its smooth part, which needs the continuity of $\hat\Psi$. The continuity is the hypothesis `hc` (later `hcont`, `HatContinuous`) of Lemma 17.2.10, Theorem 17.2.17 and §17.4 from Lemma 17.4.6 on. The two results that the text derives from Lemma 17.1.9, Lemma 17.1.10 and Proposition 17.1.11, are proved below without it, by a counting argument. The same point is behind Lemma 17.2.10.

### XVII.6 The bound on p. 749, Lemma 17.1.10, Proposition 17.1.11 and Definition 17.1.12: the properness property, pp. 749–751

**Statement.** *The bound on p. 749* (new in the 2021 edition; stronger): for $q \in O$ there are at most $\sharp G'$ elements of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ over $q$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.ncard_fiber_le⟧; given one of them, $a_0 = (q, \phi_0, q'_0)$, they are exactly the $(q, \Psi'(g', q'_0) \circ \phi_0, g' \ast q'_0)$, $g' \in G'$, each once ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.fiberEquiv⟧, so there are exactly $\sharp G'$ of them ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.ncard_fiber_eq⟧, a finite set in any case ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.finite_fiber⟧. *Lemma 17.1.10* (p. 749, new in the 2021 edition; stronger) ⟦Chapter17/TransitionProperness · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_10⟧: for uniformizers $\Psi, \Psi'$ of a polyfold structure and a sequence $(q_k, \phi_k, q'_k)$ in $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $q_k \to q$ in $O$ and $q'_k \to q'$ in $O'$, there are an element $a \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $s(a) = q$, $t(a) = q'$ and a strictly increasing subsequence converging to $a$. *Proposition 17.1.11* (p. 750, new in the 2021 edition; stronger) ⟦Chapter17/TransitionProperness · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_11⟧: for every uniformizer $\Psi$ of $F$ and every $q \in O$ there is an open $U(q) \ni q$ such that for every uniformizer $\Psi'$ of $F$ the map $t : s^{-1}(\mathrm{cl}_O U(q)) \to O'$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is proper. *Definition 17.1.12* (p. 751, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter17/TransitionProperness · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasPropernessProperty⟧: this is the properness property of $C$; every polyfold structure has it ⟦Chapter17/TransitionProperness · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_12⟧.

*Readings.* "Proper" is properness of the map $t$ restricted to the subspace $s^{-1}(\mathrm{cl}_O U(q))$, the form used in Definition 7.1.3 (3); $U(q)$ is chosen before $\Psi'$, as printed. Lemma 17.1.10 and Proposition 17.1.11 are proved for every polyfold structure. The text's proof of Lemma 17.1.10 uses the sc-smoothness of $m$ together with Lemma 17.1.9, which as we read it needs the continuity of $\hat\Psi'$; the formalization replaces it by counting over the fibres of $s$, which uses only Definition 17.1.8 (a).

**Proof of the bound on p. 749.** *Step 1, the map $G' \to$ fibre.* For $g' \in G'$, the composite of $\hat\Psi'(g', q'_0) = (q'_0, \Psi'(g', q'_0), g' \ast q'_0)$ and $a_0$ is the tuple $(q, \Psi'(g', q'_0) \circ \phi_0, g' \ast q'_0)$, an element over $q$. *Step 2, the inverse.* For $a = (q, \phi, p)$ over $q$, the morphism $\phi \circ \phi_0^{-1} : \Psi'(q'_0) \to \Psi'(p)$ is, by fullness of $\Psi'$, $\Psi'(g', q'_0)$ for some $g'$ with $g' \ast q'_0 = p$; send $a$ to this $g'$. *Step 3, inverse bijections.* Starting from $g'$, $(\Psi'(g', q'_0) \circ \phi_0) \circ \phi_0^{-1} = \Psi'(g', q'_0)$, and $\Psi'$ is injective on morphisms, so $g'$ comes back. Starting from $a$, $\Psi'(g', q'_0) \circ \phi_0 = \phi \circ \phi_0^{-1} \circ \phi_0 = \phi$ and $g' \ast q'_0 = p$, so $a$ comes back. *Step 4, counting.* If the fibre over $q$ is empty its cardinality is $0$; otherwise Step 3 gives a bijection with $G'$. The bound is the inequality, and the equality is the stronger form. A related fact used later: $q$ is a source of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ iff $\Psi(q)$ is isomorphic to an object of $\Psi'(O')$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.src_mem_range_iff⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 17.1.10.** *Step 1, an element over the limits* ⟦Chapter17/TransitionProperness · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_mor_of_tendsto⟧. The maps $q \mapsto |\Psi(q)|$ and $q' \mapsto |\Psi'(q')|$ are continuous into $|C|$, and $|\Psi(q_k)| = |\Psi'(q'_k)|$ because $\phi_k$ is an isomorphism. Since $|C|$ is metrizable, hence Hausdorff, the two limits agree: $|\Psi(q)| = |\Psi'(q')|$, so there is $\phi : \Psi(q) \to \Psi'(q')$, and $a_0 = (q, \phi, q')$ is an element of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ over $q$ with target $q'$.

*Step 2, the sheets near $q$* ⟦Chapter17/TransitionProperness · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.eq_sheet_of_src_mem⟧. By the bound, the elements over $q$ are $\gamma_{g'}$, $g' \in G'$, pairwise distinct. They are finitely many points of the Hausdorff space $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, so they have pairwise disjoint open neighbourhoods. By Definition 17.1.8 (a), around each $\gamma_{g'}$ there is a sheet on which $s$ is an sc-diffeomorphism, with inverse $\sigma_{g'}$; shrink it into the disjoint neighbourhood. Let $N$ be the intersection of the $\sharp G'$ images, an open neighbourhood of $q$. For $x \in N$, the $\sigma_{g'}(x)$ are elements over $x$ lying in pairwise disjoint sets, hence $\sharp G'$ distinct elements over $x$. By the bound there are at most $\sharp G'$ elements over $x$, so every element $b$ with $s(b) \in N$ equals $\sigma_{g'}(s(b))$ for some $g'$. Each $\sigma_{g'}$ is continuous at $q$ and satisfies $s(\sigma_{g'}(x)) = x$.

*Step 3, one sheet infinitely often.* Eventually $q_k \in N$, so eventually $(q_k, \phi_k, q'_k) = \sigma_{g'}(q_k)$ for some $g'$. Since $G'$ is finite, one $g'$ occurs for infinitely many $k$; extract the subsequence of those $k$.

*Step 4, the limit.* Along it, $(q_k, \phi_k, q'_k) = \sigma_{g'}(q_k) \to \sigma_{g'}(q) =: a$, by continuity of $\sigma_{g'}$ at $q$. Then $s(a) = q$, and $t(a) = \lim t(\sigma_{g'}(q_k)) = \lim q'_k = q'$ by continuity of $t$ and uniqueness of limits in the Hausdorff space $O'$.

**Proof of Proposition 17.1.11.** *Step 1, lifting convergence from $G\backslash O$* ⟦Chapter17/TransitionProperness · GroupoidStr.exists_subseq_tendsto_smul⟧. For a finite group acting by homeomorphisms on a first countable space $O$: if $|x_k| \to |a|$ in $G\backslash O$, then $x_{k_j} \to g \ast a$ for some $g$ and some subsequence. Take a decreasing neighbourhood basis $B_n$ of $a$. The image of the interior of $B_n$ in $G\backslash O$ is open (the quotient map is open), so eventually some translate $h \ast x_k$ lies in $B_n$. If no single $h$ worked for infinitely many $k$ at every level $n$, a finite maximum of the bad levels would give a contradiction; so one $h$ has $h \ast x_k \in B_n$ frequently for every $n$, and a diagonal extraction gives $h \ast x_{k_j} \to a$, that is $x_{k_j} \to h^{-1} \ast a$.

*Step 2, the choice of $U(q)$.* The footprint of $\Psi$ is an open neighbourhood of $|\Psi(q)|$ in the metrizable, hence regular, space $|C|$, so there is a closed neighbourhood $T$ of $|\Psi(q)|$ inside the footprint. Let $U(q)$ be the preimage of the interior of $T$ under $q \mapsto |\Psi(q)|$: it is open, contains $q$, and $|\Psi(\mathrm{cl}\, U(q))| \subset T$, since $T$ is closed. This is the text's condition $\mathrm{cl}_{|C|}|\Psi(U(q))| \subset |\Psi(O)|$, and $U(q)$ does not depend on $\Psi'$.

*Step 3, reduction to sequential compactness.* $t$ is continuous on $s^{-1}(\mathrm{cl}\, U(q))$, and $O'$ is metrizable. For a compact $K' \subset O'$, the preimage of $K'$ is compact iff its image $\{a : s(a) \in \mathrm{cl}\, U(q),\ t(a) \in K'\}$ in $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is, and in the metrizable space $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ it suffices to show that this set is sequentially compact.

*Step 4, the targets.* Let $a_k = (q_k, \phi_k, q'_k)$ be a sequence in the set. $K'$ is sequentially compact, so a subsequence has $q'_k \to a' \in K'$.

*Step 5, the sources* (the step (17.5) of the text). Then $|\Psi(q_k)| = |\Psi'(q'_k)| \to |\Psi'(a')|$, and $|\Psi(q_k)| \in T$ with $T$ closed, so $|\Psi'(a')| \in T \subset |\Psi(O)|$: there is $a \in O$ with $|\Psi(a)| = |\Psi'(a')|$. Since $|\Psi|$ is an embedding of $G\backslash O$ into $|C|$, $|q_k| \to |a|$ in $G\backslash O$. By Step 1, along a further subsequence $q_k \to g \ast a$.

*Step 6, Lemma 17.1.10.* Now $q_k \to g \ast a$ and $q'_k \to a'$, so Lemma 17.1.10 gives a further subsequence converging to some $b$ with $s(b) = g \ast a$ and $t(b) = a'$. Then $s(b) \in \mathrm{cl}\, U(q)$, a closed set containing all $q_k$, and $t(b) = a' \in K'$. So the set is sequentially compact, the preimage is compact, and $t$ is proper.

**Definition 17.1.12.** The properness property is the statement of Proposition 17.1.11 as a property of $(F, M)$, so every polyfold structure has it. Witness: the reflection structure ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_hasPropernessProperty⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves Lemma 17.1.10 through the sheets $U(\gamma_i)$ of $s$ over a common $U(q)$, writes every element over $q_k$ as $\Psi'(g'_k, q'_k) \circ \phi_k$, and uses $m$ and Lemma 17.1.9. The formalization uses the same sheets but closes the argument by counting: the bound of p. 749, an equality on non-empty fibres, forces every element over a point near $q$ onto one of the $\sharp G'$ sheets through the elements over $q$. This needs neither $m$ nor the continuity of $\hat\Psi'$, so Lemma 17.1.10, Proposition 17.1.11 and with them the properness of the models hold for every polyfold structure, including those for which Lemma 17.1.9 does not hold as we read it. Proposition 17.1.11 follows the text's route: a closed neighbourhood inside the footprint, the lift of convergence from $G\backslash O$, then Lemma 17.1.10.

### XVII.7 Lemma 17.1.13 and the paragraph on p. 752: natural charts and the structure maps of $X_\Psi$, p. 752

**Statement** (same). *Admissible triples* (17.6) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple⟧: at $a = (o, \phi, o') \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$, open sets $U(o) \subset O$, $U(o, \phi, o') \ni a$ in $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ and $U(o') \subset O'$ with $s : U(o, \phi, o') \to U(o)$ and $t : U(o, \phi, o') \to U(o')$ sc-diffeomorphisms; $s$ restricted to $U(o, \phi, o')$ is a natural source-type chart and $t$ a natural target-type chart. Every element has one ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_admissibleTriple⟧. *Lemma 17.1.13* (p. 752, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_13⟧: for two admissible triples the transition map $s \circ (s|_{U_1})^{-1}$ is the identity on $s(U_1 \cap U_2)$, and likewise for $t$. *The paragraph on p. 752* (same): for a family $\Psi = (\Psi_\lambda)$ of uniformizers, the M-polyfolds $X = \bigsqcup_\lambda O_\lambda$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ModelObj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelObjPolyfold⟧ and $\mathbf X = \bigsqcup_{(\lambda, \lambda')} M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\lambda'})$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ModelMor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelMorPolyfold⟧ (17.7) form a small category $X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelGroupoid⟧ whose source and target are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_modelSrc⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_modelTgt⟧, whose inversion is an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_modelInv⟧, whose unit map is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_modelUnit⟧, and whose multiplication on $\mathbf X\,{}_s\!\times_t \mathbf X$ is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothComp_model⟧.

**Proof of the existence of admissible triples.** By Definition 17.1.8 (a), $s$ is an sc-diffeomorphism on an open $W_1 \ni a$ and $t$ on an open $W_2 \ni a$. Both restrict to the open $W_1 \cap W_2$ ⟦Chapter7/LocalDiffeo · MPolyfold.IsScDiffeoOn.restrict⟧, with images $U(o)$, $U(o')$.

**Proof of Lemma 17.1.13.** For $x = s(b)$ with $b \in U_1 \cap U_2$: $(s|_{U_1})^{-1}(s(b)) = b$, since $b \in U_1$, so $s((s|_{U_1})^{-1}(x)) = s(b) = x$. The same computation with $t$ gives the second half. The two charts of the lemma are restrictions of the one map $s$, which is why the transition is the identity; the lemma is what lets the text speak of "the" natural charts of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$.

**The groupoid $X_\Psi$.** *Step 1, the pull-back groupoid* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GroupoidStr.pullback⟧. For a map $f : X \to \mathrm{Obj}(C)$, the pull-back $f^*C$ has objects $X$ and morphisms the transition set $M(f, f)$ of Definition 17.1.5, with its unit, inversion and composition; on pairs that are not composable the composition returns its first argument, as everywhere in the formalization of Chapter 7. *Step 2, the disjoint union is a transition set* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelMorEquiv⟧. With $f(q_\lambda) = \Psi_\lambda(q_\lambda)$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelObjMap⟧, the disjoint union $\mathbf X$ is in bijection with the transition set of $X \to C \leftarrow X$: an element $(q_\lambda, \phi, q_{\lambda'})$ of the piece $(\lambda, \lambda')$ is a tuple with entries in the disjoint union. *Step 3, transport* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GroupoidStr.ofEquiv⟧. The groupoid $f^*C$ moved along this bijection is $X_\Psi$: $s(q_\lambda, \phi, q_{\lambda'}) = q_\lambda$, $t(q_\lambda, \phi, q_{\lambda'}) = q_{\lambda'}$, units $(q, 1, q)$, inverses $(q_{\lambda'}, \phi^{-1}, q_\lambda)$, and $(q_{\lambda'}, \phi', q_{\lambda''}) \circ (q_\lambda, \phi, q_{\lambda'}) = (q_\lambda, \phi' \circ \phi, q_{\lambda''})$ in the piece $(\lambda, \lambda'')$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelGroupoid_comp_of⟧. The groupoid laws come from those of the transition set.

**Proof of the paragraph on p. 752** (G.198). The M-polyfold structures of $X$ and $\mathbf X$ are Chapter 8's disjoint unions ⟦Chapter8/SigmaPolyfold · MPolyfold.sigma⟧. *Step 1, $s$ and $t$.* A map out of a disjoint union is a local sc-diffeomorphism iff it is one on every piece ⟦Chapter8/SigmaPolyfold · MPolyfold.isLocalScDiffeo_sigma_iff⟧. On the piece $(\lambda, \lambda')$, $s$ is $s : M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\lambda'}) \to O_\lambda$ followed by the inclusion of $O_\lambda$, a composite of local sc-diffeomorphisms by Definition 17.1.8 (a); likewise $t$. *Step 2, $u$.* On $O_\lambda$ the unit is $u : O_\lambda \to M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\lambda)$ followed by the inclusion of the piece $(\lambda, \lambda)$, sc-smooth by (b). *Step 3, $\iota$* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_modelInv⟧. The inversion maps the piece $(\lambda, \lambda')$ to $(\lambda', \lambda)$ by $\iota$, sc-smooth by (b); as $\iota \circ \iota = \mathrm{id}$, it is an sc-diffeomorphism. *Step 4, $m$.* The text says "it follows immediately" that $\mathbf X\,{}_s\!\times_t \mathbf X$ has a natural M-polyfold structure and that $m$ is sc-smooth. The structure is the sub-M-polyfold structure of Proposition 2.5.20, as in Definition 7.1.3 (4) ⟦Chapter7/EpGroupoid · IsScSmoothComp⟧. Sc-smoothness of $m$ on it is equivalent to its sheet form ⟦Chapter7/EpGroupoid · isScSmoothComp_iff_sheet⟧ ⟦Chapter7/EpGroupoid · IsScSmoothCompSheet⟧: for every sheet $s : W \to V$ of $\mathbf X$ with inverse $\sigma$, the map $\psi \mapsto \sigma(t(\psi)) \circ \psi$ is sc-smooth on $t^{-1}(V)$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothCompSheet_model⟧. This is proved piece by piece. Take $b_0$ in the piece $M(\Psi_i, \Psi_j)$ with $t(b_0) \in V$, and write $\sigma(t(b_0)) = a_0$, which lies in a piece $M(\Psi_j, \Psi_k)$ (its source is $t(b_0) \in O_j$). Take a sheet $s : W' \to V'$ of $M(\Psi_j, \Psi_k)$ at $a_0$, with inverse $\sigma'$. On the open set $N$ of those $b$ with $t(b) \in V$, $\sigma(t(b))$ in the image of $W'$ and $t(b) \in V'$, the two local inverses agree, $\sigma(t(b)) = \sigma'(t(b))$ in the piece $(j, k)$, because both are elements of $W'$ with source $t(b)$. There the map is $b \mapsto m(\sigma'(t(b)), b)$, the multiplication of Definition 17.1.8 (c) restricted to a sheet, which is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · MPolyfold.IsScSmoothOnFibredProduct.isScSmoothOn_sheet⟧ (the second projection of the fibred product is an sc-diffeomorphism over the sheet ⟦Chapter2/FibredProduct · MPolyfold.isScDiffeoOn_snd_fibredProduct⟧). Followed by the inclusion of the piece $(i, k)$, it is the map of $X_\Psi$ on $N$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The category $X_\Psi$ and its structure maps are built for any family $\Psi$; the covering condition enters only with Lemma 17.1.15. The "natural M-polyfold structure" on $\mathbf X\,{}_s\!\times_t \mathbf X$ is read as the sub-M-polyfold structure, and the sc-smoothness of $m$, which the text calls immediate, is proved through the sheet form of Chapter 7, reducing it on each sheet to condition (c) of one transition M-polyfold.

### XVII.8 Theorem 17.1.14 (2017: Theorem 17.1) and Lemma 17.1.15 (2017: Lemma 17.2): the model $X_\Psi$ and the equivalence $\Gamma_\Psi$, pp. 753–754

**Statement** (same). *Theorem 17.1.14* (p. 753) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_14⟧: for a covering family $\Psi$ of a polyfold structure $(F, M)$ there is an ep-groupoid on $X_\Psi$ whose groupoid is the category of p. 752 and whose object and morphism M-polyfolds are those of (17.7); if $(F, M)$ is tame, it is tame. It is the ep-groupoid $X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelEpGroupoid⟧, also an object of the category $\mathcal{EP}$ of ep-groupoids ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelEP⟧. *Lemma 17.1.15* (p. 754) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_15⟧: for a covering family, the functor $\Gamma_\Psi : X_\Psi \to C$, $q_\lambda \mapsto \Psi_\lambda(q_\lambda)$, $(q_\lambda, \phi, q_{\lambda'}) \mapsto \phi$ (17.10) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor⟧, is an equivalence of categories, and $|\Gamma_\Psi| : |X_\Psi| \to |C|$ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelOrbitHomeomorph⟧.

*Readings.* $C$ carries no M-polyfold structure, so $\Gamma_\Psi$ is a functor of groupoids and "equivalence of categories" is the equivalence of the underlying categories. The parts that do not use the covering condition are proved for any family $\Psi$; a family is covering if its footprints cover $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsCoveringFamily⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 17.1.14.** The text: only properness has to be checked, the other conditions being the paragraph of p. 752. *Step 1, the structure maps*: the paragraph on p. 752, proved above. *Step 2, properness* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_isProperMap_model⟧. Definition 7.1.3 asks, at every object $x$ of $X$, an open $V \ni x$ with $t : s^{-1}(\mathrm{cl}_X V) \to X$ proper. Let $x = q \in O_{\lambda_0}$ and take $U(q)$ from Proposition 17.1.11, so that $t : s^{-1}(\mathrm{cl}\, U(q)) \to O_\lambda$ is proper in every $M(\Psi_{\lambda_0}, \Psi_\lambda)$. Put $V = U(q)$ inside the piece $O_{\lambda_0}$ of $X$; it is open, and its closure in $X$ is its closure in $O_{\lambda_0}$, the inclusion of a piece being a closed embedding. The set $D = s^{-1}(\mathrm{cl}_X V)$ meets only the pieces $(\lambda_0, \lambda)$ of $\mathbf X$, where it is $s^{-1}(\mathrm{cl}\, U(q))$. *Step 3, proper on every piece gives proper* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · isProperMap_domRestrict_sigmaMap⟧. A map between disjoint unions, given piecewise by continuous maps $f_k : A_k \to B_{\tau(k)}$, restricted to a set $D$, is proper if every restriction $D \cap A_k \to B_{\tau(k)}$ is proper and at most one piece of $D$ lies over each piece of the target. The proof uses the characterization of proper maps as continuous closed maps with compact fibres: over each piece $B_j$ of the target, the image of a closed set and the fibre of a point come from the single piece of $D$ over $j$, or are empty. Here $\tau(\lambda_0, \lambda) = \lambda$, so at most one non-empty piece of $D$ lies over each $O_\lambda$; on the non-empty pieces the restriction is proper by Proposition 17.1.11, and on the empty ones trivially ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · isProperMap_of_isEmpty⟧. *Step 4, the ep-groupoid.* With Steps 1–3 every field of an ep-groupoid is supplied. *Step 5, tameness* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_modelEpGroupoid⟧.
