A disjoint union of M-polyfolds is tame iff every piece is ⟦Chapter8/SigmaPolyfold · MPolyfold.isTame_sigma_iff⟧; each $O_\lambda$ is the M-polyfold of a uniformizer in some $F(\alpha)$, tame if $(F, M)$ is.

**Proof of Lemma 17.1.15.** *Step 1, $\Gamma_\Psi$ is a functor.* It preserves source and target because $\phi : \Psi_\lambda(q_\lambda) \to \Psi_{\lambda'}(q_{\lambda'})$, units because the unit of $X_\Psi$ has middle entry $1$, and composition because the middle entry of a composite is $\phi' \circ \phi$.

*Step 2, full* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_full⟧. A morphism $\psi : \Psi_\lambda(q_\lambda) \to \Psi_{\lambda'}(q_{\lambda'})$ of $C$ is $\Gamma_\Psi(q_\lambda, \psi, q_{\lambda'})$, the tuple being an element of $M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\lambda'})$ by definition. *Step 3, faithful* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_faithful⟧: a morphism of $X_\Psi$ is determined by its source, its target and its middle entry. So the map on morphism sets is the "trivial bijection" of the text, for any family.

*Step 4, essentially surjective* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_essSurj⟧. Here the covering condition enters. For an object $\alpha$, $|\alpha|$ lies in the footprint of some $\Psi_\lambda$, so $|\alpha| = |\Psi_\lambda(q)|$ for some $q$, and $\Psi_\lambda(q) \cong \alpha$.

*Step 5, $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is continuous* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.continuous_modelOrbitMap⟧. The orbit map of $X_\Psi$ is a quotient map (Proposition 7.1.8, Part VII) ⟦Chapter7/Basic · EpGroupoid.proposition_7_1_8_isOpenQuotientMap⟧, and $|\Gamma_\Psi| \circ \pi$ is, on each $O_\lambda$, the continuous map $q \mapsto |\Psi_\lambda(q)|$.

*Step 6, $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is open* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isOpenMap_modelOrbitMap⟧. The image of an open $W \subset |X_\Psi|$ is the union over $\lambda$ of the images of $\pi^{-1}(W) \cap O_\lambda$ under $q \mapsto |\Psi_\lambda(q)|$, each open because this map is open (the orbit map of $G_\lambda \ltimes O_\lambda$ is open and $|\Psi_\lambda|$ is an open embedding).

*Step 7, bijective, hence a homeomorphism* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_modelOrbitMap⟧. An equivalence of groupoids induces a bijection of orbit sets ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · GroupoidFunctor.bijective_orbitMap_of_isEquivalence⟧; with Steps 5 and 6, $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is a homeomorphism. The lemma for a covering family is Steps 2–7 ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_15_of_isCovering⟧.

**The witness.** For the reflection structure and the covering family of two copies of the reflection uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionPairFamily⟧, $X_\Psi$ is a tame ep-groupoid; $(0, 1, 0)$ is a morphism from the first copy to the second, and $(0, (-1, 0), 0)$ an automorphism of $0$ in one copy other than the unit; $\Gamma_\Psi$ is an equivalence and $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionPair_model⟧. The model has non-trivial isotropy and morphisms between distinct pieces, so it is not weak.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves properness by a sequence argument: a sequence with targets in a compact set may be taken in one $M(\Psi_{\lambda_0}, \Psi_{\lambda'})$. The formalization proves the general fact that a piecewise map of disjoint unions that is proper on each piece, with at most one piece over each target piece, is proper, through closed maps with compact fibres; the neighbourhood is $U(q_{\lambda_0}) \subset O_{\lambda_0}$, as in the text. Since Proposition 17.1.11 holds for every polyfold structure, so does Theorem 17.1.14: the model needs no continuity of $\hat\Psi$. In Lemma 17.1.15 the full and faithful parts hold for every family and only essential surjectivity uses the covering condition, which with the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ is always available (Definition 17.1.6 above).

### XVII.9 Remark 17.1.16 and Definition 17.1.17 (new in the 2021 edition), with (17.13), p. 755: $|X_\Psi|$, polyfold structures and weak polyfold structures on $|C|$

**Statement** (same). *Remark 17.1.16* (p. 755) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_1_16⟧: for a covering family $\Psi$ of the polyfold construction $(F, M)$ on the GCT $C$, the orbit space $|X_\Psi|$ of the model is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_modelOrbitSpace⟧ and paracompact ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧, and if $X_\Psi$ satisfies (ZHOU), the pair $(X_\Psi, |\Gamma_\Psi|)$ is a polyfold structure on $|C|$ in the sense of Definition 16.1.1 ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelPolyfoldStructure⟧, with model $X_\Psi$ and homeomorphism $|\Gamma_\Psi|$. *Definition 17.1.17* (p. 755) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure⟧: a weak polyfold structure on a paracompact space $Z$ is a pair $(X, \beta)$ of an ep-groupoid $X$ and a homeomorphism $\beta : |X| \to Z$; (ZHOU) is not asked. The sentences after the definition: $|X|$ is then paracompact ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure.hasParacompactOrbitSpace⟧ and metrizable ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure.metrizableSpace_orbitSpace⟧, so $Z$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure.metrizableSpace⟧. *(17.13)* ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.weakStructure⟧: $(X_\Psi, |\Gamma_\Psi|)$ is a weak polyfold structure on $|C|$.

Throughout this and the following sections, $C$ is a GCT (Definition 17.1.1), $(F, M)$ a polyfold construction on it in the sense of Definition 17.1.8, a covering family $\Psi = (\Psi_\lambda)_{\lambda \in \Lambda}$ is a family of uniformizers of $F$ whose footprints cover $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.CoveringFamily⟧, and $X_\Psi$ is the ep-groupoid of Theorem 17.1.14 above, with objects $\bigsqcup_\lambda O_\lambda$ and morphisms $\bigsqcup_{\lambda,\mu} M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu)$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelEP⟧. The functor $\Gamma_\Psi : X_\Psi \to C$ of (17.10) sends $q_\lambda$ to $\Psi_\lambda(q_\lambda)$ and $(q_\lambda, \varphi, q_\mu)$ to $\varphi$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor⟧; by Lemma 17.1.15 above it is an equivalence of categories and $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_15⟧, which the formalization packages as a homeomorphism $|X_\Psi| \cong |C|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelOrbitHomeomorph⟧.

**Proof of Remark 17.1.16.** *Step 1, metrizable.* $|C|$ is metrizable, as Definition 17.1.1 requires of a GCT. $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is a homeomorphism, in particular an embedding of $|X_\Psi|$ into a metrizable space, so $|X_\Psi|$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_modelOrbitSpace⟧.

*Step 2, paracompact.* A metrizable space is paracompact (A. H. Stone's theorem, in Mathlib), so $|X_\Psi|$ is paracompact ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧. This is condition (1) of Definition 16.1.1.

*Step 3, the polyfold structure.* Under (ZHOU) for $X_\Psi$, condition (2) of Definition 16.1.1 holds as well, and the structure is assembled from $X_\Psi$, Step 2, the hypothesis and the homeomorphism $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelPolyfoldStructure⟧ ⟦Chapter16/PolyfoldStructure · PolyfoldStructure⟧. That its model is $X_\Psi$ and its homeomorphism is $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ holds by construction ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_1_16⟧.

**Proof of the sentences after Definition 17.1.17.** *Step 1.* $\beta$ is a homeomorphism, so its inverse is a closed embedding of $|X|$ into the paracompact space $Z$, and a closed subspace of a paracompact space is paracompact ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure.hasParacompactOrbitSpace⟧. *Step 2.* Theorem 7.3.1 (Part VII) gives that the orbit space of an ep-groupoid with paracompact orbit space is metrizable ⟦Chapter7/OrbitTopology · EpGroupoid.theorem_7_3_1_metrizable⟧, so $|X|$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure.metrizableSpace_orbitSpace⟧. *Step 3.* $\beta^{-1}$ embeds $Z$ into $|X|$, so $Z$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure.metrizableSpace⟧. A weak polyfold structure is an orbit chart in the sense of Remark 10.3.18 ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · WeakPolyfoldStructure.toOrbitChart⟧, and every polyfold structure of Definition 16.1.1 is a weak one, by forgetting (1) and (2) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · PolyfoldStructure.toWeakPolyfoldStructure⟧.

**Proof of (17.13).** Definition 17.1.17 asks that $Z = |C|$ be paracompact. $|C|$ is metrizable, hence paracompact ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.paracompactSpace_orbitSpace⟧. The pair $(X_\Psi, |\Gamma_\Psi|)$, with the homeomorphism of Lemma 17.1.15, is then a weak polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.weakStructure⟧, with model $X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.weakStructure_X⟧ and homeomorphism $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.weakStructure_β⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Nowhere in substance. The text derives the paracompactness of $|X_\Psi|$ from its metrizability, as Step 2 does; for weak polyfold structures it goes the other way, from the paracompactness of $Z$ through Theorem 7.3.1, and the formalization follows each route as printed. (ZHOU) in Remark 17.1.16 is a hypothesis on $X_\Psi$, as in the text; by Proposition 17.1.24 below it does not depend on the covering family.

### XVII.10 The construction on pp. 755–756 (2017: §17.1): the inclusions $A$, $A'$, the weak fibred product and the diagram (17.15)

**Statement** (same). For covering families $\Psi$ (index $\Lambda$) and $\Psi'$ (index $\Lambda'$) let $\Psi'' = \Psi \sqcup \Psi'$, indexed by the disjoint union $\Lambda \sqcup \Lambda'$. The inclusions $A : X_\Psi \to X_{\Psi''}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.inclLeft⟧ and $A' : X_{\Psi'} \to X_{\Psi''}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.inclRight⟧ are sc-smooth equivalences of ep-groupoids ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_inclLeft⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_inclRight⟧ with $\Gamma_{\Psi''} \circ A = \Gamma_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_comp_inclLeft⟧ and $\Gamma_{\Psi''} \circ A' = \Gamma_{\Psi'}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_comp_inclRight⟧. The weak fibred product $X_\Psi \times_{X_{\Psi''}} X_{\Psi'}$ is defined ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.admissible_incl⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFibredProduct⟧, and its two projections form the diagram (17.15) $d : X_\Psi \xleftarrow{\pi_1} X_\Psi \times_{X_{\Psi''}} X_{\Psi'} \xrightarrow{\pi_2} X_{\Psi'}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.compareDiagram⟧, whose class is the generalized map $f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = [d]$ of (17.14) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.compare⟧. The characterization: for every family $\Phi$ containing $\Psi$ and $\Psi'$, $i(\Psi' \to \Phi) \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = i(\Psi \to \Phi)$ in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.compare_comp_incl⟧, where $i$ is the localization functor of Chapter 10.

*Reading.* $\Psi \sqcup \Psi'$ is the family indexed by the sum $\Lambda \sqcup \Lambda'$, so for $\Psi = \Psi'$ every uniformizer occurs twice; the text's notation for the disjoint union presumably intends this. The text says that one readily verifies that $A$ and $A'$ are equivalences; this is point G.199, settled by the steps below.

**The reindexing functors.** The inclusions are instances of one construction. For a family $\Phi$ indexed by $\Lambda$ and a map $\sigma : K \to \Lambda$, the functor $X_{\Phi \circ \sigma} \to X_\Phi$ sends $q_k$ to $q_{\sigma(k)}$ and $(q_k, \varphi, q_l)$ to $(q_{\sigma(k)}, \varphi, q_{\sigma(l)})$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelMap⟧.

*Step 1, it is an ep-functor.* On every piece it is the identity of $O_k$, respectively of $M(\Phi_{\sigma(k)}, \Phi_{\sigma(l)})$, so it is sc-smooth on objects and on morphisms; it commutes with source, target and units by definition, and with composition because the composition of $X_\Phi$ is computed in $C$ on the middle components ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelGroupoid_comp_of⟧.

*Step 2, compatible with $\Gamma$.* $\Gamma_\Phi$ composed with it is $\Gamma_{\Phi \circ \sigma}$, on the nose ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_comp_modelMap⟧.

*Step 3, an equivalence when $\Phi \circ \sigma$ covers* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_modelMap⟧. If $\Phi \circ \sigma$ covers $|C|$, so does $\Phi$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsCoveringFamily.of_comp⟧. By Lemma 17.1.15, $\Gamma_\Phi$ and $\Gamma_\Phi \circ (\text{reindexing}) = \Gamma_{\Phi\circ\sigma}$ are equivalences of categories ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_15_of_isCovering⟧, so the reindexing functor is an equivalence of categories (two-out-of-three for equivalences, in Mathlib). On objects it is a local sc-diffeomorphism, being the identity on each piece. Lemma 10.1.2 (Part X) ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · EpFunctor.lemma_10_1_2⟧ turns these two facts into an equivalence of ep-groupoids.

*Step 4, the inclusion $X_\Psi \to X_\Phi$.* For $\Phi \circ \sigma = \Psi$ the inclusion is the reindexing functor preceded by the identification of $X_\Psi$ with $X_{\Phi \circ \sigma}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.incl⟧. It commutes with $\Gamma$ on objects ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.incl_obj⟧ and as functors ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_comp_incl⟧, it is an equivalence when $\Psi$ covers ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_incl⟧, inclusions compose ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.incl_comp⟧, and the inclusion of a family into itself is the identity ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.incl_id⟧.

**Proof for $A$, $A'$ and the diagram.** *Step 1.* $A$ and $A'$ are the inclusions along the two embeddings of $\Lambda$ and $\Lambda'$ into $\Lambda \sqcup \Lambda'$. Since $\Psi$ and $\Psi'$ cover, Step 4 above makes them sc-smooth equivalences and gives $\Gamma_{\Psi''} \circ A = \Gamma_\Psi$, $\Gamma_{\Psi''} \circ A' = \Gamma_{\Psi'}$, which is the text's claim on p. 756.

*Step 2, the weak fibred product.* The weak fibred product of Chapter 10 ⟦Chapter10/Localization · EPLocalization.wfp⟧ needs one of the two functors to be an equivalence; $A'$ is one, so the pair is admissible ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.admissible_incl⟧ ⟦Chapter10/WeakFibredProductTheorem · WeakFibredProduct.Admissible.of_isEquivalence_right⟧. Its objects are triples $(x, \varphi'', z)$ of $x \in X_\Psi$, $z \in X_{\Psi'}$ and a morphism $\varphi'' : A(x) \to A'(z)$ of $X_{\Psi''}$.

*Step 3, the diagram.* $\pi_1$ is an equivalence because $A'$ is (Theorem 10.2.2, Part X) ⟦Chapter10/WeakFibredProductTheorem · WeakFibredProduct.isEquivalence_projLeft⟧, so $d$ is a diagram in the sense of Chapter 10, with left leg an equivalence ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.compareDiagram⟧, and its class $[d]$ is a morphism of the localization ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationCategory · EPLocalization.EPLoc.homMk⟧.

**Proof of the characterization** ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.compare_comp_incl⟧. Let $\Phi$ be indexed by $K$, with $\Phi \circ \sigma = \Psi$ and $\Phi \circ \sigma' = \Psi'$.

*Step 1, factor through $\Psi''$.* The map $\Lambda \sqcup \Lambda' \to K$ given by $\sigma$ and $\sigma'$ realizes $\Psi''$ inside $\Phi$, and by the composition of inclusions, $A$ followed by the inclusion $X_{\Psi''} \to X_\Phi$ is the inclusion $X_\Psi \to X_\Phi$, and likewise for $A'$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.incl_comp⟧.

*Step 2, the square of the weak fibred product.* $A \circ \pi_1$ and $A' \circ \pi_2$ are naturally equivalent ⟦Chapter10/WeakFibredProductTheorem · WeakFibredProduct.naturallyEquivalent⟧, and naturally equivalent ep-functors have the same image in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationUniversal · EPLocalization.map_eq_of_naturallyEquivalent⟧. With Step 1, $i(\pi_1) \circ i(X_\Psi \to X_\Phi) = i(\pi_2) \circ i(X_{\Psi'} \to X_\Phi)$ (composites written in diagrammatic order).

*Step 3, invert $\pi_1$.* $i(\pi_1)$ is invertible because $\pi_1$ is an equivalence ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationCategory · EPLocalization.EPLoc.isInvertedBy_localizationFunctor⟧, and $[d] = i(\pi_1)^{-1} \circ i(\pi_2)$ ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationCategory · EPLocalization.EPLoc.homMk_eq⟧. Multiplying Step 2 by $i(\pi_1)^{-1}$ gives the identity. When $\Psi'$ covers, the inclusion $X_{\Psi'} \to X_\Phi$ is an equivalence, so $f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = i(\Psi' \to \Phi)^{-1} \circ i(\Psi \to \Phi)$ for every such $\Phi$: the generalized map does not depend on the family used to compare.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text works with the one union $\Psi \sqcup \Psi'$. The formalization proves, in addition, the characterization through any family $\Phi$ containing both, which is what makes Theorem 17.1.18 and the composition law of pp. 761–765 short (below). The equivalence of $A$ and $A'$, which the text leaves to the reader, is reduced to Lemma 17.1.15 and Lemma 10.1.2.

### XVII.11 Theorem 17.1.18 (2017: Theorem 17.2) and Remark 17.1.19 (new in the 2021 edition), pp. 756–757: the canonical generalized isomorphisms $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$

**Statement** (same). *Theorem 17.1.18* (p. 756) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_18⟧: for covering families $\Psi$, $\Psi'$, $\Psi''$, the generalized map $f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = [d]$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison⟧ is a generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_comparison⟧; $f_{\Psi,\Psi} = 1_{X_\Psi}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_self⟧; $f_{\Psi',\Psi''} \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = f_{\Psi,\Psi''}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_comp⟧; $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}| \circ |f_{\Psi,\Psi'}| = |\Gamma_\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.orbitMap_comparison⟧; and $|f_{\Psi,\Psi'}|$ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_orbitMap_comparison⟧. The identities are equalities of morphisms of $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ (Definition 10.3.12). *Remark 17.1.19* (p. 757) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_1_19⟧: for every diagram $X_\Psi \xleftarrow{F} A \xrightarrow{G} X_{\Psi'}$ representing $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ and every object $x$ of $X_\Psi$, there are an object $a$ of $A$ and a morphism $\varphi : F(a) \to x$, and $\Gamma_\Psi(x)$ is isomorphic to $\Gamma_{\Psi'}(G(a))$ in $C$.

The text's discussion before the theorem, which it closes with "one also verifies", is point G.200; the theorem summarizes it, and each clause is proved below. The weak polyfold structures that the 2021 statement also mentions are (17.13) above. The 2017 conclusion that $|C|$ is a paracompact polyfold is not part of the 2021 statement; it is Theorem 17.1.26 under (ZHOU) below.

**Proof of Theorem 17.1.18.** *Step 1, a generalized isomorphism* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_comparison⟧. The class of a diagram is invertible iff its right leg is an equivalence (Theorem 10.3.10, Part X) ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationCategory · EPLocalization.EPLoc.theorem_10_3_10⟧. The right leg $\pi_2$ is an equivalence because $A$ is one, since $\Psi$ covers (Theorem 10.2.2) ⟦Chapter10/WeakFibredProductTheorem · WeakFibredProduct.isEquivalence_projRight⟧.

*Step 2, $f_{\Psi,\Psi} = 1$* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_self⟧. Apply the characterization of the previous section ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_comp_incl⟧ with $\Phi = \Psi$ and both maps the identity of the index set. The inclusion of $\Psi$ into itself is the identity functor ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.incl_id⟧, so $1 \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi} = 1$.

*Step 3, the cocycle identity* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_comp⟧. Let $\Phi = \Psi \sqcup (\Psi' \sqcup \Psi'')$. The characterization, applied three times with the evident inclusions of the index sets, gives $j'' \circ f_{\Psi',\Psi''} = j'$, $j' \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = j$ and $j'' \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi''} = j$, where $j, j', j''$ are the images in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ of the inclusions of $X_\Psi$, $X_{\Psi'}$, $X_{\Psi''}$ into $X_\Phi$. Hence $j'' \circ (f_{\Psi',\Psi''} \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi'}) = j'' \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi''}$. Since $\Psi''$ covers, its inclusion is an equivalence ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_incl⟧ and $j''$ is invertible ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationCategory · EPLocalization.EPLoc.isInvertedBy_localizationFunctor⟧; cancelling it gives the identity. No fibred product of three diagrams enters.

*Step 4, the orbit identity* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.orbitMap_comparison⟧. The orbit map of $[d]$ is $|\pi_2| \circ |\pi_1|^{-1}$, and $|\pi_1|$ is a bijection because $\pi_1$ is an equivalence. For a point $q$ of $|X_\Psi|$ choose a class $r$ of the fibred product with $|\pi_1|(r) = q$, and an object $p = (x, \varphi'', z)$ in it. Then $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}|(|[d]|(q)) = |\Psi'_\mu(z)|$ and $|\Gamma_\Psi|(q) = |\Psi_\lambda(x)|$, and $\Gamma_{\Psi''}(\varphi'')$ is a morphism of $C$ from $\Gamma_\Psi(x)$ to $\Gamma_{\Psi'}(z)$, since $\Gamma_{\Psi''}$ commutes with the inclusions on objects ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.incl_obj⟧. So the two orbit classes agree.

*Step 5, a homeomorphism* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_orbitMap_comparison⟧. By Step 4 and the injectivity of $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}|$, $|f_{\Psi,\Psi'}| = |\Gamma_{\Psi'}|^{-1} \circ |\Gamma_\Psi|$, a composite of homeomorphisms (Lemma 17.1.15).

**Proof of Remark 17.1.19** ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_1_19⟧. *Step 1.* $F$ is an equivalence, so $|F|$ is bijective, and there is an object $a$ of $A$ with $|F(a)| = |x|$, that is a morphism $\varphi : F(a) \to x$ of $X_\Psi$. *Step 2.* $[F, G] = f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, so the orbit maps agree, and Step 4 above gives $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}(G(a))| = |\Gamma_\Psi(F(a))| = |\Gamma_\Psi(x)|$, which is the isomorphism of the objects of $C$.

**Witnesses.** *The reflection.* On the reflection GCT $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with its tautological structure ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction⟧, take one copy of the tautological uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionSingleFamily⟧ and two copies ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionPairFamily⟧. The model of the two copies is tame, has a morphism between its pieces and an automorphism of $0$ other than the unit ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionPair_model⟧. Theorem 17.1.18 holds for the pair of families, and the weak fibred product has an object $p$ with $\tau(p) = (-1, 0)$, a morphism $0 \to 0$ of $C$ other than the unit ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_comparison⟧ (this is the witness of Theorem 17.1.27 below). *Overlapping uniformizers.* For $\{\Psi_0\}$ and $\{\Psi_0, \Psi_1, \Psi_2\}$, with $\Psi_1 = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (-2, 2)$ and $\Psi_2 = \{1\} \ltimes (1, 3)$, the model is tame and has a morphism between the pieces of $\Psi_1$ and $\Psi_2$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlap_model⟧, and Theorem 17.1.18 holds ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlap_comparison⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The identity $f_{\Psi,\Psi} = 1$ and the cocycle identity are proved by comparing with the inclusions into one larger family, not by simplifying fibred products of diagrams; the outcome is the same equality in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$. Remark 17.1.19 says that the two squares commute "with the obvious care"; the formal statement makes the care explicit: they commute up to isomorphism in $C$, through a morphism of $X_\Psi$.

### XVII.12 Definitions 17.1.20, 17.1.21 (new in the 2021 edition), p. 757, and the composition of comparison diagrams on pp. 761–765, (17.20)–(17.30) (2017: §17.1): the covering category and its embedding

**Statement.** *Definition 17.1.20* (same) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.CoveringCategory⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_20⟧: the covering category $\mathrm{COV}_C$ has the covering families as objects and exactly one morphism between any ordered pair; it is a connected simple system ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · IsConnectedSimpleSystem⟧, every morphism is an isomorphism, and the only endomorphism is the identity. *Definition 17.1.21* (same) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.coveringEmbedding⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_21⟧: the natural embedding $\mathrm{COV}_C \to \mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ sends $\Psi$ to $X_\Psi$ and the morphism $\Psi \to \Psi'$ to $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, every image a generalized isomorphism. *The composition on pp. 761–765* (restated) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.compare_comp_incl⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_comp⟧: the composite of the classes of the diagrams $d_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ and $d_{\Psi',\Psi''}$ is the class of $d_{\Psi,\Psi''}$, which makes the assignment of Definition 17.1.21 a functor.

**Proof of Definition 17.1.20.** $\mathrm{COV}_C$ is Mathlib's codiscrete category on the covering families, whose hom-sets are a one-point type. *Step 1.* Every hom-set has exactly one element, so the category is a connected simple system. *Step 2.* The unique morphism $\Psi' \to \Psi$ is inverse to the unique morphism $\Psi \to \Psi'$, both composites being the unique endomorphisms. *Step 3.* The unique endomorphism is the identity.

**Proof of Definition 17.1.21.** *Step 1, the functor* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.coveringEmbedding⟧. On objects $\Psi \mapsto X_\Psi$, on the morphism $\Psi \to \Psi'$ the class $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$. The identity law is $f_{\Psi,\Psi} = 1$ and the composition law is the cocycle identity, both from Theorem 17.1.18. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_21⟧. The images are generalized isomorphisms by Theorem 17.1.18 (1).

**Proof of the composition law of pp. 761–765.** The text forms the fibred product of two comparison diagrams, simplifies it along (17.24)–(17.30), and observes that any path of simplifications leads to the diagram of the pair $(\Psi, \Psi'')$. *Step 1.* By the characterization of the construction on pp. 755–756 ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.compare_comp_incl⟧, each $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ is determined in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ by $i(\Psi' \to \Phi) \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = i(\Psi \to \Phi)$ for any family $\Phi$ containing both. *Step 2.* Taking $\Phi = \Psi \sqcup \Psi' \sqcup \Psi''$ and cancelling the invertible inclusion of $\Psi''$, as in Step 3 of the proof of Theorem 17.1.18, gives $f_{\Psi',\Psi''} \circ f_{\Psi,\Psi'} = f_{\Psi,\Psi''}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_comp⟧. *Step 3.* With it, $\mathrm{COV}_C \to \mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ is a functor ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.coveringEmbedding⟧. This is the composition law the text's simplifications serve: the embedding of $\mathrm{COV}_C$ sends the composite of $\Psi \to \Psi' \to \Psi''$ to the class of $d_{\Psi,\Psi''}$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text's (17.20)–(17.30) describe the geometric content of composing correspondences, the fibred product of three diagrams and the associativity of its simplifications. These are not written out on the level of diagrams; only the equality of classes in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ that they establish is proved, by comparison with an inclusion into one larger family (point G.203, settled in this form). Definition 17.1.20 defines a category; that it is a connected simple system with only isomorphisms is the text's description, proved here.

### XVII.13 Definition 17.1.22 and Propositions 17.1.23, 17.1.24 (new in the 2021 edition), p. 758: (LR), (SLR), (LUC), (SLUC) and (ZHOU) for $C$

**Statement** (same). *Definition 17.1.22* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_22⟧: $C$ (with its polyfold construction) has the local regularity property (LR) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsLR⟧, (SLR) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsSLR⟧, (LUC) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsLUC⟧, (SLUC) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsSLUC⟧ or (ZHOU) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsZhou⟧ if the model $X_\Psi$ of some covering family has it ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasModelProperty⟧; for any given covering family $\Psi$, each holds for $C$ iff it holds for $X_\Psi$. *Proposition 17.1.23* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_23⟧: if $C$ satisfies (ZHOU), it has (LUC), (SLUC), (LR) and (SLR). *Proposition 17.1.24* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_24⟧: if $C$ has one of the five properties, the model of every covering family has it. The paragraph before Definition 17.1.22, that the properties pass between the models along the $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ "in the obvious way", and the citations of Propositions 17.1.23, 17.1.24 are point G.201, settled by the proofs below.

**Proof.** *Step 1, invariance.* Call a property $P$ of ep-groupoids invariant if $P(X) \Leftrightarrow P(Y)$ whenever there is a generalized isomorphism $X \to Y$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsGenIsoInvariant⟧. Theorem 10.3.21 (Part X) ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationLR · EPLocalization.EPLoc.theorem_10_3_21_all⟧ gives the invariance of (LR) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGenIsoInvariant_isLR⟧, (LUC) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGenIsoInvariant_isLUC⟧, (SLR) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGenIsoInvariant_isSLR⟧, (SLUC) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGenIsoInvariant_isSLUC⟧ and (ZHOU) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGenIsoInvariant_isZhou⟧. Effectiveness, used in the next section, is invariant by Chapter 16 ⟦Chapter16/PolyfoldMaps · EPLoc.isEffective_iff_of_isGeneralizedIso⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGenIsoInvariant_isEffective⟧.

*Step 2, one model decides* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasModelProperty.forall⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasModelProperty_iff⟧. If $P(X_{\Psi_0})$ holds for some covering family $\Psi_0$ and $P$ is invariant, then for every covering family $\Psi$, $f_{\Psi_0,\Psi}$ is a generalized isomorphism (Theorem 17.1.18), so $P(X_\Psi)$. Conversely $P(X_\Psi)$ for the given $\Psi$ is an instance of "some covering family". With Step 1 this is Definition 17.1.22 in the form stated, and Proposition 17.1.24 ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_24⟧. The forms used most often are named ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsZhou.model⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsLR.model⟧.

*Step 3, Proposition 17.1.23* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_23⟧. Let $X_\Psi$ satisfy (ZHOU). Theorem 7.4.13 (Part VII) gives (SLUC) for $X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter8/SmoothRegularAssembly · EP.theorem_7_4_13_all_of_isZhou⟧ and Theorem 7.4.24 gives (SLR) ⟦Chapter8/SmoothRegularAssembly · EP.theorem_7_4_24_all_of_isZhou⟧. (SLUC) is (LUC) for every index raise $X^i$, and (SLR) is (LR) for every $X^i$, so their cases $i = 0$ are (LUC) and (LR). All four hold on the same model, which witnesses the four properties of $C$.

**Translation GCTs.** For a translation GCT $K \ltimes U$ with its tautological structure $F(x) = \{\Psi_0\}$, $\Psi_0 = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tautConstruction⟧, the five properties are those of the ep-groupoid $K \ltimes U$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tautConstruction_hasModelProperty_iff⟧. *Step 1, the piece functor.* For a uniformizer $\Psi_\lambda$ of a family with $\hat\Psi_\lambda$ continuous, the functor $G_\lambda \ltimes O_\lambda \to X_\Psi$, $q \mapsto q_\lambda$, $(g, q) \mapsto \hat\Psi_\lambda(g, q) = (q, \Psi_\lambda(g, q), g * q)$, is an sc-smooth functor ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.pieceFunctor⟧; sc-smoothness on morphisms is that of $\hat\Psi_\lambda$ when it is continuous ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_hat_of_continuous⟧. *Step 2, an equivalence when the footprint of $\Psi_\lambda$ is $|C|$* ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_pieceFunctor⟧. Faithful because $\hat\Psi_\lambda$ is injective (Lemma 17.1.9, its bijectivity part, which holds without continuity) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_9_bijective⟧; full because every morphism of $C$ between $\Psi_\lambda(q)$ and $\Psi_\lambda(q')$ is $\Psi_\lambda(g, q)$ with $g * q = q'$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.exists_smul_eq_mor⟧; essentially surjective because every $\Psi_\mu(q)$ has its class in the footprint of $\Psi_\lambda$; and a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects, being the inclusion of a piece. Lemma 10.1.2 concludes. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasModelProperty_iff_piece⟧. The piece functor is inverted by the localization, so an invariant property holds for $C$ iff it holds for $G_\lambda \ltimes O_\lambda$. *Step 4.* The tautological uniformizer has footprint $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.tautological_footprint⟧ and continuous $\hat\Psi_0$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_continuous_hat⟧, and its covering family is $\{\Psi_0\}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tautSingleFamily⟧, so Step 3 applies with the equivalence $K \ltimes U \to X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_tautModelFunctor⟧.

**Witnesses.** $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action and its tautological structure satisfies (ZHOU), hence all five properties ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_properties⟧, from (ZHOU) for $\mathbb Z/3 \times \mathbb R \rightrightarrows \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter7/Zhou · EpGroupoid.Examples.isZhou_trivialZ3⟧; the isotropy of every object is $\mathbb Z/3$. The reflection GCT $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ has (LUC) ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isLUC⟧, from Chapter 7 ⟦Chapter7/ReducedCriterion · EpGroupoid.Examples.isLUC_reflection⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Definition 17.1.22 asks "some covering set"; the formalization adds the equivalence with any given covering set, which is Proposition 17.1.24 together with the trivial converse. The text cites Theorems 7.4.13, 7.4.24 and 10.3.21 without proof; the citations are carried out on one model.

### XVII.14 Proposition 17.1.25 (new in the 2021 edition), p. 758: uniqueness of $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$

**Statement** (does not hold as we read it; holds for effective $C$ or an effective target model). *As printed* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.Proposition_17_1_25⟧: if $C$ has (LUC), then for all covering families $\Psi, \Psi'$, every generalized isomorphism $h : X_\Psi \to X_{\Psi'}$ with $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}| \circ |h| = |\Gamma_\Psi|$ equals $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$. *With (LR)* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.Proposition_17_1_25_LR⟧: the same with (LR) in place of (LUC). The statement names "the local regularity property (LUC)"; (LUC) is the local unique continuation property of Definition 7.2.19, and the local regularity property is (LR) of Definition 7.4.19, which the proof uses through Theorem 10.3.19. Both readings are formalized. Neither holds as we read it ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_proposition_17_1_25⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_proposition_17_1_25_LR⟧. *What is proved.* The step $|h| = |f_{\Psi,\Psi'}|$ holds with no hypothesis on $C$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.orbitMap_eq_comparison⟧. The conclusion holds under (LR) when $X_{\Psi'}$ is effective ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_25_of_isEffective⟧, under (LR) when $C$ is effective, that is when some (hence every) model is effective in the sense of Definition 7.2.1 ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsEffective⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_25_of_isEffective'⟧, and under (ZHOU) when $C$ is effective ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_25_of_isZhou⟧. The added hypothesis is effectiveness (of $C$ or of the target model).

The text's proof has two steps: every $X_\Psi$ has the local regularity property, so by Theorem 10.3.19 a generalized isomorphism is determined by its orbit map; and $|f_{\Psi,\Psi'}| = |\Gamma_{\Psi'}|^{-1} \circ |\Gamma_\Psi| = |h|$. The second step holds as printed; the first is Theorem 10.3.19, which does not hold as we read it for non-effective targets (Part X; Findings, Chapter 10, item 58). Proposition 17.1.25 inherits exactly this (Findings, Chapter 17, item 2).

**Proof of the step $|h| = |f_{\Psi,\Psi'}|$** ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.orbitMap_eq_comparison⟧. For $q \in |X_\Psi|$, $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}|(|h|(q)) = |\Gamma_\Psi|(q) = |\Gamma_{\Psi'}|(|f_{\Psi,\Psi'}|(q))$, the first by hypothesis, the second by Theorem 17.1.18. $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}|$ is injective (Lemma 17.1.15), so $|h|(q) = |f_{\Psi,\Psi'}|(q)$. Neither (LUC) nor (LR) nor $h$ being a generalized isomorphism is used.

**Proof that Proposition 17.1.25 does not hold as we read it.** The witness is the GCT $C = \mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$, the action of $\mathbb Z/3$ on $\mathbb R$ trivial, with its tautological polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3TautConstruction⟧ and the covering family $\Psi = \{\Psi_0\}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Family⟧. Every hypothesis of the printed statement is a hypothesis on this $C$, and every one holds.

*Step 1, the properties.* The model $X_\Psi$ is equivalent to the ep-groupoid $\mathbb Z/3 \times \mathbb R \rightrightarrows \mathbb R$ of Part X (Translation GCTs, Step 4, in the previous section). That ep-groupoid satisfies (ZHOU), since every isotropy element acts as the identity ⟦Chapter7/Zhou · EpGroupoid.Examples.isZhou_trivialZ3⟧. So $C$ satisfies (ZHOU), and by Proposition 17.1.23 also (LUC), (SLUC), (LR), (SLR) ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_properties⟧. In particular both readings of the hypothesis hold.

*Step 2, the generalized isomorphism $h$.* Let $e : \mathbb Z/3 \times \mathbb R \rightrightarrows \mathbb R \to X_\Psi$ be the class of the equivalence of Step 1 in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$, an isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3ModelIso⟧. Let $[\mathrm{Inv}]$ be the class of the diagram $X \xleftarrow{1} X \xrightarrow{\mathrm{Inv}} X$, where $\mathrm{Inv}$ is the identity on objects and inverts morphisms; since $\mathbb Z/3$ is abelian and acts trivially, $\mathrm{Inv}$ is an ep-functor and an equivalence, and $[\mathrm{Inv}]$ is a generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationLRCounterexample · LocalizationLRCounterexample.genInv⟧ ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationLRCounterexample · LocalizationLRCounterexample.isGeneralizedIso_genInv⟧. Put $h = e^{-1} \circ [\mathrm{Inv}] \circ e : X_\Psi \to X_\Psi$ (composites in the order of maps) ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3TwistedIso⟧. It is a composite of isomorphisms of the localization, hence a generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationCategory · EPLocalization.EPLoc.isGeneralizedIso_iff_isIso⟧.

*Step 3, the orbit condition.* Orbit maps compose (Proposition 10.3.11) ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationCategory · EPLocalization.EPLoc.proposition_10_3_11⟧, and $|[\mathrm{Inv}]| = \mathrm{id}$, because $\mathrm{Inv}$ is the identity on objects ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationLRCounterexample · LocalizationLRCounterexample.orbitMap_genInv⟧. Hence $|h| = |e|^{-1} \circ |e| = \mathrm{id}$, and $|\Gamma_\Psi| \circ |h| = |\Gamma_\Psi|$.

*Step 4, $h \ne f_{\Psi,\Psi}$.* By Theorem 17.1.18, $f_{\Psi,\Psi} = 1$. If $h = 1$, then $[\mathrm{Inv}] = e \circ h \circ e^{-1} = e \circ e^{-1} = 1$. But $[\mathrm{Inv}] \ne 1$ ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationLRCounterexample · LocalizationLRCounterexample.genInv_ne_genId⟧: the two classes would be equal only if the functors $1$ and $\mathrm{Inv}$ were connected by a natural transformation after a refinement, that is a family $\tau_x \in \mathbb Z/3$ with $\tau_x g = g^{-1} \tau_x$ for all $g$, which does not hold for a generator. This is the argument of Part X by which Theorem 10.3.19 does not hold as we read it ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationLRCounterexample · LocalizationLRCounterexample.not_theorem_10_3_19⟧. So $h \ne f_{\Psi,\Psi}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_counterexample⟧.

*Step 5, conclusion.* Steps 1–4 contradict the statement under (LUC) ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_proposition_17_1_25⟧ and under (LR) ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_proposition_17_1_25_LR⟧. The model is not effective ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_isEffective_z3Taut⟧, from Chapter 7 ⟦Chapter7/NonVacuity · EpGroupoid.Examples.not_isEffective_trivialZ3⟧: the non-trivial elements of $\mathbb Z/3$ act as the identity near every object. Since the witness satisfies (ZHOU), (ZHOU) alone does not restore the statement either, which is why the (ZHOU) form below also asks effectiveness.

**Proof of the effective forms.** *Step 1, effective target* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_25_of_isEffective⟧. $X_\Psi$ has (LR) by Proposition 17.1.24. Theorem 10.3.19 for an effective target (Part X) ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationLRCounterexample · EPLocalization.EPLoc.theorem_10_3_19_of_isEffective⟧ says that two generalized isomorphisms $X \to Y$ with equal orbit maps are equal if one of $X$, $Y$ has (LR) and $Y$ is effective. Apply it to $h$ and $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, both generalized isomorphisms, with equal orbit maps by the step above. *Step 2, effective $C$* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_25_of_isEffective'⟧. Effectiveness is invariant (previous section, Step 1), so if one model is effective, so is $X_{\Psi'}$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsEffective.model⟧, and Step 1 applies. *Step 3, (ZHOU)* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_1_25_of_isZhou⟧. (ZHOU) gives (LR) (Proposition 17.1.23), and Step 2 applies.

**Witnesses of the added hypothesis.** (LR) and effectiveness together: the translation GCT $\{1\} \ltimes \mathbb R$ satisfies (ZHOU), hence (LR), and is effective ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unitTaut_isLR_isEffective⟧, (ZHOU) coming from trivial isotropy ⟦Chapter7/Zhou · EpGroupoid.isZhou_of_subsingleton⟧, and the effective form applies to it ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unitTaut_proposition_17_1_25⟧; this witness is weak (trivial isotropy). Effectiveness with non-trivial isotropy: the reflection GCT $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ is effective ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isEffective⟧ ⟦Chapter7/NonVacuity · EpGroupoid.Examples.isEffective_reflection⟧ and has (LUC) ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isLUC⟧; (LR) for it is not proved, so the only witness of both hypotheses at once has trivial isotropy.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text's two steps are separated and each tested. The second holds without hypotheses. The first is Theorem 10.3.19; the example of Part X is carried to a GCT with a polyfold structure, where it satisfies every printed hypothesis, including (ZHOU). The proved forms add effectiveness, the condition under which Part X proves Theorem 10.3.19, and keep the text's proof otherwise. Nothing later in Chapter 17 uses the uniqueness: Theorem 17.1.26 and the class $c_C$ need only the existence of the $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$.

### XVII.15 Theorem 17.1.26 (new in the 2021 edition), p. 759: the class $c_C$ of polyfold structures on $|C|$

**Statement** (same) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_26⟧: if $C$ satisfies (ZHOU), then $|C|$ is metrizable, and for every covering family $\Psi$, $X_\Psi$ satisfies (ZHOU), $(X_\Psi, |\Gamma_\Psi|)$ is a polyfold structure on $|C|$ with model $X_\Psi$ and homeomorphism $|\Gamma_\Psi|$, and its class is $c_C$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.polyfoldClass⟧. Any two such structures are equivalent in the sense of Definition 16.1.2 ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelPolyfoldStructure_equiv⟧, and every covering family represents $c_C$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.polyfoldClass_eq⟧. The theorem summarizes the paragraph before it (point G.202, settled).

**Proof.** *Step 1, metrizability.* $|C|$ is metrizable by Definition 17.1.1; no argument is needed.

*Step 2, (ZHOU) for every model.* By Proposition 17.1.24 ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsZhou.model⟧. So Remark 17.1.16 gives the polyfold structure $(X_\Psi, |\Gamma_\Psi|)$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelPolyfoldStructure⟧.

*Step 3, any two are equivalent* ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelPolyfoldStructure_equiv⟧. Definition 16.1.2 asks for a generalized isomorphism $g : X_\Psi \to X_{\Psi'}$ with $|\Gamma_\Psi| = |\Gamma_{\Psi'}| \circ |g|$ ⟦Chapter16/PolyfoldStructure · PolyfoldStructure.Equiv⟧. Take $g = f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$: it is a generalized isomorphism and satisfies the orbit identity, by Theorem 17.1.18.

*Step 4, the class.* (ZHOU) for $C$ provides a covering family whose model satisfies (ZHOU); $c_C$ is the class of its structure ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.polyfoldClass⟧. By Step 3 every other covering family gives the same class ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.polyfoldClass_eq⟧, so $c_C$ does not depend on the chosen family.

**Witness.** $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ (isotropy $\mathbb Z/3$ at every object) carries the class $c_C$, represented by its model ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_polyfoldClass⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* None. The polyfold $(|C|, c_C)$ is the polyfold of Chapter 16 (Part XVI, §XVI.1); the natural assignment $(C, F, M) \mapsto (|C|, c_C)$ of the text is the definition of $c_C$ together with Step 4. As noted in the previous section, the equivalence of the structures uses only the existence of the $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, not their uniqueness.

### XVII.16 Theorem 17.1.27 (2017: Remark 17.2), pp. 759–761, (17.17)–(17.19): the canonical natural transformation

**Statement** (stronger) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_27⟧: for covering families $\Psi$, $\Psi'$ and $\Psi'' = \Psi \sqcup \Psi'$, the functors $\Gamma \circ \pi_1$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctorLeft⟧ and $\Gamma' \circ \pi_2$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctorRight⟧ from the weak fibred product $X \times_{X''} X'$ to $C$ are equivalences of categories ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_modelFunctorLeft⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_modelFunctorRight⟧, and $\tau(q_\lambda, (q_\lambda, \varphi'', q_{\mu'}), q_{\mu'}) = \varphi''$ of (17.19) is a natural transformation $\Gamma \circ \pi_1 \to \Gamma' \circ \pi_2$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.canonicalNatTrans⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.canonicalNatTrans_app⟧, moreover a natural isomorphism. *(17.18)* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fibredProductObjEquiv⟧: the objects of the weak fibred product are identified with $\bigsqcup_{\lambda \in \Lambda, \mu' \in \Lambda'} M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\mu'})$ by $(q_\lambda, (q_\lambda, \varphi'', q_{\mu'}), q_{\mu'}) \mapsto (q_\lambda, \varphi'', q_{\mu'})$, and under it $\tau$ is the middle component ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.canonicalNatTrans_app_eq⟧.

$C$ carries no M-polyfold structure, so $\tau$ is a natural transformation of functors of categories, between the functors of the underlying categories.

**Proof.** *Step 1, $\tau$ is well typed.* For an object $p = (x, \varphi'', z)$ of the weak fibred product, $\Gamma_{\Psi''}(\varphi'')$ has source $\Gamma_{\Psi''}(A(x)) = \Gamma_\Psi(x)$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.src_canonical⟧ and target $\Gamma_{\Psi''}(A'(z)) = \Gamma_{\Psi'}(z)$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tgt_canonical⟧, using $\Gamma_{\Psi''} \circ A = \Gamma_\Psi$ on objects. So $\tau(p) = \Gamma_{\Psi''}(\varphi'')$, which is the morphism $\varphi''$ of $C$, is a morphism from $(\Gamma \circ \pi_1)(p)$ to $(\Gamma' \circ \pi_2)(p)$.

*Step 2, naturality.* The weak fibred product carries the natural transformation $A \circ \pi_1 \to A' \circ \pi_2$ whose component at $p$ is $\varphi''$ ⟦Chapter10/WeakFibredProductTheorem · WeakFibredProduct.natTrans⟧. For a morphism $m : p \to p'$, its naturality square is an equation between composites in $X_{\Psi''}$. Applying the functor $\Gamma_{\Psi''}$, which preserves composition of composable morphisms, gives the naturality square of $\tau$; the composability conditions are the source and target identities of the natural transformation and of $A$, $A'$.

*Step 3, equivalences.* $\pi_1$ is an equivalence of ep-groupoids (Theorem 10.2.2, since $A'$ is one), hence of categories, and $\Gamma_\Psi$ is an equivalence of categories by Lemma 17.1.15; composites of equivalences are equivalences ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_modelFunctorLeft⟧. Likewise $\Gamma' \circ \pi_2$, from $A$ being an equivalence ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_modelFunctorRight⟧.

*Step 4, a natural isomorphism.* $C$ is a groupoid, so every component of $\tau$ is invertible, and a natural transformation with invertible components is a natural isomorphism.

*Step 5, (17.18)* ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fibredProductObjEquiv⟧. The map sends $(x, \varphi'', z)$ with $x \in O_\lambda$, $z \in O_{\mu'}$ to the transition element $(x, \Gamma_{\Psi''}(\varphi''), z) \in M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\mu'})$, using Step 1. The inverse sends $(q, \varphi, q') \in M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\mu'})$ to the object $(q_\lambda, ((\lambda, \mu'), (q, \varphi, q')), q'_{\mu'})$, the middle term a morphism of $X_{\Psi''}$ from the piece $\lambda$ to the piece $\mu'$. The two composites are identities because a morphism of $X_{\Psi''}$ is determined by its source, its target and its morphism of $C$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelMorEquiv⟧.

**Witness.** In the reflection example of Theorem 17.1.18 above, $\tau(p) = (-1, 0)$ for an object $p$ of the weak fibred product, a morphism $0 \to 0$ other than the unit ⟦Chapter17/ModelExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_comparison⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The statement is stronger: $\tau$ is a natural isomorphism, which the text does not state; it is immediate since $C$ is a groupoid. The 2017 text had the statement as Remark 17.2. The redundancy the text notes in (17.18) is made a bijection.

### XVII.17 The regularity of objects, $mI(\alpha)$ and the filtration $C_\infty \to \cdots \to C_0 = C$, pp. 765–766, and Definition 17.1.30 (2017: Definition 17.7), p. 767: the degeneracy functor and $\partial C$

**Statement.** *The regularity* (same) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasRegularity⟧: an object $\alpha$ has regularity $m$ if $\alpha \cong \Psi(q)$ for a uniformizer $\Psi$ of $F$ and a point $q \in O_m$. It does not depend on the choice: for every uniformizer $\Psi$ of $F$ and every $q$ with $\Psi(q) \cong \alpha$, $\alpha$ has regularity $m$ iff $q \in O_m$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasRegularity_iff⟧; in the text's form, for $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and $\Psi(q) = \alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regularity_eq_of_mem⟧. Isomorphic objects have the same regularity ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasRegularity.of_isomorphic⟧; regularity $k$ implies regularity $m \le k$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasRegularity.mono⟧; when the footprints cover $|C|$ every object has regularity $0$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasRegularity_zero⟧. $mI(\alpha) \in \mathbb N \cup \{\infty\}$ is the supremum of the regularities ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.maxRegularity⟧ and equals the maximal level $ml(q)$ of any $q$ with $\Psi(q) \cong \alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.maxRegularity_eq⟧. *The filtration* (same): $C_m$ has the objects of regularity $m$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regSet⟧, $C_\infty$ the smooth ones ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regSetInf⟧; the sets decrease ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regSet_antitone⟧, $C_0 = C$ when the footprints cover ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regSet_zero⟧, each is saturated, so the filtration descends to isomorphism classes ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSaturated_regSet⟧, and the arrows are the inclusions of full subcategories ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regSub⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regIncl⟧. In a model, $\Gamma_\Psi(x)$ has regularity $m$ iff $x$ lies on level $m$ of $X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasRegularity_modelFunctor_obj⟧. *Definition 17.1.30* (restated) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_30⟧: $d(\alpha) = d_O(q)$ for $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and $\Psi(q) = \alpha$, equally for any uniformizer of $F$ and any $q$ with $\Psi(q) \cong \alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_eq⟧; $d$ is constant on isomorphism classes ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_eq_of_iso⟧; the boundary $\partial C$ is the full subcategory on the objects with $d(\alpha) \ge 1$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.boundarySet⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.boundary⟧, saturated ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSaturated_boundarySet⟧; under (ZHOU), $d$ is the degeneracy index of the polyfold $(|C|, c_C)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_polyfoldClass⟧.

*Readings.* "Pick $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$" presupposes $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, the standing reading of Definition 17.1.6 (point G.196). The formalization defines the regularity and $d$ through any uniformizer of $F$ whose footprint contains the class of $\alpha$; this needs no choice in $F(\alpha)$, and the theorems above show that it is the text's definition for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$. An object in no footprint has no regularity, and $d = 0$ there. "Functor $d : C \to \mathbb N$" is read as a function on objects constant on isomorphism classes, a functor to the discrete category $\mathbb N$. That the regularity is well defined, "since $s$ and $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms", is point G.204, settled by Steps 1–2.

**Proof of the regularity statements.** *Step 1, an isomorphism is a transition element* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_tr_of_isomorphic⟧. An isomorphism $\varphi : \Psi(q) \to \Psi'(q')$ of $C$ is the point $(q, \varphi, q')$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$.

*Step 2, levels pass along it* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.mem_levelSet_iff_of_isomorphic⟧. By Definition 17.1.8 (a), $s : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to O$ and $t : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to O'$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_trSrc⟧, and a local sc-diffeomorphism preserves and reflects the levels ⟦Chapter7/LocalDiffeo · MPolyfold.IsLocalScDiffeo.mem_levelSet_iff⟧. So $q \in O_m$ iff $(q, \varphi, q')$ lies on level $m$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ iff $q' \in O'_m$. Hence the maximal levels agree ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.maxLevel_eq_of_isomorphic⟧, and the degeneracy indices agree, local sc-diffeomorphisms preserving $d$ ⟦Chapter7/LocalDiffeo · MPolyfold.IsLocalScDiffeo.degIndex_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_eq_of_isomorphic⟧.

*Step 3, independence* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasRegularity_iff⟧. If $\alpha \cong \Psi'(q')$ with $q' \in O'_m$ and $\alpha \cong \Psi(q)$, then $\Psi'(q') \cong \Psi(q)$, and Step 2 gives $q \in O_m$; the converse is the definition. The text's form takes $\Psi(q) = \alpha$ and the identity isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regularity_eq_of_mem⟧.

*Step 4, the rest.* Invariance under isomorphism composes the isomorphisms; monotonicity is that of the levels of $O$; regularity $0$ is membership in a footprint ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasRegularity_zero_iff⟧, and a covering family exists when the footprints cover ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.exists_coveringFamily_iff⟧, which gives $C_0 = C$. $mI(\alpha) = ml(q)$ by Step 3 applied to every $m$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.maxRegularity_eq⟧; for $\alpha$ in some footprint, $m \le mI(\alpha)$ iff $\alpha$ has regularity $m$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.le_maxRegularity_iff⟧, and $mI(\alpha) = \infty$ iff $\alpha$ is smooth ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.maxRegularity_eq_top_iff⟧. Saturation is invariance under isomorphism. In a model, Step 3 with the identity isomorphism and the levels of a disjoint union give the statement on $\Gamma_\Psi(x)$.

**Proof of Definition 17.1.30.** *Step 1, the definition.* $d(\alpha)$ is $d_O(q)$ for a choice of $\Psi$ and $q$ with $\Psi(q) \cong \alpha$, if there is one, and $0$ otherwise ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex⟧. *Step 2, independence* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_eq⟧. For any other $\Phi$, $q'$ with $\Phi(q') \cong \alpha$, the chosen $\Psi(q)$ and $\Phi(q')$ are isomorphic, and Step 2 of the previous proof gives $d_O(q) = d_{O'}(q')$. The text's form is the case $\Phi \in F(\alpha)$, $\Phi(q') = \alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_30⟧. *Step 3, a functor to $\mathbb N$* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_eq_of_iso⟧. If $\alpha \cong \beta$, either some $\Psi(q) \cong \alpha$, and then also $\cong \beta$, so both values are $d_O(q)$ by Step 2; or no footprint contains the common class, and both values are $0$. *Step 4, the boundary.* $\partial C$ is the full subcategory on $\{d \ge 1\}$, saturated by Step 3. *Step 5, under (ZHOU)* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_polyfoldClass⟧. Pick a covering family and $q \in O_\lambda$ with $|\Psi_\lambda(q)| = |\alpha|$. The degeneracy index of the polyfold $(|C|, c_C)$ at $|\alpha|$ is computed in any structure of the class (Definition 16.1.14, Part XVI) ⟦Chapter16/PolyfoldStructure · PolyfoldClass.definition_16_1_14⟧, here $(X_\Psi, |\Gamma_\Psi|)$, where it is $d_{X_\Psi}(q_\lambda) = d_{O_\lambda}(q)$, and by Step 2 this is $d(\alpha)$.

**Witness.** The translation GCT $\{1\} \ltimes [0, \infty)$ with its tautological structure ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.cornerTautConstruction⟧ has $d(0) = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.cornerTaut_degIndex_zero⟧, from the degeneracy index of the half-line at $0$ ⟦Chapter2/TameBoundary · MPolyfold.quadrantPolyfold_degIndex_zero⟧, so $\partial C \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.cornerTaut_boundarySet_nonempty⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text picks $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$; the formalization allows any uniformizer of $F$ through any isomorphic object, which is equivalent by the independence statements and avoids a choice in $F(\alpha)$. The text's reason, that $s$ and $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms, is exactly Step 2. The filtration's arrows are inclusions of full subcategories, defined for any inclusion of sets of objects ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fullSubIncl⟧.

### XVII.18 Theorem 17.1.28 (2017: Theorem 17.3), p. 766: the GCT $C^1$ and the polyfold structure $(F^1, M_{F^1})$

**Statement** (restated). *The GCT* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_28_gct⟧: when the footprints of $F$ cover $|C|$, the full subcategory $C_1$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regSub⟧ with the topology on $|C_1|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneTopology⟧ is a GCT $C^1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneGCT⟧; for every covering family, $|\Gamma^1_\Psi| : |X^1_\Psi| \to |C^1|$ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_modelOrbitEquivOne⟧, $|C^1|$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_levelOne⟧, and $|C^1| \to |C|$ is a continuous injection onto the classes of regularity $1$. *The polyfold structure* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_28⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneConstruction⟧: $F^1(\alpha)$ consists of the lifts $\Psi^1 : G \ltimes O^1 \to C^1$ of the $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneF⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneUnif⟧, and for $L = \Psi^1$, $L' = \Psi'^1$ the M-polyfold $M_{F^1}(L, L')$ is $M(\Psi, \Psi')^1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltrPolyfold⟧ through the natural identification $(q, \varphi, q') \mapsto (q, \varphi, q')$, a bijective local sc-diffeomorphism keeping the points and the morphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneTransEquiv⟧. Also: the regularity of $C^1$ is that of $C$ shifted by one ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOne_hasRegularity_iff⟧, and $F^1(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for all $\alpha$ when $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for all $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOne_hasUniformizers⟧. Under (ZHOU), $(X^1_\Psi, |\Gamma^1_\Psi|)$ is a polyfold structure on $|C^1|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOnePolyfoldStructure⟧, its class does not depend on $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOnePolyfoldClass⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOnePolyfoldClass_eq⟧, and $|C^1|$ is homeomorphic to the raised polyfold $(|C|, c_C)^1$ of §16.2 over the inclusions into $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneHomeomorphRaised⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.raiseIncl_levelOneHomeomorphRaised⟧.

*The added hypothesis.* The topology needs a covering family, that is footprints covering $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.HasCoveringUniformizers⟧, the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ in its weaker form (point G.196). Witness: $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_hasCoveringUniformizers⟧, not weak in the isotropy (every object has isotropy $\mathbb Z/3$), weak in that the action is trivial. *Readings.* The map $\Psi \mapsto \Psi^1$ need not be injective, so a lift $L$ need not determine $\Psi$; "$M_{F^1}(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1) := (M(\Psi, \Psi'))^1$" is read for one chosen $\Psi$ with $L = \Psi^1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneData⟧. The topology of $|C_1|$ is the one of p. 766, transported from $|X^1|$; it is finer in general than the subspace topology of $|C_1| \subseteq |C|$. The text calls the theorem obvious (point G.205, settled).

**Proof of the GCT part.** *Step 1, the functor $\Gamma^1_\Psi$* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctorOne⟧. The index raise $X^1_\Psi$ of Chapter 7 has as objects the points of level $1$ of $X_\Psi$. $\Gamma_\Psi$ maps them to objects of regularity $1$ (previous section), so $\Gamma_\Psi$ restricted to $X^1_\Psi$ corestricts to $C_1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GroupoidFunctor.corestrict⟧. On orbit spaces, $|C_1 \to C| \circ |\Gamma^1_\Psi| = |\Gamma_\Psi| \circ |X^1_\Psi \to X_\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.orbitIncl_modelFunctorOne⟧.

*Step 2, $|\Gamma^1_\Psi|$ is bijective for a covering family* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bijective_orbitMap_modelFunctorOne⟧. Injective: $|C_1| \to |C|$ is injective, objects of a full subcategory being isomorphic in it iff in $C$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GroupoidStr.injective_orbitMap_fullSubInclusion⟧; $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is injective (Lemma 17.1.15); and $|X^1| \to |X|$ is injective (Section 7.3). Surjective: for $\alpha \in C_1$, $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is onto, so $\alpha \cong \Gamma_\Psi(x)$ for some $x$, and $x$ has level $1$ because $\alpha$ has regularity $1$; then $x$ is an object of $X^1_\Psi$ whose image is the class of $\alpha$.

*Step 3, independence of the family* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelOrbitEquivOne_eq_comp⟧. For two covering families, $|\Gamma^1_\Psi| = |\Gamma^1_{\Psi'}| \circ |f^1_{\Psi,\Psi'}|$, with $f^1_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ the index raise of the generalized isomorphism $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ (Chapter 16) ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · EPLocalization.EPLoc.liftOneHom⟧. After the injective map $|C_1| \to |C|$, both sides become $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ on $|X^1_\Psi| \subseteq |X_\Psi|$: the left by Step 1, the right by Step 1 for $\Psi'$, the compatibility of the raise with the inclusions ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · EPLocalization.EPLoc.orbitIncl_orbitMap_liftOneHom⟧ and Theorem 17.1.18.

*Step 4, the topology* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneTopology⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_modelOrbitEquivOne⟧. Choose a covering family $\Psi_0$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneCover⟧ and give $|C_1|$ the topology transported along the bijection $|\Gamma^1_{\Psi_0}|$. For any other covering family, $|\Gamma^1_\Psi| = |\Gamma^1_{\Psi_0}| \circ |f^1_{\Psi,\Psi_0}|$ by Step 3; the raise of a generalized isomorphism is a generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · EPLocalization.EPLoc.isGeneralizedIso_liftOneHom⟧, its orbit map is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter16/TangentPolyfold · EPLocalization.EPLoc.isHomeomorph_orbitMap_of_isGeneralizedIso⟧, so $|\Gamma^1_\Psi|$ is a homeomorphism. The topology does not depend on the choice.

*Step 5, metrizable* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_levelOne⟧. $|X_{\Psi_0}|$ is paracompact (Remark 17.1.16), so $|X^1_{\Psi_0}|$ is metrizable (Section 7.3, Part VII) ⟦Chapter7/Lift · EpGroupoid.metrizableSpace_liftOne_orbitSpace⟧, and $|C^1|$ is homeomorphic to it.

*Step 6, continuity of $|C^1| \to |C|$* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.continuous_orbitInclLevelOne⟧. For the transported topology it suffices that the composite with $|\Gamma^1_{\Psi_0}|$ be continuous; by Step 1 it is $|\Gamma_{\Psi_0}| \circ |X^1 \to X|$, continuous. Its image is the set of classes of regularity $1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GroupoidStr.range_orbitMap_fullSubInclusion⟧.

*Step 7, the GCT* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneGCT⟧. Objects are those of regularity $1$, morphisms those of $C$ between them; the morphism sets are finite, being subsets of those of $C$ (Definition 17.1.1); the topology is Step 4, metrizable by Step 5.

**Proof of the polyfold structure.** *Step 1, the lifted uniformizer* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneUnif⟧. For $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$, the M-polyfold is $O^1$ with the shifted structure, $G$ acts on $O^1$ because sc-smooth maps preserve the levels ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneMulAction⟧, and the functor $\Psi^1 : G \ltimes O^1 \to C_1$ is $\Psi$ on the points of level $1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneFunctor⟧. It is full ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneFunctor_full⟧ (a morphism $\Psi(q) \to \Psi(q')$ is $\Psi(g, q)$ with $g * q = q'$, Definition 17.1.3), faithful ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneFunctor_faithful⟧ and injective on objects ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_levelOneFunctor_obj⟧, since $\Psi$ is.

*Step 2, the open embedding* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isOpenEmbedding_levelOneFunctor⟧. Definition 17.1.3 (3) for $\Psi^1$, which the text leaves to the reader. Adjoin $\Psi$ to the chosen covering family ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.CoveringFamily.adjoin⟧; then $|\Psi^1|$ factors as $|\Gamma^1| \circ \pi \circ \iota$, with $\iota : O^1 \to X^1$ the inclusion of the new piece, a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_adjoinIncl⟧, $\pi$ the open quotient map of $X^1$ (Proposition 7.1.8) and $|\Gamma^1|$ a homeomorphism by the GCT part. So $|\Psi^1|$ is continuous and open. It is injective: if $\Psi(x) \cong \Psi(y)$ in $C_1$, then $y = g * x$ for some $g$ by fullness of $\Psi$, so $x$ and $y$ have the same orbit.

*Step 3, the transition sets* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneTransEquiv⟧. A point $(q, \varphi, q')$ of $M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$, with $q, q'$ of level $1$, is a point of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ on level $1$, and conversely, because $s$ and $t$ preserve and reflect the levels ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tr_mem_levelSet_iff⟧. The transition M-polyfold $M_{F^1}(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$ is the transition set with the topology and the M-polyfold structure of $M(\Psi, \Psi')^1$ transported along this bijection ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.LTr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltrHomeomorph⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltrPolyfold⟧, so that the identification is a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltr_isLocalScDiffeo_homeomorph⟧.

*Step 4, Definition 17.1.8 (a), (b)* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltr_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltr_isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltr_isScSmoothMap_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltr_isScSmoothMap_inv⟧. $s$ and $t$ on level $1$ are the index raises of the local sc-diffeomorphisms $s$, $t$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, hence local sc-diffeomorphisms. The unit map and the inversion are index raises of sc-smooth maps ⟦Chapter2/StrongBundleLevelOne · MPolyfold.IsScSmoothMap.levelOne⟧, composed with the identification of Step 3.

*Step 5, Definition 17.1.8 (c), the multiplication* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltr_isScSmoothMap_mul⟧. The multiplication is defined on the fibred product $M(\Psi'^1, \Psi''^1)\, {}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$, and the index raise of a fibred product is not identified with the fibred product of index raises. The proof is local, through a criterion: if near every point the map factors as $k \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$ with $k$ sc-smooth on an open set containing the second components, it is sc-smooth on the fibred product ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · MPolyfold.isScSmoothOnFibredProduct_of_local⟧. Near a point $(a_0, b_0)$, let $\sigma$ be a local inverse (sheet) of $s$ at $a_0$ on level $0$. For $(a, b)$ near $(a_0, b_0)$ in the fibred product, $a = \sigma(t(b))$, so $m(a, b) = m(\sigma(t(b)), b) =: k_0(b)$. $k_0$ is sc-smooth on level $0$ by Definition 17.1.8 (c) for $M$ on the sheet ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · MPolyfold.IsScSmoothOnFibredProduct.isScSmoothOn_sheet⟧; it maps level $1$ to level $1$, since the source of $k_0(b)$ is the source of $b$; so its index raise $k_1$ is sc-smooth, and the multiplication on level $1$ is $k_1 \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$ near $(a_0, b_0)$, conjugated by the identifications of Step 3. This is how the text's "obvious" verification goes through.

*Step 6, the construction* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneConstruction⟧. $F^1(\alpha)$ is the set of the $\Psi^1$ with $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$; $\Psi^1$ lies at $\alpha$ because the point $q$ with $\Psi(q) = \alpha$ has level $1$ when $\alpha$ has regularity $1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneUnif_isAt⟧. For $L, L' \in F^1$ with the chosen $\Psi, \Psi'$, $M(L, L')$ carries the structure of Step 3, and Steps 4–5 give Definition 17.1.8 (a)–(c). The identification is bijective, a local sc-diffeomorphism, and keeps the points and the morphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneInclOf_spec⟧, which is the content of the theorem ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_28⟧.

*Step 7, the shifted regularity and $F^1(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$.* For $x \in O_L = O^1$, $x$ has level $m$ in $O^1$ iff it has level $m+1$ in $O$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneObjCast_mem_levelSet_iff⟧, so the regularity of $C^1$ is that of $C$ shifted by one ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOne_hasRegularity_iff⟧. If $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$, then every $\alpha \in C_1$ has some $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and $\Psi^1 \in F^1(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOne_hasUniformizers⟧.

**Proof of the (ZHOU) form.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOnePolyfoldStructure⟧. $(X^1_\Psi, |\Gamma^1_\Psi|)$: the raise of a polyfold structure has paracompact orbit space and (ZHOU) (Part XVI) ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · PolyfoldStructure.liftOne_paracompact⟧ ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · PolyfoldStructure.liftOne_isZhou⟧, and $|\Gamma^1_\Psi|$ is a homeomorphism by the GCT part. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOnePolyfoldStructure_equiv⟧. Two such structures are equivalent through $f^1_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, by Step 3 of the GCT part. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneHomeomorphRaised⟧. The raised polyfold $(|C|, c_C)^1$ of §16.2 is $|X^1_{\Psi_0}|$ for a structure of $c_C$ (Part XVI, §XVI.6) ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · PolyfoldClass.raiseHomeomorph⟧; composing with $|\Gamma^1_{\Psi_0}|^{-1}$ gives the homeomorphism, and its compatibility with the inclusions into $|C|$ is Step 1 of the GCT part with the compatibility of the raise ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · PolyfoldClass.raiseIncl_raiseMap⟧.

**Witness.** For $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $C^1$ carries the class $[(X^1_\Psi, |\Gamma^1_\Psi|)]$, $X^1_\Psi$ satisfies (ZHOU), (SLUC), (SLR), and $|C^1|$ is the raised polyfold $(|C|, c_C)^1$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_levelOne⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text calls the theorem obvious. The formalization makes three choices visible: the topology of $|C_1|$ is transported from $|X^1|$ and shown independent of the family; $M_{F^1}(L, L')$ is built from one chosen $\Psi$ with $L = \Psi^1$, since the lift need not determine $\Psi$; and the multiplication on level $1$ is proved sc-smooth on sheets of $s$ rather than by identifying the raise of a fibred product. The open embedding of Definition 17.1.3 (3) for $\Psi^1$ is proved through a covering family containing $\Psi$.

**For every pair of uniformizers** (does not hold as we read it; Findings, Chapter 17, item 16) ⟦Examples/EveryUniformizer/Level · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_28_not_every_pair⟧. The text sets $M_{F^1}(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1) := M(\Psi, \Psi')^1$ with the identification $(q, \varphi, q') \mapsto (q, \varphi, q')$. This presupposes that $M(\Psi, \Psi')^1$ depends only on $\Psi^1$ and $\Psi'^1$. As we read Definition 17.1.8, the structures on different transition sets are tied only by (a)–(c), and two uniformizers with the same lift may carry structures that differ over their common level-$1$ set. *Statement.* There are a GCT with a polyfold structure, $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$, and uniformizers $\Psi \ne \Psi'$ in $F(\alpha)$ for an object $\alpha$ of level $1$, with $\Psi^1 = \Psi'^1$, such that for no polyfold structure $S_1$ on $C^1$ containing $\Psi^1$ are both identifications $M_{S_1}(\Psi^1, \Psi^1) \to M(\Psi, \Psi)^1$ and $M_{S_1}(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1) \to M(\Psi, \Psi')^1$ homeomorphisms. *Proof.*
1. *Two uniformizers and a gauge.* Let $\mathbb Z/3$ act trivially on an M-polyfold $U$, $C = \mathbb Z/3 \ltimes U$, and $D \subset U$. The functors $\Psi_b$, $b \in \{0, 1\}$, are the identity on objects; $\Psi_0(g, x) = (g, x)$, and $\Psi_1(g, x) = (g, x)$ for $x \in D$, $(g^{-1}, x)$ for $x \notin D$ ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.twFunctor⟧. Both are local uniformizers at every point ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.unif⟧, distinct when $D \ne U$ ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.unif_false_ne_true⟧.
2. *The polyfold structure* ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.gaugeConstruction⟧.
