For a function $c : U \to \mathbb Z/3$, not necessarily continuous, put $\gamma(\Psi_1) = c$, $\gamma(\Psi_0) = 1$, and give $M(\Psi_b, \Psi_{b'})$ the structure of $\mathbb Z/3 \times U$ transported along $\rho(q, (k, q), q) = (k\,\gamma(\Psi_{b'})(q)^{-1}\gamma(\Psi_b)(q), q)$ ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.gRho⟧. Since $\mathbb Z/3$ is commutative, $\rho$ turns $s, t, u, \iota, m$ into the structure maps of the ep-groupoid $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes U$, so (a)–(c) of Definition 17.1.8 hold, with $F(x) = \{\Psi_0, \Psi_1\}$ non-empty ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.gaugeConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧. On $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_1)$ the identities $(q, (1, q), q)$ have $\rho$-image $(c(q)^{-1}, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.gRho_false_true⟧.
3. *The same lift.* With $D = U_1$, $\Psi_0^1 = \Psi_1^1$ ⟦Examples/EveryUniformizer/Level · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.levelOneUnif_false_eq_true⟧.
4. *The key step* ⟦Examples/EveryUniformizer/Level · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.continuous_of_isHomeomorph_levelOneTransEquiv⟧. Suppose both identifications are homeomorphisms for some $S_1$. The unit map $U_1 \to M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)^1$ is continuous; through the first identification, the equality $\Psi_0^1 = \Psi_1^1$ (which moves a transition set continuously and keeps the morphisms ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerGauge · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_continuous_cast⟧), and the second identification, it gives a continuous map $U_1 \to M(\Psi_0, \Psi_1)^1$ sending $q$ to the identity at $q$. Composing with the continuous $\rho$ of step 2 shows that $q \mapsto c(q)^{-1}$ is continuous on $U_1$.
5. *A discontinuous gauge* ⟦Examples/EveryUniformizer/Level · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.not_continuous_harmGauge⟧. On the weighted $\ell^2$ space $H$, which has points of level $0$ not of level $1$, let $c = 1$ at $0$ and $c = g$ elsewhere. Along the path $t \mapsto tv$ in $H_1$ it jumps at $t = 0$, so it is not continuous on $H_1$, against step 4. $\square$

For the representative chosen by $\mathrm{levelOneData}$ the identification holds, which is the restated theorem above.

### XVII.19 The levels $C^{i+1} := (C^i)^1$, the topology of $|C_i|$, pp. 765–766, and Corollary 17.1.29 (new in the 2021 edition), p. 766

**Statement.** *Corollary 17.1.29* (same) ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.corollary_17_1_29⟧: if $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ and $C$ satisfies (ZHOU), (SLUC) or (SLR), so does $C^1$ with $(F^1, M_{F^1})$. For every covering family $L$ of $F^1$, the model $X_L$ has the property ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.corollary_17_1_29_of_coveringFamily⟧; for every covering family $\Psi$ of $F$, so does the index raise $X^1_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.corollary_17_1_29_model⟧; the comparison is the equivalence $X_L \to X^1_\Phi$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneModelFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_levelOneModelFunctor⟧. *The levels* (same) ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.level_spec⟧: for a GCT with a polyfold structure and $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold⟧, $C^0 = C$ and $C^{i+1} = (C^i)^1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.level⟧; the inclusion $C^i \to C$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelIncl⟧ is injective with image $C_i$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.range_levelIncl_obj⟧, the regularity of $C^i$ is that of $C$ shifted by $i$, $|C^i| \to |C_i|$ is a bijection, and $|C_i|$ carries the transported topology ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelTopology⟧, metrizable ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.metrizableSpace_level⟧. Corollary 17.1.29 holds for every $C^i$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.level_properties⟧. *The topology of $|C_i|$ through the models* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.theorem_17_1_28_levels⟧: for every covering family $\Psi$ of $F$ and every $i$, $|\Gamma^i_\Psi| : |X^i_\Psi| \to |C_i|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctorLevel⟧ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.isHomeomorph_modelOrbitLevel⟧, the topology of $|C_i|$ is the one transported from $|X^i_\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelTopology_eq_coinduced⟧, $|X^i_\Psi|$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · EP.metrizableSpace_lift_orbitSpace⟧, and the raised comparisons $f^i_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · EPLocalization.EPLoc.liftHomLevel⟧ are generalized isomorphisms compatible with them. Under (ZHOU), $(X^i_\Psi, |\Gamma^i_\Psi|)$ is a polyfold structure on $|C^i|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelPolyfoldStructure⟧, any two equivalent ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelPolyfoldStructure_equiv⟧.

*The added hypothesis.* $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.HasUniformizers⟧, the standing reading (point G.196), named `hu` in Corollary 17.1.29 and carried in the structure of a uniformized polyfold for the levels. It is needed because "$C^1$ satisfies (ZHOU)" asks, by Definition 17.1.22, for a covering family of $F^1$, and because iterating $C \mapsto C^1$ needs footprints covering at each level: when only the footprints of $F$ cover $|C|$, those of $F^1$ need not cover $|C^1|$, since the base points of the $\Psi \in F$ need not have regularity $1$; $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for all $\alpha$ passes to $F^1$ (previous section, Step 7). Witness: $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ as a uniformized polyfold ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructuresExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Uniformized⟧, every object of every regularity ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructuresExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_regSet⟧, with $C^i$ non-empty, uniformized, with a covering family, and (ZHOU), (SLUC), (SLR) for every $i$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructuresExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Uniformized_level⟧, and $|\Gamma^2_\Psi|$ a homeomorphism onto a non-empty $|C_2|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Uniformized_levelOrbit⟧; the witness is not effective, and the action is trivial. The text derives the corollary from the ep-groupoid case (point G.206, settled) and the metrizability of $|C_i|$ through "paracompact, Hausdorff and locally metrizable" (point G.204, settled).

**Proof of Corollary 17.1.29.** *Step 1, the model form* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.corollary_17_1_29_model⟧. By Proposition 17.1.24, $X_\Psi$ has the property. (ZHOU) passes to the raise $X^1_\Psi$ (Propositions 7.4.8, 7.4.9, through Chapter 16) ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · PolyfoldStructure.liftOne_isZhou⟧. (SLUC) is (LUC) for every $X^i$, and $(X^1)^i = X^{i+1}$ ⟦Chapter7/Zhou · EP.lift_succ'⟧, so (SLUC) for $X$ gives (SLUC) for $X^1$; likewise (SLR).

*Step 2, a model of $C^1$ is generalized isomorphic to a raise* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_levelOneModelHom⟧. Let $L = (L_\lambda)$ be a covering family of $F^1$, with $L_\lambda = \Psi_\lambda^1$ for the chosen $\Psi_\lambda$, and let $\Phi = (\Psi_\lambda)_\lambda \sqcup \Psi_0$ with $\Psi_0$ a covering family of $F$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneModelFamily⟧; the second part makes $\Phi$ a covering family, which $(\Psi_\lambda)$ alone need not be. The functor $X_L \to X^1_\Phi$ sends $(\lambda, q)$ to $(\lambda, q)$ in the piece $O_\lambda^1$ and a morphism $((\lambda, \mu), a)$ to $((\lambda, \mu), a)$, $a$ read in $M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu)^1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneModelFunctor⟧. It is faithful ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneModelFunctor_faithful⟧ and full ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneModelFunctor_full⟧, because both models are full on $C$ (Lemma 17.1.15) and a morphism of a model is determined by its source, target and morphism of $C$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelMor_ext⟧; essentially surjective ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneModelFunctor_essSurj⟧, because $L$ covers $|C^1|$: every object $w$ of $X^1_\Phi$ has $\Gamma_\Phi(w)$ of regularity $1$, isomorphic to some $L_\lambda(q)$, and the isomorphism is a morphism of $X_\Phi$ which lies on level $1$ since its source does; and a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_levelOneModelFunctor_obj⟧. Lemma 10.1.2 makes it an equivalence of ep-groupoids ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_levelOneModelFunctor⟧, and its class is a generalized isomorphism.

*Step 3, every model of $C^1$* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.corollary_17_1_29_of_coveringFamily⟧. An invariant property holds for $X_L$ iff it holds for $X^1_\Phi$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneModel_iff⟧, and Step 1 for the covering family $\Phi$ gives it.

*Step 4, Corollary 17.1.29* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.corollary_17_1_29⟧. Under $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for all $\alpha$, $F^1(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for all $\alpha$ (Theorem 17.1.28, Step 7), so the footprints of $F^1$ cover $|C^1|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.hasCoveringUniformizers_of_hasUniformizers⟧, a covering family $L$ of $F^1$ exists, and Step 3 gives the property for $X_L$, which is Definition 17.1.22 for $C^1$.

**Proof of the statements on the levels.** *Step 1, the levels* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.level⟧. A uniformized polyfold has covering footprints ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.hasCoveringUniformizers⟧, so $C^1$ is defined, with $(F^1, M_{F^1})$, and is uniformized again ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelOne⟧; the levels are defined by recursion.

*Step 2, the inclusion and the shift.* $C^{i+1} \to C$ is $C^i \to C$ after $C^{i+1} = (C^i)_1 \to C^i$; it is injective on objects ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.injective_levelIncl_obj⟧ and reflects isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.isomorphic_levelIncl_iff⟧. By induction with the shift by one of Theorem 17.1.28, an object has regularity $m$ in $C^i$ iff it has regularity $m + i$ in $C$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.hasRegularity_level_iff⟧, so the image is $C_i$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.range_levelIncl_obj⟧ and $|C^i| \to |C_i|$ is a bijection ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.bijective_orbitMap_levelEquivSub⟧.

*Step 3, the topology of $|C_i|$* ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelTopology⟧ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.metrizableSpace_level⟧. It is transported from the GCT $C^i$ along this bijection, hence metrizable; for $i = 1$ it is the topology of Theorem 17.1.28 ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelTopology_one⟧. Corollary 17.1.29 iterates along the levels by induction ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.level_properties⟧.

*Step 4, the comparison with $X^i_\Psi$* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.exists_levelModel_comparison⟧. For every covering family $\Psi$ of $F$ and every $i$ there are a covering family $L$ of $F^i$ and a generalized isomorphism $f : X^i_\Psi \to X_L$ with $|C^i \to C| \circ |\Gamma_L| \circ |f| = |\Gamma_\Psi| \circ |X^i_\Psi \to X_\Psi|$. Induction on $i$. For $i = 0$, $L = \Psi$ and $f = 1$. For $i + 1$: from the data $(d, f)$ at level $i$, choose a covering family $L$ of $F^{i+1}$; Step 2 of the proof of Corollary 17.1.29, applied to $C^i$, gives the generalized isomorphism $g : X_L \to X^1_e$, $e = L \sqcup d$, and the comparison $k = f_{d,e}$ of Theorem 17.1.18. The composite $f^1 \circ k^1 \circ g^{-1}$, with $f^1$, $k^1$ the raises, is a generalized isomorphism, and the orbit identity follows by chasing a point through the raises ⟦Chapter16/RaisedPolyfold · EPLocalization.EPLoc.orbitIncl_orbitMap_liftOneHom⟧, the identity $|\Gamma_e| \circ |k| = |\Gamma_d|$ and the inductive hypothesis.

*Step 5, the homeomorphism* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.isHomeomorph_modelOrbitLevel⟧. By Step 4, $|\Gamma^i_\Psi| = (|C^i| \cong |C_i|) \circ |\Gamma_L| \circ |f|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.modelFunctorLevel_eq_comp⟧, a composite of homeomorphisms: the transport of Step 3, Lemma 17.1.15 for $C^i$, and the orbit map of a generalized isomorphism. So the topology of $|C_i|$ is the one transported from $|X^i_\Psi|$, for every $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelTopology_eq_coinduced⟧. Independently, $|X^i|$ is metrizable when $|X|$ is paracompact, the route of the text through Section 7.3 ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · EP.metrizableSpace_lift_orbitSpace⟧; and for two covering families $|\Gamma^i_\Psi| = |\Gamma^i_{\Psi'}| \circ |f^i_{\Psi,\Psi'}|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelOrbitLevel_eq_comp⟧ with $f^i_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ a generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · EPLocalization.EPLoc.isGeneralizedIso_liftHomLevel⟧.

*Step 6, under (ZHOU)* ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelPolyfoldStructure⟧. (ZHOU) passes to every $X^i$ (Propositions 7.4.8, 7.4.9, iterated) ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · EP.isZhou_lift⟧, paracompactness of the orbit space too ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · EP.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_lift⟧, so $(X^i_\Psi, |\Gamma^i_\Psi|)$ is a polyfold structure on $|C^i|$, and two of them are equivalent through $f^i_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelOrbitSpaces · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelPolyfoldStructure_equiv⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text derives Corollary 17.1.29 from the ep-groupoid case in one line. The covering families of $F^1$ need not come from covering families of $F$, so the proof passes through the equivalence $X_L \to X^1_\Phi$ of Step 2. The text obtains the metrizability of $|C_i|$ from "paracompact, Hausdorff and locally metrizable"; the formalization proves that route for $|X^i|$ and then transports along the homeomorphism $|\Gamma^i_\Psi|$, which also shows that the topology of the GCT $C^i$ and the topology transported from $|X^i_\Psi|$ agree for every $i$. Iterating needs $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ at each level, which the shift of Theorem 17.1.28 supplies.

### XVII.20 The maps $\sigma$ and $T\sigma$ of a transition element (2017: §17.2), pp. 767–768

Throughout §17.2, $C = (C, F, M)$ is a GCT with a polyfold structure (Definition 17.1.8) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction⟧, $\mathcal U$ is the set of uniformizers that occur in some $F(\alpha)$, and $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is the transition M-polyfold of $\Psi, \Psi' \in \mathcal U$. An element $a = (o, \varphi, o')$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ has an *admissible triple* $(U(o), U(o, \varphi, o'), U(o'))$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple⟧: an open neighbourhood $U(o, \varphi, o')$ of $a$ on which $s$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto the open set $U(o)$ and $t$ an sc-diffeomorphism onto $U(o')$; one exists for every $a$ by Definition 17.1.8 (a) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_admissibleTriple⟧. The elementary facts about the local inverse $(s|U(o,\varphi,o'))^{-1}$ — its domain and the sheet are open, it inverts $s$ on both sides, it sends $s(a)$ back to $a$ — are recorded once ⟦Chapter17/AdmissibleTripleBasic · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.isOpen_srcSet⟧ ⟦Chapter17/AdmissibleTripleBasic · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.srcInv_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter17/AdmissibleTripleBasic · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.src_srcInv⟧. A tangent vector $h$ of an M-polyfold $O$ has a base point $\tau(h) \in O_1$, written $q = \tau(h)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.basePt⟧.

**Statement** (same). For an admissible triple of $a = (o, \varphi, o')$ the map $\sigma = t \circ (s|U(o, \varphi, o'))^{-1} : U(o) \to U(o')$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.sigma⟧ is an sc-diffeomorphism with inverse $s \circ (t|U(o,\varphi,o'))^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.sigmaInv⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.isScDiffeoOn_sigma⟧, and $\sigma(o) = o'$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.sigma_src⟧. The tangent $T\sigma : TO \to TO'$ of $a$ is defined through a chosen admissible triple ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.triple⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap⟧, and on the vectors over $s(a)$ it does not depend on the triple ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_eq⟧. It satisfies three laws: $T\sigma = \mathrm{id}$ over $q$ for the unit $(q, 1, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_unit⟧; $T\sigma_{a \circ b} = T\sigma_a \circ T\sigma_b$ over $s(b)$ for composable $a, b$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_comp⟧; and $T\sigma_{a^{-1}} \circ T\sigma_a = \mathrm{id}$ over $s(a)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_inv_transMap⟧. On the fibres, $T\sigma(q) : T_qO \to T_{q'}O'$ is a topological linear isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transTangent⟧.

**Proof.** *Step 1, $\sigma$ is an sc-diffeomorphism.* $s|U(o,\varphi,o')$ and $t|U(o,\varphi,o')$ are sc-diffeomorphisms onto open sets, so $\sigma$ is the composite of the inverse of the first with the second, an sc-diffeomorphism by the composition of sc-diffeomorphisms (Part I). On the sheet, $\sigma(s(b)) = t(b)$ for every $b$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.sigma_eq⟧.

*Step 2, the germ of $\sigma$.* Two admissible triples, of possibly different elements, give the same $\sigma$ on $s(U \cap U')$, the image of the intersection of their sheets ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.sigma_eqOn⟧: for $b$ in both sheets both $\sigma$'s send $s(b)$ to $t(b)$. Since $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, this image is open ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.isOpen_src_image⟧. This is the content of Lemma 17.1.13 at the level of $\sigma$.

*Step 3, independence of the triple.* For two triples of $a$, the two $\sigma$'s agree on the open set $s(U \cap U')$, which contains $s(a)$. The tangent of an sc-smooth map at a point depends only on its germ (Part I) ⟦Chapter2/TangentMapLocal · MPolyfold.tangentMapOn_congr⟧, so the two $T\sigma$'s agree on every vector over $s(a)$ (`transMap_eq`). The same argument gives $T\sigma_b = T\sigma_a$ at the vectors over $s(b)$ for $b$ on the sheet of a triple of $a$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_eq_of_mem⟧. Away from the vectors over $s(a)$ the map `transMap` has values without meaning; every statement below restricts to vectors over $s(a)$, and $T\sigma$ sends these to vectors over $t(a)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.base_transMap⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_mem_fiber⟧.

*Step 4, a continuous local section of $s$ is $(s|U)^{-1}$.* If $f$ is a continuous map from an open $W \ni s(a)$ into $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $s \circ f = \mathrm{id}$ and $f(s(a)) = a$, then $f = (s|U)^{-1}$ on an open neighbourhood of $s(a)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.exists_srcInv_eq⟧: on $W \cap f^{-1}(U(o,\varphi,o'))$, which is open by continuity and contains $s(a)$, $f(p)$ lies on the sheet and has source $p$, so it is $(s|U)^{-1}(p)$. This is the device by which every "for $p$ near $q$" of the text is proved.

*Step 5, the unit law.* For $a = (q, 1, q) \in M(\Psi, \Psi)$, the unit map $p \mapsto (p, 1, p)$ is sc-smooth, hence continuous (Definition 17.1.8 (b)) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trUnit⟧, and it is a section of $s$ through $a$. By Step 4 it equals $(s|U)^{-1}$ near $q$, so $\sigma = t \circ (s|U)^{-1}$ is the identity near $q$, and $T\sigma(h) = h$ for $h$ over $q$.

*Step 6, the composition law.* Let $a \in M(\Psi', \Psi'')$, $b \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $s(a) = t(b)$. The text says $\sigma_{a \circ b} = \sigma_a \circ \sigma_b$ "for $p$ near $q$"; the formal proof supplies the neighbourhood from the continuity of the multiplication (Definition 17.1.8 (c)) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trMul⟧. Put $W = \{p \in U(s(b)) : t((s|U_b)^{-1}(p)) \in U(s(a))\}$, open and containing $s(b)$. On $W$ the map $p \mapsto m\big((s|U_a)^{-1}(\sigma_b(p)),\ (s|U_b)^{-1}(p)\big)$ is continuous (the pair lies in the fibred product, and $m$ restricted to the sheet over $U_a$ is sc-smooth), it is a section of $s$, and at $p = s(b)$ it is $a \circ b$. By Step 4 it equals $(s|U_{ab})^{-1}$ near $s(b)$, and applying $t$ gives $\sigma_{a\circ b} = \sigma_a \circ \sigma_b$ there. The chain rule for tangents of sc-smooth maps on open sets ⟦Chapter2/TangentMapLocal · MPolyfold.tangentMapOn_comp⟧ and Step 3 give the law on vectors over $s(b)$. A variant for any element with the entries of $a \circ b$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_comp'⟧ and a three-fold version ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_three_eq⟧ follow.

*Step 7, inverses and the fibre map.* $a^{-1} \circ a$ is the unit at $s(a)$, so Steps 5 and 6 give $T\sigma_{a^{-1}} \circ T\sigma_a = \mathrm{id}$ over $s(a)$, and symmetrically ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_transMap_inv⟧. On the fibre over a level-1 point, $T\sigma$ is the continuous linear map of Theorem 2.3.16 ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transTangentCLM⟧ ⟦Chapter2/TangentMapLinear · MPolyfold.tangentMapOnCLM⟧, and the inverse law makes it a topological linear isomorphism with inverse the tangent of $a^{-1}$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text attaches $\sigma$ to $a$ through "an" admissible triple and uses implicitly that nothing depends on the choice; the formalization proves it (Steps 2–3). The neighbourhoods "near $q$" of the text are obtained from Step 4, the only new tool, which needs the continuity of the section; for the unit and the multiplication this continuity is Definition 17.1.8 (b), (c). The same tool, applied to $p \mapsto \hat\Psi(g, p)$, is where the continuity of $\hat\Psi$ enters Lemma 17.2.10 below.

### XVII.21 Lemma 17.2.1 (2017: Lemma 17.3), Definitions 17.2.2 and 17.2.3 (2017: Definitions 17.8, 17.9): tangent vectors and $T_\alpha C$, pp. 767–769

**Statement** (same). For an object $\alpha$, a *tuple* $(\alpha, \Psi, h)$ consists of $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and $h \in TO$ whose base point $q = \tau(h)$ has $\Psi(q) = \alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentTuple⟧. For two tuples, $(q, 1_\alpha, q') \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is a transition element ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unitTr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentTuple.tr⟧, and $(\alpha, \Psi, h) \sim (\alpha, \Psi', h')$ iff $T\sigma(h) = h'$ for its $\sigma$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentRel⟧. *Lemma 17.2.1* (p. 768): $\sim$ is an equivalence relation ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_1⟧. *Definition 17.2.2* (p. 768): a tangent vector at $\alpha$ is a class $[\alpha, \Psi, h]$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentVector⟧, and $[\alpha,\Psi,h] = [\alpha,\Psi',h']$ iff the tuples are related ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentVector_eq_iff⟧. *Definition 17.2.3* (p. 769): $T_\alpha C$ is the set of classes ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentSpaceAt⟧, a vector space with $[\alpha, \Psi, h] + \lambda[\alpha, \Psi, k] = [\alpha, \Psi, h + \lambda k]$ for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_2_3⟧.

*Readings.* $T_\alpha C$ is non-empty exactly when $\alpha$ has regularity at least $1$ and $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$; this pair of conditions is named ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentObj⟧, and a tangent vector forces it ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentObj_of_tangentSpaceAt⟧: the base point of $h$ is on level $1$, so $\alpha = \Psi(q)$ is on level $1$ by the independence of the regularity of Theorem 17.1.28 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regularity_eq_of_mem⟧. Under the standing reading $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ (`HasUniformizers`, the reading of Definition 17.1.6 stated in §17.1) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.HasUniformizers⟧ the condition is $\alpha \in C_1$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentObj_iff⟧. Because the tuples contain uniformizers, $T_\alpha C$ lives one universe above $C$; this is why the tangent category below is represented in the fibres of reference uniformizers.

**Proof of Lemma 17.2.1.** *Reflexivity.* For a tuple with $\Psi' = \Psi$ and $q' = q$, the element is the unit $(q, 1_\alpha, q)$, and $T\sigma(h) = h$ by the unit law ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_unitTr_self⟧.

*Symmetry.* If $T\sigma(h) = h'$ for $\sigma$ of $(q, 1_\alpha, q')$, then $(q', 1_\alpha, q)$ is the inverse of $(q, 1_\alpha, q')$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unitTr_inv⟧, and the inverse law gives $T\sigma'(h') = T\sigma'(T\sigma(h)) = h$.

*Transitivity.* $(q', 1_\alpha, q'') \circ (q, 1_\alpha, q') = (q, 1_\alpha, q'')$ in $M(\Psi, \Psi'')$, since $1_\alpha \circ 1_\alpha = 1_\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unitTr_comp⟧. The composition law then gives $T\sigma''(h) = T\sigma'(T\sigma(h))$ over $q$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_unitTr_comp⟧, so $h \mapsto h' \mapsto h''$ composes. The text's "$\sigma' \circ \sigma = \sigma''$ near $q$" is exactly Step 6 of the previous section.

The relation is packaged as a setoid ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentSetoid⟧ and $T_\alpha C$ is its quotient; every element is a class ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentVector_surjective⟧.

**Proof of Definition 17.2.3.** The vector space structure is transported along one chart (next section): a reference uniformizer $\Psi_\alpha \in F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.refUnif⟧ with its level-1 point $q_\alpha$ over $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.refPoint⟧ gives the bijection $S_{\Psi_\alpha} : T_\alpha C \to T_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.refChart⟧, and $T_\alpha C$ receives the addition, scalar multiplication and topology of $T_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$. The printed formula holds for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, not only for $\Psi_\alpha$: $S_\Psi^{-1}$ is the inverse of the continuous linear equivalence of Proposition 17.2.4 below, so it is additive and homogeneous, and $S_\Psi^{-1}(h) + \lambda S_\Psi^{-1}(k) = S_\Psi^{-1}(h + \lambda k)$ with $S_\Psi^{-1}(h) = [\alpha, \Psi, h]$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text defines the operations by the printed formula and leaves the well-definedness implicit (it is what Proposition 17.2.4 shows); the formalization transports the structure along the reference chart and proves the formula for every $\Psi$. The two give the same structure.

### XVII.22 Propositions 17.2.4 (2017: Proposition 17.1) and 17.2.5 (2017: Proposition 17.2): $T_\alpha C$ is Banachable, and the functor $\mu_C$, pp. 769–770

**Statement.** *Proposition 17.2.4* (same). For $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and the level-1 point $q$ with $\Psi(q) = \alpha$, the chart $S_\Psi : T_\alpha C \to T_qO$, $[\alpha, \Psi', h'] \mapsto T\sigma(h')$ for $\sigma$ of $(q', 1_\alpha, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChart⟧, with $S_\Psi[\alpha, \Psi, h] = h$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChart_mk_self⟧, is a topological linear isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartCLE⟧, and $S_{\Psi'} \circ S_\Psi^{-1} = T\sigma(q)$ for $\sigma$ of $(q, 1_\alpha, q')$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChart_symm_trans⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_4⟧. *Banachable* ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_4_banachable⟧: for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and every chart $\varphi$ of $O$ around $q$, the norm pulled back through $S_\Psi$ and the fibre chart of $\varphi$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentNormedAddCommGroup⟧ induces the topology of $T_\alpha C$ and is complete. *Smooth objects* ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_4_sc⟧: if $\alpha$ is smooth, every chart $T_\alpha C \to T_qO \to E$ through any $\Psi$ and any chart of $O$ has an sc-subspace as image, and the change between two such charts is the restriction of an sc-operator. *Proposition 17.2.5* (restated) ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_5⟧: for $\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$ between objects with tangent spaces, $T\varphi[\alpha, \Psi, h] = [\alpha', \Psi', T\sigma(h)]$, $\sigma$ of $(q, \varphi, q') \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ((17.31)) ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.morTr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMorAt⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMorAt_mk⟧, is well defined and independent of $\Psi' \in F(\alpha')$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMorAt_indep⟧; it is a topological linear isomorphism $T_\alpha C \to T_{\alpha'}C$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMorCLE⟧, $T1_\alpha = \mathrm{id}$ and $T(\psi \circ \varphi) = T\psi \circ T\varphi$.

*Readings.* "Banachable" is read as a topological vector space whose topology is induced by a complete norm; the norm depends on the chart up to equivalence and is a construction, not an instance, as for $T_xX$ in Chapter 2. "sc-Banachable (using an obvious definition)" is read through the charts, as in the statement. The category $\mathrm{Ban}$ is not built: $\mu_C$ is given by its map on objects $\alpha \mapsto T_\alpha C$ and on morphisms $\varphi \mapsto T\varphi$, with the functor laws.

**Proof of Proposition 17.2.4.** *Step 1, the chart is well defined and bijective.* On a tuple $(\alpha, \Psi', h')$, $S_\Psi$ takes $T\sigma(h')$ for $\sigma$ of $(q', 1_\alpha, q)$. Related tuples give the same value: if $T\sigma_1(h_1) = h_2$ for $(q_1, 1, q_2)$, then $T\sigma_{(q_2,1,q)}(h_2) = T\sigma_{(q_2,1,q)}T\sigma_{(q_1,1,q_2)}(h_1) = T\sigma_{(q_1,1,q)}(h_1)$ by the composition law. The inverse sends $v \in T_qO$ to $[\alpha, \Psi, v]$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChart_symm_apply⟧: one composite is $T\sigma$ of the unit, the identity, and the other is the relation itself.

*Step 2, the change of charts.* $S_{\Psi'}(S_\Psi^{-1}(v)) = S_{\Psi'}[\alpha, \Psi, v] = T\sigma(v)$ for $\sigma$ of $(q, 1_\alpha, q')$, by the definition of $S_{\Psi'}$ (`tangentChart_symm_trans`).

*Step 3, every chart is a topological linear isomorphism.* The structure of $T_\alpha C$ is that of $T_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ transported along $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$, so $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ is a topological linear isomorphism by construction ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.refChartCLE⟧. For any $\Psi$, $S_\Psi = T\sigma(q_\alpha) \circ S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ by Step 2 ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.coe_tangentChartCLE⟧, and $T\sigma(q_\alpha)$ of $(q_\alpha, 1_\alpha, q)$ is a topological linear isomorphism of fibres (previous section, Step 7). So $S_\Psi$ is one too, and the structure does not depend on the reference.

*Step 4, Banachable.* The fibre $T_qO$ carries, through a chart $\varphi$ of $O$ around $q$, a continuous linear isomorphism with the closed subspace of the model space $E_0 \times E_1$ given by the tangent of the retract ⟦Chapter2/TangentSpaceLinear · MPolyfold.tangentFiberContinuousLinearEquiv⟧. Composing with $S_\Psi$ gives a linear injection $L$ of $T_\alpha C$ into a Banach space, which is a homeomorphism onto its image; the norm $\|L(\cdot)\|$ induces the topology (the topology of a pulled-back norm is the induced topology, and $L$ is an embedding), and $L$ is an isometry onto a complete space, so the norm is complete.

*Step 5, smooth objects.* If $\alpha$ is smooth, the point $q$ is smooth in $O$ for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ (Theorem 17.1.28's independence of the regularity, applied at every level). For a smooth point the image of the fibre chart is an sc-subspace (Part I) ⟦Chapter2/TangentSpaceLinear · MPolyfold.isScSubspace_tangentFiberModel⟧. For a second chart through $\Psi'$ and $\varphi'$, the change of charts is $T\sigma(q)$ read in the charts (Step 2), and the tangent of the sc-smooth map $\sigma$ at a smooth point is, read in charts, the restriction of an sc-operator ⟦Chapter2/TangentMapLinear · MPolyfold.exists_clm_tangentMapOnFiber⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 17.2.5.** *Step 1, well defined.* For related tuples $(\alpha, \Psi_1, h_1) \sim (\alpha, \Psi_2, h_2)$, the elements $(q_2, \varphi, q') \circ (q_1, 1_\alpha, q_2)$ and $(q_1, \varphi, q')$ have the same entries, so the composition law gives the same $T\sigma$ value; this is the quotient lift in `tangentMorAt`.

*Step 2, independence of $\Psi'$.* For two choices $\Psi'_1, \Psi'_2 \in F(\alpha')$, $(q'_1, 1_{\alpha'}, q'_2) \circ (q, \varphi, q'_1)$ has the entries of $(q, \varphi, q'_2)$, since $1_{\alpha'} \circ \varphi = \varphi$; by the composition law the two values are related tuples, hence equal classes (`tangentMorAt_indep`).

*Step 3, the unit and the composition.* $T1_\alpha = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMorAt_unit⟧: $(q, 1_\alpha, q')$ is the element of the relation itself. $T(\psi \circ \varphi) = T\psi \circ T\varphi$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMorAt_comp⟧: $(q', \psi, q'') \circ (q, \varphi, q') = (q, \psi \circ \varphi, q'')$, and the composition law.

*Step 4, linear and continuous.* Through the reference charts, $T\varphi = S_{\Psi_{\alpha'}}^{-1} \circ T\sigma(q_\alpha) \circ S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ with $\sigma$ of $(q_\alpha, \varphi, q_{\alpha'})$; this composite of topological linear isomorphisms is `tangentMorCLE`, and it agrees with (17.31) for every choice of $\Psi'$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.coe_tangentMorCLE⟧. The functor laws for `tangentMorCLE` follow from Step 3 by extensionality.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* None in the mathematics. The independence of $S_\Psi$ of the reference is the text's "$S_{\Psi'} \circ S_\Psi^{-1} = T\sigma(q)$"; the formalization makes the reference explicit because Lean needs one structure on $T_\alpha C$.

### XVII.23 Definition 17.2.6, Lemma 17.2.7, Remark 17.2.8 (new in the 2021 edition): the tangent category $TC$ and the projection $P$, pp. 770–771

**Statement.** *Definition 17.2.6* (restated). An object of $TC$ is an object $\alpha$ with $T_\alpha C \ne \emptyset$ together with a vector of the reference fibre $T_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentCatObj⟧; through $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ the objects are exactly the pairs $(\alpha, e)$, $e \in T_\alpha C$, that is, the tangent vectors ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatObjEquiv⟧. A morphism is a source object $\bar h$ and a morphism $\varphi$ of $C$ with $s(\varphi) = P(\bar h)$ and $T_{t(\varphi)}C \neq \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentCatMor⟧, its target being $T\varphi(\bar h)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentCatMor.tgt⟧; the morphisms are the text's tuples $(\bar h, \varphi, T\varphi(\bar h))$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatMorEquiv⟧, and the target of a morphism is $T\varphi$ of its source ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_2_6⟧. With composition $(\bar h', \psi) \circ (\bar h, \varphi) = (\bar h, \psi \circ \varphi)$, units $(\bar h, 1_\alpha)$ and inverses $(T\varphi(\bar h), \varphi^{-1})$ this is a groupoid ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatGroupoid⟧. *Lemma 17.2.7* (same): $TC$ is a groupoidal category ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCategory⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_7⟧, with finite morphism sets ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.finite_tangentCatMor⟧. *Remark 17.2.8* (same): the morphisms are the pairs $(\varphi, e)$ with $e \in T_{s(\varphi)}C$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.remarkMorEquiv⟧, with $s(\varphi, e) = (s(\varphi), e)$ and $t(\varphi, e) = (t(\varphi), T\varphi(e))$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_2_8⟧. *(17.33), (17.34)*: $P : TC \to C_1$, $P(\bar h) = \alpha$, $P(\bar h, \varphi) = \varphi$, is a functor into the full subgroupoid $C_1$ of the objects of regularity $\ge 1$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatProj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.regSub⟧, surjective on objects under $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.surjective_tangentCatProj_obj⟧.

*Readings.* $TC$ is built in the universe of $TO$, one above that of $C$ (Lean's `max u 1`); the book's objects, classes over all uniformizers, would live one universe higher still, and the reference fibre represents them faithfully. The condition $T_{t(\varphi)}C \ne \emptyset$ in a morphism is automatic for $t(\varphi)$ isomorphic to an object of $C_1$ when $F(t(\varphi)) \neq \emptyset$, which is the standing reading.

**Proof that $TC$ is a groupoid.** *Step 1, equality of objects.* Two objects over the same $\alpha$ are equal iff their vectors agree; over equal objects $\alpha = \beta$ given by different proofs, the criterion is that $T\sigma$ of $(q_\alpha, 1, q_\beta)$ sends one vector to the other ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentCatObj.ext_of⟧, and conversely equal objects satisfy it ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentCatObj.transMap_vec_of_eq⟧. A morphism is determined by its source and its $\varphi$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentCatMor.ext'⟧.

*Step 2, the target.* For $(\bar h, \varphi)$ with $\bar h = (\alpha, v)$, $v \in T_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$, the target is $(t(\varphi), T\sigma(v))$ with $\sigma$ of $(q_\alpha, \varphi, q_{t(\varphi)}) \in M(\Psi_\alpha, \Psi_{t(\varphi)})$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.refMorVec⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.refTr⟧; read through the reference charts this is (17.31).

*Step 3, the laws.* The target of a composite is the target of the second morphism: by the composition law for $T\sigma$, $T\sigma_{(q_\beta, \psi, q_\gamma)} \circ T\sigma_{(q_\alpha, \varphi, q_\beta)} = T\sigma_{(q_\alpha, \psi \circ \varphi, q_\gamma)}$. The target of the unit $(\bar h, 1_\alpha)$ is $\bar h$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TangentCatObj.tgt_unitMor⟧, by the unit law, with Step 1 to handle $1_\alpha \circ 1_\alpha$. For the inverse, $T\sigma_{\varphi^{-1}} \circ T\sigma_\varphi = \mathrm{id}$ gives that $(T\varphi(\bar h), \varphi^{-1})$ has target $\bar h$. Associativity and the unit and inverse laws reduce to those of $C$ on the second component, since a morphism is determined by its source and $\varphi$ (Step 1).

**Proof of Lemma 17.2.7.** A morphism $\bar h \to \bar h'$ is determined by its $\varphi$, which lies in the finite set $\mathrm{mor}_C(\alpha, \alpha')$ (Definition 17.1.1); so the morphism set injects into a finite set (`finite_tangentCatMor`).

**Proof of Definition 17.2.6 and Remark 17.2.8 in the text's form.** `tangentCatObjEquiv` sends $(\alpha, v)$ to $(\alpha, S_{\Psi_\alpha}^{-1}(v))$; it is a bijection because $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ is. Under it, the target $(t(\varphi), T\sigma(v))$ of Step 2 is $(t(\varphi), S_{\Psi_{t(\varphi)}}^{-1} T\sigma(q_\alpha) S_{\Psi_\alpha}(e))$, which is `tangentMorCLE` $\varphi$ applied to $e$ (Proposition 17.2.5), so the target is $T\varphi$ of the source (`definition_17_2_6`). Remark 17.2.8 is the same bijection on morphisms: $(\bar h, \varphi) \mapsto (\varphi, [s(\varphi), \Psi_{\alpha}, v])$, inverted by $(\varphi, e) \mapsto (S_{\Psi_{s\varphi}}(e), \varphi)$; its two formulas are the first component of the source and `definition_17_2_6`.

**Proof that $P$ is a functor.** On objects $P(\alpha, v) = \alpha$, which lies on level $1$ by `TangentObj`; on morphisms $P(\bar h, \varphi) = \varphi$. Source, target, units and composites are preserved by construction, the composite of $TC$ being $\psi \circ \varphi$ on the second component. Under $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$ every $\alpha \in C_1$ carries the zero vector, so $P$ is surjective on objects.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text's objects are the classes $[\alpha, \Psi, h]$; the formalization stores the representative in the fibre of one reference uniformizer per object and proves the identification with the text's description. This changes nothing in the mathematics and avoids a universe jump. A morphism is stored as $(\bar h, \varphi)$ because the third entry of the text's tuple is determined.

### XVII.24 Lemma 17.2.9 (2017: Lemma 17.4) and Lemma 17.2.10 (new in the 2021 edition): the tangent uniformizer $T\Psi$, (17.35)–(17.40), pp. 771–773

**Statement.** *(17.35), (17.36)*: for $\Psi \in \mathcal U$ the tangent uniformizer on objects is $T\Psi(h) = [\Psi(\tau(h)), \Psi', T\sigma(h)]$ for $\Psi' \in F(\Psi(\tau(h)))$ and $\sigma$ of $(\tau(h), 1, q')$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifObj⟧, built with the reference uniformizer; its base is $\Psi(\tau(h))$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifObj_base⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentObj_of_unif⟧. *Lemma 17.2.9* (same): the class does not depend on $\Psi'$ nor on $q'$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_9⟧, so (17.36) holds for every choice ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifObj_spec⟧. $G$ acts on $TO$ by $g \ast h = T(g \ast \cdot)(h)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.tangentMulAction⟧, and $G \ltimes TO$ is the translation groupoid ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTranslation⟧. *(17.39)*: $T\Psi(g, h) = (T\Psi(h), \Psi(g, q))$, $q = \tau(h)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifMor⟧. *Lemma 17.2.10* (narrower): as printed, that $T\Psi : G \ltimes TO \to TC$ is a functor, it does not hold as we read it ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_lemma_17_2_10⟧. What holds for every $\Psi$: $T\Psi$ respects sources, composites and units ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_10_mor⟧, and the target of $T\Psi(g, h)$ is $T\Psi(T\sigma(h))$ for $\sigma$ of $\hat\Psi(g, q)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifMor_tgt⟧. If $\hat\Psi$ is continuous, $T\sigma(h) = g \ast h$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_hat_of_continuous⟧ and $T\Psi$ is a functor ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_10_of_continuous⟧, with (17.40) ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifMor_tgt_of_continuous⟧.

*Hypotheses.* $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ (`hU`), the standing reading: (17.36) picks a $\Psi' \in F(\Psi(\tau(h)))$. For Lemma 17.2.10, $\hat\Psi$ continuous (`hc`); witness the reflection construction $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $x \mapsto -x$, with its tautological uniformizer, whose $\hat\Psi$ is continuous ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_continuous_hat⟧, so that $T\Psi$ is a functor ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionTangentUnifFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_lemma_17_2_10⟧. The witness is not weak: the group acts non-trivially, and $0$ has an automorphism other than the unit.

**Proof of the action on $TO$.** $g \ast h = T(g \ast \cdot)(h)$, the tangent of the sc-smooth map $p \mapsto g \ast p$ (Definition 17.1.3). $1 \ast h = h$ because the tangent of the identity is the identity ⟦Chapter2/TangentMap · MPolyfold.tangentMap_id⟧, and $(gg') \ast h = g \ast (g' \ast h)$ by the chain rule ⟦Chapter2/TangentMap · MPolyfold.tangentMap_comp⟧. The base point of $g \ast h$ is $g \ast \tau(h)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.levelVal_tangentProj_smul⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.basePt_smul⟧. The group in Lean is $G$ lifted to the universe of $TO$, which only matches universes.

**Proof of Lemma 17.2.9.** For two choices $(\Psi_1, q_1)$, $(\Psi_2, q_2)$ the tuples $(\alpha, \Psi_1, T\sigma_1(h))$ and $(\alpha, \Psi_2, T\sigma_2(h))$ are related: $(q_1, 1, q_2) \circ (q, 1, q_1) = (q, 1, q_2)$, so by the composition law $T\sigma_{(q_1,1,q_2)} T\sigma_1(h) = T\sigma_2(h)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_unitTr_comp⟧. Taking for one of the choices the reference uniformizer gives (17.36) for every choice.

**Proof of the laws that hold for every $\Psi$.** *Sources* hold by definition. *Units*: $T\Psi(1, h) = (T\Psi(h), \Psi(1, q)) = (T\Psi(h), 1_{\Psi(q)})$, since $\Psi$ is a functor ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifMor_one⟧. *Composites*: for $(g, g' \ast h) \circ (g', h) = (gg', h)$, $T\Psi$ of it is $(T\Psi(h), \Psi(gg', q))$, and $\Psi(gg', q) = \Psi(g, g' q) \circ \Psi(g', q)$; the composite in $TC$ is $(T\Psi(h), \Psi(g, g'q) \circ \Psi(g', q))$ by definition of the composition of $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifMor_comp⟧. Neither law involves a target of $TC$, which is why they hold without hypotheses. *The target*: the target of $T\Psi(g, h)$ is $T\varphi(T\Psi(h))$ for $\varphi = \Psi(g, q)$. Through the reference uniformizers this is the three-fold composite $T\sigma_{(q_{\beta}, 1, q_{\beta'})} T\sigma_{(q_\alpha, \varphi, q_\beta)} T\sigma_{(q, 1, q_\alpha)}(h)$, whose elements compose to an element with the entries of $(q_{\beta'}\text{-part}) \circ \hat\Psi(g, q)$; the three-fold composition law gives $T\Psi(T\sigma(h))$ with $\sigma$ of $\hat\Psi(g, q) = (q, \Psi(g,q), gq) \in M(\Psi, \Psi)$ (`tangentUnifMor_tgt`).

**Proof of Lemma 17.2.10 for continuous $\hat\Psi$.** *Step 1.* If $\hat\Psi$ is continuous, $p \mapsto \hat\Psi(g, p)$ is a continuous section of $s$ through $\hat\Psi(g, q)$. By Step 4 of the first section of §17.2 it equals $(s|U)^{-1}$ near $q$ for a triple of $\hat\Psi(g, q)$, so $\sigma = t \circ (s|U)^{-1}$ is $p \mapsto g \ast p$ near $q$, and $T\sigma(h) = T(g \ast \cdot)(h) = g \ast h$ (`transMap_hat_of_continuous`). *Step 2.* With the target law of the previous paragraph, the target of $T\Psi(g, h)$ is $T\Psi(g \ast h)$, which is (17.40), the identity the text calls obvious. *Step 3.* With the laws that hold for every $\Psi$, $T\Psi$ is a functor (`tangentUnifFunctor`).

**Proof that Lemma 17.2.10 does not hold as we read it.** The claim in Lean: for every GCT $C$, every polyfold structure with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, every $\Psi$ and every $(g, h)$, the target of $T\Psi(g, h)$ is $T\Psi(g \ast h)$. The example is a twisted polyfold structure on a translation GCT.

*Step 1, twisted structures.* For a finite group $K$ acting sc-smoothly on an M-polyfold $U$, a *twist* is a bijection $\beta$ of $K \times U$ over the identity of $U$ that commutes with source and target and preserves units, inverses and composites ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TranslationTwist⟧. It gives a polyfold structure on the translation GCT $K \ltimes U$ with $F(x) = \{\Psi_0\}$ for the tautological uniformizer, and with $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$ the set of transition elements carrying the M-polyfold structure of $K \times U$ transported along $e_\beta(g, p) = (p, \beta(g, p), g \ast p)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistHomeomorph⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twistConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧. Then $\hat\Psi_0 = e_\beta \circ \beta^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_hat_eq⟧, continuous when $\beta^{-1}$ is ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.twist_continuous_hat⟧. Definition 17.1.8 holds for every twist, continuous or not, since it only constrains the structure maps $s$, $t$, $u$, $\iota$, $m$ of $M(\Psi_0,\Psi_0)$, which in the coordinates $e_\beta$ are those of $K \times U$.

*Step 2, the example.* $K = \mathbb Z/2 \times \mathbb Z/2$ acts on $\mathbb R$ through the first factor, $(u, v) \ast y = uy$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapAction⟧. The twist exchanges the two factors over $0$: $\beta((u, v), p) = ((v, u), p)$ for $p = 0$ and $((u, v), p)$ otherwise ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapTwistFun⟧. It is a twist ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapTwist⟧: over $0$ every element of $K$ fixes the point, so $\beta$ commutes with the target; it preserves units and inverses, and composites because $p = 0$ iff $g p = 0$. This gives the polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapConstruction⟧ with $F \neq \emptyset$ everywhere ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧, and $\Psi_0$ at $0$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapUnif⟧.

*Step 3, the vector.* Take $h = (0, 1) \in T_0\mathbb R$ in the chart $y \mapsto y$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapTangent⟧, with base point $0$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.basePt_fstSwapTangent⟧, and $g = (1, -1)$. Then $g \ast \cdot$ is the identity of $\mathbb R$, so $g \ast h = T(\mathrm{id})h = h$, which in the chart is $(0, 1)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartMap_tangentMap_one⟧.

*Step 4, $T\sigma$ of $\hat\Psi(g, 0)$.* $\hat\Psi(g, 0) = (0, (1,-1), 0)$ as a morphism, and $\beta^{-1}((1, -1), 0) = ((-1, 1), 0)$, so $\hat\Psi(g, 0) = e_\beta((-1, 1), 0)$. The sheet through it in the coordinates $e_\beta$ is $p \mapsto e_\beta((-1, 1), p) = (p, \beta((-1,1), p), -p)$, a continuous section of $s$ with $t$-component $p \mapsto -p$. By the section lemma ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transMap_eq_tangentMap_of_section⟧ (Step 4 of the first section, with $t \circ f$ sc-smooth), $T\sigma(h) = T(y \mapsto -y)(h)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwap_transMap_hat⟧, which in the chart is $(0, -1)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartMap_tangentMap_neg⟧.

*Step 5, conclusion.* If the target of $T\Psi_0(g, h)$ were $T\Psi_0(g \ast h)$, then by the target law $T\Psi_0(T\sigma(h)) = T\Psi_0(g \ast h)$, and since $T\Psi_0$ is injective on objects ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_tangentUnifObj⟧, $T(y \mapsto -y)h = T(\mathrm{id})h$; in the chart the second coordinates give $-1 = 1$.

*The relation to Lemma 17.1.9.* This is the same point as Lemma 17.1.9 (Findings, Chapter 17, item 1): Definition 17.1.8 does not tie the topology of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$ to $G \times O$, and presumably intends $\hat\Psi$ continuous, under which both lemmas hold (`lemma_17_1_9_of_continuous`, §17.1) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_9_of_continuous⟧. The text's "obvious identity" (17.40) is the last clause of Lemma 17.1.9, that $t$ on the sheet through $\hat\Psi(g, q)$ is $g \ast \cdot$. The counterexample of Lemma 17.1.9, $\mathbb Z/3$ acting trivially on $\mathbb R$ with a twist ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_lemma_17_1_9⟧, does not serve here: every $g \ast \cdot$ is the identity there, so $\sigma = g \ast \cdot$ whatever the twist. A counterexample to Lemma 17.2.10 needs a twist at a fixed point exchanging two isotropy elements with different linearizations, which is why the example uses $\mathbb Z/2 \times \mathbb Z/2$ acting through one factor. In this example $\hat\Psi_0$ is not continuous at $((1,-1), 0)$, in line with the reading.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves Lemma 17.2.10 from (17.40); the formalization splits the functor laws into those that hold for every $\Psi$ and the target law, which is exactly (17.40), and proves the latter under the continuity of $\hat\Psi$. The hypothesis is the same one as in Lemma 17.1.9; it is carried to Theorem 17.2.17 and §17.4.

### XVII.25 Lemma 17.2.11 (2017: Lemma 17.5), Proposition 17.2.12 (new in the 2021 edition), Definition 17.2.13 (2017: Definition 17.10): the transition sets $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ and their natural topology, pp. 774–776

**Statement.** $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ is the transition set of Definition 17.1.5 for the maps $T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2$ on objects: triples $(k, \Phi, k')$ with $k \in TO_1$, $k' \in TO_2$ and $\Phi : T\Psi_1(k) \to T\Psi_2(k')$ in $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TTr⟧. For an admissible triple of $(o, \varphi, o') \in M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$, write $(s|U)^{-1}(q) = (q, \varphi_q, \sigma(q))$; then $\Phi_k = (T\Psi_1(k), \varphi_{\tau(k)})$ is a morphism of $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.chartMor⟧ with target $T\Psi_2(T\sigma(k))$ ((17.41)) ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.chartMor_tgt⟧, and (17.42) is the map $TU(o) \to M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$, $k \mapsto (k, \Phi_k, T\sigma(k))$, on the vectors over $U(o)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrChart⟧; its last entry is the tangent of $\sigma$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrChart_tgt⟧. *Lemma 17.2.11* (same) ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_11⟧: (1) every element $(k, \Phi, k')$ is the value at $k$ of the map (17.42) of an admissible triple of an element $(o, \varphi, o')$ with $o$ on level $1$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_11_one⟧; (2) if two maps (17.42) take a common value at $k_1$ and $k_2$, then $k_1 = k_2$, and the two maps agree on the vectors over an open neighbourhood $W$ of $\tau(k_1)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_2_11_two⟧. *Proposition 17.2.12* (same) ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_12⟧: there is a unique topology on $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ for which every map (17.42) is an open embedding. *Definition 17.2.13* (same): this is the natural topology ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrTopology⟧, and $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ with it is written $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ again ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TTrM⟧.

*Hypothesis.* $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (`hU`), the standing reading, through $T\Psi$. *Readings.* $TU(o)$ is the set of tangent vectors over the level-1 points of $U(o)$; the map (17.42) is defined for every admissible triple of every element, without a regularity hypothesis on $o$. Part (2) is read as: equal values force the transition map $k \mapsto k$, and the two maps agree near the common point ("the data near $q_0$ coincide"). The topology of Proposition 17.2.12 is read as the final topology of all the maps (17.42), one for every element and every admissible triple; the basis of the text's proof gives the same open sets.

**Proof of (17.41).** Let $b = (s|U)^{-1}(\tau(k)) = (\tau(k), \varphi_{\tau(k)}, \sigma(\tau(k)))$. The target of $\Phi_k$ is $T\varphi_{\tau(k)}(T\Psi_1(k))$, which by the three-fold composition law of $T\sigma$ (as in the target law of the previous section) is $T\Psi_2(T\sigma_b(k))$. And $T\sigma_b(k)$ is the tangent of $\sigma$ at $k$, since $b$ lies on the sheet of the triple (Step 3 of the first section of §17.2).

**Proof of Lemma 17.2.11.** *(1).* Given $x = (k, \Phi, k')$, let $o = (\tau(k), \varphi, \tau(k'))$ with $\varphi$ the morphism of $C$ underlying $\Phi$; it is an element of $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ because the source and target of $\Phi$ lie over $\Psi_1(\tau(k))$ and $\Psi_2(\tau(k'))$. For the chosen triple of $o$, $(s|U)^{-1}(\tau(k)) = o$, so the map (17.42) at $k$ has morphism $\varphi$ and source $T\Psi_1(k)$; these determine a morphism of $TC$, which is $\Phi$. Its last entry $T\sigma(k)$ satisfies $T\Psi_2(T\sigma(k)) = {}$ target of $\Phi = T\Psi_2(k')$ by (17.41), and $T\Psi_2$ is injective on objects ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_tangentUnifObj⟧ (because $\Psi_2$ is injective on objects and the maps $T\sigma$ are bijective on fibres), so $T\sigma(k) = k'$. The base point $\tau(k)$ is on level $1$.

*(2).* Equal values have equal first entries, so $k_1 = k_2 =: k_0$. Put $q_0 = \tau(k_0)$. The morphisms agree, $(s|U_1)^{-1}(q_0)$ and $(s|U_2)^{-1}(q_0)$ have the same morphism of $C$; their sources are $q_0$, and their targets are the base points of the equal last entries. So $(s|U_1)^{-1}(q_0) = (s|U_2)^{-1}(q_0) =: b$, and the first triple is an admissible triple of $b$ as well. Now $(s|U_2)^{-1}$ is a continuous section of $s$ through $b$ on $U_2(o)$, so by the section lemma ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.exists_srcInv_eq⟧ it agrees with $(s|U_1)^{-1}$ on an open $W \ni q_0$ inside both domains. For $k$ over $W$ both maps (17.42) have the same morphism $\varphi_{\tau(k)}$ and the same last entry $T\sigma_{(s|U)^{-1}(\tau(k))}(k)$, hence agree.

**Proof of Proposition 17.2.12.** *Step 1, a general criterion.* For a family of injective maps $\varphi_i : U_i \to X$, the final topology ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · chartTopology⟧ (a set is open iff every preimage is) ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · isOpen_chartTopology_iff⟧ makes every $\varphi_i$ an open embedding provided $\varphi_i^{-1}(\varphi_j(W))$ is open for every open $W \subseteq U_j$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · isOpenEmbedding_chartTopology⟧: then $\varphi_i$ is continuous by definition, injective, and open because the image of an open set has open preimages under every $\varphi_j$. Conversely, if the $\varphi_i$ cover $X$, any topology in which every $\varphi_i$ is an open embedding is the final topology ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · eq_chartTopology⟧: every set is the union of the images of its preimages.

*Step 2, the hypotheses.* Each map (17.42) is injective, its first entry being $k$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_tangentTrChart⟧. The overlap condition holds ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isOpen_tangentTrChart_preimage_image⟧: if $k$ is in the preimage under the first map of the image under the second of an open set, Lemma 17.2.11 (2) gives an open $W$ around $\tau(k)$ on which the two maps agree, so the vectors over $W$ in the open set form an open neighbourhood of $k$ inside the preimage. The maps cover $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ by Lemma 17.2.11 (1). Step 1 gives existence and uniqueness; the index set of the family is the set of pairs of an element of $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ and an admissible triple of it ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TTrIndex⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text constructs a basis $B$ of open sets; the formalization defines the topology as the final topology of the charts and proves the criterion once. The two give the same topology, by the uniqueness part of Proposition 17.2.12. "(1) is obvious" is proved.

### XVII.26 Propositions 17.2.14 (2017: Proposition 17.3) and 17.2.15 (2017: Proposition 17.4): the natural M-polyfold structure of $M(T\Psi, T\Psi')$, pp. 776–781

**Statement.** *Proposition 17.2.14* (same) ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_14⟧: for the natural topology, $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_TTrM⟧, $s$ and $t$ are local homeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_14_src_tgt⟧, the inversion $\iota$ is a homeomorphism, and the unit and multiplication maps are continuous. *Proposition 17.2.15* (same) ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_15⟧: $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ carries an M-polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrPolyfold⟧ for which every map (17.42) is an sc-diffeomorphism of $TU(o)$ onto an open set, with inverse $s$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_tangentTrChartExt⟧; $s$, $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTr_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTr_isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧, $\iota$ is an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTr_isScDiffeoOn_inv⟧, and the unit and multiplication maps are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTr_isScSmoothMap_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTr_isScSmoothMap_mul⟧.

*Hypothesis.* $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (`hU`). *Readings.* "Natural M-polyfold structure" is read as a structure in which the maps (17.42) are charts, that is, sc-diffeomorphisms onto open sets. The text leaves the details of both propositions to the reader (G.207, G.208); they are the steps below.

**The key construction: $TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2) \cong M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$.** The text proves metrizability through the Nagata–Smirnov theorem. The formalization instead identifies $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ with Part I's tangent $TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ of the M-polyfold $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$, which is metrizable and an M-polyfold already.

*Step 1, $Ts$ and $Tt$.* On $TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ take the tangents $Ts$, $Tt$ of the source and target maps ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.trTSrc⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.trTTgt⟧ and the base point $p(v) \in M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)_1$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.trBase⟧. On a sheet $U(o,\varphi,o')$, $t = \sigma \circ s$, so $Tt = T\sigma \circ Ts$ there ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.trTTgt_eq_tangentMapOn⟧, and in general $Tt(v) = T\sigma(Ts(v))$ for $\sigma$ of $p(v)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.trTTgt_eq_transMap⟧.

*Step 2, the map.* An element of $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ is determined by its source and its morphism of $C$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.TTr.ext'⟧: the source of $\Phi$ is $T\Psi_1(k)$, and its target $T\Psi_2(k')$ determines $k'$ by injectivity. Define $v \mapsto (Ts\, v, (T\Psi_1(Ts\, v), \text{morphism of } p(v)), Tt\, v)$, the map (17.42) of the chosen triple of $p(v)$ at $Ts\, v$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrMap⟧. On the sheet of any triple $T$ it is the map (17.42) of $T$ at $Ts\, v$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrMap_eq_chart⟧, by the criterion of the previous sentence (the morphism of $p(v)$ is that of $(s|U)^{-1}(\tau(Ts\, v))$, since $p(v)$ is on the sheet).

*Step 3, bijective.* Injective ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_tangentTrMap⟧: equal values give $Ts\, v = Ts\, w$, equal morphisms and (by Step 1) $Tt\, v = Tt\, w$, hence $p(v) = p(w)$; since $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, $Ts$ is injective on each fibre, so $v = w$. Surjective ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.surjective_tangentTrMap⟧: by Lemma 17.2.11 (1) every element is the map (17.42) of a triple at some $k$, and this is the value at $v = (Ts|TU(o,\varphi,o'))^{-1}(k)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentLift⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrMap_lift⟧. So it is a bijection ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrEquiv⟧.

*Step 4, a homeomorphism for the natural topology* ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrHomeomorph⟧. The inverse, composed with the map (17.42) of a triple, is $(Ts|TU(o,\varphi,o'))^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrEquiv_symm_chart⟧, an sc-diffeomorphism onto an open set ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_tangentLift⟧, hence an open embedding. A map out of $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ whose composites with the maps (17.42) are open embeddings is a local homeomorphism for the natural topology ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_of_comp_tangentTrChart⟧ (the images of the maps (17.42) are open and cover, by Proposition 17.2.12 and Lemma 17.2.11 (1)). A bijective local homeomorphism is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_tangentTrEquiv_symm⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 17.2.14.** *Metrizable, Hausdorff, paracompact.* $TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ is metrizable (Part I) ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · MPolyfold.metrizableSpace_tangentSpace⟧, and these properties pass along the homeomorphism of Step 4. *$s$ and $t$.* On the image of a map (17.42), $s$ is the inverse of the map, so $s \circ (17.42)$ is the inclusion of the open set $TU(o)$, an open embedding; $t \circ (17.42) = T\sigma$ on $TU(o)$, which is an open embedding because $\sigma$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto an open set ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isOpenEmbedding_tangentSigma⟧. The criterion of Step 4 gives local homeomorphisms. *$\iota$, $u$, $m$.* These follow from Proposition 17.2.15, their sc-smoothness giving continuity, because the topology underlying the M-polyfold structure is the natural topology.

**Proof of Proposition 17.2.15.** *Step 1, the structure.* Transport the tangent M-polyfold structure of $TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ (Part I) ⟦Chapter2/TangentBundle · MPolyfold.tangentPolyfold⟧ along the homeomorphism of Step 4; the homeomorphism is then an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_tangentTrHomeomorph⟧.

*Step 2, the maps (17.42) are charts.* A map (17.42), extended to all of $TO_1$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrChartExt⟧, is the composite of $(Ts|TU(o,\varphi,o'))^{-1}$, an sc-diffeomorphism from $TU(o)$ onto the vectors over the sheet, with the transported identification. So it is an sc-diffeomorphism of $TU(o)$ onto an open set, with inverse $s$ (which under the identification is $Ts$).

*Step 3, $s$, $t$, $u$, $\iota$.* Under the identification, $s = Ts$, $t = Tt$, $u = Tu$, $\iota = T\iota$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTr_inv_eq⟧. Definition 17.1.8 (a), (b) for $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ give that $s$, $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms and $u$, $\iota$ sc-smooth, and the tangent of a local sc-diffeomorphism is a local sc-diffeomorphism, the tangent of an sc-smooth map sc-smooth (Part I) ⟦Chapter2/TangentMap · MPolyfold.tangentMap_isScSmooth⟧. The inversion is its own inverse up to the identification, so it is an sc-diffeomorphism.

*Step 4, the multiplication.* The fibred product of two transition M-polyfolds is not itself treated as an M-polyfold; sc-smoothness on it is checked locally ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · MPolyfold.isScSmoothOnFibredProduct_of_local⟧. Near a pair $(a_0, b_0)$, choose a local inverse $\sigma'$ of $s$ near the base point of $a_0$ in $M(\Psi_2, \Psi_3)$. Then on the pairs $(a, b)$ with $t(b)$ near, the composite is $m(\sigma'(t(b)), b)$, a function of $b$ alone. The map $b \mapsto m(\sigma'(t(b)), b)$ is sc-smooth on the open set where $t(b)$ is in the domain of $\sigma'$ (Definition 17.1.8 (c) on a sheet). Its tangent, composed with the identifications, is the multiplication of $M(T\Psi_\ast, T\Psi_\ast)$ on these pairs composed with the second projection: the source entries agree because the source of the composite is that of $b$, and the morphisms agree because $\sigma'(t(p(v)))$ is the base point of the first factor. So the multiplication is locally sc-smooth.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text's Nagata–Smirnov argument (Hausdorff, locally metrizable, locally complete, completely regular, paracompact through a locally finite closed cover pulled back along $P$) is replaced by the homeomorphism with $TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$, which carries metrizability and the M-polyfold structure at once. The structure is the transport of Part I's tangent, and the reading "natural" is proved: the maps (17.42) are its charts. This also gives the projection $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2) \to M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)_1$ of pp. 780–781 as the tangent projection under the identification ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrProj⟧, sc-smooth into the shifted M-polyfold ⟦Chapter17/TangentTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_tangentTrProj⟧.

### XVII.27 Proposition 17.2.16 (2017: Proposition 17.5): $|TC|$, the GCT $TC$ and $|P|$, (17.52), pp. 781–783

**Statement** (stronger). For a covering family $\Psi = (\Psi_\lambda)$ with model $X_\Psi$ (Theorem 17.1.14), the functor $T\Gamma_\Psi : T(X_\Psi) \to TC$ of (17.52) ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelFunctor⟧ sends a vector $h_\lambda$ to $T\Psi_\lambda(h_\lambda)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelObj⟧ and a vector $w$ of $TM(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu)$ to the morphism of $(Ts\, w, \Phi, Tt\, w) \in M(T\Psi_\lambda, T\Psi_\mu)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelMor⟧. *Proposition 17.2.16* ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_16⟧: for every covering family, $T\Gamma_\Psi$ is full ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelFunctor_full⟧, faithful ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelFunctor_faithful⟧ and essentially surjective ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelFunctor_essSurj⟧, $|T\Gamma_\Psi|$ is a bijection ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bijective_orbitMap_tangentModelFunctor⟧ and a homeomorphism for the natural topology of $|TC|$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentOrbitTopology⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_tangentModelOrbitEquiv⟧; this topology is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_tangentOrbit⟧, so $TC$ is a GCT ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentGCT⟧; and $|P| : |TC| \to |C_1|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.continuous_orbitMap_tangentCatProj⟧ for the topology of $|C_1|$ of Theorem 17.1.28 ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneTopology⟧.

*Hypothesis.* $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (`hU`). *Readings.* In (17.52), "$\Psi \in F(\Psi_\lambda(q_\lambda))$" is the reference uniformizer, so $T\Gamma(h_\lambda) = T\Psi_\lambda(h_\lambda)$; by Lemma 17.2.9 the class does not depend on the choice. "Natural topology" is read as the topology for which $|T\Gamma_\Psi|$ is a homeomorphism, taken for one chosen covering family ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCover⟧ and proved the same for all. *Verdict* "stronger": $T\Gamma_\Psi$ is moreover full, faithful and essentially surjective, an equivalence of categories.

**Proof.** *Step 1, the tangent of a disjoint union.* The objects of $X_\Psi$ form $\bigsqcup_\lambda O_\lambda$ and the morphisms $\bigsqcup M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu)$. Part I's tangent of a disjoint union is the disjoint union of the tangents: every vector is $T(\iota_\lambda)(k)$ for the inclusion $\iota_\lambda$ of a summand ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · MPolyfold.exists_sigmaTangent⟧, the summand and the vector are determined ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · MPolyfold.sigmaTangent_mk_inj⟧, and functions are defined summand by summand ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · MPolyfold.sigmaTangentElim⟧. The inclusions are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter8/SigmaPolyfold · MPolyfold.isLocalScDiffeo_sigmaMk⟧. The structure maps of $T(X_\Psi)$, the tangent ep-groupoid of Theorem 8.1.1 ⟦Chapter8/TangentEp · EpGroupoid.tangentEpGroupoid⟧, are computed on the summands: source $Ts$, target $Tt$, unit $Tu$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tSrc_modelTMorMk⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tTgt_modelTMorMk⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tUnit_modelTObjMk⟧.

*Step 2, a functor.* On a summand, $T\Gamma$ on morphisms is the morphism part of the identification $TM(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu) \cong M(T\Psi_\lambda, T\Psi_\mu)$ of the previous section. Its source is $T\Psi_\lambda(Ts\, w)$ and its target $T\Psi_\mu(Tt\, w)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelMor_src⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelMor_tgt⟧, and its morphism of $C$ is that of the base point ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelMor_mor⟧. Composites: a morphism of $TC$ is determined by its source and its morphism of $C$, and the base point of a composite in $T(X_\Psi)$ is the composite of the base points, whose morphism is the composite in $C$. Units: $Tu$ goes to the unit of $M(T\Psi, T\Psi)$.

*Step 3, full and faithful.* Full: a morphism $\psi : T\Psi_\lambda(k) \to T\Psi_\mu(k')$ of $TC$ is an element $(k, \psi, k')$ of $M(T\Psi_\lambda, T\Psi_\mu)$; by the surjectivity of the identification it comes from a vector $w$, and $T(\iota_{(\lambda,\mu)})(w)$ is a preimage with the right source and target. Faithful: two morphisms with the same source, target and image lie in the same summand (Step 1) and have the same source and morphism of $C$; by the injectivity of the identification they are equal.

*Step 4, essentially surjective.* Every object of $TC$ over $\Psi_0(q)$ is $T\Psi_0(k)$ for some $k$ over $q$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_tangentUnifObj_eq⟧: for the object $(\alpha, v)$ with $\alpha = \Psi_0(q)$, take $k = T\sigma(v)$ for $\sigma$ of $(q_\alpha, 1, q)$; then $T\Psi_0(k)$ has vector $T\sigma_{(q, 1, q_\alpha)} T\sigma_{(q_\alpha,1,q)}(v) = v$ by the three-fold law. Given any object $e$ over $\beta$, the covering property gives $\lambda$ and $q$ with $\Psi_\lambda(q)$ isomorphic to $\beta$ by some $\varphi$; then $\Psi_\lambda(q)$ is on level $1$ (regularity is invariant under isomorphism), the morphism $(e, \varphi^{-1})$ of $TC$ has target over $\Psi_\lambda(q)$, which is $T\Psi_\lambda(k)$ for some $k$, and its inverse is an isomorphism $T\Gamma(k) \to e$.

*Step 5, the orbit map.* A full functor has an injective orbit map, an essentially surjective one a surjective orbit map ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · GroupoidFunctor.injective_orbitMap_of_full⟧ ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · GroupoidFunctor.surjective_orbitMap_iff⟧; so $|T\Gamma_\Psi|$ is a bijection ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelOrbitEquiv⟧. The topology of $|TC|$ is the one transported from $|T(X_\Psi)|$ along it, for the chosen covering family.

*Step 6, independence of the covering family.* For covering families $\Psi$, $\Psi'$, let $\Psi'' = \Psi \sqcup \Psi'$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.CoveringFamily.union⟧, a covering family. The inclusion of the index sets gives functors $X_\Psi \to X_{\Psi''}$, $X_{\Psi'} \to X_{\Psi''}$ (§17.1, Theorem 17.1.18) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelMap⟧, which are equivalences of ep-groupoids since both families cover ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_modelMap⟧. Their tangents are equivalences (Theorem 10.1.7, Part X) ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · EpFunctor.theorem_10_1_7⟧, whose orbit maps are homeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_orbitMap_tangent_modelMap⟧. On objects $T\Gamma_{\Psi''} \circ T(X_\Psi \to X_{\Psi''}) = T\Gamma_\Psi$, since the inclusion of a summand is the inclusion of the same summand ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelObj_modelMap⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.orbitMap_tangentModelFunctor_modelMap⟧. So, with $\Psi'$ the chosen family, $|T\Gamma_\Psi| = |T\Gamma_{\Psi'}| \circ |T(X_{\Psi'} \to X_{\Psi''})|^{-1} \circ |T(X_\Psi \to X_{\Psi''})|$, a composite of homeomorphisms. This is the text's "easily verified" (G.209).

*Step 7, metrizable.* $|T(X_\Psi)|$ is metrizable (Theorem 8.1.15, Part VIII) ⟦Chapter8/TangentOrbit · EpGroupoid.metrizableSpace_tangent_orbitSpace⟧, using that $|X_\Psi|$ is paracompact ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧; the homeomorphism carries it to $|TC|$. With Lemma 17.2.7 this makes $TC$ a GCT.

*Step 8, $|P|$ continuous.* On objects, $P \circ T\Gamma_\Psi = \Gamma^1_\Psi \circ p$ with $p : T(X_\Psi) \to X^1_\Psi$ the tangent projection ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatProj_tangentModelObj⟧ ⟦Chapter8/TangentEp · EpGroupoid.tangentProjFunctor⟧ and $\Gamma^1_\Psi$ the functor of Theorem 17.1.28 ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctorOne⟧. So $|P| \circ |T\Gamma_\Psi| = |\Gamma^1_\Psi| \circ |p|$; $|p|$ is continuous (orbit maps of ep-functors are continuous), $|\Gamma^1_\Psi|$ a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/LevelOneCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_modelOrbitEquivOne⟧, and the topology of $|TC|$ is the final one along $|T\Gamma_\Psi|$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Steps 3 and 4 show that $T\Gamma_\Psi$ is an equivalence of categories, more than the bijection of orbit spaces the text uses. The independence of the covering family, which the text calls easily verified, goes through the union of two families and Theorem 10.1.7.

### XVII.28 The construction on p. 783 and Theorem 17.2.17 (2017: Theorem 17.4): the tangent polyfold and the bundle $P : TC \to C_1$, pp. 783–784

**Statement.** *The construction on p. 783* (narrower).
