For $\hat\Psi$ continuous, $T\Psi$ is a local uniformizer of the GCT $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnif⟧: on the M-polyfold $TO$, with the group $G$ acting by $g \ast h$, the functor of Lemma 17.2.10, injective on objects, full ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifFunctor_full⟧ and faithful ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifFunctor_faithful⟧, and $|T\Psi|$ an open embedding ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isOpenEmbedding_tangentUnifFunctor⟧. $TF(\beta)$ is the set of the $T\Psi$, $\Psi \in F(P\beta)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentF⟧, and with $M(T\Psi, T\Psi')$ of Proposition 17.2.15, $(TF, M)$ is a polyfold structure on $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentConstruction⟧ with $TF(\beta) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧. The bundle maps $M(T\Psi, T\Psi') \to M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrBundleProj⟧ are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_tangentTrBundleProj⟧ and compatible with $s$, $t$ and the morphisms ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentTrBundleProj_spec⟧. For every family $\Phi$ of $TF$ and $\Psi = {}$ the chosen uniformizers with $\Phi_\lambda = T\Psi_\lambda$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentData⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentFamilyData⟧, the model $X^{TF}_\Phi$ and the tangent $T(X_\Psi)$ are isomorphic over $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelIsoFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEquivalence_tangentModelIsoFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelIso_spec⟧, and on the reference cover ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentRefCover⟧ all functors to $TC$ are equivalences ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModel_equivalence⟧. *Theorem 17.2.17* (narrower) ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_2_17_hyp⟧: for a polyfold structure with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ and every $\hat\Psi$ continuous, the GCT $TC$ has groupoid the tangent category; $(TF, M)$ is a polyfold structure on it with uniformizers at every object, $TF(\beta) = \{T\Psi : \Psi \in F(P\beta)\}$; $P \circ T\Psi = \Psi^1 \circ (\mathrm{Id} \ltimes \tau)$ on objects and morphisms; $T\Psi$ is fibrewise $S_\Psi^{-1}$; the maps $M(T\Psi, T\Psi') \to M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$ are sc-smooth and compatible with $s$, $t$ and the morphisms; and $|P|$ is continuous.

*Hypotheses.* `hU`, the standing reading. `hcont`: every $\hat\Psi$ is continuous, the hypothesis of Lemma 17.2.10, under which $T\Psi$ is a functor; the construction of $T\Psi$ as a uniformizer needs it (G.210). Witnesses: the reflection construction, every $\hat\Psi$ of which is continuous ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_continuous_hat_all⟧, with its tangent construction ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionTangentConstruction⟧ and the conclusions of the theorem ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_theorem_17_2_17⟧ ($\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, non-trivial isotropy at $0$; its structure has one uniformizer at each point); and every slice construction, overlapping uniformizers included ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction_continuous_hat⟧. Neither witness is weak in the group, the first has a non-trivial action; the first has the minimal shape of one global uniformizer, which the second complements. *Readings.* "Polyfold bundle" is read as the pair of structures $(TF, M)$ on $TC$ and $(F^1, M_{F^1})$ on $C_1$ (Theorem 17.1.28) related by $P$ as listed; no strong bundle structure is involved, $TC \to C_1$ being a tangent bundle without a double filtration. $TF$ is indexed through $P$, which is the text's $TF_P = TF \circ P$; $M(L, L')$ is built from a chosen $\Psi$ with $L = T\Psi$, as Theorem 17.1.28 does for $C_1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneData⟧.

**Proof that $T\Psi$ is a local uniformizer.** *Step 1, full.* An isomorphism $\psi : T\Psi(k) \to T\Psi(k')$ of $TC$ has a morphism $\psi_C : \Psi(\tau k) \to \Psi(\tau k')$ of $C$, which by Definition 17.1.3 is $\Psi(g, \tau k)$ for some $g$ with $g \tau(k) = \tau(k')$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.exists_smul_eq_mor⟧. Then $T\Psi(g, k)$ and $\psi$ have the same source and morphism, hence are equal; and $T\Psi(g \ast k) = {}$ target of $T\Psi(g, k)$ (Lemma 17.2.10 with $\hat\Psi$ continuous) $= T\Psi(k')$, so $g \ast k = k'$ by injectivity ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnif_exists_smul⟧.

*Step 2, faithful.* Two morphisms $(g, k)$, $(g', k)$ with the same image have $\Psi(g, \tau k) = \Psi(g', \tau k)$ and $g\tau k = g'\tau k$, and $\Psi$ is faithful, so $g = g'$.

*Step 3, $|T\Psi|$ an open embedding.* Adjoin $\Psi$ to the chosen covering family ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.CoveringFamily.adjoin⟧; for the new family $\Psi'$, $|T\Psi| \circ \pi = |T\Gamma_{\Psi'}| \circ \pi \circ T(\iota_\Psi)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnif_orbitMap_π⟧. The right side is continuous ($|T\Gamma_{\Psi'}|$ is a homeomorphism by Proposition 17.2.16, $\pi$ is continuous and open by Proposition 7.1.8 ⟦Chapter7/Basic · EpGroupoid.proposition_7_1_8_isOpenQuotientMap⟧, and $T(\iota_\Psi)$ is a local homeomorphism), so $|T\Psi|$ is continuous on the quotient. It is injective by Step 1: if $T\Psi(k)$ and $T\Psi(k')$ are isomorphic in $TC$, then $k' = g \ast k$. It is open, since the image of an open set is the image under the right side of its saturated preimage, and each map on the right is open.

*Step 4, the uniformizer.* With these, $T\Psi$ satisfies Definition 17.1.3 for the GCT $TC$: the M-polyfold $TO$ (Part I), the finite group $G$ acting by the sc-smooth maps $T(g \ast \cdot)$, the functor of Lemma 17.2.10 injective on objects (`injective_tangentUnifObj`), full and faithful, with $|T\Psi|$ an open embedding.

**Proof that $(TF, M)$ is a polyfold structure.** *Step 1, $T\Psi$ is at $\beta$.* For $\Psi \in F(P\beta)$, $\beta$ lies over $\Psi(q)$ for some $q$, so $\beta = T\Psi(k)$ for some $k$ (Step 4 of the previous section) ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnif_isAt⟧. *Step 2, the transition M-polyfolds.* For $L = T\Psi$, $L' = T\Psi'$ with the chosen $\Psi$, $\Psi'$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentData_eq⟧, $M(L, L')$ is $M(T\Psi, T\Psi')$ with the structure of Proposition 17.2.15, and Definition 17.1.8 (a)–(c) for it are Proposition 17.2.15 ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMOf⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMulOf⟧. *Step 3, non-empty.* For $\beta$ over $\alpha$, any $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ gives $T\Psi \in TF(\beta)$.

**Proof of the bundle properties.** $P(T\Psi(k)) = \Psi(\tau(k)) = \Psi^1(\tau(k))$ and $P(T\Psi(g, k)) = \Psi(g, \tau k) = \Psi^1(g, \tau k)$ by definition ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatProj_tangentUnifObj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatProj_tangentUnifMor⟧, with $\Psi^1$ the uniformizer of $C_1$ of Theorem 17.1.28 ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelOneFunctor⟧. Fibrewise, $T\Psi(k) = S_\Psi^{-1}(k)$ under the identification of Definition 17.2.6 ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCatObjEquiv_tangentUnifObj⟧: $S_\Psi$ of the vector of $T\Psi(k)$ is $T\sigma_{(q_\alpha, 1, q)} T\sigma_{(q, 1, q_\alpha)}(k) = k$. The bundle map $M(T\Psi, T\Psi') \to M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$ is the projection of the previous section read in $M(\Psi, \Psi')_1 \cong M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$ (Theorem 17.1.28) ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ltrHomeomorph⟧; it is sc-smooth as a composite of sc-smooth maps, and its source, target and morphism are $\tau$ of the source, $\tau$ of the target and the morphism of $C$. $|P|$ is continuous by Proposition 17.2.16.

**Proof of the comparison of models.** *Step 1, the functor.* For a family $\Phi$ of $TF$ indexed by $\iota$ (lifted to the universe of $TC$) ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentFamilyLift⟧, the objects of $X^{TF}_\Phi$ are $\bigsqcup_\lambda TO_\lambda$, which is $T(\bigsqcup_\lambda O_\lambda)$ by Step 1 of the previous section; the morphisms are $\bigsqcup M(T\Psi_\lambda, T\Psi_\mu)$, which is $\bigsqcup TM(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu) = T(\bigsqcup M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu))$ by Proposition 17.2.15's identification. The functor is these identifications ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tmFunObj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tmFunMor⟧; that it respects the structure maps is checked through the faithful functor $T\Gamma_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tm_eq_of_tangentModelMor⟧, both sides having the same source, target and image in $TC$.

*Step 2, an isomorphism.* It is bijective on objects and morphisms ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bijective_tangentModelIsoFunctor_obj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bijective_tangentModelIsoFunctor_mor⟧ and a local sc-diffeomorphism on both ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_tangentModelIsoFunctor_obj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_tangentModelIsoFunctor_mor⟧. A functor of groupoids bijective on objects and morphisms is full, faithful and essentially surjective, so by Lemma 10.1.2 ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · EpFunctor.lemma_10_1_2⟧ it is an equivalence of ep-groupoids. The triangle with $TC$ commutes on objects and morphisms ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelFunctor_comp_tangentModelIsoFunctor_obj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelFunctor_comp_tangentModelIsoFunctor_mor⟧.

*Step 3, the reference cover.* The tangent uniformizers $T\Psi_\alpha$ of the reference uniformizers, one for each object $\alpha$ with $T_\alpha C \neq \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentRefFamily⟧, form a covering family of $TF$ indexed in the universe of $C$: every object of $TC$ lies in the footprint of the $T\Psi_\alpha$ of its base. On it, $X^{TF} \to TC$ is an equivalence with a homeomorphic orbit map (Lemma 17.1.15 for $(TF, M)$) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_15⟧, and from the triangle $T\Gamma_\Psi : T(X_\Psi) \to TC$ is an equivalence of categories.

*Step 4, a witness with isotropy.* On the reflection construction, the family $(T\Psi_0)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflTangentUnif⟧ covers $|TC|$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionTangentCover⟧. The zero vector over $0$ is fixed by $T(y \mapsto -y)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMap_neg_reflZeroTangent⟧, so $T\Psi_0(-1, 0)$ is an automorphism of $T\Psi_0(0)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflTangentAut⟧, other than the unit, whose image in $T(X_\Psi)$ is not the unit either ⟦Chapter17/TangentModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionTangentFamily_isotropy⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text summarizes the construction; the formalization writes it out (G.210). Two points are narrower. First, the hypothesis `hcont`, the same as in Lemma 17.2.10. Second, a covering family $\Phi$ of $TF$ need not give a covering family $\Psi$ of $C$, since the objects of $TC$ lie over $C_1$ and the footprints of the $\Psi_\lambda$ need only cover the image of $C_1$; the isomorphism of models holds for every $\Phi$, covering or not. The comparison of $X^{TF}_\Phi$ with $T(X_\Psi)$ is not in the text; it is the reading under which the construction "associates to a polyfold another one".

### XVII.29 Remark 17.2.18 (2017: Remark 17.3): the partial cone $C_\alpha C$ and the reduced tangent space $T^R_\alpha C$, p. 784

**Statement** (restated). For an object $\alpha$ with $T_\alpha C \ne \emptyset$, the partial cone $C_\alpha C \subseteq T_\alpha C$ is the preimage under $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ of the partial cone $C_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ of Definition 2.5.8 ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.partialCone⟧ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.tangentPartialCone⟧, and the reduced tangent space $T^R_\alpha C$ the preimage of $T^R_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reducedTangent⟧ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.tangentReducedSpace⟧. For every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, every chart of $O$ around the point $q$ over $\alpha$ and every presentation of its partial quadrant, $C_\alpha C = S_\Psi^{-1}(C_qO)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.partialCone_eq⟧ and $T^R_\alpha C = S_\Psi^{-1}(T^R_qO)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reducedTangent_eq⟧; $T^R_\alpha C = C_\alpha C \cap (-C_\alpha C)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.mem_reducedTangent_iff_lineality⟧; and for tame $C$ and smooth $\alpha$, $\dim(T_\alpha C / T^R_\alpha C) = d(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.finrank_quotient_reducedTangent_eq_degIndex⟧, so $C_\alpha C \ne T_\alpha C$ on $\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.partialCone_ne_univ⟧. Witness: the corner of $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes [0, \infty) \times \mathbb R$, $-1$ acting by $(a, y) \mapsto (a, -y)$, a boundary object with a non-trivial automorphism, at which $C_\alpha C$ contains $T^R_\alpha C$, is a proper subset, and the quotient has dimension $d(\alpha) \ge 1$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialConeExamples · GCT.VectorBundle.HalfSpaceReflection.partialCone_corner⟧.

*Reading.* The text defines $C_\alpha C$ and $T^R_\alpha C$ in an sc-smooth model and pushes them forward along the tangent of the equivalence $\Gamma$. The formalization defines them through the charts $S_\Psi$; the two agree, since $T\Gamma$ is built from the same maps $T\sigma$ (Proposition 17.2.16). The independence of the choices, which the text describes as "in the obvious way", is the content proved (G.211). Good position of a finite-dimensional subspace to $C_\alpha C$ is used for $\ker f'(\alpha)$ in Chapter 18 and formalized there.

**Proof.** *Step 1, the maps $T\sigma(q)$ preserve partial cones* ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.mem_partialConeFiber_transTangent_iff⟧. For $a = (q, \varphi, q')$, $T\sigma(q)$ is the tangent of the sc-diffeomorphism $\sigma$ at $q$. The tangent of an sc-diffeomorphism maps $C_xX$ onto $C_{f(x)}Y$ and $T^R_xX$ onto $T^R_{f(x)}Y$, read in any charts and presentations ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.mem_partialConeFiber_tangentMapOnCLM_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.mem_reducedTangentFiber_tangentMapOnCLM_iff⟧: in the chart transported along $f$, $Tf$ is the identity of the model space, and Proposition 2.4.10 makes the partial cone independent of the chart and the presentation.

*Step 2, independence of $\Psi$.* $S_\Psi = T\sigma(q_\alpha) \circ S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ for $\sigma$ of $(q_\alpha, 1_\alpha, q)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartCLE_apply⟧, so $S_\Psi(e) \in C_qO$ iff $S_{\Psi_\alpha}(e) \in C_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ by Step 1, for the chosen chart at $q_\alpha$ and any chart at $q$. That is $C_\alpha C = S_\Psi^{-1}(C_qO)$. For $T^R$: in a partial quadrant, $T^R = C \cap (-C)$; so $T^R_\alpha C = C_\alpha C \cap (-C_\alpha C)$ through the linear chart, and the first identity gives the second.

*Step 3, the codimension.* $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ is a linear isomorphism mapping $T^R_\alpha C$ onto $T^R_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$, so the quotients are isomorphic. For tame $O$ and a smooth point, $\dim(T_xO / T^R_xO) = d_O(x)$ (Definition 2.5.8 in a tame chart) ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.finrank_quotient_tangentReducedSpace_eq_degIndex⟧, and $d_O(q_\alpha) = d_C(\alpha)$ by Definition 17.1.30 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_30⟧. On $\partial C$, $d(\alpha) \ge 1$, so $T^R_\alpha C \ne T_\alpha C$ and hence $C_\alpha C \ne T_\alpha C$, since $C_\alpha C = T_\alpha C$ would give $T^R_\alpha C = T_\alpha C$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reducedTangent_subset_partialCone⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The definition goes through one reference chart and is proved independent of all the choices; the text's push-forward along $T\Gamma$ is not used, but by Proposition 17.2.16 it gives the same sets. The formal statements live in Chapter 18's files because Theorem 18.1.5 is where they are used.

### XVII.30 Definitions 17.3.1, 17.3.2 and Remark 17.3.3 (2017: Definitions 17.11, 17.12, Remark 17.4): subpolyfolds, p. 784

Throughout §§17.3–17.4, $C$ is a GCT with a polyfold structure $(F, M)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction⟧; a uniformizer of $(F, M)$ is a local uniformizer $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ lying in some $F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.Unif⟧, and $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is the transition M-polyfold of Definition 17.1.8 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.Tr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.transPolyfold⟧, whose source and target maps are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_trSrc⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_trTgt⟧. A covering family is a family of uniformizers whose footprints cover $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.CoveringFamily⟧, $X_\Psi$ its model ep-groupoid ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelEP⟧ and $\Gamma_\Psi : X_\Psi \to C$ the natural equivalence ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor⟧ (Theorem 17.1.14, Lemma 17.1.15).

**Statement** (same, for all three). *Definition 17.3.1* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsSubpolyfold⟧: a set $A$ of objects of $C$ is a subpolyfold if it is saturated and, for every object $\alpha \in A$ and every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$, the set $A_\Psi = \Psi^{-1}(A) \subset O$ is a sub-M-polyfold of $O$. *Definition 17.3.2* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsSubpolyfoldModel⟧: $A$ is saturated and there is a covering family $\Psi$ such that $\Gamma_\Psi^{-1}(A)$ is an ep-subgroupoid of $X_\Psi$ (Definition 7.1.21). *Remark 17.3.3* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_3_3⟧: if $A$ satisfies Definition 17.3.2, then $\Gamma_{\Psi'}^{-1}(A)$ is an ep-subgroupoid of $X_{\Psi'}$ for every covering family $\Psi'$; equivalently ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubpolyfoldModel_iff_forall⟧, Definition 17.3.2 holds iff $A$ is saturated, a covering family exists, and the preimage is an ep-subgroupoid in every model.

*Readings.* "Saturated full subcategory" is read as a saturated set $A$ of objects, $\pi^{-1}(\pi(A)) = A$ ⟦Chapter7/Groupoid · GroupoidStr.IsSaturated⟧; the full subcategory on it is built in §17.3's next step. "The set of all objects $A_\Psi \subset O$ with $\Psi(A_\Psi) \subset A$" is read as the largest such set, $\Psi^{-1}(A)$. "A suitable sc-smooth model" is the model of a covering family (p. 752), so Definition 17.3.2 includes the existence of one.

**Three facts about sub-M-polyfolds.** The proofs of this section and the next rest on three general facts of Part II, proved in the file of §17.3.

*Step 1, a local criterion* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · MPolyfold.exists_retraction_of_isScDiffeoOn⟧ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · MPolyfold.isSubMPolyfold_of_forall_isScDiffeoOn⟧. Suppose that near every $x \in A$ there are an sc-diffeomorphism $f : W \to V$ of open sets, with inverse $g$, and a sub-M-polyfold $B$ of the target such that $A \cap W = W \cap f^{-1}(B)$. Then $A$ is a sub-M-polyfold. Near $f(x)$ there is an sc-smooth retraction $r$ onto $B$, defined on an open $V_0$; on the open set $W' = W \cap f^{-1}((V \cap V_0) \cap r^{-1}(V \cap V_0))$ the map $g \circ r \circ f$ is sc-smooth, maps $W'$ into itself, is idempotent and has image $A \cap W'$, because $r$ fixes $B \cap V_0$ and $y \in A \iff f(y) \in B$ on $W$. This is the text's "$\sigma^{-1}$ maps $A_\Psi \cap U(x')$ onto $A_{\Psi'} \cap U(a')$" made into a statement.

*Step 2, preimages* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · MPolyfold.IsLocalScDiffeo.isSubMPolyfold_preimage⟧. The preimage of a sub-M-polyfold under a local sc-diffeomorphism is a sub-M-polyfold: Step 1 with the local inverses of the map.

*Step 3, disjoint unions* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · MPolyfold.isSubMPolyfold_sigma_iff⟧. A subset of a disjoint union $\bigsqcup_i X_i$ of M-polyfolds is a sub-M-polyfold iff its trace on every summand is one: one direction is Step 2 for the inclusions of the summands, which are local sc-diffeomorphisms; the other is Step 1 with the inverse of an inclusion.

Two further facts are used for the witnesses: a smooth point is a sub-M-polyfold, by the constant retraction ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · MPolyfold.isSubMPolyfold_singleton⟧ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · MPolyfold.isScSmoothMap_const_of_smoothSet⟧; and a dense set with dense complement is not one ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · MPolyfold.not_isSubMPolyfold_of_dense⟧, since a retraction $r : V \to V$ onto $A \cap V$ is the identity on the dense set $A \cap V$, hence on $V$ by continuity, so $V \subset A$, which the dense complement forbids.

**The preimage in a model.** *Step 1.* $\Gamma_\Psi^{-1}(A)$ is saturated for saturated $A$ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSaturated_preimage_modelFunctor⟧, because the preimage of a saturated set under any functor is saturated ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GroupoidFunctor.isSaturated_preimage⟧: if $s(\phi) \in F^{-1}(A)$ then $F(\phi)$ has its source in $A$, so its target $F(t(\phi))$ is in $A$. *Step 2.* The object M-polyfold of $X_\Psi$ is $\bigsqcup_\lambda O_\lambda$ and $\Gamma_\Psi$ is $\Psi_\lambda$ on $O_\lambda$, so by Step 3 above $\Gamma_\Psi^{-1}(A)$ is a sub-M-polyfold iff every $A_{\Psi_\lambda} = \Psi_\lambda^{-1}(A)$ is one ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubMPolyfold_preimage_modelFunctor_iff⟧. *Step 3.* An ep-subgroupoid is a saturated sub-M-polyfold of the objects, so for saturated $A$: $\Gamma_\Psi^{-1}(A)$ is an ep-subgroupoid iff every $A_{\Psi_\lambda}$ is a sub-M-polyfold of $O_\lambda$ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEpSubgroupoid_preimage_modelFunctor_iff⟧.

**The transfer argument.** This is the text's local argument of p. 785 as a lemma ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubMPolyfold_of_forall_tr⟧: let $A$ be saturated and $\Psi'$ a uniformizer such that every $a' \in A_{\Psi'}$ is the source of some $(a', \phi, x) \in M(\Psi', \Psi)$ for a uniformizer $\Psi$ with $A_\Psi$ a sub-M-polyfold. Then $A_{\Psi'}$ is a sub-M-polyfold.

*Step 1, a sheet.* Since $s : M(\Psi', \Psi) \to O'$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, there is an sc-diffeomorphism $\sigma$ from an open $W \ni a'$ onto an open neighbourhood of $(a', \phi, x)$, inverse to $s$.

*Step 2, saturation.* For $y \in W$, $\sigma(y)$ is a morphism $\Psi'(y) \to \Psi(t(\sigma(y)))$ of $C$; since $A$ is saturated, $\Psi'(y) \in A \iff \Psi(t(\sigma(y))) \in A$ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GroupoidStr.src_mem_iff_tgt_mem_of_isSaturated⟧. So $A_{\Psi'} \cap W = W \cap \sigma^{-1}(t^{-1}(A_\Psi))$.

*Step 3.* $t^{-1}(A_\Psi)$ is a sub-M-polyfold of $M(\Psi', \Psi)$ by Step 2 of the facts above ($t$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism), and the local criterion with the sc-diffeomorphism $\sigma$ concludes.

Two consequences. *A covering family transfers to every uniformizer* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubMPolyfold_of_isCoveringFamily⟧: if $A_{\Psi_\lambda}$ is a sub-M-polyfold for every member of a covering family, then $A_{\Psi'}$ is one for every uniformizer $\Psi'$ of $(F, M)$; for $a' \in O'$ the orbit $|\Psi'(a')|$ lies in the footprint of some $\Psi_\lambda$, so $\Psi'(a') \cong \Psi_\lambda(q)$ by some $\phi$, and $(a', \phi, q)$ is the transition element the transfer lemma asks for ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.toTr⟧. *Definition 17.3.1 transfers to every uniformizer when $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ on $A$* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsSubpolyfold.isSubMPolyfold⟧: at $a'$ with $\Psi'(a') \in A$ pick $\Psi \in F(\Psi'(a'))$ and $q$ with $\Psi(q) = \Psi'(a')$; then $(a', 1, q) \in M(\Psi', \Psi)$ and $A_\Psi$ is a sub-M-polyfold by Definition 17.3.1.

**Proof of Remark 17.3.3.** Let $A$ satisfy Definition 17.3.2 through the covering family $\Psi$. By Step 3 of the preimage in a model every $A_{\Psi_\lambda}$ is a sub-M-polyfold; by the covering-family transfer $A_{\Psi'_\mu}$ is one for every member of any other covering family $\Psi'$; by Step 3 again $\Gamma_{\Psi'}^{-1}(A)$ is an ep-subgroupoid. The equivalent form follows, the covering family of the hypothesis witnessing that one exists.

**Witnesses.** *The point $0$ of $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isSubpolyfold_zero⟧. In the reflection polyfold ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction⟧, $\{0\}$ is saturated ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isSaturated_zero⟧, $0$ is a smooth point of $\mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rn_one_zero_mem_smoothSet⟧, so for the one-member covering family of the tautological uniformizer $\{0\}$ satisfies Definition 17.3.2, hence (by the next section's direction $17.3.2 \Rightarrow 17.3.1$) Definition 17.3.1; the tautological uniformizer lies in $F(0)$ and $0$ has an automorphism other than the unit, so the witness has non-trivial isotropy. *Overlapping uniformizers* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlap_isSubpolyfold_zero⟧: for the construction with $\Psi_0 : \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $\Psi_1 : \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (-2, 2)$ and $\Psi_2 : \{1\} \ltimes (1, 3)$ ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.overlapConstruction⟧, $\{0\}$ is a subpolyfold in both senses and the transfer makes $A_\Psi$ a sub-M-polyfold for every uniformizer, $\Psi_2$ (with $A_{\Psi_2} = \emptyset$) included. *A saturated set that is not a subpolyfold* ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_not_isSubpolyfold_rat⟧: the rational points of $\mathbb R$ are saturated ($x \in \mathbb Q \iff -x \in \mathbb Q$) ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isSaturated_ratPoints⟧, dense with dense complement, hence not a sub-M-polyfold ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_isSubMPolyfold_ratPoints⟧, so Definition 17.3.1 does not hold at $\alpha = 0$ for the tautological uniformizer.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text states Remark 17.3.3 after Definition 17.3.2 and relies on Proposition 17.3.4 for it; the formalization proves it directly, through the transfer argument, without Proposition 17.3.4 and without $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$. The text's "it only has to be checked for a covering family" after Definition 17.3.1 is the covering-family transfer.

### XVII.31 Proposition 17.3.4 (new in the 2021 edition): the two definitions agree, pp. 784–785

**Statement** (explicit). ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_4⟧: if $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every object $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasUniformizers⟧, then for every set $A$ of objects, $A$ satisfies Definition 17.3.1 iff it satisfies Definition 17.3.2. The form with the hypotheses used ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_4_of_nonempty⟧: if the footprints of $F$ cover $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HasCoveringUniformizers⟧ and $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for the objects $\alpha$ of $A$, the two definitions agree. Both are equivalent to the condition on all uniformizers ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubpolyfold_iff_forall_unif⟧: $A$ is saturated and $A_\Psi$ is a sub-M-polyfold for every uniformizer $\Psi$ of $(F, M)$.

*The standing reading.* The direction $17.3.1 \Rightarrow 17.3.2$ needs, at a point $a$ of a model with $\Gamma(a) \in A$, a uniformizer at $\Gamma(a)$ to which Definition 17.3.1 applies; the uniformizer $\Psi_\lambda$ of the covering family whose domain contains $a$ need not lie in any $F(\alpha)$ with $\alpha \in A$. We read the proposition with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, the standing reading of §17.1 (point G.196), the hypothesis named `hS`. The witness is every structure with uniformizers, e.g. the reflection polyfold ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧; it is not weak, the reflection having non-trivial isotropy at $0$.

**Proof of $17.3.2 \Rightarrow 17.3.1$** (no hypothesis) ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubpolyfold_of_isSubpolyfoldModel⟧. *Step 1.* By the preimage in a model (previous section, Step 3), every $A_{\Psi_\lambda}$ of the covering family is a sub-M-polyfold. *Step 2.* By the covering-family transfer, $A_\Psi$ is a sub-M-polyfold for every uniformizer $\Psi$ of $(F, M)$, in particular for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ with $\alpha \in A$. Saturation is part of both definitions. This is the text's argument with $(a', \phi', x') \in M(\Psi', \Psi)$.

**Proof of $17.3.1 \Rightarrow 17.3.2$.** *Step 1, a covering family.* Footprints covering $|C|$ give a covering family ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.exists_coveringFamily_iff⟧, and $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for all $\alpha$ gives footprints covering $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.hasCoveringUniformizers_of_hasUniformizers⟧. *Step 2, every member.* By the second transfer of the previous section, Definition 17.3.1 with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ on $A$ makes $A_{\Psi_\lambda}$ a sub-M-polyfold for every member $\Psi_\lambda$; the transition element used at $a$ is $(a, 1, q) \in M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi)$ with $\Psi \in F(\Psi_\lambda(a))$, $\Psi(q) = \Psi_\lambda(a)$. This is the text's local argument of the other direction, run in the reverse direction. *Step 3.* By the preimage in a model, $\Gamma_\Psi^{-1}(A)$ is an ep-subgroupoid. The form over all uniformizers is the second transfer together with the fact that every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ is a uniformizer of $(F, M)$.

**Why the reading is needed** ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_4_empty_family⟧. There are a polyfold structure on $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ whose footprints cover $|C|$ and a set $A$ satisfying Definition 17.3.1 but not 17.3.2.

*Step 1, smaller uniformizer sets.* For sets $F'(\alpha) \subset F(\alpha)$, $(F', M)$ is again a polyfold structure with the same transition M-polyfolds ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.restrictF⟧ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unifOfSubset⟧.

*Step 2, the structure.* Take $F'(x) = F(x)$ for irrational $x$ and $F'(x) = \emptyset$ for rational $x$ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.irrationalF⟧ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.irrationalConstruction⟧. The tautological uniformizer lies in $F'(\sqrt 2)$ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.irrationalUnif⟧ and its footprint is all of $|C|$, so the footprints of $F'$ cover $|C|$.

*Step 3, Definition 17.3.1 holds vacuously* for $A$ the rational points: $A$ is saturated, and there is no $\Psi \in F'(\alpha)$ with $\alpha \in A$.

*Step 4, Definition 17.3.2 does not hold.* If $\Gamma_\Psi^{-1}(A)$ were an ep-subgroupoid of some model, the covering-family transfer would make $A$ itself, the preimage under the tautological uniformizer, a sub-M-polyfold of $\mathbb R$, which it is not (dense with dense complement).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves the two directions by the same local argument and does not mention $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$; the formalization makes the hypothesis explicit, shows by the example why it is needed, and records that the direction $17.3.2 \Rightarrow 17.3.1$ needs nothing. The form under footprints covering $|C|$ and $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ on $A$ only is the sharpest; the form over all uniformizers is the one Proposition 17.3.6 uses.

### XVII.32 The restricted uniformizers, the level-wise embeddings, local faces and Lemma 17.3.5 (2017: §17.3, Lemma 17.6), pp. 785–786

**Statement** (same). *The GCT $A$* (pp. 785–786) ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.sub⟧: for a set $A$ of objects, the full subcategory on $A$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.fullSubGroupoidal⟧ ⟦Chapter7/Groupoid · GroupoidStr.fullSub⟧ with $|A|$ carrying the topology induced by the injective map $|A| \to |C|$; then $|A| \to |C|$ is a topological embedding ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.isEmbedding_subOrbitMap⟧. *The left vertical map* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GroupoidStr.isEmbedding_orbitMap_translationSub⟧: for a $G$-invariant $B \subset O$, $G$ acting by homeomorphisms, $|G \ltimes B| \to |G \ltimes O|$ is a topological embedding. *The restricted uniformizer* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.LocalUniformizer.restrict⟧: for saturated $A$ and a local uniformizer $\Psi$ with $A_\Psi$ a sub-M-polyfold, $\Psi_A : G \ltimes A_\Psi \to A$ is a local uniformizer of the GCT $A$; $|\Psi_A|$ is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of $|A|$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.LocalUniformizer.isOpenEmbedding_orbitMap_restrictFunctor⟧. *Lemma 17.3.5* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_3_5⟧: for uniformizers $\Psi, \Psi'$ with $A_\Psi, A_{\Psi'}$ sub-M-polyfolds, the set $s^{-1}(A_\Psi) \subset M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.restrictTransSet⟧ is a sub-M-polyfold; it equals $t^{-1}(A_{\Psi'})$; near every element a sheet $s : U \to V \subset O$ maps $U \cap s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$ onto $V \cap A_\Psi$; and the map $M(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A) \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$, $(o, \phi, o') \mapsto (o, \phi, o')$, is injective with image $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$. *Level-wise* (p. 786): for every level $m$, the left vertical map $|G \ltimes (A_\Psi)_m| \to |G \ltimes O_m|$ is a topological embedding ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.LocalUniformizer.isEmbedding_orbitMap_restrict_level⟧, and for a subpolyfold $A$ (with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$) the right vertical map $|A_m| \to |C_m|$ is a topological embedding, $|A_m|$ and $|C_m|$ carrying the topologies of p. 766 for $(F_A, M)$ and $(F, M)$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEmbedding_subRegOrbitMap⟧. *Local faces* (p. 786) ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubpolyfold_of_isFaceIn⟧: for a tame $C$ with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, an open face-structured $V \subset |C|$ and a face $\theta$ of $V$, the full subcategory $\pi^{-1}(\theta)$ is a subpolyfold of $C$; for $V = |C|$ this is Proposition 17.3.8 (§XVII.34).

**Proof of the embeddings.** *Step 1, $|A| \to |C|$.* The map is injective: two objects of $A$ isomorphic in $C$ are isomorphic in the full subcategory, since every morphism between objects of $A$ is a morphism of $A$; with the induced topology it is an embedding by definition, and $|A|$ is metrizable as a subspace of $|C|$. *Step 2, $|G \ltimes B| \to |G \ltimes O|$.* It is continuous, as a map of quotients induced by the inclusion, and injective: if $x, y \in B$ have $g x = y$ in $O$, they have it in $B$. For the topology, an open $W \subset |G \ltimes B|$ has $\pi_B^{-1}(W) = B \cap W_0$ with $W_0$ open in $O$; then $\pi_O(W_0)$ is open, since the orbit map of a translation groupoid is open ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidStr.isOpenMap_π_translation⟧, and $W$ is the preimage of $\pi_O(W_0)$: a point $b \in B$ with $g b' = b$ for some $b' \in W_0$ has $b' = g^{-1} b \in B$, by invariance.

**Proof that $\Psi_A$ is a local uniformizer.** *Step 1, the action.* $A_\Psi$ is $G$-invariant ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.LocalUniformizer.smul_mem_preimage⟧: $\Psi(g, q)$ is a morphism $\Psi(q) \to \Psi(g q)$ and $A$ is saturated. So $G$ acts on $A_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.LocalUniformizer.restrictMulAction⟧, by sc-smooth maps of the sub-M-polyfold structure of $A_\Psi$ (Proposition 2.3.19, Part II) ⟦Chapter2/SubMPolyfold · MPolyfold.subPolyfold⟧. *Step 2, the functor* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.LocalUniformizer.restrictFunctor⟧: $q \mapsto \Psi(q)$, $(g, q) \mapsto \Psi(g, q)$, landing in $A$; it is a functor because composition in the full subcategory is that of $C$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GroupoidStr.fullSub_comp_coe⟧. *Step 3, injective, full, faithful.* Injectivity on objects and faithfulness are those of $\Psi$; fullness: a morphism $\psi : \Psi(x) \to \Psi(x')$ of $A$ is a morphism of $C$, so $\psi = \Psi(g, x)$ with $g x = x'$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.exists_smul_eq_mor⟧, and $(g, x)$ is a morphism of $G \ltimes A_\Psi$. *Step 4, the square of p. 786.* The composite $|A| \leftarrow |G \ltimes A_\Psi|$ followed by $|A| \to |C|$ equals $|\Psi| \circ (|G \ltimes A_\Psi| \to |G \ltimes O|)$. The right side is an embedding (Definition 17.1.3 (3) for $\Psi$ and the left vertical map), and $|A| \to |C|$ is an embedding, so $|\Psi_A|$ is an embedding. Its image is the preimage of the footprint of $\Psi$ in $|A|$: an object $a \in A$ with $\Psi(q) \cong a$ has $\Psi(q) \in A$ by saturation. The footprint is open, so the image is open.

**Proof of Lemma 17.3.5.** *Step 1, the sub-M-polyfold* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubMPolyfold_restrictTransSet⟧: $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism and $A_\Psi$ a sub-M-polyfold, so $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$ is one (the preimage fact of the first section). *Step 2, source and target* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.mem_restrictTransSet_iff_tgt⟧: $(o, \phi, o')$ has $\phi : \Psi(o) \to \Psi'(o')$, so $\Psi(o) \in A \iff \Psi'(o') \in A$ by saturation. *Step 3, the sheets.* For an element $b$, take the sheet $s : U \to V$ given by the local sc-diffeomorphism $s$; its inverse $g$ maps $V \cap A_\Psi$ into $U \cap s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$ because $s(g(y)) = y$, so $s(U \cap s^{-1}(A_\Psi)) = V \cap A_\Psi$. *Step 4, the inclusion* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.restrictTransEquiv⟧: $(o, \phi, o') \mapsto (o, \phi, o')$ is a bijection of $M(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A)$ onto $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$, the inverse sending $(o, \phi, o')$ with $\Psi(o) \in A$ to itself, read in $A_\Psi$, $A$, $A_{\Psi'}$ (Step 2 gives $o' \in A_{\Psi'}$).

**Proof of the level-wise statement, the left vertical map.** *Step 1, the levels of a sub-M-polyfold* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.mem_subPolyfold_levelSet_iff⟧: a chart of the induced structure on $A$ (Proposition 2.3.19) is a chart of $X$ restricted to $W \cap A$, with the same sc-Banach space, so $A_m = A \cap X_m$. *Step 2, $A_m \to X_m$ is an embedding* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.isEmbedding_subLevelIncl⟧: the inclusion is continuous on level $m$ because $A \to X$ is sc-smooth, and injective. Near a point $a$, a retraction $r$ onto $A$ on a neighbourhood $V$ of $a$ (Definition 2.3.18) gives the sc-smooth map $i^{-1} \circ r$ (Proposition 2.3.19 (2)), continuous $V_m \to A_m$, which is a left inverse of the inclusion on $A \cap V$; so the inclusion induces the topology of $A_m$ near $a$. Its range is $\{x \in X_m \mid x \in A\}$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.range_subLevelIncl⟧. *Step 3, the actions on the levels.* A group acting by sc-smooth maps preserves the levels and acts on $X_m$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.levelMulAction⟧ by homeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.continuous_levelSmul⟧; on a $G$-invariant $A$ it acts by sc-smooth maps of the induced structure ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.subSmul_isScSmooth⟧, and $A_m \to X_m$ is equivariant ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.subLevelIncl_smul⟧. *Step 4, orbit spaces* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GroupoidStr.isEmbedding_orbitMap_translationMap⟧: for an equivariant topological embedding $j : B \to O$, $G$ acting on $O$ by homeomorphisms, the map $|G \ltimes B| \to |G \ltimes O|$ of the functor $(g, b) \mapsto (g, j(b))$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GroupoidStr.translationMapFunctor⟧ is continuous, as a map of quotients, and injective: $g\,j(x) = j(y)$ gives $j(gx) = j(y)$, so $gx = y$. For $W$ open in $|G \ltimes B|$, $\pi_B^{-1}(W) = j^{-1}(W_0)$ with $W_0$ open in $O$, and $W$ is the preimage of $\pi_O(W_0)$, which is open ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidStr.isOpenMap_π_translation⟧: if $g\,o = j(b)$ with $o \in W_0$, then $j(g^{-1} b) = o$, so $g^{-1} b \in \pi_B^{-1}(W)$, and $g^{-1} b$ and $b$ have the same class. *Step 5* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.isEmbedding_orbitMap_levelSub⟧: Step 4 for $j = (A_m \to X_m)$, by Steps 2 and 3. For $\Psi_A$ it is the case $X = O$, $A = A_\Psi$, invariant because $A$ is saturated ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.LocalUniformizer.smul_mem_preimage⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.LocalUniformizer.isEmbedding_orbitMap_restrict_level⟧. *Witness* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.isEmbedding_orbitMap_levelSub_univ⟧: $A = X$, for any group acting by sc-smooth maps; the hypotheses are the text's.

**Proof of the level-wise statement, the right vertical map.** *Step 1, regularity in $A$* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasRegularity_sub_iff⟧: an object $\alpha$ of $A$ has regularity $m$ for $(F_A, M)$ if and only if it has it for $(F, M)$. A uniformizer of $F_A$ at $\alpha$ is $\Psi_A$ with $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, and a point of $A_\Psi$ is in $(A_\Psi)_m$ exactly when it is in $O_m$ (Step 1 above); conversely, $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ gives $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ with a point $q$ over $\alpha$, and $q \in A_\Psi$. So the inclusion induces $|A_m| \to |C_m|$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subRegOrbitMap⟧, injective because both are full subcategories ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_subRegOrbitMap⟧; $F_A(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ follows from $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasUniformizers_subConstruction⟧. *Step 2, a local criterion* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · isEmbedding_of_forall_local⟧: an injective map is an embedding if every point has a neighbourhood $U$ such that $e^{-1}(W') \cap U$ is open for open $W'$ and every $W \cap U$, $W$ open, is some $e^{-1}(W')$, $W'$ open. Such a $U$ is the range of an open embedding $f$ when $e \circ f = g \circ J$ for an open embedding $g$ and an embedding $J$, and every point of the range of $g$ in the range of $e$ comes from the range of $J$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · local_of_charts⟧. *Step 3, reduction to charts* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEmbedding_subRegOrbitMap_of_charts⟧: suppose $|A_m|$ and $|C_m|$ carry topologies for which the maps $|\Psi_m| : |G \ltimes O_m| \to |C_m|$ of the functors $\Psi$ on level $m$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.levelFunctor⟧ and the maps $|L_m|$ for $L \in F_A$ are open embeddings. At $|a|$, take $\Psi \in F(a)$ and $L = \Psi_A$; then $f = |L_m|$, $g = |\Psi_m|$ and $J$ the left vertical map on level $m$ (an embedding by the previous proof) satisfy Step 2: the square commutes on representatives, and if $|\Psi(x)|$, $x \in O_m$, lies in the image of $|A_m|$, then $\Psi(x)$ is isomorphic to an object of $A$, so it lies in $A$ by saturation, and $x$ is in the range of $(A_\Psi)_m \to O_m$. *Step 4, the charts of $|C_m|$* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.isOpenEmbedding_levelFunctor⟧: the topology of $|C_m|$ (p. 766) is transported from the GCT $C^m = (C^{m-1})^1$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelTopology⟧. For $\alpha \in C_m$ and $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ there is a lift ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.LevelLift⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelLift⟧, built by induction on $m$: $\Psi$ itself for $m = 0$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelLiftZero⟧, and from $m$ to $m + 1$ the lifted uniformizer $L^1$ of Theorem 17.1.28 ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelLiftSucc⟧, with $(X^1)_j \cong X_{j+1}$ ⟦Chapter2/TangentLift · MPolyfold.shiftLevelHomeomorph⟧. The lift is a uniformizer $L$ of $C^m$ over $\alpha$ with a map $O_L \to O$, a group isomorphism $G_L \cong G$, equivariance, $\Psi$ on the image, and $(O_L)_j \cong O_{j+m}$ for every $j$. With $X \cong X_0$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · MPolyfold.levelZeroHomeomorph⟧ this gives an equivariant homeomorphism $O_L \cong O_m$ along $G_L \cong G$, hence $|G_L \ltimes O_L| \cong |G \ltimes O_m|$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GroupoidStr.translationHomeomorph⟧, and $|\Psi_m|$ is $|L|$ between this homeomorphism and $|C^m| \cong |C_m|$ ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.UniformizedPolyfold.levelOrbitHomeomorph⟧. The map $|L|$ is an open embedding by Definition 17.1.3 for $C^m$, so $|\Psi_m|$ is one. *Step 5* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEmbedding_subRegOrbitMap⟧: Step 3 with the charts of Step 4, for $C$ with $(F, M)$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.uniformized⟧ and for $A$ with $(F_A, M)$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subUniformized⟧. For $m = 1$ the topologies are those of Theorem 17.1.28 and the charts the maps $|\Psi^1|$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isEmbedding_subRegOrbitMap_one⟧. *Witness* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isEmbedding_subRegOrbitMap⟧: $A = \{0\}$ in $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, every $m$, $|A_m|$ non-empty.

**Proof that local faces are subpolyfolds** ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSubpolyfold_of_isFaceIn⟧. *Step 1, open subsets.* For an open $U \subset X$ with its sub-M-polyfold structure, $x \mapsto x$ is an sc-diffeomorphism $U \to U$, inverse to the inclusion ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · MPolyfold.isScDiffeoOn_openIncl_symm⟧; so the degeneracy index of $U$ is that of $X$ (Proposition 2.4.4) ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · MPolyfold.degIndex_subPolyfold_open⟧, and a sub-M-polyfold of $U$ contained in $U$ is a sub-M-polyfold of $X$ ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · MPolyfold.isSubMPolyfold_of_subPolyfold_open⟧. *Step 2, the polyfold $C_V$* ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.openSubConstruction⟧: the full subcategory over the open $V$, with the uniformizers $\Psi$ restricted to the open $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}(V)) \subset O$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.LocalUniformizer.restrictOver⟧; it is tame ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_openSubConstruction⟧ with $F_V(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.hasUniformizers_openSubConstruction⟧. By Step 1 its degeneracy index is that of $C$ ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_openSub⟧, so $|C_V| \to |C|$, an open embedding onto $V$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.isOpenEmbedding_subOrbitMap_objOver⟧, keeps $d_{|\cdot|}$ ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndexVia_subOrbitMap⟧ and is a $d$-homeomorphism onto $V$ ⟦Chapter7/TameBoundaryEp · TameBoundary.IsDHomeoOn⟧. *Step 3, faces in $C_V$.* The face $\theta$ of $V$ is the image of a face $\theta_V$ of $|C_V|$ ⟦Chapter7/TameBoundaryEp · TameBoundary.IsDHomeoOn.exists_isFaceIn_of_image⟧, and $C_V$ is face-structured, since a $d$-homeomorphism keeps the number of faces through a point and $V$ is face-structured ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isFaceStructuredVia_iff_isFaceStructuredOpen⟧. Proposition 17.3.8 for $C_V$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_8⟧ makes $\pi^{-1}(\theta_V)$ a subpolyfold of $C_V$. *Step 4, back to $C$.* $\pi^{-1}(\theta)$ is saturated. For $\alpha$ over $\theta$ and $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, $\alpha$ lies over $V$, the restriction of $\Psi$ is in $F_V(\alpha)$, and $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}(\theta))$ is the preimage of $\pi^{-1}(\theta_V)$ under it, a sub-M-polyfold of the open $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}(V))$ contained in it; by Step 1 it is a sub-M-polyfold of $O$. *Witness* ⟦Chapter18/SubpolyfoldLocalFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrant_isSubpolyfold_of_isFaceIn⟧: the quadrant polyfold $[0, \infty)^2 \oplus W$, a face through the corner; weak: $V = |C|$, the case of Proposition 17.3.8, with the trivial group.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* None of substance. The text's "clearly" for the uniformizer properties of $\Psi_A$ is Steps 1–4; "even true level-wise" is read with the level topologies of Definition 2.3.8 on $(A_\Psi)_m$ and $O_m$ and with the topologies of p. 766 on $|A_m|$ and $|C_m|$, and "local face" as a face of an open face-structured subset of $|C|$, in a tame $C$ with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$.

### XVII.33 Proposition 17.3.6 (2017: Proposition 17.6): the induced polyfold structure $(F_A, M)$, p. 786

**Statement** (restated). ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_6⟧: for a subpolyfold $A$ of $C$ (Definition 17.3.1), the full subcategory $A$, as the GCT of the previous section, carries a polyfold structure $(F_A, M)$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subConstruction⟧ with (1) $F_A(\alpha) = \{\Psi_A : \Psi \in F(\alpha)\}$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.mem_subF_iff⟧; (2) every uniformizer $L$ of $(F_A, M)$ is $\Psi_A$ for a chosen uniformizer $\Psi$ of $(F, M)$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subData⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subData_eq⟧, and for two such $L = \Psi_A$, $L' = \Psi'_A$ the map $(o, \phi, o') \mapsto (o, \phi, o')$ from $M_{F_A}(L, L')$ to $s^{-1}(A_\Psi) \subset M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is bijective, a local sc-diffeomorphism for the sub-M-polyfold structure of $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$, sc-smooth into $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, and keeps the morphism and the objects. Witness ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_subConstruction⟧: for $A = \{0\}$ in $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $F_A(0)$ contains a restricted uniformizer with the non-trivial group $\mathbb Z/2$ and a non-empty domain.

*Reading.* The text does not say, when two uniformizers $\Psi_1 \ne \Psi_2$ of $F$ have the same restriction, which ambient $M(\Psi_i, \Psi')$ gives $M(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A)$ its structure. One is chosen, and every statement holds for it; that the structure does not depend on the choice is not claimed by the text and not formalized. The identification with $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$ cannot hold for every pair of uniformizers with the same restriction (below).

**Proof.** *Step 1, $F_A$* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subF⟧: $F_A(\alpha)$ is the set of restrictions $\Psi_A$, $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, with $A_\Psi$ a sub-M-polyfold; for a subpolyfold this is every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, which is (1). Each $\Psi_A$ is a uniformizer at $\alpha$, since $\Psi$ is ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.LocalUniformizer.restrict_isAt⟧.

*Step 2, the transition M-polyfolds* ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.RTr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rtrHomeomorph⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rtrPolyfold⟧: $M(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A)$ receives the topology that makes the bijection of Lemma 17.3.5 a homeomorphism onto $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$, and the M-polyfold structure of the sub-M-polyfold $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$ transported along it. It is Hausdorff and paracompact as a closed subspace of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ would be: the homeomorphism is a closed embedding onto a sub-M-polyfold, which is paracompact.

*Step 3, Definition 17.1.8 for the restrictions.* (a) $s, t : M(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A) \to A_\Psi, A_{\Psi'}$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rtr_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rtr_isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧: the restriction of the local sc-diffeomorphism $s$ to $s^{-1}(A_\Psi) \to A_\Psi$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism of sub-M-polyfolds, composed with the transport of Step 2. (b) The unit map and the inversion are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rtr_isScSmoothMap_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rtr_isScSmoothMap_inv⟧, as restrictions of the ambient ones to sub-M-polyfolds that they map into each other. (c) The multiplication $M(\Psi'_A, \Psi''_A) {}_s\times_t M(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A) \to M(\Psi_A, \Psi''_A)$ is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.rtr_isScSmoothMap_mul⟧: the fibred product of the restricted sets maps into the ambient fibred product by an sc-smooth map (its two components are inclusions of sub-M-polyfolds composed with the transports), the ambient multiplication is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trMul⟧, its values lie in $s^{-1}(A_\Psi)$, and the composite agrees with the multiplication of $M(\Psi_A, \Psi''_A)$ because composition in $A$ is that of $C$.

*Step 4, assembling.* For uniformizers $L, L'$ of $(F_A, M)$ the structure of Steps 2–3 is taken at the chosen $\Psi = {}$ `subData` $L$, $\Psi' = {}$ `subData` $L'$ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subMOf⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subSrcOf⟧ ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subMulOf⟧. This gives the polyfold structure, and (2) is read off ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.subInclOf_spec⟧: the inclusion is the bijection of Lemma 17.3.5, a local sc-diffeomorphism by construction, sc-smooth into $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ after composing with the inclusion of the sub-M-polyfold.

*Step 5, the witness.* $\{0\}$ is a subpolyfold (first section); the restriction of the tautological uniformizer lies in $F_A(0)$, its group is $\mathbb Z/2$ (in Lean the units of $\mathbb Z$, with $-1 \ne 1$) and its domain is $\{0\}$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text says the structure is "natural" and that the structure maps are restrictions of the ambient ones; the formalization makes the dependence on the ambient uniformizer explicit through the choice, and proves each axiom of Definition 17.1.8 by restriction.

**For every pair of uniformizers** (does not hold as we read it; Findings, Chapter 17, item 16) ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerSub · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_6_not_every_pair⟧. The text identifies $M(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A)$ with $s^{-1}(A_\Psi) \subset M(\Psi, \Psi')$, which presupposes that this sub-M-polyfold depends only on $\Psi_A$ and $\Psi'_A$. As we read Definition 17.1.8, two uniformizers that agree on $A_\Psi$ may carry transition structures that differ over $A$. *Statement.* There are a GCT with a polyfold structure, $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$, a subpolyfold $A$, and uniformizers $\Psi \ne \Psi'$ in $F(\alpha)$ for some $\alpha \in A$, with $\Psi_A = \Psi'_A$, such that for no polyfold structure $S_A$ on $A$ containing $\Psi_A$ are both identifications $M_{S_A}(\Psi_A, \Psi_A) \to s^{-1}(A_\Psi) \subset M(\Psi, \Psi)$ and $M_{S_A}(\Psi_A, \Psi'_A) \to s^{-1}(A_\Psi) \subset M(\Psi, \Psi')$ homeomorphisms. *Proof.* The construction of §XVII.18 (the two uniformizers and a gauge) on $C = \mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action and $D = A = (0, \infty)$ ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerSub · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.gaugeSub⟧.
1. $A$ is a subpolyfold ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerSub · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.isSubpolyfold_gaugeSub⟧, and $\Psi_0$, $\Psi_1$ differ off $A$ and have the same restriction ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerSub · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.restrict_false_eq_true⟧.
2. *The key step* ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerSub · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.continuous_of_isHomeomorph_restrictTransEquiv⟧. As in §XVII.18, step 4, with the unit map $A \to s^{-1}(A) \subset M(\Psi_0, \Psi_0)$: if both identifications are homeomorphisms, $q \mapsto c(q)^{-1}$ is continuous on $A$.
3. With $c = 1$ on $(0, 1]$ and $c = g$ on $(1, \infty)$, $c$ is not continuous on $A$ ⟦Chapter17/EveryUniformizerSub · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.EveryUniformizer.not_continuous_stepGauge⟧. $\square$

For the representative chosen by $\mathrm{subData}$ the identification holds, which is the restated proposition above.

### XVII.34 Definition 17.3.7 and Proposition 17.3.8 (2017: Definition 17.13, Proposition 17.7): faces of a tame polyfold, pp. 786–787

**Statement** (same, for both). *The degeneracy index of $|C|$* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndexVia⟧: for a covering family $c$, $d_{|C|}(z) = d_{|X_c|}(|\Gamma_c|^{-1}(z))$, the degeneracy index of the orbit space of the model transported along the homeomorphism $|\Gamma_c|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelOrbitHomeomorph⟧. It does not depend on $c$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndexVia_eq⟧ and is the degeneracy functor $d$ of Definition 17.1.30 on isomorphism classes, $d_{|C|}(|\alpha|) = d(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndexVia_π⟧. *Definition 17.3.7* (p. 786): a face of $C$ is a set $\theta \subset |C|$ that is the closure of a connected component of $\{d_{|C|} = 1\}$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsFaceVia⟧, with the full subcategory $C_\theta$ on $\pi^{-1}(\theta)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.faceSub⟧; $C$ is face-structured if its polyfold structure is tame and every $z \in |C|$ lies in exactly $d_{|C|}(z)$ faces ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsFaceStructuredVia⟧. *The correspondence* (pp. 786–787): $\theta$ is a face of $C$ iff $|\Gamma_{c'}|^{-1}(\theta)$ is a face of the ep-groupoid $X_{c'}$ (Definition 11.1.3), for any two covering families ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isFaceVia_iff_isOrbitFace⟧; for tame $C$, $C$ is face-structured iff $X_{c'}$ is ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isFaceStructuredVia_iff⟧. *Proposition 17.3.8* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_8⟧: if $C$ is face-structured and $\theta$ a face, $\pi^{-1}(\theta)$ satisfies Definition 17.3.2 and Definition 17.3.1.

**Proof that $d_{|C|}$ does not depend on the family.** *Step 1.* $|\Gamma_c|$ preserves the index by definition ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndexVia_orbitMap⟧. *Step 2.* For two families $c, c'$ the comparison $X_c \to X_{c'}$ of Theorem 17.1.18 ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.comparison⟧ is a generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_comparison⟧ with $|\Gamma_{c'}| \circ |f_{c,c'}| = |\Gamma_c|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.orbitMap_comparison⟧. *Step 3.* Generalized isomorphisms preserve the degeneracy index of orbit spaces (Part XI) ⟦Chapter11/EpEquivalenceGeometry · EPLocalization.EPLoc.degIndexOrbit_orbitMap⟧. The chain $d_c(|\Gamma_c| w) = d_{X_c}(w) = d_{X_{c'}}(|f| w) = d_{c'}(|\Gamma_{c'}| |f| w) = d_{c'}(|\Gamma_c| w)$ gives the claim, $|\Gamma_c|$ being surjective.

**Proof that $d_{|C|}$ is the index of Definition 17.1.30.** *Step 1.* For a member $\Psi_i$ of $c$ and $q$ in its domain, $d_{|C|}(|\Psi_i(q)|) = d_{O_i}(q)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndexVia_π_obj⟧: the orbit of $(i, q)$ in $X_c$ maps to $|\Psi_i(q)|$, and the index of a disjoint union is that of the summand. *Step 2.* For an arbitrary uniformizer $\Psi$, enlarge $c$ to the covering family $c \sqcup \{\Psi\}$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.CoveringFamily.extend⟧ and use independence of the family ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndexVia_π_obj_of_unif⟧. *Step 3.* For an object $\alpha$, pick $\Psi_i$ and $q$ with $|\Psi_i(q)| = |\alpha|$; Definition 17.1.30 defines $d(\alpha) = d_{O_i}(q)$ for any such choice ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex⟧, and Step 1 concludes.

**Proof of the correspondence.** *Step 1, independence of the family.* The face notions use $d_{|C|}$ only, so they do not depend on $c$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isFaceVia_congr⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isFaceStructuredVia_congr⟧. *Step 2, Part II's language.* A face of $C$ is a face in the sense of Part II's `IsFaceIn` for $d_{|C|}$ on the whole space ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isFaceVia_iff_isFaceIn⟧ ⟦Chapter2/TameBoundary · TameBoundary.IsFaceIn⟧, and face-structured is Part II's face-structured open set $|C|$ with tameness ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isFaceStructuredVia_iff_isFaceStructuredOpen⟧. The same holds for an ep-groupoid ⟦Chapter11/EpEquivalenceGeometryFaces · EpGroupoid.isOrbitFace_iff_isFaceIn⟧ ⟦Chapter11/EpEquivalenceGeometryFaces · EpGroupoid.isEpFaceStructured_iff⟧. *Step 3, transport.* $|\Gamma_{c'}|$ is a homeomorphism preserving the index, and Part II's face notions are carried in both directions by such a map; so faces of $|C|$ and of $|X_{c'}|$ correspond, and face structures too. *Step 4, tameness.* If $C$ is tame (every uniformizer of $F$ tame), the model is tame ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_modelEpGroupoid⟧; the converse is not needed, since the tameness of $C$ is assumed in the statement, as in the text.

**Proof of Proposition 17.3.8.** *Step 1.* $\pi^{-1}(\theta)$ is saturated ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isSaturated_π_preimage⟧. *Step 2.* The model $X_c$ is face-structured (the correspondence), and $|\Gamma_c|^{-1}(\theta)$ is a face of it. *Step 3.* In a face-structured ep-groupoid, the full subgroupoid over a face is an ep-subgroupoid (Part XI) ⟦Chapter11/FaceEpGroupoids · EpGroupoid.isEpSubgroupoid_faceObj⟧; its object set is $\Gamma_c^{-1}(\pi^{-1}(\theta))$. This is Definition 17.3.2. *Step 4.* Definition 17.3.1 follows from the direction $17.3.2 \Rightarrow 17.3.1$ of Proposition 17.3.4, which needs no hypothesis.

**The witness: the quadrant** ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrant_proposition_17_3_8⟧. *Step 1, the structure.* The translation GCT of the trivial group on $[0, \infty)^2 \oplus W$, with $F(x)$ the tautological uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrantConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrantFamily⟧. *Step 2.* The orbit map is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrant_isHomeomorph_π⟧ and $d_{|C|}$ is the index of the quadrant ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrant_degIndexVia⟧. *Step 3.* The quadrant is tame and face-structured (Part II) ⟦Chapter2/TameBoundaryExample · TameBoundaryExample.isFaceStructuredOpen_quadrant⟧, so $C$ is; the corner has $d = 2$, lies in exactly two faces, and a face through it is a subpolyfold in both senses, by Proposition 17.3.8. The witness is weak in its group (trivial), not in its boundary (a corner of index $2$).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text derives the correspondence of faces from "the discussion after Definition 11.1.3"; the formalization proves it through the invariance of the degeneracy index under generalized isomorphisms (Part XI) and the transport of Part II's face notions along an index-preserving homeomorphism (point G.212). $d_{|C|}$ is defined through a model and shown to be the degeneracy functor of Definition 17.1.30, which the text identifies by its wording "the induced degeneracy index". Proposition 17.3.8 is proved in both senses; the sense of Definition 17.3.1 needs no $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ here.

### XVII.35 Good open neighbourhoods (17.53)–(17.55) and Definition 17.4.1 (new in the 2021 edition): the faces $F_{U(\bar o)}$, pp. 787–788

From here on $C$ carries a tame polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsTame⟧ (hypothesis `hT`), so the domain $O$ of every uniformizer is a tame M-polyfold ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_unif⟧, and Part II's tame boundary $bO$ is available: its points over $x \in \partial O$ are the face germs $[x, V, \theta]$ at $x$, two being equal iff the faces agree near $x$ (Definition 2.7.10) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bO⟧ ⟦Chapter2/TameBoundary · TameBoundary.BSpace⟧.

**Statement** (same). *Good open neighbourhoods* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd⟧: for a uniformizer $\Psi$ and $\bar o \in O$, a good open neighbourhood is a natural neighbourhood $V(\bar o)$ of $\bar o$ in the ep-groupoid $G \ltimes O$ with the properties (17.53) of Definition 14.1.2 ⟦Chapter14/GoodBoundary · EpGroupoid.IsGoodBoundaryNbhd⟧; $U(\bar o) = V(\bar o) \cap \partial O$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.bdry⟧. *Definition 17.4.1* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faces⟧: $F_{U(\bar o)}$ is the set of faces of the open set $V(\bar o)$ in the sense of Definition 2.7.6. With the remarks of p. 787 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_4_1⟧: every face contains $\bar o$ and lies in $U(\bar o)$; their union is $U(\bar o)$ (17.55); there are $d_O(\bar o)$ of them; and they depend only on $U(\bar o)$, not on $V(\bar o)$. *Existence* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_goodNbhd⟧: for tame $O$ and $\bar o \in \partial O$, inside every open neighbourhood of $\bar o$ there is a good one (Proposition 14.1.1).

*Reading.* The faces of $U(\bar o)$ are the $\theta_i$ of (17.53) (4), the faces of the open set $V(\bar o)$ in the sense of Definition 2.7.6.

**Proof.** *Step 1, the face structure.* (17.53) (4) provides a family $\theta_i$ of $d_O(\bar o)$ sets with $d_O(y) = \sharp\{i : y \in \operatorname{cl}\theta_i\}$ on $V(\bar o)$; this makes $V(\bar o)$ a face-structured open set ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.isFaceStructuredOpen⟧ in which every face is one of the $\theta_i$. *Step 2, centred at $\bar o$* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.isCenteredAt⟧ ⟦Chapter2/TameBoundary · TameBoundary.IsCenteredAt⟧: every $\theta_i$ has $\bar o$ in its closure, so every face contains $\bar o$; hence $d_O \le d_O(\bar o)$ on $V(\bar o)$ (17.54) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.degIndex_le⟧, $V(\bar o)$ lies in Part II's set $N_{O,\bar o}$ of Definition 2.7.6 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.mem_faceStructuredNbhdsAt⟧, and the faces are the faces through $\bar o$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faces_eq_facesThrough⟧. *Step 3, (17.55).* $V(\bar o) \cap \partial O$ is the union of the $V(\bar o) \cap \operatorname{cl}\theta_i$, so every face lies in $U(\bar o)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.subset_bdry⟧ and the faces cover it ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.sUnion_faces⟧. *Step 4, the number.* In a face-structured open set the number of faces through a point is its index; at $\bar o$ this is $d_O(\bar o)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.encard_faces⟧. *Step 5, only $U(\bar o)$ matters* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faces_eq_of_bdry_eq⟧. The faces are described intrinsically in $U(\bar o)$: $\theta$ is a face iff $\theta = U(\bar o) \cap \operatorname{cl}(K)$ for a connected component $K$ of $U(\bar o) \cap \{d_O = 1\}$. Indeed $V(\bar o) \cap \{d_O = 1\} = U(\bar o) \cap \{d_O = 1\}$ (index $1$ points are boundary points), and $V(\bar o) \cap \operatorname{cl} K = U(\bar o) \cap \operatorname{cl} K$ because $\partial O$ is closed. Two good neighbourhoods with the same $U(\bar o)$ therefore have the same faces. *Step 6, existence* is Proposition 14.1.1 for the ep-groupoid $G \ltimes O$ ⟦Chapter14/GoodBoundary · EpGroupoid.exists_isGoodBoundaryNbhd⟧.

**Face germs of a good neighbourhood.** A face $\theta \in F_{U(\bar o)}$ gives Part II's admissible triple $(\bar o, V(\bar o), \theta)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.triple⟧ and so a face germ $[\bar o, V(\bar o), \theta] \in bO$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.germ⟧. *Every face germ at $\bar o$ is the germ of a face of $U(\bar o)$* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.exists_germ_eq⟧: a representative $(\bar o, V_0, \theta_0)$ restricts to $V_0 \cap V(\bar o)$, which is again in $N_{O,\bar o}$, giving a face $\theta''$ there ⟦Chapter2/TameBoundary · TameBoundary.exists_face_restrict⟧; its extension to $V(\bar o)$ is a face of $V(\bar o)$ through $\bar o$, and Part II's criterion for equal classes (equal extensions) identifies the three germs. *Distinct faces have distinct germs* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.germ_injective⟧, a face of $V(\bar o)$ being its own extension. So $\theta \mapsto [\bar o, V(\bar o), \theta]$ is a bijection of $F_{U(\bar o)}$ onto the face germs at $\bar o$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* None. The text recalls Definition 14.1.2 and states the properties on p. 787; they are proved from (17.53).

### XVII.36 (17.56)–(17.59) and Definition 17.4.2 (new in the 2021 edition): the objects of $bC$, p. 789

**Statement** (restated). *Transport of face germs* (p. 789) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GermLift⟧: for $h = (\bar o, \phi, \bar o') \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$, a face germ $p$ at $\bar o$ and a face germ $p'$ at $\bar o'$, $h$ carries $p$ to $p'$ if, for an admissible triple of $h$ (open neighbourhoods $W(h)$, $W(\bar o)$, $W'(\bar o')$ on which $s$ and $t$ are sc-diffeomorphisms) ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple⟧, the local sc-diffeomorphism $f = t \circ (s|W(h))^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.germMap⟧ carries $p$ to $p'$ in the sense of Part VII's lifts ⟦Chapter7/TameBoundaryEp · TameBoundary.IsLiftOf⟧. *The tuples* (17.57) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple⟧: $(\alpha, \Psi, U(\bar o), \theta)$ with $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$, a good neighbourhood $U(\bar o)$ and $\theta \in F_{U(\bar o)}$. *The relation* (17.56), (17.58) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.Equiv⟧: $(\alpha, \Psi, U(\bar o), \theta) \sim (\alpha', \Psi', U'(\bar o'), \theta')$ iff $\alpha = \alpha'$ and $h = (\bar o, 1_\alpha, \bar o')$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.idTr⟧ carries the germ of $\theta$ to that of $\theta'$; it is an equivalence relation ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryTupleSetoid⟧. *Definition 17.4.2* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BookObj⟧: the objects of $bC$ are the classes, with the forgetful map (17.59) $[\alpha, \Psi, U(\bar o), \theta] \mapsto \alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BookObj.base⟧. *The model of the objects* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BObj⟧: an object $\alpha$ with a face germ at the point $\bar o_{\mathrm{ref}}$ of a reference uniformizer $\Psi_{\mathrm{ref}} \in F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryRefUnif⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.refPt⟧. ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_4_2⟧: two tuples have the same class iff (17.58), and the map $[t] \mapsto$ (its germ carried to the reference uniformizer) is a bijection ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bookObjEquiv⟧ of the classes onto the model, preserving the map to $C$.

*Readings.* "$\theta$ and $\theta'$ correspond near $\bar o$ and $\bar o'$ under $f$" (the condition $f(\theta \cap W(\bar o)) = \theta' \cap W'(\bar o')$ for suitable small neighbourhoods) is read on face germs, the classes of Part II's $bO$, which record exactly the equality of faces near a point. The classes live one universe up (they range over all uniformizers); $bC$ is built on the model, which needs one uniformizer in every $F(\alpha)$, the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (hypothesis `hS`); `bookObjEquiv` identifies the two.

**The laws of transport.** *Step 1, $f$.* $f$ is an sc-diffeomorphism of $W(\bar o)$ onto $W'(\bar o')$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.AdmissibleTriple.isScDiffeoOn_germMap⟧, hence preserves the degeneracy index and moves face germs. Its germ at $\bar o$ depends only on $h$, not on the admissible triple ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germMap_eventuallyEq⟧: two triples give local inverses of $s$ through $h$ that agree near $\bar o$. *Step 2, independence of the triple* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germLift_iff_forall⟧: since lifts along maps with the same germ agree, "for some triple" and "for every triple" coincide. *Step 3, uniqueness and existence* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germLift_unique⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_germLift⟧: the lift of a germ along an index-preserving local homeomorphism exists and is unique ⟦Chapter7/TameBoundaryEp · TameBoundary.exists_isLiftOf⟧; this is the text's "$\theta'$ is uniquely determined by $\theta$" of p. 790, and defines $f_*$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germPush⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germLift_germPush⟧. *Step 4, unit, inverse, composite.* The germ of $f$ for a unit $(q, 1, q)$ is the identity, by continuity of the unit map $u$ of Definition 17.1.8 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germMap_unit_eventuallyEq⟧; that of $h^{-1}$ is inverse to that of $h$, by continuity of the inversion ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germMap_inv_comp_eventuallyEq⟧; that of a composite is the composite, by continuity of the multiplication ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germMap_comp_eventuallyEq⟧. Hence units preserve germs ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germLift_unit⟧, $h^{-1}$ carries $f_*p$ back to $p$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germLift_inv⟧, and $(h' \circ h)_* = h'_* \circ h_*$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryGerms · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germLift_comp⟧.

**Proof that (17.58) is an equivalence relation.** *Reflexivity* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.equiv_refl⟧: for $t = t'$, $h = (\bar o, 1_\alpha, \bar o)$ is the unit ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.idTr_self⟧, which preserves germs. *Symmetry* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.equiv_symm⟧: the inverse of $(\bar o, 1_\alpha, \bar o')$ is $(\bar o', 1_\alpha, \bar o)$, since $1_\alpha^{-1} = 1_\alpha$; apply the inverse law. *Transitivity* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.equiv_trans⟧: the composite of $(\bar o', 1_\alpha, \bar o'')$ and $(\bar o, 1_\alpha, \bar o')$ is $(\bar o, 1_\alpha, \bar o'')$; apply the composite law.

**Proof of Definition 17.4.2.** *Step 1, the map to the model* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.toBObj⟧: a tuple $t$ goes to $(\alpha, h_*[\bar o, V, \theta])$ with $h = (\bar o, 1_\alpha, \bar o_{\mathrm{ref}}) \in M(\Psi, \Psi_{\mathrm{ref}})$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.toRefTr⟧. *Step 2, the kernel* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.toBObj_eq_iff⟧: $t, t'$ have the same image iff $t \sim t'$. If the images agree, then $\alpha = \alpha'$, and composing the transport of $t$ to the reference with the inverse of that of $t'$ gives a transport by $(\bar o, 1_\alpha, \bar o')$ of the germ of $t$ to that of $t'$ (the morphism is $1_\alpha^{-1} \circ 1_\alpha = 1_\alpha$). Conversely, composing (17.58) with the transport of $t'$ to the reference gives a transport of the germ of $t$ to the image of $t'$, and uniqueness of transport gives equal images. *Step 3, surjectivity* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.toBObj_surjective⟧: an element $(\alpha, p)$ of the model has $p$ a germ at $\bar o_{\mathrm{ref}}$, so $\bar o_{\mathrm{ref}} \in \partial O_{\mathrm{ref}}$; a good neighbourhood exists there, $p$ is the germ of one of its faces $\theta$, and the tuple $(\alpha, \Psi_{\mathrm{ref}}, U, \theta)$ maps to $(\alpha, p)$, its transport being by the unit. *Step 4.* Steps 2–3 make the induced map on classes a bijection; it commutes with the forgetful maps by construction.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text forms the classes directly; the formalization proves that (17.58) is an equivalence relation, which the text asserts, and replaces the classes by an equivalent model with one reference uniformizer per object. This changes no statement about $bC$ but keeps $bC$ in the universe of $C$.

### XVII.37 Definitions 17.4.3 and 17.4.4 (new in the 2021 edition): the morphisms of $bC$ and the forgetful functor, (17.60)–(17.63), pp. 789–791
