*Step 5, (i).* Every object of $bC$ is the class of a tuple $t = (\alpha, \Psi, U(\bar o), \theta)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.BdryTuple.toBObj_surjective⟧ with $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$; centring gives that $G$ fixes $\bar o$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsCentred.fixesPoint⟧, so $t$ is data $d$, and $\Psi_\theta(\bar o)$ is the object of $t$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTopology · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceObj_center⟧. So $\Psi_\theta \in F_{bC}$ of that object.

*Step 6, (ii)* holds by the definition of $F_{bC}$.

*Step 7, (iii).* The map $\iota$ for the chosen data is $(x, \Phi, x') \mapsto (x, \varphi, x')$, the identification (17.74) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.ftrHomeomorph⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ftrInclOf⟧. It is bijective and, by the definition of the structure in Definition 17.4.14, a local sc-diffeomorphism onto the sub-M-polyfold $M(\Psi, \Psi')^*_{\theta,\theta'}$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.isSubMPolyfold_transFaceSet⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.ftr_isLocalScDiffeo_homeomorph⟧; composed with the sc-smooth inclusion of the sub-M-polyfold it is sc-smooth into $M(\Psi, \Psi')$; and it keeps $\varphi$, the objects $\Psi(x) = B(\Psi_\theta(x))$ and $\Psi'(x') = B(\Psi'_{\theta'}(x'))$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ftrInclOf_spec⟧.

**Proof of tameness.** *Step 1, the objects.* Each face $\theta$, with its structure as a sub-M-polyfold of $O$, has a tame induced structure in the sense of Definition 2.5.18 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTame · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.hasTameInducedStructure_face⟧: a face of a face-structured open subset of a tame M-polyfold has one (Theorem 2.5.17 on the open set) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTame · MPolyfold.hasTameInducedStructure_of_isFaceIn⟧. So the object M-polyfold of every $\Psi_\theta$ is tame ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTame · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.isTame_faceSubPolyfold⟧, which is the tameness of $(F_{bC}, M)$ (Definition 17.1.5). *Step 2, the transition M-polyfolds.* The source map $M(L, L') \to \theta$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism onto a tame M-polyfold, so $M(L, L')$ is tame ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_trSrc⟧. The same holds through $\iota$ and the tame induced structure of $M(\Psi, \Psi')^*_{\theta,\theta'}$ of Lemma 17.4.13 as we read it ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTame · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.hasTameInducedStructure_transFaceSet⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTame · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.isTame_ftrPolyfold⟧, with faces in the sense of Definition 2.5.18 (Findings, Chapter 17, item 6). These are "the tame M-polyfolds above" of p. 811.

**The witness: the swap polyfold.** Let $W$ be an sc-Banach space and $C = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes ([0,\infty)^2 \oplus W)$ the translation GCT in which $-1$ exchanges the two coordinates of $[0,\infty)^2$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.coord_neg_one_smul⟧.

*Step 1, the slices.* Three slices of the overlapping-uniformizer construction ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapSlices⟧: all of $[0,\infty)^2 \oplus W$ with the group $\mathbb Z/2$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapSlice₀⟧, and the open sets $\{p_0 < p_1\}$, $\{p_1 < p_0\}$ with the trivial group ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapSliceLt⟧. They are overlapping ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapSlices_overlapping⟧, so the slice construction is a polyfold structure ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction⟧.

*Step 2, the uniformizers.* $F(x)$ consists of the slice uniformizers whose slice contains $x$ and whose group fixes $x$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapF⟧: on the diagonal $p_0 = p_1$ the inclusion of $[0,\infty)^2 \oplus W$ with the group $\mathbb Z/2$, off it the inclusion of the half containing $x$ with the trivial group. The construction is the restriction of the slice construction to these sets ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.restrictF⟧.

*Step 3, the hypotheses.* It is tame, the quadrant being tame ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapConstruction_isTame⟧; $F(x) \ne \emptyset$, by the trichotomy $p_0 < p_1$, $p_0 = p_1$, $p_1 < p_0$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧; $\hat\Psi$ is continuous for every slice construction ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction_continuous_hat⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapConstruction_hatContinuous⟧; and every uniformizer is centred, its group fixing the point ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapConstruction_isCentred⟧.

*Step 4, the corner* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swap_tameBoundary_witness⟧. The corner uniformizer $\Psi_0$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapUnif₀⟧ lies in $F(0)$, fixes the corner, and $-1$ moves the point $(1, 0)$, so the group acts non-trivially. The degeneracy index of the corner is $2$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.degIndex_swapUnif₀⟧, so a good neighbourhood of the corner has two faces. Every face of an open subset lies in a coordinate face $\{p_k = 0\}$ and meets the stratum where only $p_k$ vanishes ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_part_of_isFaceIn⟧; $-1$ sends that stratum to the other one, so $-1$ moves every face ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapGen_smul_ne⟧. Hence $-1$ exchanges the two faces $\theta \ne \theta'$, each $G_\theta$ is trivial, and by Lemma 17.4.8 the two face uniformizers have the same image $|\Psi_\theta(\theta)| = |\Psi_{\theta'}(\theta')|$ in $|bC|$.

*Step 5, the theorem holds for it* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swap_theorem_17_4_26⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTame · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swap_theorem_17_4_26_tame⟧: $(F_{bC}, M)$ has a uniformizer at every object and is tame with tame transition M-polyfolds.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text states Theorem 17.4.26 as the summary of the construction (point G.215); the formalization states what the summary contains (uniformizers at every object, the description of $F_{bC}$, the identification of the transition sets with $M(\Psi, \Psi')^*_{\theta,\theta'}$) and adds the tameness that p. 811 uses. The transition construction is attached to a choice of data per uniformizer, where the text speaks of "the" construction $M$ of Theorem 17.4.16. The verdict is "narrower" through the hypotheses $\hat\Psi$ continuous and centred uniformizers.

### XVII.48 The diagrams (17.82)–(17.84) (new in the 2021 edition), pp. 809–811: $\Theta$, $\hat\Psi : \Theta \to bC$ and $F : \Theta \to G \ltimes O$

**Statement** (narrower). Choose $\Psi$, $\bar o$ with $G$ fixing $\bar o$, and a good neighbourhood $U(\bar o) = V(\bar o) \cap \partial O$. *(17.84)* (p. 811) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.display_17_84⟧: for faces $\theta$ of $U(\bar o)$ and $\theta'$ of $U'(\bar o')$, the map $M(\Psi_\theta, \Psi'_{\theta'}) \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$, $(x, \Phi, x') \mapsto (x, \varphi, x')$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceTrToTr⟧, is injective with image $M(\Psi, \Psi')^*_{\theta,\theta'}$, a local sc-diffeomorphism onto it, sc-smooth into $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, and compatible with $s$, $t$ and $\Phi \mapsto \varphi$. *(17.82)* (p. 810) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.display_17_82⟧: the inclusion $\theta \to O$ is sc-smooth, $B(\Psi_\theta(x)) = \Psi(x)$, and $M(\Psi_\theta, \Psi_{\theta'}) \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$ is sc-smooth and over the structure maps. *(17.83)* (p. 811) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.display_17_83⟧: if $\hat\Psi$ is continuous, the faces cover $U(\bar o)$ and $\sigma^{-1}(|\Psi(V(\bar o))|) = \bigcup_\theta |\Psi_\theta(\theta)|$. *The ep-groupoid $\Theta$* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.display_17_82_Theta⟧: under the hypotheses of Theorem 17.4.26, the model ep-groupoid $\Theta$ of the face uniformizers $(\Psi_\theta)_\theta$ of $U(\bar o)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceThetaEp⟧ is tame; $\hat\Psi : \Theta \to bC$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceHat⟧ is full and faithful; the ep-functor $F : \Theta \to G \ltimes O$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceForget⟧ makes the square $\Psi \circ F = B \circ \hat\Psi$ commute and is the only functor that does; $F$ maps the objects of $\Theta$ onto $U(\bar o)$; and $\sigma^{-1}(|\Psi(V(\bar o))|) = |\hat\Psi|(|\Theta|)$. Also, for any data $d, d'$ of $L = \Psi_\theta$, $L' = \Psi'_{\theta'}$, the map $M(L, L') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ftrIncl_isScSmoothMap⟧.

*Readings.* (1) The text's $\Theta = \bigsqcup_\theta \theta$, "naturally a tame M-polyfold" and with the morphisms $\bigsqcup M(\Psi_\theta, \Psi_{\theta'})$ an ep-groupoid, is read as the model ep-groupoid (Theorem 17.1.14) of a polyfold construction on the GCT $bC$ whose uniformizers are the $\Psi_\theta$ for the fixed $U(\bar o)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceConstruction⟧, with the transition M-polyfolds of Definition 17.4.14 for the given data. (2) $G \ltimes O$ is read as the model $X_{(\Psi)}$ of the one-element family $(\Psi)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.singleFamily⟧: its objects are the points of $O$ and its morphisms the elements of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$, which $\hat\Psi$ identifies with $G \times O$ (Lemma 17.1.15 and (17.3)). (3) "$F$ surjective on objects" in (17.83) is read as: onto $U(\bar o) = V(\bar o) \cap \partial O$, the part of $V(\bar o)$ over $\partial C$. (4) (17.84) is stated for one pair of faces; the disjoint unions over all faces are not formed as M-polyfolds, the maps being componentwise (point G.215). The hypotheses are those of Theorem 17.4.26; (17.82) and (17.84) themselves need neither continuity of $\hat\Psi$ nor centring, (17.83) needs continuity and the fixed point.

**Proof of (17.84).** *Step 1, injectivity* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceTrToTr_injective⟧: an element of $M(\Psi_\theta, \Psi'_{\theta'})$ is determined by $x$, $\varphi$ and $x'$, since the objects of $bC$ over $x$ and $x'$ in the faces are $\Psi_\theta(x)$, $\Psi'_{\theta'}(x')$, and a morphism of $bC$ between given objects is determined by its underlying morphism. *Step 2, the image* is $M(\Psi, \Psi')^*_{\theta,\theta'}$: the identification (17.71), $M(\Psi_\theta, \Psi'_{\theta'}) \simeq M(\Psi, \Psi')^*_{\theta,\theta'}$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceTrEquiv⟧, here in the form of the homeomorphism of Definition 17.4.14 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.ftrHomeomorph⟧, whose values lie in the set and whose inverse covers it. *Step 3.* By Definition 17.4.14 the structure on $M(\Psi_\theta, \Psi'_{\theta'})$ makes this homeomorphism a local sc-diffeomorphism onto the sub-M-polyfold ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransitionStructure · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.ftr_isLocalScDiffeo_homeomorph⟧; composing with the sc-smooth inclusion of a sub-M-polyfold gives sc-smoothness into $M(\Psi, \Psi')$. *Step 4,* source, target and the underlying morphism agree by construction.

**Proof of (17.82).** The inclusion of the sub-M-polyfold $\theta$ is sc-smooth; $B(\Psi_\theta(x)) = \Psi(x)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceObj_α⟧; and the statement on morphisms is (17.84) with $\Psi' = \Psi$, $U'(\bar o') = U(\bar o)$.

**Proof of (17.83).** The faces cover $U(\bar o)$ ((17.55), Definition 17.4.1) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.sUnion_faces⟧; the equality of sets is (17.79) of the previous sections, proved from (17.68) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTopology · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.exists_mem_faceImage⟧.

**Proof for $\Theta$.** *Step 1, the data are canonical.* For the faces of $U(\bar o)$, $\theta \mapsto \Psi_\theta$ is injective ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceUnif_injective⟧: $\Psi_\theta(\bar o)$ is the object given by the face germ of $\theta$ at $\bar o$, and distinct faces have distinct germs ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceObj_inj⟧. So the data used for $\Psi_\theta$ in the construction of the faces is $(\Psi, \bar o, U(\bar o), \theta)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceDataAt_faceFamily⟧, and the transition M-polyfolds of $\Theta$ are those of Definition 17.4.14 for the given good neighbourhood. When $\Psi \in F(\Psi(\bar o))$ these uniformizers belong to $F_{bC}$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceF_subset⟧.

*Step 2, $\Theta$ and $\hat\Psi$.* $\Theta$ is the model ep-groupoid of the family $(\Psi_\theta)_{\theta \in F_{U(\bar o)}}$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceFamily⟧ (Theorem 17.1.14) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelEpGroupoid⟧, tame because the construction of the faces is tame ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceConstruction_isTame⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_modelEpGroupoid⟧. $\hat\Psi$ is its functor $\Gamma$ of Lemma 17.1.15 ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor⟧, full and faithful ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_full⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_faithful⟧.

*Step 3, $F$.* On objects $(\theta, x) \mapsto x$; on morphisms, $(x, \Phi, x') \mapsto (x, \varphi, x')$ by the map of (17.84). It respects composition, because the composite in $bC$ has the composite underlying morphism ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceForget_mor_comp⟧, and units. It is sc-smooth on objects, a disjoint union of the inclusions $\theta \to O$, and on morphisms, a disjoint union of the maps of (17.84) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ftrIncl_isScSmoothMap⟧. So $F$ is an ep-functor.

*Step 4, the square.* $\Psi(F(\theta, x)) = \Psi(x) = B(\Psi_\theta(x))$ on objects, and both composites send a morphism to $\varphi$.

*Step 5, uniqueness of $F$* (the text: "$F$ is uniquely determined by the rest of the diagram"). If $F'$ also makes the square commute, then $\Psi(F'(z)) = \Psi(F(z))$, so $F'(z) = F(z)$ because $\Psi$ is injective on objects; on morphisms, $F'(m)$ and $F(m)$ have the same source, target and image under the faithful functor of $X_{(\Psi)}$, so they agree.

*Step 6, (17.83) for $\Theta$.* The objects of $\Theta$ go onto $\bigcup_\theta \theta = U(\bar o)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.subset_bdry⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.sUnion_faces⟧, and $\sigma^{-1}(|\Psi(V(\bar o))|) = \bigcup_\theta |\Psi_\theta(\theta)| = |\hat\Psi|(|\Theta|)$ by (17.83).

**Witness** ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swap_display_17_82_Theta⟧. In the swap polyfold at the corner uniformizer, a good neighbourhood of the corner has two faces $\theta \ne \theta' = (-1)\theta$; the corner gives an object of $\Theta$ in each, and $F$ sends both to the corner of $O$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryFaceGroupoid · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.GoodNbhd.faceForget_obj⟧. So $\Theta$ has two components and $F$ is not injective on objects, the configuration the diagram is meant to keep track of.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text forms the disjoint unions $\bigsqcup_\theta \theta$ and $\bigsqcup M(\Psi_\theta, \Psi_{\theta'})$ by hand; the formalization obtains them as the model ep-groupoid of Theorem 17.1.14 for a polyfold construction on $bC$, so that the ep-groupoid axioms come from §17.1 and need not be checked again. Correspondingly $G \ltimes O$ is the model of the one-element family, isomorphic to the translation groupoid through $\hat\Psi$. The two readings, (3) and (4), are the forms in which the text's statements are used.

### XVII.49 The diagram on pp. 811–812 (new in the 2021 edition): $b\Gamma : bX \to bC$ over $\Gamma : X \to C$

**Statement** (restated). ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.diagram_bX_bC⟧: for a tame polyfold structure with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ and a covering family $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.CoveringFamily⟧, with $X = X_\Psi$ its model ep-groupoid and $bX$ the tame boundary ep-groupoid of $X$ (Theorem 7.6.9, Part VII) ⟦Chapter7/TameBoundaryEpStructure · EpGroupoid.boundaryEpGroupoid⟧: (1) $bX$ is tame ⟦Chapter7/TameBoundaryEpStructure · EpGroupoid.isTame_boundaryEpGroupoid⟧; (2) the square $B \circ b\Gamma = \Gamma \circ F$ commutes ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor_square⟧, with $B : bC \to C$ the forgetful functor, $\Gamma = \Gamma_\Psi$ of Lemma 17.1.15, $F : bX \to X$ the forgetful ep-functor of $bX$ ⟦Chapter7/TameBoundaryEpStructure · EpGroupoid.boundaryEpFunctor⟧ and $b\Gamma$ the functor below ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor⟧; (3) $b\Gamma$ and $\Gamma$ are equivalences of categories ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor_isEquivalence⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_15⟧; (4) $|b\Gamma|$ is a bijection ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bijective_orbitMap_bdryModelFunctor⟧ and $|\Gamma|$ a homeomorphism. *Two covering families* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelObj_modelMap⟧: for $\Psi = \Phi \circ \sigma$, the lift $b(X_{\Phi \circ \sigma}) \to b(X_\Phi)$ of the reindexing functor commutes with $b\Gamma$ on objects and lies over the reindexing functor; with $\sigma$ the two inclusions into a union of families this is the embedding of the two diagrams into the diagram of the union.

*Readings and hypotheses.* (1) $bX$ is read as the tame boundary ep-groupoid of the ep-groupoid $X_\Psi$, and $F$ as its forgetful ep-functor: the text calls the vertical arrows the tame boundary constructions in the ep-groupoid and in the polyfold context. (2) The text replaces $O$ by $V(\bar o)$ at boundary points before building $X$; this is not needed, any covering family will do. (3) The hypotheses are tameness and $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$; neither continuity of $\hat\Psi$ nor centring enters, because the diagram is one of categories and $bC$ enters only as a groupoid. (4) The compatibility with the reindexing functors is proved on objects; on morphisms it is not formalized.

**Proof: $b\Gamma$ on objects.** An object of $bX$ over $(\lambda, x)$ is a face germ of $X = \bigsqcup_\lambda O_\lambda$ at $(\lambda, x)$. *Step 1, it comes from a face germ of $O_\lambda$* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsComponentGerm⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_isComponentGerm⟧: the inclusion $O_\lambda \to X$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto its open image, and every face germ over the image of an sc-diffeomorphism is the image of a face germ over its domain ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · TameBoundary.exists_isLiftOf_of_isScDiffeoOn⟧, a lift along an sc-diffeomorphism being a lift back along its inverse ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · TameBoundary.IsLiftOf.symm_of_isScDiffeoOn⟧. *Step 2, uniqueness* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsComponentGerm.sigma_eq⟧: the index $\lambda$ is determined by the base point, and the germ $q$ of $O_\lambda$ by injectivity of lifts along an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · TameBoundary.IsLiftOf.inj_of_isScDiffeoOn⟧. Call it the component germ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.componentGerm⟧. *Step 3, the object of $bC$.* $b\Gamma(p)$ is the object of $bC$ given by $q$, the class $[\Psi_\lambda(x), \Psi_\lambda, U(x), \theta]$ of (17.57) ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelObj⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.ptObj⟧. It lies over $\Gamma(F(p)) = \Psi_\lambda(x)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelObj_α⟧.

**Proof: $b\Gamma$ on morphisms.** A morphism of $bX$ is a face germ $\Phi$ of the morphism M-polyfold $\mathbf X = \bigsqcup M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\lambda'})$ over some $h = (x, \varphi, x')$. *Step 1, $h$ carries the source germ to the target germ* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.germLift_of_isComponentGerm⟧, which is condition (2) of Definition 17.4.3, $\theta' = f_*\theta$ for $f = t \circ (s|_{W(h)})^{-1}$. Take an admissible triple $T$ of $h$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_admissibleTriple⟧ and a local inverse $\sigma$ of the source map of $\mathbf X$ at $\Phi$. Near $s(h)$ the two local sections $\sigma \circ (O_\lambda \to X)$ and $(\mathbf X$-inclusion$) \circ (s|_T)^{-1}$ of $s$ agree, because $s$ is injective on the sheet. Hence $t \circ \sigma$ equals, near the base point, the inclusion of $O_{\lambda'}$ composed with $f$. Since the target germ of $\Phi$ is the push-forward of its source germ along $t \circ \sigma$, it is the image of the push-forward of $q$ along $f$, and by uniqueness of component germs that push-forward is $q'$. *Step 2.* So $(b\Gamma(s(\Phi)), \varphi, b\Gamma(t(\Phi)))$ is a morphism of $bC$ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bLift_bdryModelObj⟧, and $b\Gamma(\Phi)$ is defined as this morphism. *Step 3, functoriality*: the composite in $bX$ lies over the composite in $X$, whose underlying morphism is the composite in $C$; units go to units.

**Proof of the square** ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor_square⟧: on objects it is $B(b\Gamma(p)) = \Gamma(F(p))$, and on morphisms both sides give $\varphi$.

**Proof that $b\Gamma$ is an equivalence.** *Step 1, faithful* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor_faithful⟧: if two morphisms of $bX$ with the same source and target have the same image, their base points in $\mathbf X$ have the same image under the faithful functor $\Gamma$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_faithful⟧, so they lie over the same element of $\mathbf X$; a face germ of $\mathbf X$ is determined by its base point and its source germ, the source map being a local sc-diffeomorphism.

*Step 2, full* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor_full⟧. Let $\Xi : b\Gamma(p) \to b\Gamma(p')$ be a morphism of $bC$ over $\varphi : \Psi_\lambda(x) \to \Psi_{\lambda'}(x')$. Then $h = (x, \varphi, x') \in M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_{\lambda'})$ carries the component germ $q$ of $p$ to that of $p'$. Lift $p$ along the local inverse of the source map of $\mathbf X$ at $h$ to a face germ $\Phi$ of $\mathbf X$ with source $p$. Its target is the image of a germ $r$ of $O_{\lambda'}$, and by Step 1 of the previous paragraph $h$ carries $q$ to $r$; since germ lifts are unique, $r = q'$, so the target of $\Phi$ is $p'$, and $b\Gamma(\Phi) = \Xi$.

*Step 3, essentially surjective for a covering family* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor_essSurj⟧. Let $a$ be an object of $bC$ over $\alpha$. Since $\Gamma$ is essentially surjective for a covering family ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_essSurj⟧, there are $z = (\lambda, x)$ and $\varphi : \Psi_\lambda(x) \to \alpha$. Represent $a$ by a face germ $q_a$ at the reference point of its reference uniformizer, push $q_a$ along $(\bar o_{\mathrm{ref}}, \varphi^{-1}, x)$ to a face germ of $O_\lambda$ at $x$, and take its image $p$ in $bX$. Then $b\Gamma(p)$ is isomorphic to $a$ through the morphism of $bC$ over $\varphi^{-1}$ given by this push-forward.

*Step 4.* Full, faithful and essentially surjective: an equivalence ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelFunctor_isEquivalence⟧, and an equivalence induces a bijection of orbit spaces ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · GroupoidFunctor.bijective_orbitMap_of_isEquivalence⟧. For $\Gamma$, Lemma 17.1.15 gives an equivalence with $|\Gamma|$ a homeomorphism.

**Proof for two covering families** ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelObj_modelMap⟧. *Step 1.* The reindexing functor $X_{\Phi \circ \sigma} \to X_\Phi$ (Theorem 17.1.18) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.modelMap⟧ is, on objects, a disjoint union of identities, hence a local sc-diffeomorphism and a degeneracy-preserving local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isDLocalHomeo_modelMap_obj⟧, so it lifts to face germs ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.bdryModelMap⟧. *Step 2.* If $p$ comes from the germ $q$ of $O_{\sigma(k)}$, its lift comes from the same $q$, by composing the two lifts; so both have the same image under $b\Gamma$, and the lift lies over the reindexing functor.

**Witness** ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swap_diagram_bX_bC⟧. The swap polyfold is tame and has uniformizers at every object, hence a covering family ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.hasCoveringUniformizers_of_hasUniformizers⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.exists_coveringFamily_iff⟧. Its $bC$ has the face objects at the corner, so by essential surjectivity of $b\Gamma$ the tame boundary ep-groupoid $bX$ of its model is non-empty.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text describes the diagram and asserts that its horizontal arrows are equivalences and that the diagrams of two covering families embed into that of their union. The formalization builds $b\Gamma$ explicitly on face germs and proves fullness, faithfulness and essential surjectivity; the one geometric input is Step 1 on morphisms, that the face germs of $\mathbf X$ realize the lifting condition of Definition 17.4.3. The embedding into the diagram of the union is proved on objects; its form on morphisms is not formalized.

### XVII.50 Witnesses, the hypotheses and what is not formalized in §§17.1–17.4

**The three standing conditions.** Each is a named hypothesis, used exactly where the text uses it, and each has a witness.

*(1) $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$* ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.HasUniformizers⟧, or footprints covering $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.UniformizerConstruction.HasCoveringUniformizers⟧ (point G.196; Findings, Chapter 17, item 3). Definition 17.1.6 does not exclude the case where it does not hold: the construction with every $F(\alpha)$ empty satisfies Definition 17.1.8 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.emptyConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_hasUniformizers_empty⟧, and Proposition 17.3.4 does not hold for a structure on the reflection whose footprints cover but whose $F$ is empty at the rational points ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_4_empty_family⟧ (§XVII.31). It enters in the model and the comparison of §17.1 from p. 752 on, Theorem 17.1.28 ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_1_28⟧, Corollary 17.1.29 ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.corollary_17_1_29⟧, all of §17.2, Proposition 17.3.4 ⟦Chapter17/Subpolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_3_4⟧ and §17.4. Witnesses: the reflection structure ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧ (non-trivial action, isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ at $0$; not weak); the tautological structure on $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_hasCoveringUniformizers⟧ and its uniformized form ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructuresExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Uniformized⟧, which serve Theorem 17.1.28 and the levels (non-trivial isotropy, trivial action, not effective).

*(2) $\hat\Psi$ continuous* ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.HatContinuous⟧ (Findings, Chapter 17, item 1). Without it Lemma 17.1.9 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_lemma_17_1_9⟧ and Lemma 17.2.10 ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.not_lemma_17_2_10⟧ do not hold as we read them, on the twisted structures ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.z3Construction⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.fstSwapConstruction⟧ (§§XVII.5, XVII.24). It enters in Lemma 17.1.9 ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_9_of_continuous⟧, Lemma 17.2.10, the construction on p. 783 and Theorem 17.2.17 ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.theorem_17_2_17_hyp⟧, and §17.4 from Lemma 17.4.6 on, except Lemmas 17.4.10–17.4.18 and the diagram of p. 812. It is not used by Lemma 17.1.10 and Proposition 17.1.11, which are proved by counting (§XVII.6). Witnesses: the reflection ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_lemma_17_1_9⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_continuous_hat_all⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_theorem_17_2_17⟧ (non-trivial action; not weak); every slice structure, overlapping uniformizers included ⟦Chapter17/OverlappingUniformizers · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.sliceConstruction_continuous_hat⟧ (not weak); the swap of the quadrant ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapConstruction_hatContinuous⟧ (non-empty boundary, non-trivial group; not weak); the quadrant ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrant_hatContinuous⟧ (weak: trivial group).

*(3) $G$ fixes the point over $\alpha$* ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsCentredAt⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsCentred⟧ (point G.195; Findings, Chapter 17, item 4). It enters where the text uses $G = G_\alpha$: the isotropy statement of §XVII.2 and §17.4 from Definition 17.4.5 on (with the same exceptions as (2)). Witnesses: the reflection at $0$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GCT.LocalUniformizer.reflection_isCentredAt⟧ (not weak); the swap of the quadrant ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swapConstruction_isCentred⟧, where $\mathbb Z/2$ exchanges the two faces at the corner (not weak); the quadrant ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryUniformizer · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrant_isCentred⟧ (weak: trivial group).

**The other hypotheses.** *Effectiveness* in the proved forms of Proposition 17.1.25 (§XVII.14; Findings, Chapter 17, item 2): witness the tautological structure on a point with the trivial group, which has (LR) and is effective ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unitTaut_isLR_isEffective⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.unitTaut_proposition_17_1_25⟧ (weak: trivial isotropy); the reflection is effective with non-trivial isotropy ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isEffective⟧, and has (LUC). *Tameness* and *(ZHOU)* are assumed where the text assumes them: tameness in §17.4 and Proposition 17.3.8 (witness the quadrant ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldFaces · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.quadrant_proposition_17_3_8⟧, weak in its group, not in its boundary: a corner with $d = 2$), (ZHOU) in Theorem 17.1.26 and Corollary 17.1.29. A non-empty boundary in Definition 17.1.30 is witnessed by the corner $\{1\} \ltimes [0, \infty)$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelPropertiesExamples · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.cornerTaut_boundarySet_nonempty⟧. The tame boundary $bC$ is non-empty for the swap of the quadrant, both in Theorem 17.4.26 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swap_theorem_17_4_26⟧ and in the diagram of p. 812 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryDiagram · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.swap_diagram_bX_bC⟧.

**Literal forms that do not hold, given as the reason for a reading.** Lemma 17.4.13 with the faces of Definition 2.5.19 ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTransition · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_4_13_literal⟧ (Findings, Chapter 17, item 6), and Definition 17.4.17 with "$z \in |bC|$" ⟦Chapter17/TameBoundaryTopology · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_4_18_literal⟧ (Findings, Chapter 17, item 7).

**What is not formalized in §§17.1–17.4.**
- Remark 17.1.7 (a), a recipe with set-many choices and no mathematical content (point G.197, nothing to formalize); (b), (c) and (1)–(3) are formalized (§XVII.3).
- The associativity of the simplifications (17.24)–(17.30) on the level of diagrams; the composition law they serve is proved in $\mathcal{EP}(E^{-1})$ (§XVII.12; point G.203).
- The category $\mathrm{Ban}$; $\mu_C$ is given by its maps and the functor laws (§XVII.22).
- The independence of the chosen ambient uniformizer in Theorem 17.1.28, in the construction of $(TF, M)$ on p. 783, in Proposition 17.3.6 and in Theorem 17.4.26; the text does not claim it (§§XVII.18, XVII.28, XVII.33, XVII.47). For Theorem 17.1.28 and Proposition 17.3.6, the identification with the ambient transition sets cannot hold for every pair of uniformizers with one structure on $C^1$, resp. $A$ (§§XVII.18, XVII.33).
- Remark 17.4.7, a motivating remark (point G.213, nothing to formalize) (§XVII.38).
- $G \ltimes \bigsqcup_\theta \theta$ as a groupoid, the homeomorphism (17.69) beyond the identification of sets (point G.214, whose topological content is Lemma 17.4.20), and the disjoint unions of (17.84) as M-polyfolds; the statements are proved componentwise (§§XVII.39, XVII.44, XVII.48).
- The compatibility of $b\Gamma$ with the reindexing functors on morphisms; on objects it is proved (§XVII.49).

### XVII.51 Definition 17.5.1 (2017: 17.14), Proposition 17.5.2 (2017: 17.8) and the identities of p. 813: branched ep⁺-subcategories, $\Gamma^*$ and $\Gamma_*$, pp. 812–813

Throughout §§XVII.51–XVII.61, $C$ is a GCT with a polyfold structure $(F, M)$ (Definition 17.1.8), $X_\Psi$ the model of a family $\Psi = (\Psi_\lambda)$ of uniformizers from $F$, with the equivalence $\Gamma_\Psi : X_\Psi \to C$ (Theorem 17.1.14, Lemma 17.1.15), and $f = f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ the canonical generalized isomorphism between the models of two covering families (Theorem 17.1.18) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · PolyfoldConstruction.comparison⟧. A function on objects with values in $\mathbb Q$ is a *functor* to $\mathbb Q$ if it is constant along morphisms ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidStr.IsFunctorial⟧; $\mathbb Q$ is a category with only identities, so this is all a functor $C \to \mathbb Q^+$ can be.

**Statement** (same). *Definition 17.5.1* (p. 812) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.IsBranchedSubcategory⟧: $\Theta : C \to \mathbb Q$ is a branched ep⁺-subcategory if $\Theta \ge 0$, $\Theta$ is a functor, and for every object $\alpha$ and every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ the function $\Theta \circ \Psi$ on $G \ltimes O$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid (Definition 9.1.2). *The sentence after it*: it suffices to check this on a covering family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_5_1_covering⟧. *The pull-back and push-forward* (p. 812): $\Gamma^*\Theta = \Theta \circ \Gamma_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.modelPull⟧, and $(\Gamma_*\Theta)(\alpha) = \Theta(x)$ for an object $x$ of $X_\Psi$ with a morphism $\Gamma(x) \to \alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.modelPush⟧. *Proposition 17.5.2* (p. 813): (1) for a covering family and a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid $\Theta$ of $X_\Psi$, $\Gamma_*\Theta$ is a branched ep⁺-subcategory ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_2_one⟧; (2) for a branched ep⁺-subcategory $\Theta$, $\Gamma^*\Theta$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of $X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_2_two⟧. *The identities of p. 813*: $f^*(\Gamma')^*\Theta = \Gamma^*\Theta$, $f_*\Gamma^*\Theta = (\Gamma')^*\Theta$ and $\Gamma_*\Theta' = \Gamma'_* f_*\Theta'$.

*Readings.* $\mathbb Q^+$ is $\mathbb Q$ with the condition $\Theta \ge 0$, as in Chapter 9. The first identity is printed $f_*(\Gamma')^*\Theta = \Gamma^*\Theta$; $(\Gamma')^*\Theta$ lives on $X'$ and $f : X \to X'$, so we read $f_*$ there as the pull-back $f^*$, and prove the push-forward form $f_*\Gamma^*\Theta = (\Gamma')^*\Theta$ as well, which is the form the proof of Proposition 17.5.5 and p. 815 use. The topological clause of Definition 9.1.2 (1), that $|\mathrm{supp}(\Theta \circ \Psi)|$ is paracompact, holds for every subset: $|G \ltimes O|$ is metrizable (Definition 17.1.2), hence every subspace is paracompact ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.paracompactSpace_subset⟧.

**The push-forward along a functor of groupoids.** For a functor $F : G \to H$ and $\Theta$ on the objects of $G$, $F_*\Theta(y)$ is $\Theta(x)$ for a chosen $x$ with $F(x)$ isomorphic to $y$, and $0$ if there is none ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidFunctor.pushTheta⟧. *Well-definedness* ("one easily verifies", p. 812) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidFunctor.pushTheta_eq⟧: if $F$ is full and $\Theta$ a functor, then $F_*\Theta(y) = \Theta(x)$ for *every* $x$ with $F(x) \cong y$. *Proof.* Let $x_0$ be the chosen object. Then $F(x_0) \cong y \cong F(x)$, so $F(x_0) \cong F(x)$; fullness lifts the isomorphism to one $x_0 \to x$ in $G$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidFunctor.isomorphic_of_full⟧, and $\Theta(x_0) = \Theta(x)$ because $\Theta$ is constant along morphisms ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · IsFunctorial.eq_of_isomorphic⟧. Three consequences follow at once. $F_*\Theta$ is a functor for full $F$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidFunctor.isFunctorial_pushTheta⟧: for a morphism $\psi$ of $H$, either some $F(x)$ is isomorphic to $s\psi$, and then also to $t\psi$ through $\psi$, and both values are $\Theta(x)$; or none is isomorphic to $s\psi$, and none to $t\psi$ either (compose with $\psi^{-1}$), and both values are $0$. $F^*F_*\Theta = \Theta$ for full $F$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidFunctor.pushTheta_comp_obj⟧, and $F_*F^*\Theta' = \Theta'$ for full and essentially surjective $F$ and a functor $\Theta'$ on $H$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidFunctor.pushTheta_comp_obj_eq⟧. Non-negativity is preserved ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidFunctor.pushTheta_nonneg⟧. Applied to $\Gamma_\Psi$, which is full ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_full⟧, these give $\Gamma^*\Gamma_*\Theta = \Theta$ for every family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.modelPull_modelPush⟧ and $\Gamma_*\Gamma^*\Theta = \Theta$ for a covering family, where $\Gamma_\Psi$ is essentially surjective ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.modelPush_modelPull⟧.

**Proof of the covering-set sentence.** One direction is immediate: a covering family consists of uniformizers from $F$, so the condition for all of $F$ gives it for the family. For the converse, assume $\Theta \ge 0$, $\Theta$ a functor, and $\Theta \circ \Phi_\lambda$ branched for every member of a covering family $\Phi$; let $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ be any uniformizer ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isBranchedEpSubgroupoid_unif_of_isCoveringFamily⟧.

*Step 1, a transition element through every point.* For $q \in O$, the orbit $|\Psi(q)|$ lies in the footprint of some $\Phi_\lambda$ since the footprints cover $|C|$, so there are $q'$ in the domain of $\Phi_\lambda$ and a morphism $\varphi : \Psi(q) \to \Phi_\lambda(q')$; the triple $a = (q, \varphi, q')$ is an element of $M(\Psi, \Phi_\lambda)$ with source $q$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.exists_tr_of_isCoveringFamily⟧. Since $\Theta$ is a functor, $\Theta(\Phi_\lambda(t(a))) = \Theta(\Psi(s(a)))$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.theta_tgt_eq⟧.

*Step 2, the support is in the smooth points.* If $\Theta(\Psi(q)) > 0$, then $t(a)$ lies in the support of $\Theta \circ \Phi_\lambda$, hence is a smooth point; source and target of $M(\Psi, \Phi_\lambda)$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms (Definition 17.1.8), so $s(a) = q$ is smooth if and only if $t(a)$ is ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.src_mem_smoothSet_iff⟧.

*Step 3, local branching structures move along transition sets* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.localBranching_of_tr⟧. Take an admissible triple for $a$ (Lemma 17.1.13): open neighbourhoods on which $s$ and $t$ are sc-diffeomorphisms, and the sc-diffeomorphism $\sigma^{-1} = s \circ (t|)^{-1}$ from a neighbourhood of $t(a)$ to one of $s(a)$. For $u$ near $t(a)$ the transition element $(t|)^{-1}(u)$ is a morphism $\Psi(\sigma^{-1}(u)) \to \Phi_\lambda(u)$, so $\Theta(\Psi(\sigma^{-1}(u))) = \Theta(\Phi_\lambda(u))$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.theta_sigmaInv_eq⟧. A local branching structure of $\Theta \circ \Phi_\lambda$ at $t(a)$ (an open set, finitely many sub-M-polyfolds with weights) is carried by $\sigma^{-1}$ to one of $\Theta \circ \Psi$ at $s(a)$, the transported weights summing to the right values by the identity just proved (Chapter 9's transport of a local branching structure along an sc-diffeomorphism). Sheets with a tame induced structure go to sheets with a tame induced structure, since tameness of an induced structure survives intersection with open sets and images under sc-diffeomorphisms.

*Step 4, assembly.* Non-negativity and the functor property of $\Theta \circ \Psi$ come from those of $\Theta$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.isFunctorial_comp⟧; paracompactness of the orbit support is automatic (above); Steps 1–3 give smoothness of the support and a local branching structure at every point of it. This is Definition 9.1.2 for $\Theta \circ \Psi$, and the sentence follows ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_5_1_covering⟧. A branched ep⁺-subcategory gives a branched $\Theta \circ \Psi$ for every uniformizer of $F$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · IsBranchedSubcategory.unif⟧.

**The model.** The key lemma ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isBranchedEpSubgroupoid_modelPull_iff⟧: for $\Theta \ge 0$ a functor on $C$, $\Theta \circ \Gamma_\Psi$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of $X_\Psi$ if and only if every $\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda$ is one. *Proof.* The objects of $X_\Psi$ are the disjoint union of the $O_\lambda$, and the summand inclusion $O_\lambda \to \bigsqcup O_\mu$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto an open piece. A local branching structure of $\Gamma^*\Theta$ at $(\lambda, q)$ restricts to the summand and becomes one of $\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda$ at $q$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.localBranching_summand⟧; conversely a local branching structure of $\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda$ at $q$ is pushed into the summand ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.localBranching_of_summand⟧; tame sheets stay tame in both directions. Smoothness of a point of the summand is smoothness in the sum ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.mem_smoothSet_model_iff⟧. Paracompactness of $|\mathrm{supp}|$ holds on both sides because $|X_\Psi|$ is metrizable: $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ is continuous, injective (since $\Gamma_\Psi$ is full) and open, hence an embedding into the metrizable $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_modelOrbitSpace_of_family⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.paracompactSpace_model_subset⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 17.5.2.** The text calls both parts obvious. (2) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_2_two⟧: each $\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda$ is branched because $\Theta$ is a branched ep⁺-subcategory, and the key lemma gives $\Gamma^*\Theta$ branched. This holds for every family, covering or not. (1) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_2_one⟧: $\Gamma_*\Theta$ is non-negative and a functor (the push-forward along the full $\Gamma_\Psi$) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isFunctorial_modelPush⟧. By the covering-set sentence it suffices that $\Gamma_*\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda$ is branched for each $\lambda$, and by the key lemma (read from left to right) this is the statement that $\Gamma^*\Gamma_*\Theta$ is branched on $X_\Psi$; but $\Gamma^*\Gamma_*\Theta = \Theta$, which is branched by hypothesis.

**Proof of the identities of p. 813.** The input is Theorem 17.1.18: $|\Gamma'| \circ |f| = |\Gamma|$ ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · PolyfoldConstruction.orbitMap_comparison⟧, and $|f|$ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_orbitMap_comparison⟧. *Step 1.* If $|f|(|x|) = |y|$ for objects $x$ of $X$ and $y$ of $X'$, then $\Gamma(x)$ and $\Gamma'(y)$ are isomorphic in $C$, since their orbits agree ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isomorphic_of_orbitMap_comparison⟧. *Step 2, $f^*(\Gamma')^*\Theta = \Gamma^*\Theta$* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.pullTheta_comparison_modelPull⟧. For $x$ in $X$, choose $y$ in $X'$ with $|y| = |f|(|x|)$. The pull-back along a generalized isomorphism takes at $x$ the value of the function at any such $y$ (Part X); so the left side at $x$ is $\Theta(\Gamma'(y))$, which is $\Theta(\Gamma(x))$ by Step 1 and the functor property. *Step 3, $f_*\Gamma^*\Theta = (\Gamma')^*\Theta$* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.pushTheta_comparison_modelPull⟧. For $y$ in $X'$, use the surjectivity of $|f|$ to choose $x$ with $|f|(|x|) = |y|$; the push-forward takes at $y$ the value at $x$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · EPLoc.pushTheta_eq_of_orbitMap⟧ (itself proved from the pull-back case and $f^*f_* = \mathrm{id}$), and Step 1 finishes. *Step 4, $\Gamma_*\Theta' = \Gamma'_* f_*\Theta'$ for a functor $\Theta'$ on $X$* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.modelPush_pushTheta_comparison⟧. For $\alpha$ in $C$ choose $y$ with $\Gamma'(y) \cong \alpha$ (essential surjectivity) and $x$ with $|f|(|x|) = |y|$. The right side at $\alpha$ is $f_*\Theta'(y) = \Theta'(x)$; the left side is $\Theta'(x)$ too, because $\Gamma(x) \cong \Gamma'(y) \cong \alpha$ by Step 1 and the push-forward along the full $\Gamma$ is well defined. Finally the supports correspond: $|\Gamma|$ maps $|\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)|$ onto $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ for a covering family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.image_modelOrbitMap_orbitSupp⟧: one inclusion is the definition; for the other, an $\alpha$ with $\Theta(\alpha) > 0$ is isomorphic to some $\Gamma(x)$, and $\Theta(\Gamma(x)) = \Theta(\alpha) > 0$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The functor $\Gamma_*$ is defined for every functor of groupoids through a choice, and its independence of the choice is proved from fullness rather than taken for granted. The covering-set sentence and the identities are proved; the only reading is $f^*$ for the printed $f_*$ in the first identity (G.216, G.217 and G.218 settled).

*Witnesses* (in `BranchedSubcategoryExamples`). On the reflection polyfold $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionConstruction⟧ the function $\Theta_0 = 1_{\{0\}}$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionThetaC⟧ is a branched ep⁺-subcategory ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.isBranchedSubcategory_reflectionThetaC⟧ with $\Theta_0(0) = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionThetaC_zero⟧: $F(x) = \{\Psi_0\}$ and $\Theta_0 \circ \Psi_0$ is Chapter 11's branched ep⁺-subgroupoid $1_{\{0\}}$ of $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$. The isotropy at the point of the support is $\mathbb Z/2$, so the witness is not weak.

### XVII.52 Definitions 17.5.3 (2017: 17.15) and 17.5.4 (2017: 17.16): compact, tame, manifold-type and orbifold-type, p. 813

**Statement** (Definition 17.5.3: same; Definition 17.5.4: explicit). *Definition 17.5.3* (p. 813): (1) $\Theta$ is compact if $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta| = \pi(\{\Theta > 0\})$ is compact in $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GCT.IsCompactBranched⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GroupoidStr.orbitSupp⟧; (2) $\Theta$ is tame if $\Gamma^*\Theta$ is a tame branched ep⁺-subgroupoid (Definition 9.3.6) for a suitable covering family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.IsTameVia⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.IsTameModel⟧, which does not depend on the family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_5_3_two⟧. *Definition 17.5.4* (p. 813): (1) $\Theta$ is of manifold-type if it takes only the values $0$ and $1$ and between two objects of the support there is at most one morphism ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GCT.IsManifoldTypeBranched⟧; (2) of orbifold-type if it takes only the values $0$ and $1$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · GCT.IsOrbifoldTypeBranched⟧; (3) tame if for $\alpha \in \mathrm{supp}\,\Theta$ and $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ the function $\Theta \circ \Psi$ is tame ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.IsTameSubcategory⟧, "equivalently" if $\Theta \circ \Gamma$ is tame for an sc-smooth model ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_5_4_three⟧.

*Hypothesis.* The "equivalently" of (3) is proved under the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (`hU`, `HasUniformizers`, G.196): without it, an object of the support might carry no uniformizer, and (3) would say nothing there.

**Compactness through a model** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isCompactBranched_iff_model⟧. *Proof.* By the support identity of §XVII.51, $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ is the image of $|\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)|$ under $|\Gamma|$, which is a homeomorphism $|X_\Psi| \to |C|$ for a covering family (Lemma 17.1.15) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · PolyfoldConstruction.modelOrbitHomeomorph⟧; compactness is preserved by homeomorphic images in both directions. So $\Theta$ is compact if and only if $\Gamma^*\Theta$ is a compact branched ep⁺-subgroupoid in the sense of Definition 9.1.6.

**Proof that tameness does not depend on the covering family** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_5_3_two⟧. If $\Gamma'^*\Theta$ is tame on $X'$, then $f^*(\Gamma'^*\Theta)$ is tame on $X$, because the pull-back along a generalized isomorphism preserves tameness (Chapter 16) ⟦Chapter16/BranchedTame · EPLocalization.EPLoc.isTameBranched_pullTheta⟧, and $f^*(\Gamma'^*\Theta) = \Gamma^*\Theta$ by the first identity of p. 813. Exchanging the families gives the converse. Hence tameness for one covering family is tameness for every one ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · IsTameModel.forall⟧.

**Manifold-type and orbifold-type through a model** (the first step of the proof of Proposition 17.5.5, p. 814). *Orbifold-type* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrbifoldType_modelPull_iff⟧: if $\Gamma^*\Theta$ takes only $0$ and $1$, then so does $\Theta$, because every $\alpha$ is isomorphic to some $\Gamma(x)$ and $\Theta(\alpha) = \Theta(\Gamma(x))$; the converse is immediate. *Manifold-type* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isManifoldType_modelPull_iff⟧. From the model to $C$: let $\varphi, \psi$ be morphisms of $C$ with the same source and target in the support. Choose $\chi : \Gamma(x) \to s\varphi$ and $\chi' : \Gamma(y) \to t\varphi$ by essential surjectivity. Then $\chi'^{-1}\varphi\chi$ and $\chi'^{-1}\psi\chi$ are morphisms $\Gamma(x) \to \Gamma(y)$, and fullness lifts them to morphisms $\varphi_1, \psi_1 : x \to y$ of $X_\Psi$, both between points of $\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)$. Manifold-type on the model gives $\varphi_1 = \psi_1$, hence $\chi'^{-1}\varphi\chi = \chi'^{-1}\psi\chi$, and cancelling $\chi'^{-1}$ on the left and $\chi$ on the right gives $\varphi = \psi$. From $C$ to the model: two morphisms of $X_\Psi$ with the same source and target in the support have images in $C$ with the same source and target in $\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta$, which are equal, and faithfulness of $\Gamma_\Psi$ makes the morphisms equal ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_faithful⟧.

**Proof of the "equivalently" of Definition 17.5.4 (3)** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_5_4_three⟧. The pivot is a lemma of Chapter 9 recast as a local statement ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · EpGroupoid.isTameBranched_iff_exists⟧: $\Theta$ on an ep-groupoid is tame (Definition 9.3.6) if and only if at every point of the support there is a local branching structure all of whose sheets carry a tame induced structure. *Proof of the lemma.* A tame structure in the sense of Definition 9.3.6 is in particular such a structure. Conversely, given such a structure at $x$, choose an open neighbourhood $V$ of $x$ with the properness property (Chapter 7) and a natural representation of $G_x$ on an open set inside $V \cap U$ (Theorem 7.1.13); restricting the local branching structure to the domain of the natural representation keeps the sheets tame (intersection with an open set), and the restricted structure is the one Definition 9.3.6 asks for.

With the lemma, both directions move tame local structures along the maps of §XVII.51. *From the uniformizers to the model.* Let $(\lambda, q)$ be in the support of $\Gamma^*\Theta$ and $\alpha = \Psi_\lambda(q)$. By `hU` there is $\Psi' \in F(\alpha)$, with a point $q'$ over $\alpha$ (Definition 17.1.3). $\Theta \circ \Psi'$ is tame by (3), so the lemma gives a local branching structure with tame sheets at $q'$. The triple $(q, 1_\alpha, q')$ is an element of $M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi')$; Step 3 of §XVII.51 moves the structure to $q$, and the summand inclusion moves it into $X_\Psi$ at $(\lambda, q)$, with tame sheets throughout. *From the model to the uniformizers.* For $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and $q$ in the support of $\Theta \circ \Psi$, Step 1 of §XVII.51 gives a transition element $a$ from $q$ into some member $\Psi_\lambda$ of the covering family; the tame structure of $\Gamma^*\Theta$ at $(\lambda, t(a))$ is restricted to the summand and moved back along $a$ to $q$. So the two tameness notions of Definitions 17.5.3 (2) and 17.5.4 (3) agree under $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The two definitions of tameness are stated separately and shown to agree; the agreement needs the standing reading `hU`, which is where the verdict "explicit" comes from.

*Witnesses* (in `BranchedSubcategoryExamples`). $\Theta_0 = 1_{\{0\}}$ on $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ is of orbifold-type ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrbifoldTypeBranched_reflectionThetaC⟧ and not of manifold-type, since $\pm 1$ are two different morphisms $0 \to 0$ in the support ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.not_isManifoldTypeBranched_reflectionThetaC⟧. On $\{1\} \ltimes \mathbb R$, $1_{\{0\}}$ is a branched ep⁺-subcategory, with the local branching structure $U = \mathbb R$, $M = \{0\}$, weight $1$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.isBranchedSubcategory_unitThetaC⟧, and of manifold-type ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.isManifoldTypeBranched_unitThetaC⟧.

### XVII.53 Proposition 17.5.5 (2017: 17.9): the structure on $|\mathrm{supp}(\Theta)|$, p. 814

**Statement** (restated). For a branched ep⁺-subcategory $\Theta$: (1) if $\Theta$ is of manifold-type, $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta| \subset |C|$ has a natural structure of a smooth finite-dimensional manifold ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_5_one⟧; (2) if $\Theta$ is of orbifold-type, it has a natural orbifold structure ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_5_two⟧.

*Readings.* "Smooth manifold" is read as in Proposition 9.1.9 (1) and Remark 9.1.10 ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · EpGroupoid.IsSuppStructure⟧: an M-polyfold structure on $|\mathrm{supp}|$ for which $\mathrm{supp}$ is an ep-subgroupoid and a smooth submanifold and $a \mapsto |a|$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism $\mathrm{supp} \to |\mathrm{supp}|$; Proposition 9.1.9 (1) supplies one ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · EpGroupoid.exists_isSuppStructure⟧. "In a natural way" is read as: every covering family induces such a structure through $|\Gamma|$, and all induced structures agree. The orbifold structure is read up to equivalence, as the sub-ep-groupoid on the support together with the homeomorphism of its orbit space onto $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ (Proposition 9.1.9 (2)). $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ is paracompact, as a subspace of the metrizable $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.paracompactSpace_orbitSuppC⟧.

**The homeomorphism of the supports.** For a covering family, $|\Gamma|$ restricts to a homeomorphism $|\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)| \cong |\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.suppHomeomorph⟧: the restriction of the homeomorphism $|\Gamma_\Psi|$ of Lemma 17.1.15 to a subset, composed with the set identity of §XVII.51. For a generalized isomorphism $f$ and $\Theta = f^*\Theta'$, $|f|$ maps $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ into $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta'|$, because $\Theta(x) = \Theta'(y)$ whenever $|f|(|x|) = |y|$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · EPLoc.mapsTo_orbitMap_orbitSupp⟧, and so restricts to a map of the supports ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · EPLoc.orbitSuppMap⟧. (17.85) on the supports, $|\Gamma'| \circ |f| = |\Gamma|$, is Theorem 17.1.18 restricted ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.suppHomeomorph_orbitSuppMap⟧.

**"$|f|$ is a smooth orbifold map"** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · EPLoc.restrictSupp_orbifold⟧. Let $f = [X \xleftarrow{F} A \xrightarrow{\Phi} Y]$ be a representative (Part X), $\Theta'$ branched of orbifold-type on $Y$, $\Theta = f^*\Theta'$. *Step 1.* $F$ and $\Phi$ are equivalences ⟦Chapter11/BranchedEquivalenceLocalization · EPLoc.isEquivalence_rep_Φ⟧. *Step 2.* $\Theta_A = \Phi^*\Theta'$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of $A$ (the pull-back along an equivalence, Chapter 11) ⟦Chapter11/BranchedEquivalence · EpFunctor.isBranchedEpSubgroupoid_pullbackTheta⟧, of orbifold-type because $\Theta'$ is. *Step 3.* $\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta_A = \Phi^{-1}(\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta')$ by definition, and $= F^{-1}(\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta)$ because $\Theta$ is the push-forward of $\Theta_A$ along the full $F$. *Step 4.* $|f| \circ |F| = |\Phi|$ on orbits. So $F$ and $\Phi$ restrict to equivalences of the sub-ep-groupoids on the supports (Chapter 11's restriction of equivalences to saturated sub-ep-groupoids) ⟦Chapter11/FaceGeneralizedIso · EpFunctor.IsEquivalence.restrictSub⟧.

**$|f|$ is sc-smooth on the supports** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · EPLoc.isScSmoothOn_orbitSuppMap⟧, for any structures of Proposition 9.1.9 (1) on both sides, when $\Theta'$ is of orbifold-type. This is the argument of Proposition 16.4.3 (Part XVI). *Step 1.* Take $A$, $F$, $\Phi$, $\Theta_A$ from the previous paragraph; $\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta_A$ is an ep-subgroupoid, and the restrictions $F_s$, $\Phi_s$ are sc-smooth, $F_s$ a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects (an equivalence of ep-groupoids). *Step 2.* The key identity: $|f|(|F_s(b)|) = |\Phi_s(b)|$ for $b \in \mathrm{supp}\,\Theta_A$. *Step 3, local form.* Every point of $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ is $|F_s(b)|$ for some $b$: given $|x|$ with $x$ in the support, essential surjectivity gives $\psi : F(y) \to x$, the support is saturated so $F(y)$ is in it, and so is $y$. Near $|F_s(b)|$ choose a local inverse $g_1$ of $p : \mathrm{supp}\,\Theta \to |\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ (the structure is a local sc-diffeomorphism) and a local inverse $g_2$ of $F_s$, on the intersection $V$ of their domains. *Step 4.* On $V$, $|f| = p' \circ \Phi_s \circ g_2 \circ g_1$, a composite of sc-smooth maps; the identity on $V$ follows from Step 2 and the two inverse identities. So $|f|$ is sc-smooth near every point.

**Proof of (1).** *Existence.* For a covering family $c$, $\Gamma^*\Theta$ is branched (Proposition 17.5.2 (2)) and of manifold-type (§XVII.52), so Proposition 9.1.9 (1) gives a structure $P_Z$ on $|\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)|$ ⟦Chapter9/SupportManifold · EpGroupoid.proposition_9_1_9_one⟧, which is transported to $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ along the homeomorphism of the supports. *Independence.* For two covering families $c, c'$ and any two such structures $P_Z$, $P_Z'$, the identity of $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ is sc-smooth from the transport of $P_Z$ to that of $P_Z'$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothOn_id_transport⟧: after transport, the identity is $|f_{c,c'}|$ restricted to the supports, by (17.85) on the supports, and that map is sc-smooth by the previous paragraph applied to $f_{c,c'}$ and $\Theta' = \Gamma'^*\Theta$, with $f^*\Gamma'^*\Theta = \Gamma^*\Theta$ from §XVII.51. Exchanging $c$ and $c'$ gives smoothness of the inverse, so the identity is an sc-diffeomorphism.

**Proof of (2)** (left to the reader in the text). *On one model.* $\Gamma^*\Theta$ is branched and of orbifold-type, so Proposition 9.1.9 (2) ⟦Chapter9/SupportOrbifold · EpGroupoid.proposition_9_1_9_two⟧ makes $\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ an ep-subgroupoid and a smooth submanifold of $X_\Psi$ with a homeomorphism of its orbit space onto $|\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)|$; composed with the homeomorphism of the supports, the orbit space is homeomorphic to $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$, compatibly with $|\Gamma|$. *Between two models.* The restriction lemma above, applied to $f_{c,c'}$, gives an ep-groupoid $A$ with equivalences $F : A \to X_\Psi$, $\Phi : A \to X_{\Psi'}$ and an orbifold-type $\Theta_A$ whose support is $F^{-1}$ of one support and $\Phi^{-1}$ of the other; the compatibility $|\Gamma \circ F| = |\Gamma' \circ \Phi|$ on orbits follows from Step 4 of the restriction lemma and $|\Gamma'| \circ |f| = |\Gamma|$. So the two orbifold structures are related by a generalized isomorphism inducing the identity of $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text treats (2) as similar to (1); it is written out through the representative of $f$ (G.219 settled). The text's "unique manifold structure for which $|\Gamma|$ is a diffeomorphism" becomes the statement that all structures obtained this way agree, which is the content used later.

### XVII.54 Definition 17.5.6 (2017: 17.17), the functors $\nu$, $\mathrm{Gr}(C) \to C_\infty$, Proposition 17.5.7 (2017: 17.10) and (17.86): the tangent $T\Theta$, pp. 814–816

Here and in §§XVII.55–XVII.57 the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ is assumed (`hU`, G.196): it is needed to have the tangent GCT $TC$ (Theorem 17.2.17). $T_\alpha C$ is the set of objects of $TC$ over $\alpha$; $T\Gamma_\Psi : TX_\Psi \to TC$ is the tangent of the model functor ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelFunctor_full⟧ (full, essentially surjective for a covering family).

**Statement.** *Definition 17.5.6* (explicit; pp. 814–815): for a covering family, $T(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ is Chapter 9's tangent of the branched ep⁺-subgroupoid $\Gamma^*\Theta$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.modelTangentTheta⟧, and $(T\Gamma)_*T(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ is its push-forward to $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentThetaVia⟧; it does not depend on the family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_5_6⟧, and $T\Theta$ is this function ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangent⟧. *The functors $\nu$ and $\mathrm{Gr}(C) \to C_\infty$* (restated; pp. 815–816): $\nu(\alpha) = \mathrm{Gr}(\alpha)$, finite formal sums with non-negative rational weights of subsets of $T_\alpha C$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.GrC⟧, with $\nu(\varphi)(\sum \sigma_L L) = \sum \sigma_L\, T\varphi(L)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.grPushC⟧, as a functor $C_\infty \to \mathrm{Type}$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grFunctorC⟧, and $\mathrm{Gr}(C) = L_\nu$ its category of elements. *Proposition 17.5.7* (explicit; p. 816) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_7⟧: $T\Theta$ is a section of $\mathrm{Gr}(C) \to C_\infty$ with $T\Theta = T\Gamma_* T_{\Theta \circ \Gamma}$ for every covering family; with (17.86) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.modelGrPush_tangentValue_eq⟧.

*Readings.* $\mathrm{SET}$ is Mathlib's `Type`, $C_\infty$ the full subgroupoid of smooth objects ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · PolyfoldConstruction.regSubInf⟧, and $L_\nu$ Mathlib's category of elements. $\mathrm{Gr}(\alpha)$ is read as in Chapter 9 (G.7): formal sums of subsets; the subspaces of the text are the images of the tangent spaces of local branching structures. The formulas of Proposition 17.5.7 are stated on the objects of $C^1$ with a uniformizer (`TangentObj`), which contain the smooth objects. The text compares two covering families through $Tf$; the Lean compares each with the union of the two ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · CoveringFamily.union⟧, of which both models are summands. $T\Theta$ and its section are defined through the covering family that defines the topology of $|TC|$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentModel · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentCover⟧; the theorems below show that every covering family gives the same objects.

**The tangent on a summand.** For a branched $\Theta$, $\Gamma^*\Theta$ is branched for every family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · IsBranchedSubcategory.model⟧, and on the summand $O_\lambda$ its tangent is the tangent of $\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda$: $T(\Gamma^*\Theta)(T\iota_\lambda k) = T(\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda)(k)$ for the summand inclusion $\iota_\lambda$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.modelTangentTheta_mk⟧. This is Chapter 11's invariance of the tangent of a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid under an sc-diffeomorphism onto an open piece. The push-forward to $TC$ pulls back to $T(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ along $T\Gamma$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentThetaVia_obj⟧, by the well-definedness of push-forwards along full functors (§XVII.51), and on $T\Psi_\lambda(k)$ it is $T(\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda)(k)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentThetaVia_tangentUnifObj⟧.

**Proof of Definition 17.5.6** ("this turns out not to be the case", p. 815). *Step 1, a covering subfamily* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentThetaVia_eq_of_comp⟧. Let $\Psi \circ \sigma$ be a subfamily of $\Psi$ that still covers. For $e \in TC$, essential surjectivity of $T\Gamma_{\Psi\circ\sigma}$ gives an object $T\iota_j k$ of $TX_{\Psi\circ\sigma}$ and a morphism of $TC$ from its image to $e$. The same object, read in the summand $\sigma(j)$ of $X_\Psi$, has the same image in $TC$. Both push-forwards at $e$ are therefore values of the tangent on a summand, and both equal $T(\Theta \circ \Psi_{\sigma(j)})(k)$. *Step 2, two covering families.* Both $c$ and $c'$ are subfamilies of their union, through the two inclusions of the index sum, so Step 1 twice gives $(T\Gamma_c)_*T(\Gamma_c^*\Theta) = (T\Gamma_{c\sqcup c'})_*T(\Gamma_{c\sqcup c'}^*\Theta) = (T\Gamma_{c'})_*T(\Gamma_{c'}^*\Theta)$. Consequently $T\Theta = (T\Gamma)_*T(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ for every covering family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangent_eq⟧, $(T\Gamma)^*T\Theta = T(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangent_comp_tangentModelObj⟧, and for every uniformizer $\Psi_0$ of $F$, $T\Theta(T\Psi_0(k)) = T(\Theta \circ \Psi_0)(k)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangent_tangentUnifObj⟧ (add $\Psi_0$ to the covering family of $TC$ as one more member and apply Step 1). $T\Theta$ is non-negative ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangent_nonneg⟧ and a functor on $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.isFunctorial_branchedTangent⟧.

**The action of morphisms on $T_\alpha C$.** For $\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$, $T\varphi$ sends $\bar h \in T_\alpha C$ to the target of the morphism $(\bar h, \varphi, T\varphi(\bar h))$ of $TC$ (Definition 17.2.6) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tcMove⟧, and $T\varphi' \circ T\varphi = T(\varphi'\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tcMove_tcMove⟧. Hence $\nu(\varphi')\nu(\varphi) = \nu(\varphi'\varphi)$ on $\mathrm{Gr}$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.grPushC_comp⟧ and $\nu(1_\alpha) = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grPushC_unit⟧, which makes $\nu$ a functor on $C_\infty$, whose objects and morphisms are those of $C$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.smoothObjVal⟧ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.smoothMorVal⟧.

**Proof of (17.86) and the independence of $(x, \varphi)$** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.modelGrPush_tangentValue_eq⟧. For $x$ in $X_\Psi$ let $T_{\Theta\circ\Gamma}(x)$ be Chapter 9's tangent value of $\Gamma^*\Theta$ at $x$ (a formal sum of subsets of $T_xX$), and for $\varphi : \Gamma(x) \to \alpha$ let $T\varphi \circ T\Gamma$ act on formal sums ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.modelGrPush⟧. Given $\varphi : \Gamma(x) \to \alpha$ and $\varphi' : \Gamma(x') \to \alpha$, the claim is that both give the same element of $\mathrm{Gr}(\alpha)$. *Step 1.* $\chi = \varphi'^{-1}\varphi : \Gamma(x) \to \Gamma(x')$, and fullness gives $\psi : x \to x'$ in $X_\Psi$ with $\Gamma(\psi) = \chi$; also $\varphi' \chi = \varphi$. *Step 2.* The tangent value is natural along morphisms of the ep-groupoid: $T\psi_* T_{\Theta\circ\Gamma}(x) = T_{\Theta\circ\Gamma}(x')$ (Theorem 9.2.1 (2), with Chapter 9's independence of the tangent sums) ⟦Chapter9/Tangent · EpGroupoid.grPush_tangentValue⟧. *Step 3.* $T\Gamma$ is a functor $TX \to TC$: $T\Gamma(T\psi(a)) = T(\Gamma\psi)(T\Gamma(a))$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentModelObj_tangentMor⟧. *Step 4.* Pushing by $\varphi'$: $T\varphi'(T\Gamma(T\psi(a))) = T\varphi'(T\chi(T\Gamma(a))) = T\varphi(T\Gamma(a))$, so the images of every subset agree and the two formal sums are equal. The isotropy invariance (17.86) is the case $x = x'$.

**The section** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentSectionVia⟧. At $\alpha$, $T\Gamma_*T_{\Theta\circ\Gamma}(\alpha) = T\varphi(T\Gamma(T_{\Theta\circ\Gamma}(x)))$ for a chosen pair $(x, \varphi)$, and by (17.86) for every pair ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentSectionVia_eq⟧. On a summand, the tangent value is the image of the tangent value of $\Theta \circ \Psi_\lambda$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentValue_model_mk⟧; as in the proof of Definition 17.5.6, a covering subfamily gives the same section ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentSectionVia_eq_of_comp⟧, and so do any two covering families, through their union ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentSectionVia_covering⟧. $T\Theta$ is this section for the covering family of $TC$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangentSection⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 17.5.7** (left to the reader in the text) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_7⟧. *(2), the formula for every covering family*: the independence of the covering family composed with the independence of the pair. *(1), values in $\mathrm{Gr}(\alpha)$*: choose $(x, \varphi)$ by essential surjectivity; the tangent value at $x$ is in $\mathrm{Gr}(x)$ (weights $\ge 0$, subsets of $T_xX$), and $T\varphi \circ T\Gamma$ sends it into $\mathrm{Gr}(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.modelGrPush_mem⟧. *(3), naturality along $\chi : \alpha \to \alpha'$*: with $(x, \varphi)$ for $\alpha$, the pair $(x, \chi\varphi)$ serves for $\alpha'$, and $\nu(\chi) \circ (T\varphi \circ T\Gamma) = T(\chi\varphi) \circ T\Gamma$ on $\mathrm{Gr}(x)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.grPushC_modelGrPush⟧. *(4), uniqueness*: a family $T$ with the formula of (2) for one covering family agrees with $T\Theta$ at every $\alpha$, by evaluating both at one pair $(x, \varphi)$. As a functor, $T\Theta : C_\infty \to \mathrm{Gr}(C)$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentSectionFunctorC⟧ is a section of the projection ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentSectionFunctorC_comp_π⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The comparison of two covering families goes through their union instead of the tangent of $f$; the conclusion is the same, and it avoids the tangent of a generalized isomorphism on the tangent GCT. $C_\infty$ enters through `TangentObj` (G.220 settled).

*Witness* (in `BranchedSubcategoryExamples`). $0$ is an object of $C^1$ of the reflection polyfold with $F(0) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategoryExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentObj_reflection_zero⟧, so the formulas of Proposition 17.5.7 apply at the point of $\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta_0$ with isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$.

### XVII.55 The oriented Grassmannian $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}(C)$ and (17.87), Definition 17.5.8 (2017: 17.18) and Remark 17.5.9 (2017: 17.5): orientations, pp. 816–817

**Statement.** *The oriented Grassmannian* (restated; pp. 816–817): an oriented subset of $T C$ is a set with a dimension $n$ and a set of positive frames $\mathrm{Fin}\,n \to TC$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.OrTC⟧; $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}(\alpha)$ the formal sums of oriented subsets of $T_\alpha C$ with frames in the carrier ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.GrHatC⟧; (17.87) the forgetful map $f(\sum \sigma \hat L) = \sum \sigma L$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.grForgetC⟧; the pull-back $(T\Gamma^*\hat T)(x) = T\Gamma(x)^{-1}(\hat T(\Gamma(x)))$, preimages of carriers and frames ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.orTCPull⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.sectionPull⟧. *Definition 17.5.8* (explicit; p. 817) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.IsOrientationC⟧: an orientation of $\Theta$ is $\hat T$ with values in $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}$ on smooth objects, natural along morphisms, with $f \circ \hat T = T\Theta$, and with $T\Gamma^*\hat T$ an orientation of $\Gamma^*\Theta$ (Definition 9.3.2) for every covering family. *Remark 17.5.9* (narrower; p. 817) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_5_9_hyp⟧: the last condition may be replaced by: $T\Psi^*\hat T$ is an orientation of $\Theta \circ \Psi$ for $\alpha \in \mathrm{supp}\,\Theta$ and $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.unifSectionPull⟧.

*Readings.* "For a covering set $\Psi$" in Definition 17.5.8 is read as "for every covering family". That the pull-backs are oriented subspaces of the right dimension is part of the orientation condition on the model. *Hypothesis* `hcont` in the direction "⇒" of Remark 17.5.9: every $\hat\Psi$ is continuous. Its witness is the reflection polyfold, a non-trivial group action ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_continuous_hat_all⟧ (not weak). The reason: the naturality clause of Definition 9.3.2 on $G \ltimes O$ needs $T\Psi$ to be $G$-equivariant, which is Lemma 17.2.10 on morphisms; it holds when $\hat\Psi$ is continuous ⟦Chapter17/TangentCategory · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifMor_tgt_of_continuous⟧ and does not hold as we read it in general ⟦Chapter17/TangentUnifExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.not_lemma_17_2_10⟧ (Findings, Chapter 17, the item on Lemmas 17.1.9 and 17.2.10, where Definition 17.1.8 presumably wants $\hat\Psi$ continuous). Whether "⇒" holds without `hcont` is open; the other three clauses of Definition 9.3.2 hold for every uniformizer.

**The functors $\hat\nu$ and $f$.** $T\varphi$ acts on oriented subsets by images of the carrier and of the frames ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · OrTC.move⟧, with $T1_\alpha = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · OrTC.move_unit⟧ and $T\varphi'T\varphi = T(\varphi'\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · OrTC.move_move⟧, both from the corresponding facts for `tcMove`; on formal sums ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.grHatPushC⟧ this gives $\hat\nu(\varphi)$ mapping $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}(s\varphi)$ to $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}(t\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grHatPushC_mem⟧ functorially ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grHatPushC_comp⟧ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grHatPushC_unit⟧, hence the functor $\hat\nu$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grHatFunctorC⟧. Forgetting the orientation commutes with the action, since both act by images of the carrier: $f \circ \hat\nu(\varphi) = \nu(\varphi) \circ f$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grForgetC_grHatPushC⟧, and $f$ maps $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}(\alpha)$ into $\mathrm{Gr}(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grForgetC_mem⟧; so $f$ is a natural transformation $\hat\nu \to \nu$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grForgetNatC⟧ and induces a functor of categories of elements over $C_\infty$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grForgetFunctorC⟧ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.grForgetFunctorC_comp_π⟧.

**Orientations as section functors.** An orientation gives a section functor $\hat T_\Theta : C_\infty \to \widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}(C)$ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.orientationSectionC⟧ ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.orientationSectionC_comp_π⟧ from its fields `mem` and `natural`, and the field `lifts` gives $f \circ \hat T_\Theta = T\Theta$ on objects ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.orientationSectionC_forget_obj⟧ and as an isomorphism of functors whose components lie over identities ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.orientationSectionCForgetIso⟧. *Satisfiability.* $T\Theta = 0$ for $\Theta = 0$, because every tangent value of $0$ vanishes ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangentSection_zero⟧, and $\hat T = 0$ is then an orientation of $\Theta = 0$: every clause reduces to the zero orientation of Chapter 9 ⟦Chapter17/BranchedTangent · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrientationC_zero⟧.

**Proof of Remark 17.5.9, "⇐"** ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrientationC_of_unif⟧.
