Assume the conditions on $C$ (values in $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}$, naturality, $f \circ \hat T = T\Theta$) and that $T\Psi^*\hat T$ is an orientation of $\Theta \circ \Psi$ for $\alpha \in \mathrm{supp}\,\Theta$ and $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$. The model condition is proved for every family $\Phi$ of uniformizers, covering or not ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrientation_model_of_unif⟧. *Step 1, a criterion on an ep-groupoid* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · EpGroupoid.isOrientation_of_hasOrientedRepAt⟧: a section of $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}$ that is natural, vanishes at smooth points off the support, and has an oriented local representation (9.9) at every point of the support ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · EpGroupoid.HasOrientedRepAt⟧ is an orientation (Definition 9.3.2); values in $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · EpGroupoid.mem_grHat_of_hasOrientedRepAt⟧ and the lift of the tangent ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · EpGroupoid.grForget_eq_tangentValue⟧ follow from (9.9) and the vanishing. *Step 2, naturality on the model* ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.grHatPush_sectionPull⟧: for $\psi : x \to y$ in $X_\Phi$, $T\Gamma(y)^{-1}(T(\Gamma\psi)\hat L) = T\psi(T\Gamma(x)^{-1}\hat L)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.orTCPull_move⟧, because $T\Gamma \circ T\psi = T(\Gamma\psi) \circ T\Gamma$ and $T(\Gamma\psi)$ is injective on $T_{\Gamma(x)}C$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.tcMove_injOn⟧; naturality of $\hat T$ on $C$ then gives naturality of the pull-back. No continuity of $\hat\Psi$ is needed, because the morphisms of the model map to morphisms of $C$ through the functor $\Gamma$. *Step 3, vanishing off the support*: $T\Theta(\alpha) = 0$ off the support ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangentSection_eq_zero⟧, so $\hat T(\alpha) = 0$ there, since the weights are non-negative and $f(\hat T(\alpha)) = 0$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.eq_zero_of_not_mem_supp⟧. *Step 4, (9.9) at $(\lambda, q)$ in the support.* Let $\alpha = \Phi_\lambda(q)$ and take $\Psi' \in F(\alpha)$ with $q'$ over $\alpha$, with (9.9) for $T\Psi'^*\hat T$ at $q'$ (from the hypothesis, through the clause (9.9) of Definition 9.3.2). Adjoin $\Psi'$ to the family as an extra member. On a summand $T\Gamma^*\hat T$ is the image of $T\Psi_\lambda^*\hat T$ under $T\iota_\lambda$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.sectionPull_mk⟧, so (9.9) at $q'$ moves into the enlarged model ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.hasOrientedRepAt_model_of_unif⟧. Fullness of $\Gamma$ lifts $1_\alpha$ to a morphism $\psi$ of the enlarged model from $q'$ to $(\lambda, q)$; an oriented local representation is carried along a morphism by the local sc-diffeomorphism of its sheet ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · EpGroupoid.exists_orientedBranchingStructure_tgt⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · EpGroupoid.hasOrientedRepAt_tgt⟧; and the representation at $(\lambda, q)$ is restricted to the summand ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.hasOrientedRepAt_unif_of_model⟧ and pushed back into $X_\Phi$. Step 1 concludes.

**Proof of Remark 17.5.9, "⇒".** For every uniformizer $\Psi$ of $F$, three clauses of Definition 9.3.2 for $T\Psi^*\hat T$ hold without condition ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.unif_of_isOrientationC⟧: (9.9) at $q$ in the support is obtained by adjoining $\Psi$ to the covering family of $TC$, using the model condition there and restricting to the summand of $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.hasOrientedRepAt_unif⟧; values in $\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}}$ and the lift then follow as in Step 1, with Proposition 17.5.7 computed through $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.branchedTangentSection_eq_unif⟧. The naturality clause on $G \ltimes O$ uses $T\Psi(T(g*\cdot)k) = T(\Psi(g, q))(T\Psi(k))$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentUnifObj_tangentMor⟧, hence the commutation of $T\Psi^*$ with the morphisms of $G \ltimes O$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.unifOrPull_move⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.grHatPush_unifSectionPull⟧, for continuous $\hat\Psi$; with it $T\Psi^*\hat T$ is an orientation ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrientation_unif_of_isOrientationC⟧. With "⇐" this is the equivalence under `hcont` ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientation · PolyfoldConstruction.remark_17_5_9_hyp⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The direction "⇐" is proved as printed and even for families that do not cover. The direction "⇒" is proved with `hcont`, because of Lemma 17.2.10 (G.221 settled).

*Witnesses* (in `BranchedOrientationExamples`). On $K \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the tautological structure ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.tautConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧, $K$ finite with $k * 0 = 0$, and $\Theta_0 = 1_{\{0\}}$: the oriented $0$-dimensional subspace $(T\Psi_0(T_0\{0\}), \pm)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.pointTangentC⟧ and the section $\hat T_\pm = 1 \cdot (T_0\{0\}, \pm)$ at $0$, $0$ elsewhere ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.pointOrientationC⟧. The morphisms $(k, 0)$ fix $(T_0\{0\}, \pm)$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.move_pointTangentC⟧; $f \circ \hat T_\pm = T\Theta_0$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.pointOrientationC_lifts⟧; and $\hat T_\pm$ is an orientation, through "⇐" ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrientationC_pointOrientationC⟧. The two orientations differ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.pointOrientationC_ne⟧. Instances: the reflection, where $0$ has isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrientationC_reflection⟧, and $\{1\} \ltimes \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedOrientationExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.isOrientationC_unitTaut⟧; as section functors on the reflection ⟦Chapter17/GrassmannianFunctor · PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_orientationSectionC⟧.

### XVII.56 Proposition 17.6.1 (2017: 17.11): the Whitney sum $\oplus^k TC \to C^1$, pp. 818–819

**Statement** (narrower). The $k$-fold Whitney sum $\oplus^k TC \to C^1$ has a natural polyfold bundle structure, with uniformizers $\oplus^k T\Psi : G \ltimes \oplus^k TO \to \oplus^k TC$, $(h_1, \dots, h_k) \mapsto (T\Psi(h_1), \dots, T\Psi(h_k))$, and transition sets $M(\oplus^k T\Psi, \oplus^k T\Psi')$ identified with a $k$-fold Whitney sum of $M(T\Psi, T\Psi') \to M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$, whose source and target maps are sc-smooth local bundle isomorphisms. Formalized: on the fibres ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_6_1⟧, on the transition sets ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_6_1_transition⟧, the category $\oplus^k TC$ with the functors $\oplus^k T\Psi$ on morphisms ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumCategory · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_6_1_functor⟧, and, for continuous $\hat\Psi$ as in Theorem 17.2.17, the GCT $\oplus^k TC$ with its polyfold structure $(\oplus^k TF, M)$ and the bundle $\oplus^k P : \oplus^k TC \to C^1$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_6_1_hyp⟧.

*Readings.* $M(\oplus^k T\Psi_1, \oplus^k T\Psi_2)$ is read as the text identifies it: tuples $(b, x_1, \dots, x_k)$ of a point $b \in M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)^1$ and points $x_i \in M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ over $b$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.WTrM⟧. "Metrics for the orbit spaces" is read for the uniformizers and the transition sets. "Sc-smooth local bundle isomorphism" is read as a local sc-diffeomorphism covering $s^1$ (resp. $t^1$) that is a linear isomorphism on the fibres. $\oplus^k TC$ is read as the $k$-fold fibred power of $P : TC \to C^1$ over $C^1$: an object is an object of $C^1$ with $k$ tangent vectors, a morphism a morphism $\varphi$ of $C^1$ with $k$ morphisms of $TC$ over $\varphi$ (for $k = 0$ it is $C^1$, for $k = 1$ it is $TC$). "Clearly $\oplus^k TC$ is a GCT" is read with the topology on $|\oplus^k TC|$ for which every $|\oplus^k T\Psi|$ is an open embedding; it exists and is unique. "Polyfold bundle" is read as for Theorem 17.2.17 (§XVII.28): the structures $(\oplus^k TF, M)$ on $\oplus^k TC$ and $(F^1, M_{F^1})$ on $C^1$ related by $\oplus^k P$, fibrewise linear in each entry; no strong bundle structure is involved.

**The category $\oplus^k TC$ and the functors $\oplus^k T\Psi$** ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumCategory · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_6_1_functor⟧. *Step 1.* For any functor of groupoids $P : G \to H$ the $k$-fold fibred power, with componentwise structure maps, is a groupoid with finite morphism sets and a projection to $H$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumCategory · GroupoidFunctor.whitneyGroupoid⟧; for $P : TC \to C^1$ it is a groupoidal category ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumCategory · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyCategory⟧. *Step 2.* For continuous $\hat\Psi$ (the hypothesis `hc`, as for $T\Psi$ in §XVII.55) the $k$ functors $T\Psi$, all over $\Psi^1$, assemble into $\oplus^k T\Psi : G \ltimes \oplus^k TO \to \oplus^k TC$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumCategory · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyUnifFunctor⟧ with the printed formulas on objects and morphisms; the morphism over $(g, (h_1, \dots, h_k))$ ends at $\oplus^k T\Psi(g * h_1, \dots, g * h_k)$, and $\oplus^k P \circ \oplus^k T\Psi = \Psi^1 \circ \oplus^k p$. *Witness* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumCategory · reflection_whitneyUnif_nondegenerate⟧: $k = 2$ on the reflection, a morphism of $\oplus^2 TC$ whose source and target differ.

**On the fibres.** For $q \in O^1$, $T\Psi : T_qO \to T_{\Psi(q)}C$ is the topological linear isomorphism $h \mapsto [\Psi(q), \Psi, h]$, the inverse of the chart $S_\Psi$ of Proposition 17.2.4 ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.unifTangent⟧, and $\oplus^k T\Psi$ is its $k$-fold product ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyUnifTangent⟧. *The compatibility* ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.unifTangent_transTangent⟧: for $b = (q, \varphi, q') \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$, $T\Psi' \circ T\sigma_b = T\varphi \circ T\Psi$ on $T_qO$. *Proof.* Write $v = S_\Psi(e)$ for $e \in T_{\Psi(q)}C$, and let $w$ be the representative of $e$ in the reference uniformizer at $\Psi(q)$. Both sides, read through $S_{\Psi'}$, are the image of $w$ under the transition map of one element $c$ of $M(\text{ref}, \Psi')$ over $\varphi$: the left side by the composition rule of the transition maps (Lemma 17.1.13) applied to $b$ and the unit element of $M(\text{ref}, \Psi)$, the right side by the definition of $T\varphi$ through the reference charts (Definition 17.2.6) and the same composition rule with the unit element of $M(\text{ref}, \Psi')$. Proposition 17.6.1 on the fibres is this identity in each of the $k$ factors.

**On the transition sets.** *Step 1, the identification* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTrHomeomorph⟧: Part I's Whitney sum $\oplus_k TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ is homeomorphic to $M(\oplus^k T\Psi_1, \oplus^k T\Psi_2)$, through the identification $TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2) \cong M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ of Proposition 17.2.15 on each factor. Hence $M(\oplus^k T\Psi_1, \oplus^k T\Psi_2)$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_WTrM⟧. *Step 2, the structure* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTrPolyfold⟧: Part I's Whitney sum ⟦Chapter4/TangentWhitneySum · MPolyfold.whitneyPolyfold⟧ is an M-polyfold bundle over $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)^1$ (Section 4.4) ⟦Chapter4/TangentWhitneySum · MPolyfold.whitneySum_bundle⟧, and its structure is transported along the identification, which is then an sc-diffeomorphism. The base projection and the $k$ projections to $M(T\Psi_1, T\Psi_2)$ are sc-smooth, and a map into the fibred product is sc-smooth as soon as its base and its $k$ entries are ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothOn_wtrLift⟧. *Step 3, source and target.* Under the identification, $s$ is $\oplus^k Ts$ and $t$ is $\oplus^k Tt$. Part I's Whitney sum of a local sc-diffeomorphism is a local sc-diffeomorphism (Chapter 8) ⟦Chapter8/TangentWhitneyLocal · MPolyfold.IsLocalScDiffeo.whitneyMapOf⟧, and $s$, $t$ of $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms (Definition 17.1.8 (a)); so $s$ and $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTr_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTr_isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧, and on the fibres they are the Whitney sums of the tangent maps of local sc-diffeomorphisms, hence bijective linear ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTr_bijective_fiber_src⟧. Over $b$, $t = \oplus^k T\sigma_b \circ s$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyVec_wtrTgt_eq_transMap⟧. *Step 4, unit and inversion.* The unit is $\oplus^k Tu$ and the inversion $\oplus^k T\iota$ under the identification; they are sc-smooth, the inversion an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTr_isScSmoothMap_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTr_isScDiffeoOn_inv⟧. *Step 5, orbit spaces.* $G$ acts on $\oplus^k TO$ by $g * (h_1, \dots, h_k) = (T(g*\cdot)h_1, \dots)$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.whitneyMulAction⟧, continuously; a finite group acting by homeomorphisms on the metrizable $\oplus^k TO$ has a metrizable quotient ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.metrizableSpace_whitneyOrbit⟧. Steps 1–5 are assembled in the theorem ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_6_1_transition⟧.

**The GCT $\oplus^k TC$** (for continuous $\hat\Psi$, the hypothesis `hcont`). *Step 1, the transition sets as sets.* The transition set of $\oplus^k T\Psi_1$, $\oplus^k T\Psi_2$, triples $(v, \Phi, v')$ with $\Phi$ a morphism of $\oplus^k TC$ from $\oplus^k T\Psi_1(v)$ to $\oplus^k T\Psi_2(v')$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.WTr⟧, is in bijection with $M(\oplus^k T\Psi_1, \oplus^k T\Psi_2)$ of the previous paragraph ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrEquiv⟧, compatibly with source and target ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.src_eq_wtrSrc⟧ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.tgt_eq_wtrTgt⟧. *Step 2, the maps $|\oplus^k T\Psi|$ are injective.* $\oplus^k T\Psi$ is full ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyUnifFunctor_full⟧: a morphism $\oplus^k T\Psi(v) \to \oplus^k T\Psi(v')$ has a base morphism $\Psi(x) \to \Psi(x')$, which is $\Psi(g, x)$ with $g * x = x'$ (Definition 17.1.3), and its $k$ entries are then the $T\Psi(g, h_i)$ with $g * h_i = h'_i$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyUnif_exists_smul⟧; it is faithful ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyUnifFunctor_faithful⟧ because $\Psi$ is, and injective on objects ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_whitneyUnifObj⟧. A full functor has an injective orbit map ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.injective_whitneyOrbitChart⟧. *Step 3, the overlaps* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.preimage_whitneyOrbitChart_image⟧: the points of $\oplus^k TO$ whose class lies in $|\oplus^k T\Psi'|(W)$ are the sources of the elements of $M(\oplus^k T\Psi, \oplus^k T\Psi')$ with target over $W$. For $W$ open this set is open ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isOpen_preimage_whitneyOrbitChart_image⟧: $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, hence open, and $t$ is continuous (Step 3 on the transition sets). *Step 4, the topology* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyOrbitTopology⟧: the final topology of the maps $|\oplus^k T\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyOrbitChart⟧. With Steps 2 and 3 each of them is an open embedding ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isOpenEmbedding_whitneyOrbitChart⟧. *Step 5, uniqueness* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.eq_whitneyOrbitTopology⟧: the images cover $|\oplus^k TC|$, because $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ and every object of $\oplus^k TC$ over $\Psi(q)$ is some $\oplus^k T\Psi(v)$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyUnif_isAt⟧; so a topology for which every $|\oplus^k T\Psi|$ is an open embedding is this one. *Step 6, metrizability* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_whitneyOrbitTopology⟧: $|\oplus^k P| : |\oplus^k TC| \to |C^1|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.continuous_orbitMap_whitneyCatProj⟧, since on each chart it is $|\Psi^1|$ composed with the projection $\oplus^k TO \to O^1$; $|C^1|$ is metrizable (Theorem 17.1.28); the preimage of the footprint of $\Psi^1$ is the image of $|\oplus^k T\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.preimage_footprint_eq_range⟧, because an object over an object isomorphic to $\Psi(x)$ is isomorphic to some $\oplus^k T\Psi(v)$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.exists_whitneyUnifObj_isomorphic⟧, and this image is homeomorphic to the metrizable $G \backslash \oplus^k TO$ ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · metrizableSpace_translation_orbitSpace⟧. Smirnov's theorem pulled back along $|\oplus^k P|$ ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · metrizableSpace_of_pullback⟧ gives the metric, as for $|TX|$ in Theorem 8.1.15. *Step 7* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyGCT⟧: $\oplus^k TC$ with this topology is a GCT, and $\oplus^k T\Psi$, on the M-polyfold $\oplus^k TO$ of Part I with $G$ acting entrywise by $T(g * \cdot)$, is a local uniformizer of it ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyUnif⟧.

**The polyfold structure $(\oplus^k TF, M)$.** *Step 1, the M-polyfold $M(\oplus^k T\Psi_1, \oplus^k T\Psi_2)$* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.WTrN⟧: the transition set of Step 1 above with the topology carried over from $M(\oplus^k T\Psi_1, \oplus^k T\Psi_2)$ of the previous paragraph; the identification $\oplus_k TM(\Psi_1, \Psi_2) \cong M(\oplus^k T\Psi_1, \oplus^k T\Psi_2)$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrNEquiv⟧ carries Part I's Whitney sum to it ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrNPolyfold⟧, and is then an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_wtrNEquiv⟧. *Step 2, source, target, unit, inversion.* Under the identification $s$, $t$, $u$, $\iota$ are $\oplus^k Ts$, $\oplus^k Tt$, $\oplus^k Tu$, $\oplus^k T\iota$, Part I's Whitney sums of the structure maps of $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$: $s$ and $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrN_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrN_isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧, and $u$ and $\iota$ are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrN_isScSmoothMap_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrN_isScSmoothMap_inv⟧. *Step 3, the multiplication* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.wtrN_isScSmoothMap_mul⟧: sc-smoothness on the fibred product is local ⟦Chapter17/LevelStructures · MPolyfold.isScSmoothOnFibredProduct_of_local⟧. Near a pair $(a_1, a_2)$, take a sheet $\sigma$ of the source map of $M(\Psi_2, \Psi_3)$ through the base of $a_1$, defined on an open $V \subset O_2$ (Definition 17.1.8 (a)). On the elements $b$ of $M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$ with $t(b) \in V$ the map $k_0 : b \mapsto m(\sigma(t(b)), b)$ is sc-smooth (Definition 17.1.8 (c) for $M$, on the sheet), with $s(k_0(b)) = s(b)$ and morphism $\sigma(t(b)) \circ b$. On the open set of pairs $(p_1, p_2)$ whose first base lies in the domain of the sheet and whose second base has target in $V$, the product is $\oplus^k Tk_0$ ⟦Chapter8/TangentWhitneyLocal · MPolyfold.whitneyMapOn⟧ applied to $p_2$, read through the identifications: the sources agree, since $s \circ k_0 = s$ near $b$ and so $T(s \circ k_0) = Ts$ there, and the morphisms agree, since $\sigma$ inverts the source map on its domain, so $\sigma(t(p_2)) = p_1$ on the base. So the multiplication is an sc-smooth map composed with $\mathrm{pr}_2$ near every point. *Step 4, the structure* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyConstruction⟧: $\oplus^k TF(\beta)$ is the set of the $\oplus^k T\Psi$, $\Psi \in F(\oplus^k P(\beta))$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyF⟧, each at $\beta$ by Step 5 above; $M(L, L')$ is the M-polyfold of Step 1 for $\Psi$ chosen with $L = \oplus^k T\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyData⟧, and Steps 2 and 3 are Definition 17.1.8 (a)–(c). From $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, $\oplus^k TF(\beta) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyConstruction_hasUniformizers⟧.

**The bundle $\oplus^k P : \oplus^k TC \to C^1$.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTrBundleProj⟧: the projection $M(\oplus^k T\Psi, \oplus^k T\Psi') \to M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$, $(v, \Phi, v') \mapsto (x, \varphi, x')$, is the base projection of Part I's Whitney sum, read in $M(\Psi, \Psi')^1 = M(\Psi^1, \Psi'^1)$ (Theorem 17.1.28); it is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isScSmoothMap_whitneyTrBundleProj⟧, commutes with $s$ and $t$ and keeps the morphism of $C$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyTrBundleProj_spec⟧. *Step 2, the theorem* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_6_1_hyp⟧: for $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, continuous $\hat\Psi$ and every $k$, `whitneyGCT` has groupoid $\oplus^k TC$; its topology is the only one for which the $|\oplus^k T\Psi|$ are open embeddings; $(\oplus^k TF, M)$ has uniformizers at every object, with $\oplus^k TF(\beta)$ as in Step 4; $\oplus_k TM(\Psi, \Psi') \to M(\oplus^k T\Psi, \oplus^k T\Psi')$ is an sc-diffeomorphism; $\oplus^k P \circ \oplus^k T\Psi = \Psi^1 \circ \oplus^k p$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumCategory · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.whitneyBaseFunctor⟧; on the fibres $\oplus^k T\Psi$ is $T\Psi$ in each entry; $|\oplus^k P|^{-1}$ of the footprint of $\Psi^1$ is the footprint of $\oplus^k T\Psi$; the projections of Step 1 are sc-smooth and compatible with $s$, $t$ and the morphisms; and $|\oplus^k P|$ is continuous. *Witness* ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_proposition_17_6_1⟧: on the reflection $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, where every $\hat\Psi$ is continuous ⟦Chapter17/TangentConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_continuous_hat_all⟧, the structure $(\oplus^2 TF, M)$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumPolyfold · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.reflectionWhitneyConstruction⟧ has uniformizers at every object, and $\oplus^2 T\Psi_0$ sends $(-1, (e, e))$ to a morphism whose source and target differ: the isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ of $0$ acts non-trivially on $\oplus^2 T_0\mathbb R$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text gives the polyfold bundle $\oplus^k TC \to C^1$ with "clearly" and "easily verified"; the formalization builds it from its local ingredients (the fibre maps, the transition M-polyfold bundles with their structure maps, the orbit metrics, the category and the functors), with the hypothesis on $\hat\Psi$ of Theorem 17.2.17, which the functors $\oplus^k T\Psi$ on morphisms need (point G.222). The metric on $|\oplus^k TC|$ comes from $|C^1|$ through $|\oplus^k P|$, as for $|TX|$ in Theorem 8.1.15. For $k = 1$ the topology is not compared with that of $|TC|$ (§XVII.27). As for $(TF, M)$, $M(L, L')$ uses a chosen $\Psi$ with $L = \oplus^k T\Psi$.

*Witness.* For $k = 2$ on the reflection $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, a point of $M(\oplus^2 T\Psi_0, \oplus^2 T\Psi_0)$ over $\hat\Psi(-1, 0)$ with $t = (-1) * s \ne s$ ⟦Chapter17/WhitneySumStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_whitneyTr_nondegenerate⟧: the isotropy acts non-trivially on the tangent vectors.

### XVII.57 The independence on p. 819 and Definition 17.6.2 (2017: 17.19): sc-differential forms on $C$, p. 819

**Statement** (explicit). *Definition 17.6.2*: $\Omega^k(C)$ consists of the functors $\omega : \oplus^k TC \to \mathbb R$, alternating and linear in each argument, with $\omega_\Psi$ sc-smooth for a covering set of uniformizers ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_6_2⟧. *The sentence before it*: if $\omega_\Psi$ is sc-smooth for a covering set, it is for every uniformizer of $F$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.unifRep_mem_scForms_of_isCoveringFamily⟧.

*Readings.* $\omega$ is read as a family $\omega_\alpha$ of alternating $k$-linear maps on $T_\alpha C$ for the objects of $C^1$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.CatAltForm⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.TObj⟧, constant along the morphisms of $\oplus^k TC$: $\omega_{\alpha'}(T\varphi v_1, \dots, T\varphi v_k) = \omega_\alpha(v_1, \dots, v_k)$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.IsFunctorialForm⟧. $\omega_\Psi = \Psi^*\omega = \omega \circ \oplus^k T\Psi$ on the fibres $T_qO$, $q \in O^1$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.unifRep⟧, and "$\omega_\Psi$ sc-smooth" is read as: an sc-differential form on $O$ (Definition 4.4.1). $\Omega^k(C)$ is defined with the condition for every uniformizer of $F$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catForms⟧ (a submodule: sums, multiples and $0$ keep both conditions), and the printed form is proved equivalent. Standing reading `hU`.

**Step 1, $s^*\Psi^*\omega = t^*\Psi'^*\omega$ on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.formPullback_trSrc_unifRep⟧. At a point $a = (q, \varphi, q')$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')^1$ and tangent vectors $\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_k$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ there, $Tt(\alpha_l) = T\sigma_a(Ts(\alpha_l))$ (the target map is $\sigma_a$ composed with the source map near $a$, Lemma 17.1.13). By the compatibility of §XVII.56, $T\Psi'(T\sigma_a(Ts\,\alpha_l)) = T\varphi(T\Psi(Ts\,\alpha_l))$, and the functor property of $\omega$ removes $T\varphi$. So both pull-backs give the same value.

**Step 2, descent along a cover** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · MPolyfold.isScForm_of_formPullback_cover⟧. For jointly surjective local sc-diffeomorphisms $q_i : X_i \to Y$, a family on $Y$ whose pull-backs by all $q_i$ are sc-differential forms is an sc-differential form. *Proof.* Being a form is local (Part I). Near $y = q_i(z)$ choose a local inverse $g$ of $q_i$ on an open $V \ni y$; the restriction of the family to $V$ is $g^*$ of $q_i^*$ of it, which is a form; a chart of $Y$ at $y$ restricted to $V$ then witnesses smoothness at $y$. The pull-backs also determine the family ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · MPolyfold.formPullback_injective_of_cover⟧, since every tangent vector of $Y$ lifts.

**Step 3, the independence on p. 819** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.unifRep_mem_scForms_of_isCoveringFamily⟧. For any uniformizer $\Psi$ of $F$ and a covering family $\Phi$, the source maps $s : M(\Psi, \Phi_\lambda) \to O$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms, jointly surjective because every $q \in O$ is the source of a transition element into some $\Phi_\lambda$ (as in §XVII.51). By Step 1, $s^*\Psi^*\omega = t^*\Phi_\lambda^*\omega$, which is a form since $\Phi_\lambda^*\omega$ is. Step 2 gives that $\Psi^*\omega$ is a form. **Definition 17.6.2** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_6_2⟧ follows: the condition for every uniformizer gives it for the covering family of $TC$, and Step 3 gives the converse. *Witness:* the constant $0$-forms lie in $\Omega^0(C)$, with $\Psi^*r = r$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catConstForm_mem⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The functor $\omega$ on $\oplus^k TC$ is read pointwise as a family of alternating maps; the category $\oplus^k TC$ is not needed for it (G.223 settled).

### XVII.58 The levels $C^i$, $\Omega^k_\infty(C)$, the differential $d$ and (17.88), Definition 17.6.3 (2017: 17.20): the de Rham complex, pp. 819–820

**Statement** (restated). (pp. 819–820) The forms on the levels $C^i$ form a direct system with limit $\Omega^k_\infty(C)$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsInfty⟧, with the differential $d[\omega] = [\Gamma_* d(\Gamma^*\omega)]$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsInftyD⟧ and (17.88) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsInftyD_catFormsToInfty⟧. *Definition 17.6.3* (p. 820): $(\Omega^*_\infty(C), d)$ is the de Rham complex of $C$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_6_3⟧.

*Readings.* The direct limit over the levels $C^i$ is read through the sc-smooth models, as the second definition of $d$ on p. 820 does: a class is a family $[\omega_\Psi] \in \Omega^k_{\infty,\mathrm{ep}}(X_\Psi)$ over all covering families, with $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}^*[\omega_{\Psi'}] = [\omega_\Psi]$ for the canonical generalized isomorphisms. The forms $\Omega^k(C^i)$ for $i \ge 1$ are not formalized separately; $\Omega^k(C)$ enters through the map $\omega \mapsto [\omega]$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsToInfty⟧. This is the reading on which $d$ is defined and $d \circ d = 0$ (G.224 settled).

**The pull-back to a model.** $\Gamma^*\omega$ on $X_\Phi = \bigsqcup O_\lambda$ is the form with $\Psi_\lambda^*\omega$ on the summand $O_\lambda$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.modelRep⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.formPullback_objMk_modelRep⟧, built from Part I's form on a disjoint union ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · MPolyfold.sigmaForm⟧. *It is an sc-differential form on the ep-groupoid $X_\Phi$* ("by definition", p. 820) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.modelRep_mem_epForms⟧: it is sc-smooth on each summand because $\omega \in \Omega^k(C)$, and it is compatible with the morphisms, $s^*\Gamma^*\omega = t^*\Gamma^*\omega$ on the morphism M-polyfold $\bigsqcup M(\Psi_\lambda, \Psi_\mu)$, because on each summand this is Step 1 of §XVII.57. Its class $[\Gamma^*\omega] \in \Omega^k_{\infty,\mathrm{ep}}(X_\Phi)$ at level $0$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.modelFormInfty⟧.

**$f^*[\omega_{\Psi'}] = [\omega_\Psi]$** (used on p. 821) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.formsPullback_comparison_modelFormInfty⟧. *Step 1.* For a reindexing $\sigma$, the model map pulls $\Gamma_\Phi^*\omega$ back to $\Gamma_{\Phi\circ\sigma}^*\omega$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.formPullback_modelMap_modelRep⟧ (compare on summands), so the inclusion of the model of a subfamily pulls the class back to the class ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.formsPullback_incl_modelFormInfty⟧. *Step 2.* $f_{c,c'}$ composed with the inclusion of $X_{c'}$ into the model of the union $c \sqcup c'$ is the inclusion of $X_c$ (Theorem 17.1.18) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_comp_incl⟧. Since pull-back along generalized maps is contravariantly functorial (Part XI), $f^*[\omega_{c'}] = f^*\mathrm{incl}_{c'}^*[\omega_{c\sqcup c'}] = \mathrm{incl}_c^*[\omega_{c\sqcup c'}] = [\omega_c]$.

**$\Omega^k_\infty(C)$ and one model** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsInftyEquiv⟧. For a covering family $c_0$, the evaluation $[\omega] \mapsto [\omega]_{c_0}$ is a linear isomorphism $\Omega^k_\infty(C) \cong \Omega^k_{\infty,\mathrm{ep}}(X_{c_0})$, with inverse $\eta \mapsto (f_{c,c_0}^*\eta)_c$. *Proof.* The inverse lands in $\Omega^k_\infty(C)$ because $f_{c,c'}^*f_{c',c_0}^* = f_{c,c_0}^*$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.formsPullback_comparison_comp_apply⟧, from $f_{c',c_0} \circ f_{c,c'} = f_{c,c_0}$ (Theorem 17.1.18) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · PolyfoldConstruction.comparison_comp⟧; the two composites are the identity by the compatibility condition and by $f_{c_0,c_0} = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.formsPullback_comparison_self_apply⟧.

**The differential.** $d$ is applied componentwise: $(d[\omega])_c = d([\omega]_c)$ in $\Omega^*_{\infty,\mathrm{ep}}(X_c)$. The family is again compatible, because $d$ commutes with pull-back along generalized isomorphisms (Theorem 11.2.1 (2), Part XI) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.epFormsInftyD_formsPullback_comparison⟧. *$d \circ d = 0$* ("since it holds for ep-groupoids", p. 820) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsInftyD_catFormsInftyD⟧: componentwise from $d \circ d = 0$ on each $\Omega^*_{\infty,\mathrm{ep}}(X_c)$. *Independence of the choices* (p. 820) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsInftyEquiv_catFormsInftyD⟧: the isomorphism with one model intertwines the two differentials, by definition of the componentwise $d$. *(17.88)* ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsInftyD_catFormsToInfty⟧: on each model, $d$ of the class of $\Gamma^*\omega$ is the class at level $1$ of $d(\Gamma^*\omega)$, Chapter 11's formula for the differential of a class of level $0$. The map $\omega \mapsto [\omega]$ is linear ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.catFormsToInfty⟧, through the linearity of $\Gamma^*$ on summands ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.modelRep_add⟧.

**Proof of Definition 17.6.3** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryForms · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_6_3⟧. The three clauses are $d \circ d = 0$, the intertwining with $d$ on every model, and the compatibility of the components on two covering families with $f_{c,c'}^*$, which is the defining condition of $\Omega^k_\infty(C)$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text defines $\Omega^k_\infty(C)$ as the direct limit over the levels of $C$ and then computes $d$ on models; the formalization takes the models as the definition and proves that any one model represents the whole. Nothing in §§17.6–19 uses the forms on $C^i$ other than through a model.

### XVII.59 Theorem 17.6.4 (2017: 17.5) and (17.89): the integral over a tame oriented compact $\hat\Theta$, pp. 820–821

**Statement** (restated). For $[\omega] \in \Omega^n_\infty(C)$ and $\hat\Theta = (\Theta, \hat T_\Theta)$ tame, oriented, of dimension $n$, with $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ compact, $\int_{\hat\Theta_\Psi}[\omega_\Psi]$ does not depend on the covering family ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_4⟧, and (17.89) defines $\int_{\hat\Theta}[\omega]$ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.integral⟧.

*Readings.* An oriented branched ep⁺-subcategory is read through the models ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory⟧: a branched ep⁺-subcategory $\Theta$ together with, for every covering family, an orientation $\Gamma^*\hat T_\Theta$ of $\Gamma^*\Theta$ on $X_\Psi$ (Definition 9.3.4), the family compatible with the canonical generalized isomorphisms, $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}^*(\Gamma'^*\hat T) = \Gamma^*\hat T$. The oriented Grassmannian of $TC$ (§XVII.55) enters only through the models. $\hat\Theta_\Psi = (\Theta \circ \Gamma, \Gamma^*\hat T)$ is the oriented branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of the model ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.toOriented⟧. "Tame, oriented, of dimension $n$" is Chapter 9's notion on the model: every point of $\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ has an oriented tame structure of dimension $n$ describing $\Gamma^*\hat T$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.IsTameOrientedVia⟧. $n \ge 1$, the integration frame of Chapter 11. The integral on the model is Chapter 11's branched integral of the class $[\omega]_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.integralVia⟧.

**Step 1, $f^*\hat\Theta_{\Psi'} = \hat\Theta_\Psi$** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.pull_toOriented⟧. The function parts agree by the first identity of p. 813 (§XVII.51), and the orientations by the compatibility field of the oriented subcategory. *Step 2, $f^*[\omega_{\Psi'}] = [\omega_\Psi]$*: the defining condition of $\Omega^n_\infty(C)$ (§XVII.58). *Step 3, compactness on every model* ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.isCompactBranched_toOriented⟧: by the compactness criterion of §XVII.52. *Step 4, Theorem 11.4.1.* The branched integral is invariant under pull-back along a generalized isomorphism, with $K$ the whole support (Chapter 16's form of Theorem 11.4.1) ⟦Chapter16/BranchedSuborbifold · EPLoc.tameBranchedIntegral_pull⟧. With Steps 1–3, $\int_{\hat\Theta_\Psi}[\omega_\Psi] = \int_{f^*\hat\Theta_{\Psi'}} f^*[\omega_{\Psi'}] = \int_{\hat\Theta_{\Psi'}}[\omega_{\Psi'}]$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_4⟧.

**Tameness moves between covering families** ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.isTameOrientedVia_of_family⟧. Theorem 17.6.4 as stated assumes tameness on both families; the text assumes it of $\hat\Theta$ on $C$. *Step 1, on ep-groupoids* ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedBranched.exists_tame_pull⟧: for a generalized isomorphism $f : X \to Y$, if every point of $\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta'$ has an oriented tame structure of dimension $n$ describing $\hat T'$, so does every point of $\mathrm{supp}\,f^*\Theta'$ for $f^*\hat T'$. These are the structures that the proof of Theorem 11.4.1 builds through a representative $X \xleftarrow{F} A \xrightarrow{\Phi} Y$ (Part XI). *Step 2.* Applied to $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ and $\hat\Theta_{\Psi'}$, with Step 1 above, tameness on $\Psi'$ gives tameness on $\Psi$. So tameness on one model is tameness on every model ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.isTameOrientedVia_iff⟧, "tame, oriented, of dimension $n$" on $C$ is defined as tameness on some model ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.IsTameOriented⟧, equivalently on every model ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.isTameOriented_iff_forall⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · IsTameOriented.via⟧. The integral (17.89) on $C$ is computed on the family that the definition provides, and agrees with the integral on any covering family ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.integral_eq_integralVia⟧; Theorem 17.6.4 with the hypothesis on one family only is ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_4_book⟧.

**Oriented subcategories from one model** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.ofModel⟧. An orientation $\hat T_0$ of $\Gamma_0^*\Theta$ on one model $X_{\Psi_0}$ gives an oriented subcategory, by $\Gamma^*\hat T := f_{\Psi,\Psi_0}^*\hat T_0$ on every other model (Theorem 11.3.5); the compatibility condition follows from $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}^*f_{\Psi',\Psi_0}^* = f_{\Psi,\Psi_0}^*$, and on $X_{\Psi_0}$ itself the orientation is $\hat T_0$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.ofModel_T_self⟧. If $\hat T_0$ is described by oriented tame structures of dimension $n$, the result is tame and oriented on every model ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.ofModel_isTameOrientedVia⟧, by Step 1 of the previous paragraph.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The orientation is carried as its pull-backs to all models; the Grassmannian of $TC$ is defined (§XVII.55) but the integrals use only the models, which is what the text's proof uses.

### XVII.60 Definition 17.6.5 (2017: 17.21), Theorem 17.6.6 (2017: 17.6) and Theorem 17.6.7 (2017: 17.7): the boundary, the boundary integral and Stokes, pp. 822–823

**Statement.** *Definition 17.6.5* (same; p. 822): $\partial\Theta := \Gamma_*(\partial(\Gamma^*\Theta))$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.boundaryVia⟧, independent of the covering family ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.definition_17_6_5⟧. *Theorem 17.6.6* (same; p. 822): for $\hat\Theta$ tame, oriented, compact of dimension $n \ge 2$ and $[\omega] \in \Omega^{n-1}_\infty(C)$, $\int_{\partial\hat\Theta}[\omega] := \int_{\partial(\Gamma^*\hat\Theta)}[\omega_\Psi]$ does not depend on $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_6⟧; for $n = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_6_dim1⟧. *Theorem 17.6.7* (same; p. 823), Stokes (17.90): $\int_{\hat\Theta} d[\omega] = \int_{\partial\hat\Theta}[\omega]$, on every covering family ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_dim1_all⟧ and for the integrals on $C$ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_book_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_dim1_book_all⟧.

*Readings.* The text's $n \ge 2$ is written $m = n - 1 \ge 1$; $n = 1$ uses the boundary measures of Theorem 9.5.15 for $n = 1$ (the `_dim1` forms). $\partial\hat\Theta$ carries the induced orientation of $\partial(\Gamma^*\hat\Theta)$ on the model, and the boundary integrals are the measures of Theorems 9.5.15, 9.5.16 on the whole support. *Stokes on the model.* The text's proof is that the statement "is equivalent to the ep-groupoid version". The ep-groupoid version without partitions of unity is the assertion of Remark 9.5.19 (`StokesWithoutPartitions`) ⟦Chapter16/StokesWithoutPartitions · EP.StokesWithoutPartitions⟧. It holds for every ep-groupoid (Part IX, §IX.39) ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · EP.stokesWithoutPartitions_all⟧, in particular on every model $X_\Psi$, so Theorem 17.6.7 needs no hypothesis beyond the text's (point G.153 settled). With sc-smooth partitions of unity on the model it also follows from Theorems 9.5.17, 9.5.18 as printed ⟦Chapter16/StokesWithoutPartitions · EP.stokesWithoutPartitions_of_hasScPartitionsOfUnity⟧.

**Proof of Definition 17.6.5** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.definition_17_6_5⟧. The chain is $\Gamma_*(\partial\Gamma^*\Theta) = \Gamma'_*f_*(\partial\Gamma^*\Theta) = \Gamma'_*(\partial f_*\Gamma^*\Theta) = \Gamma'_*(\partial\Gamma'^*\Theta)$. The first equality is the third identity of p. 813 (§XVII.51), applicable because $\partial(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ is a functor on $X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.isFunctorial_boundaryTheta_toOriented⟧. The second is Theorem 11.3.8 in its push-forward form: the boundary of a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid commutes with push-forward along a generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter11/BranchedEquivalenceTangent · EPLoc.theorem_11_3_8_push⟧. The third is the second identity of p. 813, $f_*\Gamma^*\Theta = \Gamma'^*\Theta$. Moreover $\Gamma^*(\partial\Theta) = \partial(\Gamma^*\Theta)$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.modelPull_boundaryVia⟧ ($\Gamma^*\Gamma_* = \mathrm{id}$ on functors), so the boundary integrals below are over $\partial(\Gamma^*\hat\Theta)$, and $\partial\Theta$ is a functor on $C$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.isFunctorial_boundaryVia⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 17.6.6** (the text's "similar arguments", written out). *Step 1, the supports* ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · EPLoc.orbitSupp_boundaryTheta_eq_image_pullTheta⟧: for a generalized isomorphism $f$ and branched $\Theta'$, $|\mathrm{supp}\,\partial\Theta'| = |f|(|\mathrm{supp}\,\partial f^*\Theta'|)$, from Theorem 11.3.8 ($\partial f^*\Theta' = f^*\partial\Theta'$) and the support correspondence under $|f|$; so the preimage of the image of the whole support is the whole support ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · EPLoc.preimage_image_univ_boundaryTheta⟧. *Step 2, invariance* ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · EPLoc.boundaryMeasure_pull_univ⟧: Theorem 11.4.4 in its local form with the text's hypotheses ⟦Chapter11/BoundaryMeasureTextHypotheses · EPLoc.theorem_11_4_4_pull_loc⟧ gives $\int_{\partial f^*\hat\Theta'} f^*\omega'$ over $|f|^{-1}(K)$ equal to $\int_{\partial\hat\Theta'}\omega'$ over $K$; with $K$ the whole support, Step 1 identifies $|f|^{-1}(K)$ with the whole support. The same for dimension $1$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · EPLoc.boundaryBranchedMeasureDim1_pull_univ⟧. *Step 3.* Exactly as for Theorem 17.6.4, with $f = f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, $f^*\hat\Theta_{\Psi'} = \hat\Theta_\Psi$ and $f^*[\omega_{\Psi'}] = [\omega_\Psi]$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.boundaryIntegralVia⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_6⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.boundaryIntegralDim1Via⟧. On $C$: the boundary integral ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.boundaryIntegral⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.boundaryIntegralDim1⟧, and the theorem with the hypothesis on one family ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_6_book⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_6_dim1_book⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 17.6.7.** *On one model* ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_of_epStokes⟧: $d[\omega]$ has component $d([\omega]_\Psi)$ on $X_\Psi$ (§XVII.58), so the left side is $\int_{\hat\Theta_\Psi} d[\omega]_\Psi$ and the right side $\int_{\partial\hat\Theta_\Psi}[\omega]_\Psi$; `hS` asserts exactly this equality on $X_\Psi$. With `hPU` in place of `hS` ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_hyp⟧; for $n = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_dim1_of_epStokes⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_dim1_hyp⟧. *On every model from one* ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_of_epStokes_of_family⟧: Theorem 17.6.4 moves the left side from $\Psi$ to $\Psi_0$, Stokes holds on $\Psi_0$, and Theorem 17.6.6 moves the right side back. *On $C$* ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_book⟧: both integrals on $C$ are computed on $\Psi_0$, where `hS` holds; also with `hPU` ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_book_hyp⟧ and for $n = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_dim1_book⟧. *As printed.* `hS` holds on the model of every covering family ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · EP.stokesWithoutPartitions_all⟧, and the four forms above with `hS` discharged are the theorem as printed ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_dim1_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_book_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_7_dim1_book_all⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Nowhere in the statement: Theorem 17.6.7 is proved as printed. The intermediate forms carry `hS` or `hPU` on one model; `hS` is discharged on every ep-groupoid (G.225 settled, G.153 settled).

*Witness* (in `TameOrientedExamples`). The closed half-circle in the half-plane polyfold $\{1\} \ltimes ([0, \infty) \times \mathbb R)$ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneFamily⟧: $\Theta = \Gamma_*(E_*1_{\mathrm{arc}})$ for the equivalence $E$ from Chapter 9's half-plane ep-groupoid ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneTheta⟧, with the orientation $E_*\hat T$ through `ofModel` ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneArc⟧. It has non-empty support, compact $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$, is tame and oriented of dimension $1$ on every covering family, and $\partial\Theta = 1$ at an end point on every covering family ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneArc_boundaryVia_eq_one⟧; the model has sc-smooth partitions of unity ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneModel_hasScPartitionsOfUnity⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneArc_properties⟧. Stokes holds for it ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneArc_stokes⟧. The group is trivial, so the witness is weak in the isotropy.

### XVII.61 Theorems 17.6.8, 17.6.9 and 17.6.10 (new in the 2021 edition): the effective integrals under (LUC) and (17.92), pp. 823–824

**Statement.** For $C$ with (LUC) (Definition 17.1.22): *Theorem 17.6.8* (same; p. 823): $\oint_{\hat\Theta_\Psi}[\omega_\Psi]$ does not depend on $\Psi$, defining (17.91) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_8⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.integralEff⟧; *Theorem 17.6.9* (same; p. 824): the same for $\oint_{\partial\hat\Theta}$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_9⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_9_dim1⟧; *Theorem 17.6.10* (same; p. 824), (17.92): $\oint_{\hat\Theta} d[\omega] = \oint_{\partial\hat\Theta}[\omega]$, on every covering family ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_dim1_all⟧ and for the integrals on $C$ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_book_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_dim1_book_all⟧.

*Hypotheses.* None beyond the text's (LUC) of $C$: the intermediate forms carry `hS` or `hPU` on one model, as for Theorem 17.6.7, and `hS` holds on every ep-groupoid (G.153 settled).

**(LUC) on every model** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.isLUC_model⟧. (LUC) for $C$ is (LUC) on the model of some covering family, and it is invariant under generalized isomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · PolyfoldConstruction.isGenIsoInvariant_isLUC⟧; so it holds on every model.

**Proof of Theorem 17.6.8** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_8⟧. The effective integral on the model ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.integralEffVia⟧ is Chapter 11's effective branched integral, which needs (LUC) on the model; it is invariant under pull-back along a generalized isomorphism between ep-groupoids with (LUC) (Theorem 11.4.2, in Chapter 16's form) ⟦Chapter16/BranchedSuborbifold · EPLoc.tameBranchedIntegralEff_pull⟧. With $f^*\hat\Theta_{\Psi'} = \hat\Theta_\Psi$ and $f^*[\omega_{\Psi'}] = [\omega_\Psi]$ the proof is that of Theorem 17.6.4.

**Proof of Theorem 17.6.9** ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_9⟧. As Theorem 17.6.6, with Theorem 11.4.5 (the effective boundary integral is invariant under pull-back, for ep-groupoids with (LUC)) in place of Theorem 11.4.4 ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · EPLoc.boundaryMeasureEff_pull_univ⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · EPLoc.boundaryBranchedMeasureEffDim1_pull_univ⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.boundaryIntegralEffVia⟧. On $C$, with the hypothesis on one family ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_8_book⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_9_book⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_9_dim1_book⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 17.6.10.** `hS` includes the effective form of Stokes on the model, which applies because the model has (LUC) ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_of_epStokes⟧; with `hPU` ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_hyp⟧; for $n = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_dim1_of_epStokes⟧. On $C$ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_book⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_book_hyp⟧ ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedTransfer · OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_dim1_book⟧. *As printed*, with `hS` from ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · EP.stokesWithoutPartitions_all⟧: ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_dim1_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_book_all⟧ ⟦Chapter16/StokesUnconditional · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.theorem_17_6_10_dim1_book_all⟧.

*Witnesses.* $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with the trivial action and its tautological structure has (LUC), sc-smooth partitions of unity and `StokesWithoutPartitions` on the model ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokesExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.z3Model_hasScPartitionsOfUnity⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokesExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.z3Model_stokesWithoutPartitions⟧ ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokesExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.z3Taut_hypotheses_17_6_10⟧ (weak: trivial action). The half-plane polyfold has (LUC) ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlane_isLUC⟧ and the half-circle satisfies (17.92) ⟦Chapter17/TameOrientedExamples · PolyfoldConstruction.halfPlaneArc_stokes_eff⟧ (weak: trivial group).

### XVII.62 The category $E_\mu$ and Definitions 17.7.1–17.7.4 (2017: 17.22–17.25): vector bundle GCTs, strong bundle uniformizers and strong polyfold bundle constructions, pp. 824–828

**Statement.** *The functor $\mu$ and $E_\mu$* (same; pp. 824–825): for $\mu : C \to \mathrm{Ban}$ ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · GroupoidalCategory.BanachFunctor⟧ the category $E_\mu$ is a GCT ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.toGroupoidalCategory⟧ with a projection $P : E_\mu \to C$ ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.proj⟧. *Definition 17.7.1* (same; p. 825) ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · GCT.VectorBundle⟧. *Definition 17.7.2* (restated; p. 826) ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · VectorBundle.StrongBundleUniformizer⟧. *Definition 17.7.3* (restated; pp. 826–827) ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · VectorBundle.StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction⟧. *Definition 17.7.4* (restated; pp. 827–828) ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · VectorBundle.StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction⟧. G.226 settled.

**The category $E_\mu$.** $\mathrm{Ban}$ is read as real Banach spaces with topological linear isomorphisms; $\mu$ is carried by the action $(\varphi, (s\varphi, e)) \mapsto (t\varphi, \hat\varphi(e))$ with the functor laws ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.act⟧. Objects are pairs $(\alpha, e)$, $e \in \mu(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.EObj⟧, morphisms pairs $(\varphi, e)$ with $e$ over $s\varphi$ ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.EMor⟧. *Step 1, a groupoid* ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.groupoid⟧: composition is that of $C$ in the first entry; the inverse of $(\varphi, e)$ is $(\varphi^{-1}, \hat\varphi(e))$, by $\widehat{\varphi^{-1}}\hat\varphi = 1$. *Step 2, finite morphism sets* ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.finite_mor⟧: a morphism $(\alpha, e) \to (\alpha', e')$ is determined by its first entry, a morphism $\alpha \to \alpha'$ of $C$, of which there are finitely many. *Step 3, the fibres* ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.fiberEquiv⟧ ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · BanachFunctor.morFiberEquiv⟧: $P^{-1}(\alpha) \cong \mu(\alpha)$ and $P^{-1}(\varphi) \cong \mu(s\varphi)$, which carry the linear structure of p. 825.

**Definition 17.7.1** ⟦Chapter17/VectorBundleGCT · GCT.VectorBundle⟧: a functor $\mu$ with a metrizable topology on $|E_\mu|$ for which (1) every $P^{-1}(\alpha) \to |E|$ is continuous and (2) $|P|$ is surjective, continuous and open. *Witness* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflV⟧: $\mu(x) = \mathbb R$ over the reflection $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $-1$ acting by $v \mapsto -v$, which is not the identity ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflV_map_ne⟧; the topology is transported from $|\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (\mathbb R \times \mathbb R)|$.

**Definition 17.7.2.** *Readings.* "$\bar\Psi$ covers $\Psi$" is read as $P \circ \bar\Psi = \Psi \circ p$; such a functor is the same as its fibre maps $K_q \to P^{-1}(\Psi(q))$, so the structure carries the fibre maps, as Banach space isomorphisms, and builds the functor from them; (2) prints $P^{-1}(\Psi(\alpha))$, read as $P^{-1}(\Psi(q))$; "an action by strong bundle isomorphisms" is read as strong bundle maps $k \mapsto g * k$ covering $q \mapsto g * q$ (their inverses are of the same kind) ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · VectorBundle.StrongBundleUniformizerData⟧. *Step 1, the functor* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.functor⟧: $\bar\Psi(k) = (\Psi(p k), \text{fibre map}(k))$ and $\bar\Psi(g, k) = (\Psi(g, p k), \bar\Psi(k))$; it is a functor because the fibre maps are compatible with the action. *Step 2, (2) follows* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.obj_injective⟧ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.faithful⟧ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.full⟧: injectivity on objects from that of $\Psi$ and of the fibre maps; faithfulness from that of $\Psi$; fullness: a morphism $(\varphi, \bar\Psi(k)) : \bar\Psi(k) \to \bar\Psi(k')$ lies over $\varphi : \Psi(p k) \to \Psi(p k')$, which is $\Psi(g, p k)$ for some $g$ since $\Psi$ is full; then $\bar\Psi(g * k) = \hat\varphi(\bar\Psi(k)) = \bar\Psi(k')$, and injectivity gives $g * k = k'$. *Step 3, (3)*: $|\bar\Psi|$ is an open embedding with image $|P|^{-1}(U)$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizer.footprint_eq⟧. *Witness* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflUnif⟧: the trivial bundle $\mathbb R \times \mathbb R \to \mathbb R$ with $g * (x, v) = (gx, gv)$ and fibre maps $(x, v) \mapsto v$; non-trivial action and non-zero fibres ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflUnif_witness⟧.

**Definition 17.7.3 and the transition sets.** *Reading.* The printed $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to C$ is read as $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to E$, as in Definitions 17.7.2 and 17.7.4. The construction is a set $\bar F(\alpha)$ of strong bundle uniformizers whose bases lie in $F(\alpha)$. *"$(\alpha, 0)$ in the image"* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizer.exists_obj_eq_zero⟧ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.exists_zero_mem⟧: holds for every strong bundle uniformizer at $\alpha$: $\alpha = \Psi(q)$ for some $q$, and the zero of $K_q$ maps to $(\alpha, 0)$ by linearity. *The transition set* $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is the weak fibred product of $\bar\Psi$ and $\bar\Psi'$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.Trans⟧, with the natural map $(k, (\varphi, \bar\Psi(k)), k') \mapsto (p k, \varphi, p' k')$ to $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transBase⟧. *The structural maps* (the point left to the reader, G.226): for $a = (q, \varphi, q')$ and $p(k) = q$, the third entry $k' = T(a, k)$ is the lift of $\hat\varphi(\bar\Psi(k))$ to $K'_{q'}$, which exists and is unique by Definition 17.7.2 (2) ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transAct⟧. So $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is in bijection with the pull-back $s^*K$, $(k, (\varphi, \bar\Psi k), k') \mapsto ((p k, \varphi, p' k'), k)$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transEquiv⟧, and the algebra of the structural maps is $T(1_q, k) = k$, $T(b \circ a, k) = T(b, T(a, k))$, $T(a^{-1}, T(a, k)) = k$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transAct_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transAct_comp⟧ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transAct_inv⟧, each by the injectivity of the fibre maps and the functor laws of $\mu$; $T(a, \cdot)$ is linear on fibres. *Witness* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflUC⟧.

**Definition 17.7.4.** *Reading.* The strong bundle structure on $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is read as the pull-back $s^*K$ transported along the bijection above ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.transBundle⟧: the text asks $\bar s$ to be a strong bundle map covering $s$ and a fibrewise isomorphism, and any such structure is identified with $s^*K$ by $\bar s$. Then $\bar s$ is the projection $s^*K \to K$ and $\bar t(a, k) = T(a, k)$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.transT⟧. The data of the definition are the two conditions on $\bar t$: a strong bundle map covering $t$, and a local strong bundle isomorphism.

*The list on pp. 827–828, from these two conditions.* (a) $\bar s$ is a local strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_src⟧: where $s$ restricts to an sc-diffeomorphism $W \to W'$, the projection of the pull-back restricts to a strong bundle isomorphism over it. (b) $\bar\iota(a, k) = (a^{-1}, T(a, k))$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transInv⟧ is a strong bundle map covering $\iota$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_inv⟧, as the pair of $\iota$ (sc-smooth) and $\bar t$ (a strong bundle map) into the pull-back, and $\bar\iota \circ \bar\iota = 1$ by $T(a^{-1}, T(a, k)) = k$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transInv_transInv⟧. (c) $\bar u(k) = (1_{p k}, k)$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transUnit⟧ is a strong bundle map covering $u$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_unit⟧. (d) The multiplication. *Reading*: the fibred product $M(\bar\Psi', \bar\Psi'')\,{}_{\bar s}\!\times_{\bar t} M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.BarFP⟧ consists of pairs $((b, k'), (a, k))$ with $k' = T(a, k)$, so it is identified with the pull-back of $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ by $\mathrm{pr}_2$ over $M(\Psi', \Psi'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.barFPEquiv⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transFPBundle⟧, the base with its sub-M-polyfold structure of Definition 17.1.8 (c) ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transFPPolyfold⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.TransFP⟧. On it $\bar m((b, a), (a, k)) = (b \circ a, k)$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transMul⟧ is a strong bundle map covering $m$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_transMul⟧, as the pair of $m$ (sc-smooth) and the strong bundle map $((b, a), (a, k)) \mapsto k$ into the pull-back. In the text's form $\bar m((b, k'), (a, k)) = (b \circ a, k)$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transMulBar⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transMulBar_eq⟧, and $\bar t \circ \bar m$ is $\bar t$ applied twice ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transT_transMul⟧.

*Witness* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ⟧: on the sheet of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$ over $g$, $\bar t(a, k) = g * k$ is $(g * \cdot) \circ \bar s$, so it is a strong bundle map and a local strong bundle isomorphism (being a strong bundle map is local over the base). For $\bar m$, at $a = (0, (-1, 0), 0)$ and $k = (0, 1)$ one has $\bar t(a, k) = -k \ne k$ while $\bar t(\bar m((a, a), (a, k))) = k$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelMultiplication · ReflectionBundle.isStrongBundleMap_transMul_reflection⟧. A non-trivial action on non-zero fibres; the witness is not weak.

### XVII.63 The strong bundle model $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ and the strong polyfold bundle structure, pp. 825–828

**Statement** (extra). The text uses the sc-smooth models of a strong polyfold bundle construction without stating their construction; Chapter 18 relies on them. The strong bundle analogues of Theorem 17.1.14 ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_17_1_14_bundle⟧, Lemma 17.1.15 ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.lemma_17_1_15_bundle⟧ and Theorem 17.1.18 ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_17_1_18_bundle⟧ are written out (G.227 settled), and with (ZHOU) and (LR) the model is a strong polyfold bundle structure of Definition 16.3.1 ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelStructure⟧, independent of the covering family.

*Reading.* "Covering family" for $\bar\Psi$ is read as: the base family covers $|C|$. Since the footprint of $\bar\Psi$ is $|P|^{-1}$ of the footprint of $\Psi$ and $|P|$ is surjective, this is the same as the footprints of $\bar\Psi$ covering $|E|$.

**Theorem 17.1.14 for the bundle.** *Step 1, the total space* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.ModelW⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelBundle⟧: $W = \sqcup_\lambda K_\lambda \to X = \sqcup_\lambda O_\lambda$, the disjoint union of strong bundles. *Step 2, the structure map* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelMu⟧: the morphism space $\mathbf X\,{}_s\!\times_P W$ is the disjoint union of the pull-backs $s^*K_\lambda = M(\bar\Psi_\lambda, \bar\Psi_{\lambda'})$, and $\mu$ is $\bar t$ on every piece. It is a strong bundle map covering $t$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_modelMu⟧ and a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_modelMu⟧, because both properties hold on each piece (Definition 17.7.4) and are local; $\mu(1_x, w) = w$ and $\mu(\psi \circ \gamma, w) = \mu(\psi, \mu(\gamma, w))$ are $T(1, k) = k$ and $T(b \circ a, k) = T(b, T(a, k))$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelMu_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelMu_comp⟧. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelEpStrongBundle⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSEP⟧: these are the conditions of Definition 8.3.1, so $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ is a strong bundle over the ep-groupoid $X_\Psi$ of Theorem 17.1.14, an object of $\mathcal{SEP}$.

**Lemma 17.1.15 for the bundle.** *Step 1, the functor* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor⟧: $\bar\Gamma(k_\lambda) = \bar\Psi_\lambda(k_\lambda)$ and $\bar\Gamma(\varphi, k) = (\Gamma_\Psi(\varphi), \bar\Psi_\lambda(k))$; it is a functor because $\bar\Gamma(\mu(\varphi, k)) = \hat\varphi(\bar\Gamma(k))$ (the defining property of $T$). It covers $\Gamma_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proj_comp_bundleModelFunctor⟧ and is a Banach space isomorphism on every fibre ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFiber⟧. *Step 2, fully faithful* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor_full⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor_faithful⟧: a morphism $\bar\Psi_\lambda(k) \to \bar\Psi_{\lambda'}(k')$ lies over a morphism of $C$, which is $\Gamma_\Psi(\varphi)$ by the fullness of $\Gamma_\Psi$ (Lemma 17.1.15), and $\mu(\varphi, k) = k'$ by the injectivity of $\bar\Psi_{\lambda'}$; faithfulness from that of $\Gamma_\Psi$. *Step 3, essentially surjective for a covering family* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor_essSurj⟧: for $e \in E$ over $\alpha$, Lemma 17.1.15 gives $\varphi : \Gamma_\Psi(x) \to \alpha$; $\hat\varphi^{-1}(e)$ lies over $\Gamma_\Psi(x)$ and is the image of an element $w$ of the fibre over $x$ (Definition 17.7.2 (2)); then $(\varphi, \bar\Gamma(w))$ is an isomorphism $\bar\Gamma(w) \to e$. *Step 4, the orbit spaces* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isHomeomorph_bundleModelOrbitMap⟧: $|\bar\Gamma|$ is continuous and open because every $|\bar\Psi_\lambda|$ is an open embedding for the topology of Definition 17.7.1 (Definition 17.7.2 (3)), and bijective because $\bar\Gamma$ is an equivalence ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor_isEquivalence⟧. $|E|$ carries the topology of Definition 17.7.1, not a quotient topology.

**Theorem 17.1.18 for the bundle.** *Step 1, inclusions are strong bundle equivalences* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equivalences_bundleModelMap⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equivalences_bundleIncl⟧: for $\bar\Psi = \bar\Phi \circ \sigma$ the inclusion $W_{\bar\Psi} \to W_{\bar\Phi}$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleIncl⟧ covers the base inclusion, which is an equivalence when $\Psi$ covers (Theorem 17.1.18); it is injective on fibres, a local strong bundle isomorphism on objects and reflects $W_{0,1}$; such a functor is a strong bundle equivalence (Definition 10.4.1) ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · SEP.equivalences_of_isStrongBundleIsoOn⟧. *Step 2, the comparison* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleCompare⟧: the weak fibred product of strong bundles (Theorem 10.2.2) along the equivalence $W_{\bar\Psi'} \to W_{\bar\Psi \sqcup \bar\Psi'}$ gives the diagram $W_{\bar\Psi} \leftarrow W_{\bar\Psi} \times_{W_{\bar\Psi \sqcup \bar\Psi'}} W_{\bar\Psi'} \to W_{\bar\Psi'}$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleCompareDiagram⟧, whose class in $\mathcal{SEP}(\mathcal F^{-1})$ is characterized by $i(\bar\Psi' \to \bar\Phi) \circ \bar f_{\bar\Psi,\bar\Psi'} = i(\bar\Psi \to \bar\Phi)$ for every family $\bar\Phi$ containing both ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleCompare_comp_incl⟧. *Step 3, for covering families* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.BundleCoveringFamily⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleComparison⟧: $\bar f_{\bar\Psi,\bar\Psi} = 1$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleComparison_self⟧ (the characterization with $\bar\Phi = \bar\Psi$); the cocycle identity ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleComparison_comp⟧ (the characterization in $W_{\bar\Psi \sqcup \bar\Psi' \sqcup \bar\Psi''}$ for the three pairs, then cancelling the inclusion of $\bar\Psi''$, invertible by Step 1); a generalized strong bundle isomorphism (Definition 10.5.2) ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_bundleComparison⟧, since the second leg is an equivalence when $W_{\bar\Psi} \to W_{\bar\Psi \sqcup \bar\Psi'}$ is one (Theorem 10.2.2); it covers $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.baseFunctor_map_bundleComparison⟧, and $|\bar\Gamma_{\bar\Psi'}| \circ |\bar f| = |\bar\Gamma_{\bar\Psi}|$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orbitMap_bundleComparison⟧.

*Witnesses* ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflSingle⟧ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflPair⟧: one and two copies of $\bar\Psi$ over the reflection. In the model for two copies, $\mu$ sends $(0, 1)$ in one piece along $(0, (-1, 0), 0)$ to $(0, -1)$ in the other ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflPair_bundleModel⟧; the comparison for one and two copies separates the classes of $(0, 1)$ and $(0, 0)$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.refl_bundleComparison⟧.

**The strong polyfold bundle structure of Chapter 16.** *Hypotheses* `hZ` ((ZHOU) for the polyfold construction of $C$, Definition 17.1.22) and `hLR` (the local regularity property of the bundle model, Definition 8.4.2), as Definition 16.3.1 asks of a strong polyfold bundle structure; the bundle construction alone does not supply them. *Step 1* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelStructure⟧: the structure is $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ with (ZHOU) on the model, its paracompact orbit space, `hLR`, $\Gamma = |\bar\Gamma|$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelOrbitHomeomorph⟧ and $\gamma = |\Gamma|$, with $|P| \circ |\bar\Gamma| = |\Gamma| \circ |P_W|$. *Step 2, independence* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelStructure_equiv⟧: two covering families give equivalent structures (Definition 16.3.3) through $\bar f_{\bar\Psi,\bar\Psi'}$, which is a generalized strong bundle isomorphism compatible with the $\Gamma$ (above) and with the $\gamma$ (it covers $f_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, and $|\Gamma_{\Psi'}| \circ |f| = |\Gamma_\Psi|$). The strong polyfold bundle of Definition 16.3.4 ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelBundle⟧ does not depend on the family ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelBundle_eq⟧. *Step 3, (LR) for an effective $C$* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isLRLocal_of_isEffective⟧: (ZHOU) gives (LR) of the base model (Proposition 7.4.22), and an effective base with (LR) gives the local regularity property of the bundle (Proposition 8.4.3).

*Witness for `hZ`, `hLR`* ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · ReflectionBundle.reflBundleStructure⟧: the reflection has (ZHOU) ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · EpGroupoid.Examples.isZhou_reflection⟧ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_isZhou⟧ (at $0$ the reflection reverses the tangent vector, so the group of Definition 7.4.5 is trivial), together with (LUC), (SLUC), (LR), (SLR) ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.reflection_properties⟧; the bundle model has the local regularity property ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · ReflectionBundle.reflSingle_isLRLocal⟧. The structure is non-degenerate: the base model is effective and its morphism over $(-1, 0)$ is a non-unit automorphism of $0$ acting on the fibre by $1 \mapsto -1$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelStructure · ReflectionBundle.reflBundleStructure_nondegenerate⟧. The witness is not weak.

### XVII.64 Definition 17.8.1 (2017: 17.26) and Remark 17.8.2 (2017: 17.6): proper covering functors of GCTs, p. 829

**Statement.** *Definition 17.8.1* (same) ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · GroupoidFunctor.IsProperCoveringGCT⟧. *The sentence after it* (does not hold as we read it): the text adds that, in view of Definition 17.8.4, $|P| : U(y_i) \to U(z)$ will even have to be a homeomorphism; Definition 17.8.4 does not force this as we read it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absCovering_orbitMap_not_isLocalHomeomorph⟧. *Remark 17.8.2* (nothing to formalize): a recollection of the picture behind a uniformizer construction, with no statement (G.228).

**Definition 17.8.1.** A functor $P : A \to B$ of GCTs with (1) $|P|$ continuous and surjective, (2) $P$ finite-to-one and surjective on objects, (3) every $z \in |B|$ has an open $U(z)$ with $|P|^{-1}(U(z))$ the disjoint union of open neighbourhoods $U(y)$ of the points $y$ of $|P|^{-1}(z)$, and (4) $\varphi \mapsto (P(\varphi), s(\varphi))$ a bijection from the morphisms of $A$ onto Part I's fibred product $\mathbf B\,{}_s\!\times_P A$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · GroupoidFunctor.fibredMor⟧. *Readings.* The finiteness of $|P|^{-1}(z)$ is included in (3); "mutually disjoint" is pairwise disjointness; the $U(y)$ need not be connected, and nothing asks $|P| : U(y_i) \to U(z)$ to be a homeomorphism, as printed.

*Witnesses.* *Step 1, the sheet functor* ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · sheetGCTFunctor⟧ $G \ltimes (S \times O) \to G \ltimes O$, $(s, o) \mapsto o$, for a finite nonempty $S$ with trivial $G$-action ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · isProperCoveringGCT_sheet⟧: (1), (2) are clear; for (3) the orbit $|(s, o)|$ over $|x|$ is $|(s, x)|$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · π_eq_of_orbitMap⟧, so $|(s, o)| \mapsto s$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · sheetOrbit⟧ is continuous and $U(z) = |B|$, $U(y) = {}$ the sheet of $y$ work; (4): $(g, (s, o)) \mapsto ((g, o), (s, o))$ is a bijection. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · isProperCoveringGCT_reflectionSheets⟧: two copies of $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ over the reflection, $-1$ acting non-trivially on $\mathbb R$; the fibre over $|1|$ has two points ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · ncard_orbitFiber_reflectionSheets⟧.

**The sentence after the definition** ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_properCoveringModel⟧. The two-sheet model $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (\mathbb Z/2 \times \mathbb R) \to \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ (diagonal action, the reflection on $\mathbb R$) is a connected model of Definition 17.8.4 ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_properCoveringModel_connected⟧. It is a proper covering functor and $|P|$ is a continuous open surjection with finite fibres; under $|A| \cong \mathbb R$, $|B| \cong [0, \infty)$ it is $t \mapsto |t|$, which is injective on no neighbourhood of $0$, so $|P|$ is not a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absCovering_orbitMap_not_isLocalHomeomorph⟧. This is the point of Proposition 19.7.18 (the Chapter 19 item on Proposition 19.7.18).

### XVII.65 Definition 17.8.3 (2017: 17.27) and the construction (17.93)–(17.98): pointed M-polyfolds and the model of the data (i), (ii), pp. 829–831

**Statement.** *Definition 17.8.3* (same; p. 829) ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · PointedMPolyfoldCat⟧. *The construction* (same; pp. 829–831): the functor $\hat\tau : E \to G_\beta \ltimes O_\beta$ of (17.98) is a proper covering model with distinguished point $o_\beta$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · pointedModel⟧. The "trivial exercise" is written out (G.229 settled).

**Definition 17.8.3 and the data.** $\mathrm{Diff}^*_{sc}$: objects $(O, o)$, morphisms point-preserving sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · PointedScDiffeo⟧, with the forgetful functor to $\mathrm{Diff}_{sc}$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · PointedMPolyfoldCat.forget⟧. The data (i), (ii) over a covering functor $f = P_\beta : A_\beta \to G_\beta \ltimes \{\beta\}$: functors $A : A_\beta \to \mathrm{Diff}^*_{sc}$, $B : G_\beta \ltimes \{\beta\} \to \mathrm{Diff}^*_{sc}$ and $\tau : A \to B \circ P_\beta$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · PointedGeometricLift⟧. *Readings.* $G_\beta \ltimes \{\beta\}$ is the one-object groupoid of $G_\beta$, as in Definition 8.5.5; "finitely many objects" are finiteness instances on $A_\beta$ and its morphism sets, $G$ finite and discrete, and $A_\beta$ nonempty (from the surjectivity in Definition 17.8.1 (2)). *Step 1.* Forgetting the points gives a geometric lift of Definition 8.5.5 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · PointedGeometricLift.toGeometricLift⟧; $o_\beta$ is $G$-fixed ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · PointedGeometricLift.smul_basePt⟧ because every $B(g)$ preserves the point, and $\tau_\alpha(o_\alpha) = o_\beta$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringLift · PointedGeometricLift.τy_pt⟧ because $\tau_\alpha$ is a morphism of $\mathrm{Diff}^*_{sc}$.
